# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Project

Mediary — personal bilingual (Georgian/English) **media catalog** (started as movies; now movies + series + videos, with more modules planned). Monorepo:
- `backend/` — **Laravel 13 JSON API** (PHP 8.3, MySQL). No views; API only.
- `frontend/` — **React 19 + TypeScript SPA** (Vite, Tailwind v4, Radix/shadcn-style UI, TanStack Query, react-i18next).
- `Tasks.md` (repo root) — **the single source of current work; read it before starting.**

**Multi-user**: Sanctum cookie auth, per-user libraries, pluggable modules, super-admin panel. (The design doc `docs/I7-multi-tenancy-and-modules.md` was deleted 2026-09-07 — the rules live in *Auth & multi-tenancy* below.)

**Live modules today**: `movie`, `series`, **`anime`**, `video`, `song`, `book`, `board_game`, `game`, `note`, `bookmark`, `gallery` (+ **playlists**, a sub-section of `song`). Every module in `Tasks.md` §10–§15 is now built. Two cross-cutting epics constrain new schema. **§16 (the social layer) is complete**: public profiles (16.1), matching (16.2), chat with media (16.3) and chat's quota (16.4) — see *Public profiles*, *Matching* and *Chat* below. Every domain table carries a `visibility` column (`private` default) — **every new table needs one too, plus an entry in `PublicDomain::DOMAINS`** (the column alone shows nothing). §17 (per-user storage quotas) **is live in its basic form** — see *Storage quotas* below; all uploads must go through `StorageMeter`.

**Georgian is the working language of this repo**: `Tasks.md`, most code comments and all migration docblocks are in Georgian. Keep new comments in Georgian to match.

## Run (dev) — three processes, in this order

```bash
# 1. MySQL — the database now lives in XAMPP (C:\xampp\mysql\data\mediary).
#    Run XAMPP's mysqld directly (or via the XAMPP Control Panel). Do NOT also start WAMP's
#    MySQL — both use port 3306 and whichever binds it first hides the other's data.
#    Prefer the safe-start script — it also repairs the two failures this datadir keeps
#    hitting and prints the real log lines when it cannot (see "MariaDB crash recovery"):
powershell -File C:\xampp\mysql\dumps\start-mysql.ps1
#    The bare binary still works, it just diagnoses nothing:
"C:/xampp/mysql/bin/mysqld.exe" --defaults-file="C:/xampp/mysql/bin/my.ini"
#    ⚠️ No dump is committed any more (Tasks SEC-01, 2026-09-17): backend/mediary_backup.sql
#    is a local, git-ignored file — see "A database dump is never committed" under Gotchas.
#    Automatic dumps: C:\xampp\mysql\dumps\<timestamp>\ (daily 03:00 + at logon + on demand,
#    scheduled task "Mediary MySQL backup" → C:\xampp\mysql\dumps\backup.ps1, keeps 14 days)
#    ⚠️ That task was silently skipping most days until 2026-09-15 — see the note below.

# 2. Backend API  → http://localhost:8000
cd backend && php artisan serve --port=8000

# 3. Frontend SPA → http://localhost:5173
cd frontend && npm run dev
```

Root DB is `mediary`, user `root`, no password, `127.0.0.1:3306`. ⚠️ **Which is also what this desk uses today** — the block above is the ordinary setup again; see *ეს მანქანა* below for what was actually checked, and when.

## ეს მანქანა (გადამოწმებულია 2026-09-19)

⚠️ **Machine-specific facts live in this one block, with the date they were checked** (Tasks DEBT-20). They had been scattered through the file and two of them had gone stale — a claim about the machine ages faster than anything else here, so anything outside this block should describe the *code*, not the desk it runs on.

- **XAMPP's MySQL is on the default port 3306** (`C:\xampp\mysql\bin\my.ini`) and `backend/.env` matches (`DB_HOST=127.0.0.1`, `DB_PORT=3306`, database `mediary`, user `root`, no password). It is **not a service**, so it needs starting each session — `powershell -File C:\xampp\mysql\dumps\start-mysql.ps1` (that script **does** exist here; see *MariaDB crash recovery* for why it is preferred over the bare binary).
- **The app is reached at `http://localhost:5173`** (Vite) and the API at `http://localhost:8000` (`php artisan serve`). `frontend/.env` carries `VITE_API_URL=http://localhost:8000` and `backend/.env`'s `APP_URL` / `FRONTEND_URL` / `SANCTUM_STATEFUL_DOMAINS` all name `localhost` — i.e. the plain two-server dev setup.
- **`yt-dlp` and `ffmpeg` are installed and found** (`php artisan mediary:doctor` → four OK lines): `%APPDATA%\Python\Python312\Scripts\yt-dlp.exe` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\ffmpeg-9.0.1-essentials_build\bin\ffmpeg.exe`, written out in `backend/.env` because neither lands on PATH. `mysqldump`/`mysql` are found automatically at `C:/xampp/mysql/bin`. **`python -m graphify` works**; `graphify.exe` is still blocked by this machine's Application Control policy.
- ⚠️ **`mediary.local` does not exist here** — there is no hosts entry and no WAMP reverse proxy. `frontend/vite.config.ts`'s HMR host is therefore configurable (`VITE_DEV_HOST`) and defaults to plain localhost; see README's *Production* section.

⚠️ **History, for the other desktop only** (2026-09-17 → 2026-09-19): that machine ran WAMP's Apache on port 80 and its own MySQL on 3306, so XAMPP's MySQL was moved to **3307** and the app was served at **`http://mediary.local`** through a `mod_proxy` vhost that forwarded `/api`, `/sanctum` and `/storage` to `:8000` and everything else to Vite — which is why `VITE_API_URL` was deliberately empty there (same-origin, which Sanctum's cookie auth needs). None of that applies here, and none of it was a decision about *where the DB lives*: it was a workaround for two MySQL installs wanting one port.

### ⚠️ MariaDB crash recovery — read this before "fixing" a startup failure

⚠️ **Start it with `powershell -File C:\xampp\mysql\dumps\start-mysql.ps1`, not by hand** (added 2026-09-15). That script does everything the three incidents below taught, in order: it refuses to touch a live server, sweeps the replication junk, starts mysqld, and if the start died on the Aria log it rotates the log aside, repairs the tables and retries once — then `CHECK TABLE`s the Aria system tables and `REPAIR`s whatever is flagged. On anything it does *not* recognise it prints the real `[ERROR]` lines from the log instead of guessing. It logs to `dumps\start-mysql.log` and **moves, never deletes** (everything lands in `dumps\quarantine\<stamp>`). ⚠️ It starts mysqld through **`cmd /c start /B`, not `Start-Process`**: a `Start-Process` child belongs to the caller's job object, so mysqld dies the moment the launching shell exits — the server comes up, answers one ping and vanishes, with the log stopping mid-startup at `Server socket created` and **no error at all**. That is the same reason the app's own `BackgroundProcess` shells out to `start /B`.

XAMPP's panel says only *"MySQL shutdown unexpectedly… may be due to a blocked port"*, which is almost never the reason. **The message is useless; the log is not.** ⚠️ **And the log to read is `data\mysql_error.log`** (that is what `my.ini`'s `log_error` names), **not `data\<hostname>.err`** — the latter is a stale file last written on 2026-09-09, so reading it shows a week-old incident and sends you after the wrong cause. Three different real causes have hit this machine:

- **2026-09-03 — crashed Aria system tables** (`mysql.proxies_priv`, `mysql.db`, `mysql.proc`). They are **Aria** (`.MAD`/`.MAI`), so `myisamchk` does nothing; `aria_chk --safe-recover` on a stopped server is the tool. **Now prevented**: `my.ini` sets `aria_recover_options=BACKUP,FORCE` (was `BACKUP,QUICK`, which checked but never repaired), so the server repairs them at startup instead of aborting.
- **2026-09-09 — InnoDB `LSN in the future`** → `invalid undo header offset 0` → `Plugin 'InnoDB' registration as a STORAGE ENGINE failed`. The data pages were at LSN ~77 M while the redo log was at ~73.5 M, i.e. **the redo log was older than the data**. This is *not* repairable in place: `innodb_force_recovery` 1–4 all failed, only **5** started the server. The fix is always the same three steps — **force-recover → `mysqldump` → rebuild the datadir → import**.

- **2026-09-15 — a corrupt Aria log, and then 114 junk replication files.** Two failures stacked, and the second is the one that explains why "MySQL won't start" kept coming back after being fixed. First: `Cannot find checkpoint record at LSN (1,0x58e7)` → `Aria recovery failed` → `Plugin 'Aria' registration as a STORAGE ENGINE failed` → `Could not open mysql.plugin table` → `Aborting`. ⚠️ **`aria_recover_options=BACKUP,FORCE` does not cover this**: it repairs *tables*, and here the **log itself** could not be read, so nothing ever reached the tables. The fix is what the error line literally says — `aria_chk` on all Aria tables, then move `aria_log.*` **and `aria_log_control`** aside; the server builds fresh ones. Second, revealed only once the server got past plugin init: `Connection '' conflicts with existing connection ''` → `Adding new entry '' to master_info failed` → `Aborting`. The datadir held **114** `master-*.info` / `relay-log*.info` / `mysql-relay-bin*` files with garbled names (`master-@0002@0001@0011.info`, `master-@0003localhostphpmyadminpma@0012f.info`) plus a **24 KB `multi-master.info` that was almost entirely NUL bytes** — 5068 non-NUL bytes and not one printable string. ⚠️ **This machine is not a replica** (`my.ini` has no `master-*` setting at all and `server-id=1`), so all of it was junk: every `master*.info` was an empty config, no host, no user. ⚠️ **The mechanism is self-feeding, which is the whole point**: MariaDB scans the datadir for `master-*.info` on every start (multi-source replication), reads the garbage in `multi-master.info` as connection *names*, writes a **new** file per name, and then aborts on the empty one — so each failed start leaves the datadir worse than it found it. Every one of those 114 files was stamped with the timestamp of a single startup attempt. ⚠️ **A 0-byte `multi-master.info` is normal** and must not be swept — the server recreates it on every start; only a non-empty one is junk.
- ⚠️ **Aria tables can be clean and still refuse to work: `Table is from another system and must be zerofilled or repaired to be usable on this system` + `Corrupt`.** That is what `CHECK TABLE` says after the Aria log is replaced — the tables still carry transaction ids from the log that no longer exists. ⚠️ **The offline `aria_chk --zerofill` silently skips almost all of them** (it fixed 1 of 7 here, and run from `data\mysql` it cannot even find `aria_log_control` — it must be run from the datadir root as `aria_chk mysql/*.MAI`). What actually works is the **server's own `REPAIR TABLE`**, which has the correct control file already open: all seven came back `OK` immediately. ⚠️ **The server runs and serves queries in this state**, so nothing looks wrong until the next start — this is exactly the 2026-09-03 condition, and leaving it is how that incident recurs.

Rules that came out of the second incident, all load-bearing:

- ⚠️ **Never delete `ib_logfile*` to make the server start.** That is the top forum advice and it is exactly what *creates* "LSN in the future": a fresh log begins at a low LSN while the pages keep their real, higher one.
- ⚠️ **Never rebuild a datadir from `C:\xampp\mysql\backup`.** That folder is XAMPP's shipped "pristine" copy from **2019 and it is internally inconsistent** — its `ib_logfile1` is 78 minutes older than its `ibdata1`, and its log checkpoint sits at LSN ~300 k while its own pages are past 4.7 M. A datadir built from it *appears* to work and then aborts with the same error the moment anything makes InnoDB re-derive the system LSN. Use **`mysql_install_db.exe --datadir=… --port=3306`** instead; it produces a genuinely self-consistent datadir (0 errors, 0 "in the future" in the log).
- ⚠️ **Changing `innodb_log_file_size` is a destructive operation.** The startup resize deletes `ib_logfile*` and re-derives the LSN from the checkpoint — on an already-inconsistent datadir it *is* the trigger. `my.ini` therefore pins **48M, the exact size `mysql_install_db` creates**, so the server never resizes on boot. Take a dump before ever touching that number.
- ⚠️ **The data lives in the dumps, not in the datadir.** Rebuilding takes about a minute *if* a recent `mysqldump` exists; on 2026-09-09 the newest committed one was a week old. Hence the scheduled task above — and the local, **git-ignored** `backend/mediary_backup.sql` (or a `/backups` dump) should be refreshed after significant data changes. ⚠️ Since SEC-01 **no copy leaves the machine by itself** — git used to be the accidental off-machine copy, and keeping one somewhere private is now a deliberate job.
- ⚠️ **And that scheduled task was quietly not running, which was found on 2026-09-15 and is worse than it sounds.** `backup.log` held 09-09, 09-12, 09-14 and nothing else — **four of the previous six days had no dump at all** — and the 09-15 run returned `0x800710E0` ("The operator or administrator has refused the request"). Three causes, all in the task's own definition, all fixed: **`DisallowStartIfOnBatteries = True`** on a laptop (on battery at 03:00 it simply refuses), **`StartWhenAvailable = False`** (a machine that is off or asleep at 03:00 never makes the run up — for a laptop that is most nights), and **only one trigger existed** although `backup.ps1`'s own docblock says "daily + at logon". It is now daily 03:00 **+ at logon with a 10-minute delay** (the delay so mysqld is up and the run is a real dump rather than a `skipped: server not running` line), `StartWhenAvailable = True`, and both battery conditions off. ⚠️ **The lesson generalises: the backup was written, documented and registered, and still did nothing.** `LastTaskResult` and the last line of `backup.log` are the only proof it ran — check them, don't trust that the task exists. A verify run on 2026-09-15 returned `0`, `ok 2026-09-15_0956: 4 db(s), 3.97 MB`.
- ⚠️ **The system schemas are never restored from a copy** — `mysql_install_db` rebuilds them and the user DBs come back from `mysqldump`. Only `pma`@`localhost` has to be recreated by hand (`GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON \`phpmyadmin\`.*`); `root` at `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`::1` (no password) is created by the bootstrap.
- ⚠️ **`count(*)` lies under `innodb_force_recovery` ≥ 4.** That level skips the change-buffer merge, so a secondary index can read back empty. Confirm with `force index (PRIMARY)` and `CHECK TABLE` before concluding a table lost its rows.
- ⚠️ **Georgian in `mysql.exe -e "…"` is mangled to `?` by the Windows console** (the same trap as `tinker`/curl argv). Verify Georgian text through `php artisan tinker`, not through a `REGEXP '[ა-ჰ]'` on the command line.
- 🟡 **Still to do, and it is the one thing a normal session cannot do: install MariaDB as a Windows service** so the OS stops it cleanly on shutdown/logoff. Every unclean kill is a crash-recovery cycle, and that is what wears the datadir down in the first place. ⚠️ **This is the root cause of all three incidents above, and `start-mysql.ps1` only treats the symptom** — it repairs the damage a dirty stop leaves behind, it cannot stop the dirty stop from happening. Until the service exists, **quit with `mysqladmin -u root --protocol=tcp shutdown` (or the XAMPP panel's Stop), never by closing the window**. It needs an **elevated** shell — re-verified twice on 2026-09-15: `IsInRole(Administrator)` is `False` here. From an admin PowerShell:
  ```powershell
  & "C:\xampp\mysql\bin\mysqld.exe" --install MySQL --defaults-file="C:\xampp\mysql\bin\my.ini"
  Start-Service MySQL
  Set-Service MySQL -StartupType Automatic
  ```
  ⚠️ **Name it `MySQL`, not `MariaDB`** (corrected 2026-09-15 — the two names were written differently here and in the session memory). XAMPP's control panel looks the MySQL module's service up under that name, so a service called `MariaDB` starts and stops correctly but the panel keeps showing the module as unmanaged and its Service checkbox stays empty — i.e. the one thing that makes this worth doing (the panel, and therefore the OS, stopping it cleanly) is exactly what is lost. `xampp-control.ini` does not record the name, so this cannot be configured around. ⚠️ **Stop the hand-started `mysqld` first** — both would want port 3306. ⚠️ **The two `mysql|maria` services already on this machine are WAMP's** (`wampmariadb64`, `wampmysqld64` — both `Stopped`/`Manual`, pointing at `c:\wamp64\...`): that is a *different* datadir, so starting one of them hides the project's data instead of serving it. ⚠️ `queue:work` deliberately is **not** on this list — the background batch starts its own worker on demand (`BackgroundProcess`), so there is no permanent fourth process to install.

## Common commands

Backend (`backend/`):
- `php artisan migrate` / `php artisan migrate:fresh --seed` — schema / reset+seed (seeds bilingual genres **+ modules**)
- `php artisan db:seed --class=GenresSeeder` — reseed the 19 TMDB genres (ka/en) · `--class=ModulesSeeder` — reseed the module registry (`movie`, `series`, `anime`, `video`, `song`, `book`, `board_game`, `game`, `note`, `bookmark`, `gallery`)
- `php artisan mediary:bootstrap-admin --name= --email= --username= --password=` — create the first super-admin (`--promote` upgrades an existing account). **Run before `migrate` on a DB that already has records** — the ownership migration attaches them to that account.
- `php artisan mediary:storage-recalc [--user=]` — recompute `users.storage_used_bytes` from disk. The counter is normally maintained by deltas; run this after a migration, a restore from backup, or files deleted by hand.
- `php artisan notes:remind` — fire due reminders (Tasks §13.3). Normally the scheduler runs it every minute; start it with **`php artisan schedule:work`** (or a Windows Task Scheduler entry calling `schedule:run`). ⚠️ Without it the browser channel still works — the SPA's polling calls the same dispatcher — but Telegram/email only go out while the app is open. ⚠️ **Its `withoutOverlapping()` carries an explicit expiry (Tasks BUG-09).** The default is **1440 minutes — a full day** — and the mutex is only released when a run ends normally, so a Ctrl-C on `schedule:work`, a sleeping laptop or a PHP fatal mid-run left it locked and **not one reminder fired for the next 24 hours, silently**. `releaseOnTerminationSignals` does not cover that: `pcntl` does not exist on Windows, and a fatal or a sleep sends no signal at all. It is `withoutOverlapping(5)` now, and the short expiry is safe **because overlap is harmless here** — the dispatcher claims each reminder with a conditional `UPDATE` before sending, so a second runner gets 0 rows. ⚠️ `runInBackground()` was deliberately not added: it is the only scheduled command, so nothing is blocked, and it would enable a separate Windows process-spawn path — exactly where `ProcessEnv` already taught this project a lesson.
- `php artisan test` — PHPUnit tests · `./vendor/bin/pint` — format
- `php artisan tinker` — REPL. **Georgian text: pass via heredoc (stdin), never via curl `-d` args** — the Windows console mangles UTF-8 in argv to `?`.

Frontend (`frontend/`):
- `npm run dev` · `npm run build` (runs `tsc -b` typecheck then Vite build) · `npm run lint` · **`npm test`** (Vitest, `vitest run`; `npm run test:watch` to watch)
- Verify changes with `npm run build` + API curls. **Do not use Playwright/screenshots** (user preference).

Knowledge graph:
- `python -m graphify update .` — refresh the graph after code changes. ⚠️ **Do not call `graphify` / `graphify.exe`**: the bare name has no `.exe` so Bash reports `Permission denied`, and the `.exe` is blocked by this machine's **Application Control policy**. `python -m graphify` is the working entry point. (`python -m graphify.cli` exits 255 — wrong module.)

## Architecture

**Data model** (`backend/app/Models`): `Movie` and `Series` are parallel domains. Both link to shared `Genre` / `CastMember` via **polymorphic** pivots `genreables` / `castables` (morph aliases `movie`/`series` registered in `AppServiceProvider::enforceMorphMap`; pivot carries `character`, `billing_order`, **`is_manual`**). Bilingual text lives in per-entity translation tables (`movie_translations`, `series_translations`) surfaced via accessors (`title_ka`/`title_en`, `description_ka`/`description_en`) so the API shape stays flat; genres/cast have their own translation tables. `cast_members.gender` mirrors TMDB's coding (1=female, 2=male, 0/3=unspecified) and drives the gallery's female/male cast filters (§10); it is null on records synced before 2026-09-03 and is backfilled on demand. **`status` is a per-user dictionary row since 2026-09-12** (`status_id` → `statuses`, §6.4 — see *Statuses* below; the old enum column is gone); `is_favorite`; `sync_status`. **Movies have franchise/collection** (`tmdb_collection_id`, `franchise_next` badge, clustering); **series do not** (TMDB TV has no `belongs_to_collection`) but add `seasons`/`episodes`. **`Anime` is the third domain, live since 2026-09-11** (§7.1) — its own `animes`/`anime_translations` tables, controller, policy, resource and enricher, but the *shared* `genres`/`cast_members` under morph alias `anime`. ⚠️ **The set of TMDB-backed domains is one map, `App\Support\MediaDomain`** (`TYPES`, `TV_TYPES`, `castRelation()` — `movies`/`series`/`animes`, model/enricher lookup): `['movie', 'series']` used to be written out in fourteen places — validation, sync, gallery, the console command, route `whereIn` — and missing one is silent (a 422, or a domain quietly dropped from a plan). A fourth domain is one line there. ⚠️ **`Services\Enrichment\TvEnricher` is the shared body** behind `SeriesEnricher`/`AnimeEnricher`: TMDB's `/tv/*` serves both, and the only real difference is the poster morph alias.

**Manual cast management (2026-09-13)** — until then a record's cast came **only** from TMDB (`MovieEnricher`/`TvEnricher` → `$record->cast()->sync()`), so a person the source didn't list could not exist in the library at all. `Api\RecordCastController` + `App\Support\CastSync` add it: `GET /api/cast/search?q=&type=&id=` (the local dictionary **and** TMDB's `/search/person` in one response, each row tagged `source: local|tmdb` and `attached`), `POST /api/media/cast/{type}/{id}` (attach), `PATCH|DELETE /api/media/cast/{type}/{id}/{castMember}` (the pivot's role/order, and detach). UI: the cast section on `MoviePage` (all three media domains) with an "add" button, `components/CastMemberDialog.tsx` and `components/CastRoleDialog.tsx`. Eight things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`sync()` silently deletes a hand-added member** — that is the whole reason `castables.is_manual` exists. All three sync sites (`MovieEnricher`, `TvEnricher`, `ItemSyncer::applyCast`) now go through **`CastSync::fromSource()`**, which re-adds every `is_manual` row the source doesn't mention. A member present in both **stays manual**: TMDB's credits change, and the next sync would otherwise drop the user's explicit choice.
- ⚠️ **`cast_members` is global, so attaching must never create a row blindly.** `CastSync::resolve()` matches `tmdb_person_id` first, then a normalised name (case, spacing and punctuation stripped) **in both languages**. The comparison is done in PHP, not SQL: Georgian is unaffected by `LOWER()` and a `COLLATE` would answer differently on MySQL and sqlite.
- ⚠️ **Detaching never deletes the dictionary row** — someone else's film references the same person and their `gallery_images` (every account's) would be orphaned.
- ⚠️ **The permission is spelled out as `update` on the routes** (`permission:@type,update`), not derived: `EnsureModulePermission` would read POST as `create` and DELETE as `delete`, while all three are *editing the record*. Same fix `/translations/{type}/{id}` already uses.
- ⚠️ **`GET /cast/search` must stay above `GET /cast/{castMember}`** in `routes/api.php` or "search" is read as an id (the `/gallery/{type}/{id}` rule); `{castMember}` also gained `whereNumber`.
- ⚠️ **Hand entry is the normal path for a Georgian name**, not a fallback: TMDB's `/search/person` answers Latin only, so `ნინო ქასრაძე` is 0 results there. The dialog therefore always offers the manual fields, and a TMDB outage is a **200 with `tmdb: false`** (`null` = no key) rather than a 5xx — a dead source must not block manual entry.
- ⚠️ **A TMDB profile photo downloaded this way is a shared file** (`cast/photos`, named after the person id) and **does not count against the quota** — 19.4/B, the same rule posters follow.
- ⚠️ **`castables` is not a model**, so `AuditObserver` cannot see it: attach/detach are written explicitly as `AuditLog::ACTION_CAST_ATTACH`/`_DETACH`, **filed on the record** (not on the actor) — "who added this person to this film" is the film's history, and the dictionary is global.

⚠️ **A shared `{type}` endpoint must spell its permission out** (audit §A4, fixed 2026-09-14): `POST /media/sync/{type}/{id}` sat under a bare `permission:@type`, whose last URL segment is the numeric id — so `EnsureModulePermission` derived **`create`** for a call that *overwrites an existing record*. A create-only role could rewrite someone's title, description, poster, genres and cast; an update-only role got a spurious 403. It is `permission:@type,update` now, and `/lookup`, `/lookup/candidates` and `/discover` are `permission:@type,view` (they only read). The rule generalises: **derive the action only when the last segment says what happens** — otherwise write it.

**API** (`backend/routes/api.php`, controllers in `app/Http/Controllers/Api`): REST under `/api` — `movies`, `series` and `anime` each have CRUD, `PATCH .../status`, `PATCH .../favorite`, `POST .../resync`, `POST .../from-tmdb`, `POST .../bulk-status` (movies also `GET .../collection`). Shared endpoints take a `type=movie|series|anime` param (validated from `MediaDomain::rule()`): `POST /lookup/candidates`, `POST /lookup`, `GET /discover`. `GET /cast/{id}` returns both `movies`/`series` (+ `suggestions`/`series_suggestions`); `GET /genres` (+ `GET|POST /genres/{genre}/items` — list/attach/detach/move/replace the records linked to a genre); `POST /media/sync/plan` + `POST /media/sync/{type}/{id}` (bulk sync, one item per request). Account/admin surface: `POST /auth/{register,login,logout}`, `GET /auth/me`, `PATCH /auth/{profile,password}` (`profile` also carries `bio` + `profile_visibility` — Tasks 16.1), `PUT /auth/settings`, **`GET /credentials`**, **`PUT|DELETE /credentials/{provider}`**, **`POST /credentials/{provider}/test`** (§21 — per-user API keys and limits), `GET /modules`, **`PUT /modules/{key}/settings`** (per-user, per-module JSON blob stored on the `module_user` pivot), `PUT /modules/{key}/public` (shows the module on the public profile), `GET /visibility/{domain}` + `PATCH /visibility/{domain}` (list / bulk — §6.1's central manager) + `PATCH /visibility/{domain}/{id}` (one record's visibility, all nine domains), the **status dictionary** `GET|POST /statuses/{domain}`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /statuses/{domain}/{id}`, `POST /statuses/{domain}/reorder`, **`PUT /statuses/{domain}/sections`** (the sidebar layout — stage 8) (§6.4, six domains — `?user_id=` is `super_admin`-only and exists for `/purge`), `GET /matches` + `GET /matches/{username}[/{domain}]` (Tasks 16.2), the chat surface `GET /chat`, `GET /chat/unread`, `POST /chat/with/{username}`, `GET|POST /chat/{conversation}`, `PATCH /chat/{conversation}/read`, `DELETE /chat/messages/{message}` (§4.6), `PUT /chat/block/{username}` (Tasks 16.3) and the **unauthenticated** pair `GET /public/profiles/{username}[/{domain}]`, `GET /requests`, `POST /requests/{module,storage}`, `DELETE /requests/{id}`, `GET /storage` + `POST /storage/recalculate` + **`PUT /storage/allocations`** (per-module limits, Tasks 17.2) + `GET|DELETE /storage/files` (`DELETE` also takes `paths[]`/`all` — §6.2) + `POST /storage/files/download` (zip) + `GET|POST /storage/orphans[/clean]` (quota and storage management, Tasks 17.1/17.2/17.5; the orphan pair is `super_admin`-only), **`/admin/backups`** (`GET|POST`, `POST /admin/backups/import`, `GET|DELETE /admin/backups/{id}`, `GET /admin/backups/{id}/download`, `POST /admin/backups/{id}/restore` — §22, `super_admin`-only), `/admin/{users,roles,requests}` (+ `PUT /admin/users/{user}/modules`) and the audit log `GET /admin/audit`, `GET /admin/audit/meta`, **`GET /admin/audit/summary`**, `GET /admin/audit/plan`, `DELETE /admin/audit` (Tasks §4) behind **`admin_access:<resource>`** (role-grantable, Tasks 1.6) and `/admin/modules` behind `super_admin`. **Bulk video changes got real scopes (Tasks §9, 2026-09-16)** — the user's words: „ტეგების დამატებაზე უნდა ირჩევდე, რომელი ტეგის მქონეს დაუმატო ახალი ტეგი, ან რომელი ტიპის მქონეს, ან კონკრეტულს, ან ყველას". The source picker used to be two-valued (`ids` **or** one `from_type_id`); it is five scopes now. Eight things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The vocabulary was not invented — it is `PurgeService::TARGET_MODES`**: `ids | type | tag | status | all`, with `PURGE_TARGET_MODES` as the typed frontend mirror. Two dictionaries for one concept would have drifted.
- ⚠️ **The scope parameters carry a `scope_` prefix and that is load-bearing.** `tags`, `type_id` and `status` are the **action's** payload, so the same names would have made a scope point at itself: „add tag X to everything that has X".
- ⚠️ **„How many will this touch" is the server's answer now** (`GET /videos/bulk-preview`), not a number counted in the browser. Preview and write both come out of one `records()` — `/purge`'s `plan()`/`run()` rule — and the client could not have counted the tag scope correctly anyway (see below). ⚠️ **It is a `GET`**: `preview` is not in `EnsureModulePermission::UPDATE_ENDPOINTS`, so a POST would be read as `create` and a view+update role would get a spurious 403.
- ⚠️ **A tag is matched in PHP, never with `LIKE`.** A Georgian tag sits **escaped** in the JSON column (`["ინფ…"]`), so `LIKE '%ინფ%'` could never match — `Video::tagKey()` is the same function the form and `PurgeService` already use. That is why `records()` returns a Collection rather than a builder.
- ⚠️ **Status lives on `POST /videos/bulk` too, not on `/videos/bulk-status`.** Video has a status since §6.4 and its bulk change must obey the same scopes; the older endpoint only knows `ids`/`from_status`, so routing the SPA there would have created a second definition of "scope". That endpoint stays for scripts and for the other domains.
- ⚠️ **`type_id`'s `Rule::requiredIf(action === 'type')` had to survive** — it is the 2026-09-16 guard that stops a bulk operation from stripping a type, which the forms no longer allow. The dead `NO_TYPE → null` branch in the panel is gone; „no type" survives only as a *source*, because an old record may genuinely have none.
- ⚠️ **No typed confirmation here** (the user's answer): changing a type or a tag is reversible, and the typed word belongs to `/purge` — spending it elsewhere devalues it.
- ⚠️ **The errors are machine codes now** (`no_tags_given`, plus `scope_required` from `RecordStatusController`), registered in `lib/errors.ts`; they used to be Georgian sentences, i.e. a 422 rendered raw and ignored the UI language. And `bulkStatus.*` goes through **`mediaKey()`**: it said „films" literally on a panel that also serves series and anime.
- ⚠️ **Tag scopes are video-only and that is not an omission** — the media domains have no `tags` column at all. Extending them to song/book/note/bookmark was asked and deferred.

**Bulk delete (Tasks 20)** covers **every module** — `movie`/`series`/`video`/`song`/`book`/`board_game`/`game`/`note`/`bookmark`/`gallery` — and adds `POST /admin/purge/plan` → `POST /admin/purge/item` (per-item, what the UI uses) with `POST /admin/purge` kept as the single-request/scripted path and **`GET /admin/purge/records`** (§25.2 — the target account's records for the `ids` scope); all three are `super_admin`-only and every write needs `confirm: "DELETE"`. Gallery (Tasks §10 → §3/§4 → **§8**) adds `GET /gallery` (**the summary**, not a list), `GET /gallery/groups` (`by=record|actor|source|provider|module`, `gender=` on the actor cut, `previews=`), `GET /gallery/photos` (`owner=` one group · `from=<domain>` · `provider=` · `sort=new|old|random` + `seed=`), **`GET /gallery/module-photos`**, `POST /gallery/plan`, `POST /gallery/{type}/{id}`, `GET|POST /gallery/cast/{castMember}`, **`GET|POST /gallery/videos`** and **`DELETE /gallery/videos/{galleryVideo}`**, `DELETE /gallery/images/{galleryImage}`, `POST /gallery/images/{galleryImage}/primary`, plus §26's **`GET|POST /gallery/albums`**, `POST /gallery/albums/reorder`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /gallery/albums/{galleryAlbum}` and **`POST /gallery/images/move`**. The cast surface adds **`POST /cast/{castMember}/resync`** (§8.1 — TMDB person details: biography, birthday, IMDb id and the official links). ⚠️ **The theme routes are gone** (§4.5) — `PATCH /gallery/images[/{galleryImage}]` and the whole `/gallery-themes` dictionary were removed with the feature. The field builder (Tasks §6) adds `GET|PUT /modules/{key}/fields` (built-ins), `GET|PUT /modules/{key}/custom-fields` + `GET|PUT /custom-fields/{module}/{id}` (phase 3) and `POST /custom-fields/{module}/{id}/file` + `GET|DELETE /custom-fields/{module}/{id}/file/{key}` (phase 4b). Chat media (§16.3) adds `GET|DELETE /chat/files/{message}`. The audit log (§4) adds `POST /audit/visit` — the SPA's "I entered this section" signal, outside every module group. Translations (Tasks 7) add `GET /translations/summary`, **`GET /translations/usage`** (the Gemini counter + the recent-translation log), `POST /translations/plan`, `POST /translations/{type}/{id}` and `POST /translations/genres` — the last two take **`sources[]`** (`tmdb` · `gemini`; an *empty* array is a 422, an absent key means both). Videos add `POST /videos/metadata` (probe a URL before saving), `POST /videos/bulk` (bulk type/tags — Tasks 4), **`PATCH /videos/{video}/status` + `POST /videos/bulk-status`** (§6.4 — the module had no status at all before), `GET /videos/{video}/similar`, `PATCH /videos/{video}/favorite`, `POST /videos/{video}/watched`, the per-user type dictionary (`GET|POST /video-types`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /video-types/{videoType}`, `POST /video-types/reorder`), plus files (`GET|POST /videos/{video}/files`, `DELETE /video-files/{videoFile}`) and notes (`GET|POST /videos/{video}/notes`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /video-notes/{videoNote}`). Board games (module `board_game`) mirror books: `GET|POST /board-games`, `POST /board-games/lookup[/candidates]` (BGG), **`GET /board-games/shops`** (the Georgian shop scrape, §7.2), `GET|PUT|PATCH|DELETE /board-games/{boardGame}`, `PATCH /board-games/{boardGame}/{favorite,status}`, the per-user genre dictionary (`/board-game-genres` + `/reorder`), files (`GET|POST /board-games/{boardGame}/files` — `?kind=image` is the gallery, `DELETE /board-game-files/{boardGameFile}`) and notes (`/board-games/{boardGame}/notes`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /board-game-notes/{boardGameNote}`). Bookmarks (module `bookmark`, Tasks §18) add `GET|POST /bookmarks`, `POST /bookmarks/metadata` (probe a page's `<head>` before saving), `GET|PUT|PATCH|DELETE /bookmarks/{bookmark}`, `PATCH /bookmarks/{bookmark}/{favorite,status}`, `POST /bookmarks/{bookmark}/visited` and the per-user category dictionary (`GET|POST /bookmark-categories`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /bookmark-categories/{bookmarkCategory}`, `POST /bookmark-categories/reorder`). Notes (module `note`, Tasks §13) add `GET|POST /notes`, `GET|PUT|PATCH|DELETE /notes/{note}`, `PATCH /notes/{note}/{favorite,status}`, the per-user category dictionary (`GET|POST /note-categories`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /note-categories/{noteCategory}`, `POST /note-categories/reorder`), files (`GET|POST /notes/{note}/files`, `DELETE /note-files/{noteEntryFile}`), reminders (`GET|POST /notes/{note}/reminders`, **`GET /note-reminders`** — every reminder of the caller, with its note —, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /note-reminders/{noteReminder}`) and delivery (**`GET /note-reminders/due`**, `PATCH /note-notifications/{noteNotification}`). Books (module `book`) add `GET|POST /books`, `POST /books/lookup[/candidates]` (Open Library), `GET|PUT|PATCH|DELETE /books/{book}`, `PATCH /books/{book}/{favorite,status,progress}`, the per-user genre dictionary (`GET|POST /book-genres`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /book-genres/{bookGenre}`, `POST /book-genres/reorder`), files (`GET|POST /books/{book}/files`, `DELETE /book-files/{bookFile}`) and notes/quotes (`GET|POST /books/{book}/notes`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /book-notes/{bookNote}`). Games (module `game`) follow the same recipe with two twists: `GET|POST /games`, `POST /games/lookup[/candidates]` (RAWG), `GET|PUT|PATCH|DELETE /games/{game}`, `PATCH /games/{game}/{favorite,status}`, the per-user genre dictionary (`GET|POST /game-genres`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /game-genres/{gameGenre}`, `POST /game-genres/reorder`), **videos** (`GET|POST /games/{game}/videos`, `POST /games/{game}/videos/reorder`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /game-videos/{gameVideo}` — Tasks §11.2), files (`GET|POST /games/{game}/files`, `DELETE /game-files/{gameFile}`) and notes (`GET|POST /games/{game}/notes`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /game-notes/{gameNote}`). Songs (module `song`) mirror that shape: `GET|POST /songs`, `POST /songs/metadata`, `GET|PUT|PATCH|DELETE /songs/{song}`, `PATCH /songs/{song}/favorite`, `POST /songs/{song}/played`, files (`GET|POST /songs/{song}/files`, `DELETE /song-files/{songFile}`) and notes (`GET|POST /songs/{song}/notes`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /song-notes/{songNote}`) — §7.4, the per-user genre dictionary (`GET|POST /song-genres`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /song-genres/{songGenre}`, `POST /song-genres/reorder`) and playlists (`GET|POST /playlists`, `POST /playlists/reorder`, `GET|PUT|PATCH|DELETE /playlists/{playlist}`, `PUT /playlists/{playlist}/songs`, `PUT /songs/{song}/playlists` — Tasks §15). Responses shaped by `app/Http/Resources` (`MovieResource`/`SeriesResource`, …) and return **both languages**; the frontend picks per selected language. ⚠️ The list filters `genre` (movies/series), `type_id`/`tag` (videos) and `genre_id`/`tag` (songs) accept a **comma-separated list** and AND them together — that is what the right-hand filter panel sends (`Controller::slugList()`).

**Route-level code splitting (Tasks §4, 2026-09-12)** — `App.tsx` imported all 37 pages statically, so every visitor downloaded the whole app: one **1,697 kB** chunk. The pages are `lazy()` now with a `<Suspense>` inside `<main>`, and the initial payload is **862 kB across 40 chunks** (79 → 40 after the fix below). Four things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **One static import from a non-page module undoes the whole thing.** `Sidebar.tsx` imported `GALLERY_CUTS` from `pages/GalleryPage`, which dragged the gallery page — and with it the lightbox (`photo-grid`, 57 kB), `react-select` (`selectStyles`, 81 kB), the date picker (78 kB) and `WebVideoDialog` (34 kB) — into the **initial** graph. The list now lives in **`lib/galleryCuts.ts`**, which both sides import, so there is still exactly one copy. **Check `grep "from '@/pages/" src/components src/lib` after touching this**: a single hit is worth hundreds of kB.
- ⚠️ **`Suspense` sits inside `<main>`, not around the whole shell** — the header, sidebar and **the player** must stay mounted while a page chunk loads; a full-screen fallback would have made every navigation look like a cold start (and `PlayerBar` lives outside `<Routes>` precisely so playback survives navigation).
- ⚠️ **The `.then((m) => ({ default: m.X }))` is written out 37 times on purpose.** The pages use named exports, and a generic `lazyPage(load, name)` helper loses the props type — `<LibraryPage type={type} />` would stop being checked, which is the one thing `tsc` is here for.
- ⚠️ **The lazy routes now sit behind `components/ErrorBoundary.tsx`** (2026-09-13): a failed chunk (a stale deployment, a dropped connection) or a throw during render used to unmount the tree to a **blank page** with the reason only in the console. Three things about it: the boundary is **outside `Suspense`**, because `lazy()` throws the load failure during render and a boundary inside would never see it; it **clears itself on navigation** (`resetKey={location.pathname}`, done in `getDerivedStateFromProps` — a `setState` in `componentDidUpdate` costs a second render and oxlint says so), or one bad page would keep the app looking dead; and the fallback **prints `error.message` on screen**, since „something went wrong" still sends you to the console.

**Frontend tests (Tasks §3, added 2026-09-12)** — the SPA had **none**: `tsc -b` and oxlint check types and style, so every behavioural rule in `lib/` was unverified. Vitest + jsdom now run `src/**/*.test.ts` (`npm test`), and the scope is deliberate — **`lib/`'s pure functions, not components**: `embed` (the iframe allowlist and the three players' event decoder), `tags` (`dedupeTags`, the twin of `Video::normalizeTags()` — the two sides must count identically or the form shows one thing and the database stores another), `statuses` (name fallback, `statusByKey`, and `statusTone`'s role fallback), `dates` (the `sv-SE` rule — `toISOString()` shows yesterday during Tbilisi's night hours — plus `formatRelative`'s „tomorrow"/„in 3 hours") `paged` (`{data, meta}` vs. `all=1`'s bare `{data}`) `galleryGroups` (the gallery's sort/section rules: a year-less record sorts last rather than as "year 0", a multi-genre record lands in its **first** genre only, and nothing is dropped for having no genre/year/status) `reminders` (the three reminder states and the list's order — „I paused it" and „it will never fire again" are different actions, and the server's `id` order buries „what's next") and `FileViewer`'s `kindOf` (a wrong answer shows as an empty box — a PDF in a `<video>` — and neither types nor lint can see it) and `searchResults` (`highlightParts` — the match must be found **inside** a Georgian word and a query containing `(` must not be read as a pattern — and `resultPath`, where a wrong answer is a browser 404 rather than an error) and `filters` (`filterKey`'s order-insensitive comparison and `filterCount` — the two facts that decided „is the Apply button live" and „how many filters are on" on eight pages by hand). ⚠️ **The config is its own `vitest.config.ts`, not a `test` block in `vite.config.ts`** — the app config loads the React and Tailwind plugins, which pure-function tests never need. ⚠️ **jsdom is load-bearing, not comfort**: `playableEmbedSrc()` reads `window.location.origin` for YouTube, i.e. the one case `enablejsapi`/`origin` exists for could not be tested in node at all. ⚠️ **`useDragReorder`'s `moveBy()` is still untested** — it lives inside a hook, so it needs `@testing-library/react`; that dependency was not added for one function. ⚠️ **Since 2026-09-13 there are exactly two *component* tests and both are deliberate** (`components/gallery/GroupsCut.test.ts` and `components/NoteReminders.test.ts`): the bug it pins — the web-search dialogs rendered in only one of the component's two return branches — is invisible to types, to lint and to any pure-function test, and only mounting the thing catches it. The second pins which *endpoint* the reminder editor calls — a backend test can say „PATCH updates" but not „the UI sends a PATCH", which is the whole bug — and, since the rework, that the reminders **dialog** actually opens and names the note it is bound to. Both use **`react-dom/client` + React 19's `act()`** and `vi.mock` for the API modules, so **no testing library was added** and the files stay `.ts` (elements via `createElement`) so `vitest.config.ts`'s `include` doesn't have to change. ⚠️ **`vi.mock`'s factory is hoisted above every `const`**, so shared mock functions go through **`vi.hoisted`** — the wrapper-lambda workaround compiles but throws away the argument types `tsc` is here for. ⚠️ Two harness facts cost time and are worth knowing: one `act()` is not enough — react-query settles on the **next tick**, so a `flush()` helper (`setTimeout 0` inside `act`) is needed before asserting; and `TooltipProvider` must wrap the tree, because it lives in `main.tsx` and half the dialogs contain a tooltip. ⚠️ **An editable row never takes `key={i}` — `lib/rowKeys.ts` (Tasks BUG-11).** The link and DLC rows in `GameForm`, `BoardGameForm` and `NoteForm` were keyed by array index while carrying a delete button, so removing a middle row handed the old row's DOM to the next one — and with it everything that lives in the DOM rather than in state: focus, caret position, an in-flight IME composition and Radix `Select`'s own open/highlight, i.e. **an open dropdown suddenly sitting on another row's data**. `keyRow`/`keyRows` add a `_key` on creation and `unkeyRows` strips it at the payload. ⚠️ **`crypto.randomUUID()` is deliberately not used** even though it is the obvious choice: it exists **only in a secure context**, and this app is served over plain `http://` — on the other desktop at `http://mediary.local`, where it really was `undefined`. ⚠️ On *this* machine `http://localhost` happens to count as secure, which is exactly why the rule stays: the difference is invisible until the app is opened by host name (Tasks DEBT-20). Same trap `navigator.clipboard` has. A counter is enough, since the key only has to be unique within one list. ⚠️ **`tsc` cannot catch a forgotten `unkeyRows`** (`Keyed<T>[]` is structurally assignable to `T[]`; excess-property checks only apply to object literals) — what actually stops `_key` reaching the database is that all three controllers rebuild each row field by field, which is the server's behaviour and not our guarantee. ⚠️ A `key={i}` on a **read-only** list (skeletons, detail lists, `highlightParts`) is fine and 14 of them stay: no input, no deletion, nothing in the DOM to misplace. ⚠️ **`ui/feedback.test.ts` is the third of that kind (Tasks BUG-10)** and mounts the provider with **fake timers**: `onDismiss={() => dismiss(t.id)}` was a new closure on every provider render, so `ToastCard`'s `useEffect(..., [duration, onDismiss])` rebuilt its `setTimeout` on every toast added or removed **and** on every confirm opened — during a queue run the earlier toasts never reached their deadline and piled up on screen. The fix is passing the stable `dismiss` itself and calling `onDismiss(id)` inside the effect, so all three dependencies are constant for that card's life. ⚠️ **The progress bar hid the bug rather than showing it**: its CSS animation is a style, not an effect, so it ran to the end on schedule while the card stayed — „the bar finished and nothing happened" was the only visible symptom. ⚠️ Neither types nor lint can see any of this; only mounting with `vi.useFakeTimers()` and advancing between toasts does.

**List pagination (built 2026-09-12)** — every module's `index()` used to end in `->get()`, so opening a section shipped the whole library: measured on the real 353-movie database, `GET /api/movies` was **806 kB**, now **109 kB**. The single definition is **`Controller::paginated($request, $source)`** — `page`/`per_page` (default 60, capped at 500) — and every one of the ten list endpoints (`movie` · `series` · `anime` · `video` · `song` · `book` · `board_game` · `game` · `note` · `bookmark`) goes through it. Seven things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`all=1` is an explicit escape with exactly four callers** — `/purge`'s queue and tag pool, bulk status/type change, `MediaRecordPicker`/`GenreItemsManager`'s record pools, and the playlist's song pool. Those build a *scope*, so a page-2 record silently missing from the list would make the resulting delete or bulk edit wrong. Using it on a grid re-creates the problem this fixed.
- ⚠️ **The base-class constants are `LIST_PER_PAGE`/`LIST_PER_PAGE_MAX`, not `PER_PAGE`/`MAX_PER_PAGE`.** `GalleryController` already had a `private const MAX_PER_PAGE`, and narrowing an inherited constant's visibility is a **PHP fatal** — the whole suite died with "Premature end of PHP process" until the base names moved out of the way. A generic name in a base class squats on every child.
- ⚠️ **The frontend model is "one page that grows": `page` is always 1 and `per_page` increases** (`lib/paged.ts` — `useListLimit`, `readPage`, `PAGE_SIZE`; the button is `ui/show-more.tsx`). Real page-2 fetches would break the two things that compute over the *loaded prefix*: `MovieGrid`'s section grouping (`defaultGrouping`) and the franchise cluster, whose per-section totals would stop matching the list.
- ⚠️ **Movies cluster franchises inside the page**, which is only correct *because* the client always loads a prefix. `annotateFranchise()` computes the badge with its own query, so it works on any subset; `clusterByFranchise()` does not, and a real page 2 would split a collection across pages.
- ⚠️ **`per_page` must be clamped from *below* as well as above** (audit §B4, fixed 2026-09-14). `Query\Builder::limit()` **silently ignores a negative value** (`if ($value >= 0)`), so `?per_page=-1` removed the `LIMIT` altogether. `Controller::paginated()` always did `min(max(…, 1), …)`; the two endpoints that build their own paginator did not — `ChatController` (the whole conversation in one response) and, worse, `PublicProfileController`, **the only domain endpoint outside `auth:sanctum`**, which handed a stranger the entire public library. Anything that calls `paginate()` without going through `paginated()` needs the same `max(…, 1)`.
- ⚠️ **`all=1` returns no `meta`** (a whole list is not a page), so `readPage()` falls back to the array's own length — otherwise "show more" would sit there forever on a list that is already complete.
- ⚠️ **The player queue is still the *whole* filtered list**, not the loaded page: pressing play fetches `all=1` once (cached, and only on the explicit click) so a 500-song filter does not stop at 60. That rule predates pagination and deliberately survives it.
- ⚠️ **`paginated()` accepts a `Collection` as well as a query builder.** `VideoSearch::search()` ranks in PHP, so SQL can no longer take the `limit`; without that branch the one endpoint that most needs cutting would have stayed unbounded. `LibraryPage` keeps its numbered pager for `libraryPageSize > 0` and asks the server for the prefix covering the current page; at `0` (the default) it is one growing list.

**Statuses (Tasks §6.2/§6.4, built 2026-09-12)** — a record's status is a **per-user dictionary row**, not an enum. One table, `statuses` (`user_id`, `module`, `key`, `name_ka`/`name_en`, **`role`**, `icon`, `color`, `is_default`, `sort_order`), and `status_id` on `movies` · `series` · `animes` · `videos` · `note_entries` · `bookmarks`. The registry is `App\Support\StatusDomain` (domain → model + module + defaults), the behaviour is `Concerns\HasStatus`, and **one** controller serves all six (`GET|POST /api/statuses/{domain}`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /api/statuses/{domain}/{id}`, `POST .../reorder`, `PUT .../sections` — the `/visibility/{domain}` pattern). Frontend: `api/statuses.ts` + `lib/statuses.ts` (`useStatuses` / `useStatusMap` / `useMergedStatuses` / `statusName` / `statusTone`) + **`lib/statusSections.ts`** (the sidebar order, stage 8), the editor is a row in `lib/dictionaries.tsx` (`/dictionaries/<domain>-statuses`), and `components/StatusDialog.tsx` is its form. Fifteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`role` (`todo`/`doing`/`done`) is the whole reason this works.** Three places ask for *meaning*, not for a name: `MatchService` ("we both watched it"), `PurgeService` and the franchise badge. A user-typed name can't answer that (my „ნანახი" and your „ვნახე" are the same thing) and neither can an id (it's per-user) — without the column all three would fail **silently**. It is therefore required on create and never nullable.
- ⚠️ **Book, game and board game deliberately keep their enum** (the user's list). So both mechanisms are live at once and every "is this done / what statuses exist" question goes through one function — `PublicDomain::isDone()` / `PurgeService::statusesFor()` — never through an inline `if`.
- ⚠️ **The old `status` column was dropped, not kept alongside.** Two sources for one fact drift (the `hltb_*` and `Book::syncProgress()` trap). The migration therefore maps values → dictionary rows **first** and drops the column after.
- ⚠️ **`status` indexes must be dropped before the column**, and the migration reads them **out of the schema** (`Schema::getIndexes`) instead of naming them: `status` took part in three different indexes (`status`, `user_id+status`, `visibility+status`) and sqlite refuses to drop an indexed column. Hand-written names missed `movies_visibility_status_index` on the first run. Each one is rebuilt on `status_id`.
- ⚠️ **The API returns an object, not a string** (`{id, key, role, name_ka, name_en, icon, color}` or `null`) — including in `PublicDomain::card()`. The name lives in the **owner's** dictionary, so a stranger reading a public profile could not resolve a bare key; returning both separately would state one fact twice. `StatusResource::brief()` is the single shape.
- ⚠️ **`key` never changes.** Renaming edits `name_*`; the key is what ties the dictionary to old links (`?view=watched`), to `StatusDomain`'s defaults and to `PurgeService::TARGET_STATUSES`.
- ⚠️ **The relation ignores the `owner` scope** (`belongsTo(Status::class)->withoutGlobalScope('owner')`). `Status` uses `BelongsToUser`, so on a public profile or a match the other side's status would come back **empty** — the card would lose its badge and "both watched" would always be zero. The row is only reachable through `status_id` on an already-filtered record.
- ⚠️ **`$with = ['status']` on all six models.** Every list needs the badge, the filter and the role; a per-call `with()` would be forgotten in one of twenty places.
- ⚠️ **Defaults are seeded lazily and with the query builder, not `create()`** (`Status::ensureDefaults`). Lazy because a module can be enabled later (`VideoType::ensureDefaults`'s rule); query builder because the seed is written by the machine — through the model it would fire `AuditObserver` and the first page load would bury the log under twenty "status created" rows.
- ⚠️ **`AuditLogger` writes the status *key*, never `status_id`.** "3 → 4" is unreadable, and deleting the dictionary row would destroy the answer entirely — the same reason `subject_label` is stored as a copy.
- ⚠️ **Everything is deletable, including the last status and the default.** A record without a status is legal (`status_id = null`); deleting the default promotes the next row, or new records would silently get none. Deleting with `move_to` reassigns, and **`delete_records: true` deletes the records too** (stage 8, the user's answer) — exactly like every other dictionary, see *Every per-user dictionary lives on one page* below.
- ⚠️ **The sidebar layout is not a column — it is `module_user.settings.status_sections`** (stage 8, no migration): `hidden` (status keys and pseudo ids, hidden **from the sidebar only** — the status stays on records, in the filter and the form, the user's answer) and `placement` for the three **pseudo-sections** „all" · „favourite" · video's „downloaded". Those are not statuses (no row, no edit, no delete) but they sit among them, so the order is **one function, `arrangeSections()`**, read by both the dictionary page and `Sidebar`'s `sectionsFor()`. ⚠️ **A pseudo-section's place is an anchor, not an index** (`at` = `start` · `end` · the status key it follows): a new status is appended, and a „favourite" stored as „index 5 = last" would suddenly sit *before* it. ⚠️ Statuses' own order stays in `sort_order` (forms and the filter read it); one drag may change both facts, so the page sends each only when it actually changed. ⚠️ **Deleting a status also rewrites the layout** (`StatusController::forgetInLayout()`): left in `hidden`, a status re-created under the same name (`makeKey()` returns the same key) would be **born hidden**; an anchor pointing at it is moved to its previous neighbour so „favourite" keeps its place. A dead anchor on the client falls to the end rather than vanishing.
- ⚠️ **`all`/`favorite`/`downloaded` are reserved keys** (`StatusDomain::RESERVED_KEYS`, mirrored by `PSEUDO_SECTIONS`): `makeKey()` skips them, so a status named „Favorite" gets `favorite-2`. Before stage 8 it got `favorite` and `?view=favorite` silently opened the favourites instead.
- ⚠️ **`video` has a status now** — it had none before. That adds `PATCH /api/videos/{video}/status`, `POST /api/videos/bulk-status`, a `status` entry in `FieldCatalog`, sidebar sections and a `status` mode in `PurgeService::TARGET_MODES` (and in the frontend mirror `PURGE_TARGET_MODES`, whose `satisfies` types then force `PurgePage`'s maps to keep up). Its `MatchService` `both_done` is a number now, not `null`.
- ⚠️ **`watched_at` is set from the role, not the name** (`HasStatus::applyStatusKey()` — the single write point). ⚠️ Video, note and bookmark override `statusDoneColumn()` to `null`: a video's `watched_at` is a *playback* timestamp (`POST /videos/{id}/watched`), a different fact, and one column holding two facts is the trap `Book::syncProgress()` fights.
⚠️ **A count rendered beside an icon needs a fixed-width slot** — the reminder bell on `/notes` drew its number conditionally, so a note with reminders and one without had different button widths and the rows stopped lining up; the number sits in a `w-3 tabular-nums` span that is simply empty at zero. ⚠️ **The i18n keys `status.*`, `notes.statuses.*` and `bookmarks.statuses.*` are gone** — those names live in the database now, and a fixed translation key would render a renamed status under its old label. `books.statuses.*` / `games.statuses.*` / `boardGames.statuses.*` stay, because those three are still enums. The new namespace is **`statuses.*`** (dictionary chrome: titles, roles, "default").

**Status and „type" are mandatory in every module (2026-09-16)** — the user's instruction was literal: a record must not be saved without **one status and one type**, and an empty select must turn red and say so. „Type" is a different column in each module, which is exactly why the rule is easy to half-apply: `type_id` (video) · `category_id` (note, bookmark) · `genre_id` (book, board game) · the **pivot** `genre_ids` / `genres` (game, song, movie/series/anime). Nine things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The real gate is the backend, and it is `$must`, not `required` written out.** Every module controller's `validated()` opens with `$must = $record ? ['sometimes', 'required'] : ['required']` and spreads it (`[...$must, 'integer', …]`). The two halves are both load-bearing: on **create** the field must be present, on **update** `sometimes` keeps a partial `PATCH` legal (the old value is already filled, because create demanded it) while **an explicitly empty value is still a 422**. Plain `required` on update would break every partial patch in the app; plain `nullable` would let one `PATCH` strip a status that the form can no longer set.
- ⚠️ **The client check is a convenience, never the rule.** `lib/requiredPicks.ts` (`isEmptyPick` / `missingPicks` / `pickErrors`, unit-tested) exists so the answer arrives without a round trip and the field reddens — but it is one function for all eight forms because „empty" has **three** shapes here: `Select`'s empty **string**, a pivot's empty **array** and a cleared value's `null`. Each form used to decide that for itself, and missing one shape is silent: the form calls itself valid, sends, and the 422 comes back with no field marked.
- ⚠️ **The error keys are the backend's names** (`status`, `type_id`, `category_id`, `genre_id`, `genre_ids`, `genres`), never the form's camelCase. One set of keys means the same `<p className="text-destructive">` renders both the local check and the server's answer; two sets would light up different fields depending on which gate caught it.
- ⚠️ **Nothing is preselected any more.** `VideoForm` used to default to the first type and book/game/board game to `'to_read'`/`'undecided'`/`'owned'`; those are now `''` with a `validation.choose` placeholder, because a silently prefilled answer is not a choice. The enum three therefore need a cast at the payload (`form.status as (typeof BOOK_STATUSES)[number]`) — the submit guard has already proved it is non-empty.
- ⚠️ **The „no category / no genre" `SelectItem` was removed from the forms**, not merely hidden: leaving it would offer a value the server rejects. The i18n keys (`bookGenres.none`, …) stay — the **filter panel** still needs them, and there „no genre" is a legitimate question.
- ⚠️ **`POST /videos/bulk` was the hole the form fix would have left open** — its `type_id` was `nullable`, i.e. „remove the type" survived as a *bulk* operation and could strip a whole library in one request. It is `Rule::requiredIf(action === 'type')` now, and the SPA's target select no longer offers it. The source select still offers „no type", but **only when such videos actually exist** (`countOfType > 0`) — on a library where every video has a type it was a filter that could never match anything.
- ⚠️ **`storeFromTmdb` is deliberately untouched.** It takes a bare `Request` (only `tmdb_id`), so the one-click add from discover still works: genres come from TMDB and the status is filled by `HasStatus`'s `creating` hook, which already assigns `Status::defaultFor()`. That hook is what keeps „every record has a status" true **outside** the forms — without it this rule would have broken the quickest path in the app.
- ⚠️ **`songs` has no status and that is not an omission** — the domain has none at all (`StatusDomain` has no `song`), so its rule covers `genre_ids` only. Its test previously asserted that an **empty genre array clears every genre**; that behaviour is now a 422 and the test asserts the opposite.
- ⚠️ **73 existing tests failed the moment the rule went in, and that was the point** — every one of them created a record without a status or a type, i.e. the state the rule forbids. They are fixed with a per-file `…Defaults()` helper (user-aware, because the „two accounts, one film" tests create as somebody else), and `RequiredFieldsTest` now pins the rule itself: a data provider over all ten modules plus „an existing record cannot have its status cleared" and „a partial update without the field is allowed".
- ⚠️ **The dictionary delete dialog's „leave them empty" is now an explicit flag (Tasks GAP-09, answered 2026-09-18).** It is still offered — the three choices were the user's own answer on 2026-09-13 — but it travels as **`clear_records: true`**, and a request that names no intent is a **422 `move_target_required`**. Before that the server read it from `isset($data['move_to'])`, so an **absent key**, an explicit `null` and `move_to: <self>` all meant „leave them empty": a half-built form and a buggy client silently got the one state the mandatory-status rule forbids. `move_to: <self>` is its own 422 (`move_target_is_self`), and `clear_records` + `delete_records` together is a 422 rather than a silent pick.

**Roles & permissions (Tasks 1.6)** — `roles` table (`key`, names ka/en, `permissions` JSON, `is_system`) + `users.role_id`; the old `users.role` enum is **gone**. A permission is **module-internal CRUD** (`view`/`create`/`update`/`delete`), while *access* to a module stays in `module_user` — two separate mechanisms. `permissions` shape: `{"movie":["view","update"]}`; `null` means unrestricted (`super_admin`). ⚠️ **The `"*"` wildcard was removed on 2026-09-15 at the user's instruction** ("ჩახსნიე ეს ფუნქციონალი") — see *The wildcard permission is gone* below; a module added later must now be granted explicitly on every role. Enforcement is the **`permission:` middleware** (`EnsureModulePermission`) on the `module:*` route groups — the action is derived from the HTTP method, with `resync`/`watched`/`bulk-status`/`reorder` treated as `update`. ⚠️ `$user->role` is now a **relation object**: use `roleKey()` for string comparisons and `assignRole('key')` to set it (`User::creating` backfills the `user` role). Policies still check ownership only. Frontend: `useAuth().can(module, action)` for hiding buttons; `/roles` + `/roles/:id` (matrix) manage them. **The admin sections are role-grantable too (Tasks 1.6, 2026-09-06)**: `roles.permissions` also accepts the reserved keys **`admin:users` / `admin:roles` / `admin:requests` / `admin:audit`**, enforced by the **`admin_access:<resource>`** middleware. ⚠️ **No amount of module permission grants them** — the admin zone is only reachable through an explicit `admin:<resource>` key, so a power-user role holding every module still gets a 403 (pinned by `OwnershipTest::test_module_permissions_never_open_the_admin_zone`). ⚠️ **`admin/modules` and `admin/purge` stay `super_admin`-only** on purpose: disabling a module affects *every* account and purge deletes someone else's library, so neither belongs to a single section's permission. Frontend: `useAuth().canAdmin(resource[, action])` drives the links, the routes and the in-page guards — don't check `is_super_admin` directly for these three.

**A section permission is not a licence to hand out power (Tasks SEC-02, 2026-09-17)** — `PATCH /admin/users/{user}` sits behind `admin_access:users`, and until then it accepted any `role_id`, so an `admin:users` holder became `super_admin` in one request (and with it `/admin/purge`, `/admin/backups` — the whole database — and `admin/modules`). `AdminUserController::outranks()` + `Role::exceedsAdmin()` close it. Six things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **Two doors, both checked**: the **new** role (assignment) *and* the target's **current** role (editing, disabling, changing modules of, or **deleting** an account above you — `DELETE` would have wiped a super admin's library). Both answer **403 `role_escalation`**; `super_admin` passes everything.
- ⚠️ **Escalation is checked before „it's your own role"**: promoting yourself to `super_admin` is a 403, not a 422 — the 422 would hide what the request actually was. Any *other* change of your own role is **422 `cannot_change_own_role`**, super admins included (a demotion can never be undone by the demoted account itself); re-sending the same `role_id` is not a change.
- ⚠️ **„Exceeds" compares admin power only — `super_admin` and `admin:<resource>.<action>` — never module CRUD.** A module permission only acts on the holder's **own** library, so granting `movie.delete` gives the granter nothing. Counting it would have made an `admin:users`-only role unable to assign the ordinary `user` role (which holds CRUD on every module) or to manage ordinary users at all — the fix would have broken the section.
- ⚠️ **The comparison goes through `allowsAdmin()` on both sides**, the very function `EnsureAdminAccess` asks, so `permissions = null` automatically means „every section". Comparing JSON blobs would have drifted from the middleware.
- ⚠️ **`User::effectiveRole()` is the one „no role → `user`" fallback** (it was written out three times); the escalation check must compare the same role `hasPermission()` checks.
- ⚠️ **The UI hides the `super_admin` option from non-super-admins and disables your own role select — convenience only.** `me.is_super_admin` is the right test *here* (assigning `super_admin` is not role-grantable), unlike the section links above. `RoleApiTest` pins all of it, and disabling the guard turns four of its tests red.
- ⚠️ **The roles side has its own, stricter rule (Tasks SEC-03)** — otherwise `admin:roles` was the first link of the same chain (add `admin:users` to your own role, then SEC-02's door). In `AdminRoleController::store()`/`update()` **only `super_admin` changes which admin sections a role holds — adding *and* removing** (`changesAdminZone()` → 403 `role_escalation`): a role reaches every one of its holders, so SEC-02's „up to your own ceiling" is deliberately not used here. Editing **module** permissions on someone else's role still works — the SPA always sends the whole matrix, so an unchanged `admin:*` part is not a change. Changing the permissions of **your own** role (modules included) is **422 `cannot_edit_own_role`**; renaming it, or re-sending the same matrix in another key order, is not. Same order as SEC-02: the admin-zone check first, so adding `admin:users` to your own role is a 403, not a 422. ⚠️ `RolePage` still renders only for `is_super_admin` (Tasks GAP-10) — when it opens to `canAdmin('roles')`, the admin-section cards and your own role must become read-only in the UI too.

**Auth & multi-tenancy (I7)** — Sanctum **SPA cookie** mode (`$middleware->statefulApi()`, `SANCTUM_STATEFUL_DOMAINS`, `cors.supports_credentials=true`; axios uses `withCredentials`+`withXSRFToken` and hits `/sanctum/csrf-cookie` before login). Every `/api` route sits behind `auth:sanctum` except `/health` and `/auth/{register,login}`. Ownership is **per-user rows**: `movies.user_id`/`series.user_id` + the `BelongsToUser` trait (global scope `owner` + auto-fill on create), so every existing query, `withCount`, and route-model binding scopes itself — another user's record is a **404**. Policies (`MoviePolicy`/`SeriesPolicy`) are the second layer; `Gate::before` lets a super-admin pass any policy but the `owner` scope still applies to them (admin data access is only via `/api/admin/*`, which uses `withoutGlobalScope('owner')` explicitly). **`genres`/`cast_members` stay global** (shared dictionaries). ⚠️ CLI has no `Auth::id()`, so the scope is off there — `media:redownload --user=` exists for that. ⚠️ **That second layer did not actually run until 2026-09-14** (audit §A5): seventeen policy classes existed and `grep -rn "authorize(\|Gate::\|->can(" app/Http/Controllers` returned **nothing**, so ownership rested on the `owner` scope alone — which **94 call sites turn off explicitly** (`withoutGlobalScope('owner')`; all 94 are correct today, but the 95th would have gone through silently). It runs now — see *Record ownership* below. ⚠️ **`AuthController` must never call `$request->session()` unguarded** (fixed 2026-09-14): `/api/*` only *has* a session when the request came from a **stateful** domain, so `register`/`login`/`logout` returned a **500** to every other client — and, more expensively, that is why `AuthTest` could not be written at all (`postJson()` is not stateful). The single guard is `AuthController::regenerateSession()` + `hasSession()`.

**External sources — `App\Support\SourceLog` (added 2026-09-14, audit §D)** — nine clients talk to the outside world (TMDB · RAWG · IGDB · Open Library · BGG · SerpApi · Serper · Wikimedia · the Georgian shops) and all of them follow the same correct rule — "the source is down" ≠ "I found nothing". **What was missing was the reason**: twenty-odd `catch (Throwable) { return null; }` were completely silent and the whole of `app/` contained **six** real `Log::` calls, so "why did RAWG return nothing" (dead key? timeout? 429?) had no answer anywhere. Six things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The nine `get()` methods were deliberately *not* merged into one.** They only look alike: BGG returns **XML**, the shops return **HTML**, IGDB posts an APIcalypse body, SerpApi carries a quota counter. One artificial signature would have broken all nine. What is shared is exactly what is identical: the CA bundle and the failure record.
- ⚠️ **The CA bundle now lives in two places instead of fourteen** (`SourceLog::request()` and `SafeHttp`) — Windows' cURL ships no certificate list, so one forgotten `verify` silently kills a new client. `RegistryConsistencyTest::test_the_ca_bundle_is_configured_in_one_place` fails if it reappears anywhere else.
- ⚠️ **A source failure is a `warning`, never an `error`** — it is a normal state (that is why these endpoints answer 503, not 500) — and it goes to its own channel, `storage/logs/sources.log`, so the diagnostic history doesn't drown in application noise.
- ⚠️ **The status alone is not the reason**: `SourceLog::status()` also records a slice of the body, because RAWG and SerpApi write the real cause there ("The API key is not found") while the status only says 401. Verified live: a bad RAWG key now produces exactly that line.
- ⚠️ **`TmdbClient` retried a 429 by calling itself with no limit** — a prolonged rate limit would have recursed until the PHP stack or the request timed out, which on a single-threaded `artisan serve` means the whole app stops. It is capped at `MAX_RETRIES` now and the retry itself is logged.
- ⚠️ **`SerpApiClient` records only *successful* calls and that differs from `Translator` on purpose.** It looked like an inconsistency during the audit and it is not: **SerpApi does not charge for a failed search** (it refunds the credit) while **Gemini's quota counts rejected requests** — which is exactly why a 429 shows up there. Each client models its own provider; "unifying" them would make both counters wrong. `WebSearchTest::test_a_failure_is_not_cached` pins it.

**Cross-module search — `GET /api/search` (added 2026-09-14, audit §D6; rebuilt the same day)** — an eleven-module catalogue had no global search: `VideoSearch` (333 lines, mb-aware Levenshtein) serves **only** videos, and in the other ten a search is a plain `LIKE` *inside* the section. The first version answered "where did I save this" with **titles only**, which is not the question that gets asked: the user's words were "it must search absolutely everywhere — film, series, video, bookmark, **description**, cast — and the results page must say which module it was found in and show the text around the match". So `Services\Search\GlobalSearch` now carries a **registry of sources** (which texts each domain exposes), `App\Support\Snippet` cuts the context out of the matched text, and there is a real results page — **`pages/SearchPage.tsx` at `/search?q=`** — beside the header dropdown (`components/GlobalSearch.tsx`). Sixteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **It does not replace the sections' own search** and must not: theirs works with the filters and returns the full list; this one answers "where and in what context did I mention this". Different questions, different answers.
- ⚠️ **What is searched is a registry, not a `foreach` over models.** Each domain declares `columns` (real columns — the same list writes the SQL *and* the snippet), `json` (tags/platforms), `relations` (translations · cast · notes · files · game videos) and `custom` (the module's `<module>_field_values`). A column that is searched but not listed for snippets — or the reverse — is exactly how "it found it but can't say why" happens.
- ⚠️ **The snippet is the answer, not decoration.** `Snippet::around()` keeps N words on each side and is **all `mb_*`** (byte functions cut a Georgian letter in three), and it deliberately **does not trim to a word boundary next to the match**: "ინ" sits inside "ინფორმაცია", so cutting to whole words would throw away the very word that matched.
- ⚠️ **Highlighting is the SPA's job** (`lib/searchResults.ts::highlightParts`, unit-tested) — the server returns plain text, because raw HTML is never sent (the project's hard rule). It splits with `toLowerCase()` + `indexOf`, **never a `RegExp`**: a query containing `(` or `*` would be read as a pattern and silently answer a different question.
- ⚠️ **A JSON column stores Georgian escaped.** Measured, not theoretical: Eloquent's `array` cast calls `json_encode()`, so `["ინფორმაცია"]` sits in the database as `["ინფ…"]` and a plain `LIKE '%ინფ%'` **never matched a Georgian tag**. `jsonLike()` therefore also searches the escaped form — ⚠️ **with a driver-dependent number of backslashes**: MySQL's `LIKE` reads `\` as an escape by default, sqlite has none without an `ESCAPE` clause, so one pattern would have worked on one engine and silently failed on the other.
- ⚠️ **`cast_members` is the one source outside the `owner` scope** (a global dictionary), so its query explicitly demands that the person plays in **my** records — otherwise the search listed other accounts' actors, whose page here is empty.
- ⚠️ **A cast name has to answer twice**: the actor (their own group → `/actors/:id`) *and* the films they are in (a `cast` match on the movie). Only the second answers "what do I have with this person".
- ⚠️ **Title matches are ordered first in SQL, not in PHP.** With `id desc` a record whose *title* is exactly the query could land sixth — while the dropdown shows five. Plain columns use `case when … like ?`, media use `withCount` over `translations` (both work on MySQL and sqlite).
- ⚠️ **Matches are ranked by field and capped** (`FIELD_ORDER`, 4 per record): five matches in one record with the title match fifth would make the result unexplainable.
- ⚠️ **Relations are eager-loaded *filtered*, except `translations`.** Loading every note of every hit is waste; filtering `translations`, however, would take the record's **title** away — the accessors read that same relation.
- ⚠️ **FULLTEXT stays unused** — the project's existing rule (sqlite has none, and MySQL's index handles Georgian poorly). `LIKE %…%` cannot use an index, but it behaves identically in both languages, on both engines, and **finds inside a word** — which is precisely what was asked for. Measured on the live database: ~0.1 s for a two-letter query returning 1178 hits across 7 domains.
- ⚠️ **`LIKE` wildcards are escaped.** Without it `%` and `_` are pattern syntax, so searching "50%" silently returned the whole table.
- ⚠️ **Disabled modules never reach the database** (they are filtered out of the registry, not out of the results), or the search would surface a section that isn't even in the sidebar and whose page answers 403.
- ⚠️ **The group's `total` is counted separately, not `count($items)`** — the per-domain cap would otherwise always report the cap and "another 40 exist" would be lost. `domain=` asks one domain alone (the page's "show all in this section", `per_module` up to 100); the page keeps its own **overview** query alive beside it, or choosing one domain would erase every other domain's count.
- ⚠️ **Three domains are not modules** and that is why `resultPath()` exists: `cast` (global dictionary), `playlist` (inside `song`) and `gallery` (a video link). Everything else derives its route from `modules.route_base` — media open the record, the modal-based modules open their section with `?q=`, because there a record has no URL of its own.
- ⚠️ **The header's row *is* the page's content column — for the search only, since 2026-09-15.** `Header.tsx` is now three blocks: the logo in a `lg:w-60` box (the sidebar's column) and everything else — search + actions — inside the very same **`pageContainer()`** `<main>` uses, so the input's left edge sits exactly on the page header card's left edge and the actions land on the content's right edge. ⚠️ **A fixed `calc(15rem …)` does not work and that was the first attempt's bug**: the page container is `mx-auto max-w-7xl`, i.e. on a wide screen the content is **centred** inside `<main>` and its distance from the sidebar depends on the viewport width. Repeating a number can never match a centred column — only reusing the same container can. ⚠️ `flex-1` + `max-w-*` + `mx-auto` is what centres it: flexbox hands leftover free space to **auto margins** once the item is frozen at its max-width. ⚠️ **The actions moved out of that container on 2026-09-15** (the user: they belong in the corner): inside it they stopped at the *content's* right edge, so on a wide screen a finger's width of empty bar was left over and the row read as unfinished.
