They are now a direct child of `<header>` with the logo's own `px-4`, i.e. the bar breathes equally at both ends. ⚠️ **In-flow actions still pulled the search left on a wide screen, and that was fixed the same day** (the user: the search must line up with the container the page title starts in). The container centres itself only once the free space passes its `max-w`, and the two blocks reach that point at different widths: `<main>`'s space is the viewport minus the **sidebar**, the header's is that *minus the actions* — so at 1920px the page header was centred by ~40px while the search was not. The actions therefore leave the flow **exactly at the width where centring begins**, `min-[1520px]` (`max-w-7xl` 1280 + the sidebar's 15rem), and the middle block then occupies precisely `<main>`'s box. ⚠️ **The breakpoint is the whole safety argument**: below it centring never happens, so the in-flow layout is already exact and absolute positioning would only buy the old defect back — out of flow on a narrow screen the avatar overlaps the `max-w-xl` input. At 1520px the field ends at 608px and the actions start at ~820px. ⚠️ `inset-y-0` keeps the vertical centring, and `<header>` is `sticky`, i.e. positioned, so it is the containing block already.

**Server-side batches — `POST /api/batches` (added 2026-09-14, audit §D1)** — sync, gallery and translation ran **in the browser tab** (`ui/queue.tsx` sends one short request per record), so a 300-record run died when the tab was closed, the network dropped or the laptop slept. `App\Jobs\RunBatchItem` + `BatchController` hand the same plan to the server. Seven things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`Auth::setUser($user)` inside the job is the most important line in the feature.** The whole app rests on `BelongsToUser`'s global scope, which reads `Auth::id()` — and a worker has **no session**. Without it a job would see **every** user's records and a sync would overwrite them. ⚠️ It is `setUser()` and **not `onceUsingId()`**: the default guard here is **sanctum** (`RequestGuard`), which has no `onceUsingId` — the first version threw `BadMethodCallException`. Pinned by `BatchQueueTest::test_a_job_never_touches_another_users_record`.
- ⚠️ **The worker starts itself** (`queue:work --stop-when-empty` through `BackgroundProcess`, the same mechanism `yt-dlp` uses). That is what removes this task's only real prerequisite: there is **no permanent fourth process** to remember — the worker drains the queue and exits, and the next batch starts a new one. Parallel workers are harmless (`reserved_at` claims each job in the database).
- ⚠️ **A worker that cannot start is a 503 `worker_unavailable` and the batch is cancelled** — a written-but-never-run queue would have displayed as "working" and sat at 0% forever (`ytdlp_unavailable`'s rule, and the exact failure `download_status = running` taught in §B1).
- ⚠️ **The client-side queue stays, and that is deliberate.** It gives per-record feedback and honours the pacing settings, which a background run cannot. Both call the *same* services, so the outcome is identical — the only difference is who turns the loop. The hand-off is therefore an extra button, not a replacement.
- ⚠️ **Only a homogeneous remainder can be handed off**: a batch carries one `kind`, so a mixed queue would have to split into two, and the button simply doesn't appear. `add` and `purge` never go: the first completes in one request, the second is destructive and stands on a typed confirmation.
- ⚠️ **Permission is checked before dispatch, not inside the job** — inside is too late: the response has already been sent and the refusal appears nowhere. All three operations edit an existing record, so they need `update` (§A4's rule).
- ⚠️ **`tries = 1`**: retrying is harmful here — sync calls TMDB and translation spends Gemini's quota. A failure is recorded once and re-running is the user's decision. ⚠️ **But the failure has to leave the job (Tasks BUG-08).** `handle()` caught every `Throwable` and only logged it, so `failedJobs` was **always 0**, `processedJobs == totalJobs` and `failed_jobs` stayed empty: a run where every TMDB call answered 401 reported „300/300 done". It rethrows now, and the batch is not stopped by that — `allowFailures()` was already set, which is the one reason rethrowing had looked risky. The two silent `return`s (deleted user, missing record) are legitimate skips but are written to the log as well; counting `skipped` apart from `failed` is FEAT-03. ⚠️ **And rethrowing alone would have traded one silent stall for another**: `incrementFailedJobs()` does **not** decrement `pending_jobs` (the job is theoretically retryable) and `markAsFinished()` only runs at `pendingJobs === 0`, so a batch containing any failure is **never finished** in Laravel's own accounting — „running, 50%" for ever, the `download_status = running` trap again. `BatchController::payload()` therefore reports Laravel's `allJobsHaveRanExactlyOnce()` (`pending − failed === 0`) as `finished`, subtracts failures from `pending` and lets `progress` reach 100. ⚠️ Tests must set `queue.default` to `database`: `phpunit.xml` uses `sync`, which runs jobs **inline**, so a batch would be finished the moment it was created and the progress test would be meaningless. ⚠️ **A backend perf test must not measure the same route twice in one process (Tasks PERF-15).** `Illuminate\Routing\Route::getController()` caches the controller **on the route**, and a test's route collection outlives a request — so the second `getJson()` reuses the *same* controller and therefore the same injected service, warm request-scoped memos included. A `MatchService` memo that costs 9 schema queries on the first call measured **0** on the second, i.e. the experiment silently stopped being one. Resolving the service with `app()->make()` per measurement reproduces the production lifetime (one instance = one request). And assert the baseline is non-zero: two zeroes match vacuously.

**Record ownership — `EnsureRecordOwnership` (added 2026-09-14, audit §A5)** — the middleware that finally makes the policies real. It runs over every **route-bound model** and refuses anything the caller does not own. Seven things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **It is a middleware, not `$this->authorize()` in the controllers.** An explicit call has to be remembered on every new endpoint, and one forgotten call is a hole — the same reasoning that attaches `AuditObserver` in a single loop instead of logging inside controllers. Here the gate stands on the route group: a model got bound, so it got checked.
- ⚠️ **It must run *after* `SubstituteBindings`.** Before that, route parameters are still strings and the loop would find nothing to check — a gate that is silently empty. Hence `$middleware->api(append: …)`, never `prepend`.
- ⚠️ **`Gate::forUser($request->user())`, never the bare `Gate::allows()`.** The bare call resolves the user from the **guard**, not from the request. They normally coincide, but that is a coincidence rather than a contract — and it made the middleware untestable outside the full HTTP stack (the first version refused the record's own owner in a unit test).
- ⚠️ **The answer is 404, not 403** — the project's existing rule ("this record exists" is itself information), matching what `VideoDownloadController::show()` does by hand.
- ⚠️ **Two groups are checked and both are needed**: a model **with** a policy is answered by the Gate (so a domain can grow its own rule later); a model with **`BelongsToUser` but no policy** — section files, notes, gallery photos, reminders, 40+ of them — is compared directly. Writing forty near-empty policy classes would be the `makeKey()` duplication all over again, and skipping them would leave the hole exactly where it is least visible.
- ⚠️ **Everything else is skipped deliberately**: `Genre`/`CastMember`/`Module`/`Role` are **global dictionaries** (they have no owner — checking them would close `/cast/*` outright), and `Conversation`/`Message` are decided by **participation**, not ownership, which `ChatController` checks explicitly. `User` is only bound on admin routes, where `admin_access` decides.
- ⚠️ **The seventeen policies are now empty subclasses of `OwnedRecordPolicy`.** They were seventeen character-for-character copies of `return $model->user_id === $user->id;`. The classes stay so Laravel's auto-discovery keeps working and a domain can override later; the logic exists once. ⚠️ `OwnedRecordPolicy::owns()` compares with `(int)` casts — `user_id` arrives as a string on some drivers and `===` would refuse the owner. ⚠️ `RecordOwnershipTest` pins that every policy extends the base, has all three abilities (a missing one means `Gate::allows()` is false, i.e. **the whole module silently 404s**) and names a model that exists.
- ⚠️ **Its test calls the middleware directly rather than over HTTP, and that is the point.** On a normal request another user's record is already a 404 from the `owner` scope, so an HTTP test would stay green even if the policies were never invoked — which is precisely the state the audit found.

**Dictionary keys — `App\Support\DictionaryKey` (added 2026-09-14, audit §B3)** — every per-user dictionary turns a typed name into a stable `key`, and until the audit that twelve-line function was copied into **nine** models (`VideoType` · `NoteCategory` · `BookmarkCategory` · `BookGenre` · `GameGenre` · `SongGenre` · `BoardGameGenre` · `Status` · `Role`) — all nine carrying the same bug. Five things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **Truncate first, then check.** The old loop tested uniqueness on the *full* slug and only did `mb_substr($key, 0, 60)` on the way out, so the key that was checked was not the key that got written — **and the `-2` suffix that resolved the collision was cut off entirely**. Measured, not theoretical: a status name is validated to 80 characters, and `Str::slug` of 80 Georgian characters is **91** (`ჩ`→`ch`, `ძ`→`dz`, `ღ`→`gh`), so two names sharing their first 60 slug characters violated `unique(user_id, module, key)` — a **`QueryException` → 500 on an ordinary save**.
- ⚠️ **The correct version already existed in the repo**: `CustomFields::makeKey()` truncated before checking. The nine copies were simply written apart from it — which is what duplication does, and why the fix is one function taking a `callable $taken` rather than nine patched loops.
- ⚠️ **`HasDictionaryKey` covers the seven identical per-user cases** (the model supplies only `keyFallback()`); `Status` (module-scoped + `StatusDomain::RESERVED_KEYS`), `Role` (global, no `user_id`) and `CustomFields` (`_` separator, 50 chars, an in-memory `$taken` array) call `DictionaryKey::make()` directly with their own predicate. Reserved keys stay reserved — "Favorite" still cannot take `favorite`.
- ⚠️ **`withoutGlobalScope('owner')` inside the trait is mandatory.** `BelongsToUser` would cut the check to the current user, while the key may be taken in a row the scope hides (admin acting on someone else, or CLI/seeder where `Auth::id()` is null) — the uniqueness index would then be bypassed silently.
- ⚠️ **The loop is capped** (`MAX_ATTEMPTS`, then a random suffix). An unbounded `while` that queries the database on every step is a server-stopper the day something goes wrong, and the space is finite anyway once the base is truncated.

**Fetching a user-supplied URL — `App\Support\SafeHttp` (added 2026-09-14, audit §A2/§A3)** — two places let the server open an address the *user* dictates: `Services\Bookmarks\LinkMetadata` (a bookmark's `<head>`) and `Services\Serp\WebImageImporter` (a photo found on the web). Both had the same two holes, which is why the fix is **one class and not two patches**. Eight things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`'url'` validation is not a defence.** It happily passes `http://127.0.0.1:3306`, `http://169.254.169.254/` (cloud metadata) and `file:///…`. The scheme allowlist is `http`/`https` only — curl speaks gopher, ftp and file, and `file://` reads the local disk.
- ⚠️ **Redirects are followed by hand** (`allow_redirects => false` + a loop, cap `MAX_REDIRECTS`). Guzzle's automatic following checks **only the first** host, so `example.com/r` → `127.0.0.1` walks straight past any check done before the request. Every hop goes through the same filter.
- ⚠️ **The IP is pinned, not merely checked** (`CURLOPT_RESOLVE`). Between "I resolved and it looked public" and "curl resolved again" DNS can answer differently — that *is* the hole (DNS rebinding), not a theoretical one. TLS still verifies the hostname, so pinning does not weaken https.
- ⚠️ **Every resolved address must pass, not one of them.** A host can return both a public A record and `127.0.0.1`; "one good answer is enough" is exactly how curl would get handed the other one.
- ⚠️ **The range check is PHP's own filter** (`FILTER_FLAG_NO_PRIV_RANGE | NO_RES_RANGE`), never a hand-written list — it already covers 10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, `fc00::/7`, 127/8, 169.254/16, 0/8, 240/4, `::1` and `fe80::/10`. A hand-written list forgets one.
- ⚠️ **`user:pass@` is rejected**: `http://example.com@127.0.0.1/` shows a person one host and opens another.
- ⚠️ **The size cap used to be a lie.** Both callers did `$res->body()` — the whole response was already in memory — and *then* cut it; `LinkMetadata`'s docblock claimed it protected `artisan serve` from a huge page. The body is now read from a stream in chunks and **stops** at the limit, with `Content-Length` checked separately (a server may omit or lie about it). Verified live: a 100-byte cap returns exactly 100 bytes.
- ⚠️ **`truncated` is returned as its own fact** — for HTML a cut tail is fine (`<head>` is long past), for an **image** it is a broken file, so `WebImageImporter` refuses it rather than storing a corrupt photo and billing the quota. That is why `read()` deliberately fetches one byte past the limit: it is the only way to tell "stopped" from "finished".
- ⚠️ **`SafeHttp` does not `close()` the stream** — Guzzle's destructor does it when `fetch()` returns, and closing a live object would break `Http::fake()`, which hands out **one shared response** for every call. For the same reason the stream is **rewound** before reading: `body()` used to rewind internally, so doing it by hand made that our job (without it the second faked image came back empty — caught by `WebImportTest`).
- ⚠️ **The private-network block is switchable, not absolute** (`SAFE_HTTP_ALLOW_PRIVATE`, default **false**): on a personal install bookmarking a page on `192.168.x.x` is legitimate. A block is written to the log, or "why doesn't this link work" would have no answer at all.
- ⚠️ **Tests must not depend on DNS.** `SafeHttp` really resolves the host, so `example.com` in a test makes the suite need a network — and this project's tests run on sqlite `:memory:` and should work offline. `SafeHttpTest` and `WebImportTest` therefore use **`203.0.113.10`** (RFC 5737 documentation range): PHP's filter treats it as public and DNS is never consulted.
- ⚠️ **`Services\Video\VideoMetadata` is deliberately *not* on this path**: it only ever calls fixed oEmbed hosts (youtube/vimeo/dailymotion) with the user's URL as a *query parameter*, so there is nothing to resolve. A third place that downloads a user URL, though, must go through `SafeHttp` — that is the point of the class.

**Rate limiting (added 2026-09-14, after the audit)** — until then `throttle` appeared **nowhere** in the project: registration is open, so nothing stood between a weak password and a brute-force loop, and one account could spend the **shared** SerpApi (250/month) and Gemini (1500/day) budgets, which belong to the installation and not to a user. The named limiters live in **`AppServiceProvider::rateLimiters()`** and the global one is attached in `bootstrap/app.php` (`$middleware->throttleApi('api')`). Five things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The global `api` limit is 600/min and that number is load-bearing, not lazy.** The SPA's queue (`ui/queue.tsx`) sends one request per record paced by `syncDelayMs`, whose default is **200 ms** — i.e. an ordinary sync or gallery run is already ~300 requests a minute. A "sensible" 60/min would break normal work, not abuse. `RateLimitTest::test_ordinary_browsing_is_not_throttled` is the guard against someone lowering it.
- ⚠️ **The tight limits sit where the money is**: `web-search` (20/min — every call spends a SerpApi credit or a Serper page), `translate` (30/min — Gemini's free tier is ~15 RPM and `translateDelayMs` defaults to 4000 ms), `download` (10/min — one run is minutes and gigabytes). `GET /web/status` is deliberately **outside** the limiter: a button's enabled-state must not spend the budget.
- ⚠️ **`login` is keyed on IP *and* the submitted login, both.** IP alone lets one person behind NAT lock out an office; the login alone lets credential stuffing (one password against a thousand accounts) walk straight past. Two `Limit` objects from one closure.
- ⚠️ **The key is `user_id` wherever a session exists**, never the IP: two accounts in one browser must not eat each other's allowance, and two people behind one NAT must not block each other.
- ⚠️ **`throttle:download` is on the `POST` only.** Serving the file (`GET`) and freeing it (`DELETE`) are cheap, and putting them under the same ceiling would break a working feature to solve a problem it doesn't have.

**Modules (I2/I3)** — `modules` table (`key`, names ka/en, `icon`, `route_base`, `api_base`, `morph_alias`, `enabled_by_default`, `is_active`) + `module_user` pivot. `movie`/`series` are the first two entries (`ModulesSeeder`). Routes are grouped behind `module:movie` / `module:series` / `module:@type` (the last reads the `type` param for shared endpoints). Frontend nav and routes are built from `GET /api/modules` (`lib/modules.tsx` → `ModulesProvider`/`useModules`), so a disabled module has **no menu entry and no route**; `lib/media.ts` still supplies the static descriptor for domains that use the generic pages. ⚠️ The written recipe for adding a module **no longer exists** (its doc was deleted 2026-09-07); the registry checklist in `Tasks.md` §7.1 is what replaced it.

**Videos module (I5)** — the first non-media module and the proof of the recipe: `videos` table (`BelongsToUser`), `VideoController`, `VideoPolicy`, routes behind `module:video`. **Types are a per-user dictionary** (`video_types` + `videos.type_id`, `VideoType`/`VideoTypeController`/`VideoTypePolicy`, page `/video-types`) — the old `videos.kind` enum is gone; `VideoType::ensureDefaults()` lazily creates „ინფორმაციული"/„გასართობი" on first read, and the sidebar sections are built from that list with **„რჩეული" pinned last**. `videos.visibility` (`private` default) is the §16 column (`movies`/`series` have the same one) and **is edited from `/profile`'s `VisibilityManager`** — the per-record toggle was removed there on 2026-09-11 (§6.1), so the record page only shows a `VisibilityBadge`. `App\Support\VideoUrl` recognises YouTube / Vimeo / Dailymotion / direct file and builds the embed itself — **raw HTML/embed markup is never stored**; `VideoEmbed.tsx` re-checks the host against the same allowlist and sandboxes the iframe. Uploaded thumbnails, files (`video_files` — photos and documents) and notes (`video_notes`) all go to the **public** disk. `video_files`/`video_notes` cascade in SQL, but `Video::booted()` still deletes files one by one — a DB cascade fires no model event, so the file on disk and the quota counter would be left behind. Frontend registration for a non-media module is two lines: `PAGE_MODULE_KEYS` (`lib/modules.tsx`) + `MODULE_PAGES` (`App.tsx`).

**Local video download (Tasks §7.1, built 2026-09-12)** — `yt-dlp` pulls a video into the library: `Services/Video/YtDlp` (the only place the binary is invoked), `Services/Video/VideoDownloader` (quota + columns), `Console/Commands/DownloadVideoCommand`, `Api/VideoDownloadController` and `Support/BackgroundProcess`. Endpoints: **`POST|GET|DELETE /api/videos/{video}/download`** (start · serve · free) plus `GET /api/videos/download-status`. The four decisions came from the user on 2026-09-12: **`yt-dlp` + optional `ffmpeg`**, **best available quality**, **counts against the quota**, **private disk**. Eleven things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The file is columns on `videos`, not a `video_files` row** (`download_path`/`download_name`/`download_size`/`download_format`/`download_status`/`download_error`/`downloaded_at`). A video has at most one local copy, so the question is "does it have one", not "how many"; and a 1 GB file in `video_files` would render inside the attachments grid as a "document".
- ⚠️ **`download_status` distinguishes four facts and a boolean cannot**: `null` (never tried) · `running` · `failed` + `download_error` · `ready`. The UI shows a different button for each; collapsing them made "why is nothing happening" unanswerable.
- ⚠️ **The download runs detached, not in the request.** `artisan serve` is single-threaded, so a synchronous multi-minute download would freeze the whole dev server — the same reason sync/gallery/purge are queues. `Symfony\Process` is unusable here (it kills the child in `__destruct`), so `BackgroundProcess` shells out (`start /B` on Windows) to `php artisan videos:download {id}`. The SPA polls the list every 4 s **only while something is `running`**.
- ⚠️ **`videos/downloads` is private while `videos/thumbnails` stays public** — the first case of a private sub-folder inside a public root. `StorageFolder::PRIVATE_ROOTS` matches the *first* segment on purpose (so a future `notes-archive/` can't go private silently), so a second list, **`PRIVATE_FOLDERS`**, matches two segments — still segment-wise, so `videos/downloads-old` is not caught.
- ⚠️ **Such a folder must also be listed in `StorageMeter::UPLOAD_FOLDERS` separately**: the orphan scan picks one disk per folder, so scanning the public `videos` root would never see the private sub-folder and its orphans would live forever.
- ⚠️ **`referencedPaths()` is now a flat `table.column` list, not a `table => column` map** — `videos` owns *two* path columns and the map silently kept only one, which would have made the admin's orphan cleanup delete every downloaded video. `RegistryConsistencyTest`'s source-parsing regex was updated with it (its three anchors are what stop that read from passing vacuously).
- ⚠️ **Both binaries are installed on this machine since 2026-09-14, and neither needed an elevated shell** — the backlog had assumed for two days that they did, which is why the feature sat untested. `yt-dlp` is `pip install --user yt-dlp` (2026.08.19) and `ffmpeg` is gyan.dev's static build unzipped into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\` (9.0.1); **neither lands on PATH**, so `ExecutableFinder` still finds nothing and the full paths live in `backend/.env` as `YTDLP_BINARY`/`FFMPEG_BINARY` — that config existed for exactly this case. ⚠️ **The pip-generated `.exe` shim runs fine**: this machine's Application Control policy blocks `graphify.exe` specifically, not every unsigned shim, so the "invoke it through `python -m`" workaround was never needed. Verified end to end the same day: `available() === true`, a live probe (4K Big Buck Bunny → 635 s / 722 MB, i.e. the `bv*+ba` selector, so ffmpeg really is in play) and a real 19-second download that merged to `mp4` and reported `320x240 · MP4` in 5.2 s.
- ⚠️ **The quota is checked twice and both are needed**: before (yt-dlp's `filesize_approx`, so a 30-minute download doesn't start doomed) and after (the real size). Over quota after the fact **deletes the file** — a limit that can be exceeded is not a limit.
- ⚠️ **`storeLocalFile()` streams**; `storeContents()` would have read a gigabyte into a PHP string. ⚠️ **And the order is quota-first, delete-second (Tasks BUG-22).** It used to be the other way round, so on a full quota "download again" answered 413 with the old copy already gone — and worse, `deleteDownload()` only `forceFill`s the columns in memory and the `save()` never ran, so the row still said `ready` and pointed at a deleted file. The old copy's bytes are **credited** against the check (`freedBytes()`, keyed on `download_path` rather than `download_size`, since a failed run can leave the size behind): they are freed for this very download, and without the credit a record would compete with itself and replacing a file on a nearly full quota would be impossible. ⚠️ The delete cannot move into `run()` — `storeLocalFile()`'s `claim()` is an **atomic reserve**, so the new copy can never be claimed while the old one still counts.
- ⚠️ **A `running` that nobody is running must be reclaimable** (audit §B1, fixed 2026-09-14). The background process can die — the `artisan serve` terminal is closed, the machine reboots, PHP fatals — and `download_status` stayed `running` **forever**: `POST` answered 409, `DELETE` hit `deleteDownload()`'s early `return` (there is no `download_path` in that state) and did nothing, so that video could never be downloaded again without hand-written SQL. Closing a terminal is routine here, so this was reachable, not theoretical. The fix is `videos.download_started_at` + **one** predicate, `Video::downloadStale()`, asked by both `VideoDownloader::start()` and `VideoResource` (`download_stale`). ⚠️ **`updated_at` would not do**: it refreshes on any edit to the record, i.e. it answers a different question — the trap `watched_at` and `hltb_*` already taught. ⚠️ **A null start time counts as stale**: that is either a pre-migration stuck row or a hand-written one, and a permanent 409 is the wrong answer to both. ⚠️ **The UI had to change too**: while `running` the button was `disabled` and the delete button only appears for `ready`, so the backend fix alone left the dead end in place — the stale state now shows "try again", its own badge colour and text, and **stops the 4 s polling** (a dead run's status can never change by itself).
- ⚠️ **Missing binaries are a state, not a crash**: `YtDlp::available()` false → **503 `ytdlp_unavailable`** (registered in `lib/errors.ts`), and a second start while one **really** runs is **409 `download_already_running`** (two parallel runs would spend the quota twice). `ffmpeg` is optional but *decides quality*: without it `bv*+ba` cannot merge, so the selector falls back to `b` and the UI says so — what actually arrived is stored in `download_format` and shown.
- ⚠️ **`download` had to join `EnsureModulePermission::UPDATE_ENDPOINTS`** or a view+update user would get a spurious 403 from the POST. The file leaves the server through the ownership-checked `GET` only (404, never 403), `Video::deleteDownload()` releases the **recorded** `download_size` (the `StoredFile` rule) and is called from `Video::booted()` so `PurgeService` frees it too.

**Bookmarks module (Tasks §18)** — `bookmark`, built 2026-09-07 after the user picked it (and deferred anime) in `DECISIONS.md` §10. It is the **second module with no external source at all** (the first is `note`), so there is no candidates→draft flow: one probe, `POST /bookmarks/metadata`, reads the page's own `<head>`. Two tables: `bookmarks` (`BelongsToUser`, `HasCustomFields`, `visibility`) and `bookmark_categories` (**per-user dictionary**, `/bookmark-categories`, `ensureDefaults()` seeds 6 lazily). Routes: `GET|POST /bookmarks`, `POST /bookmarks/metadata`, `GET|PUT|PATCH|DELETE /bookmarks/{bookmark}`, `PATCH /bookmarks/{bookmark}/{favorite,status}`, `POST /bookmarks/{bookmark}/visited`, plus the dictionary (`GET|POST /bookmark-categories`, `PUT|PATCH|DELETE /bookmark-categories/{bookmarkCategory}`, `POST /bookmark-categories/reorder`). Five things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The category is a single column (`category_id`), not a pivot** — deliberately unlike `song`/`game`. A category here is a *folder* (a link lives in one place) and `tags` covers every other axis; that is `note`'s exact precedent. Consequence: the list filter is `whereIn` (so a comma-separated list is **OR**, not AND) while tags stay AND.
- ⚠️ **`domain` is a column, not an accessor.** Grouping and filtering happen on it, which SQL cannot do without a column. `Bookmark::applyUrl()` is the **only** place it is written (it lowercases and strips `www.`, so `example.com` and `www.example.com` are one group).
- ⚠️ **og:image is never downloaded** — it stays as a remote URL in `image_url`. Downloading it would spend quota on something already public. The only metered upload is `thumbnail_path` (`bookmarks/thumbnails`), exactly like a song's.
- ⚠️ **There is no unique index on `url`.** The column is 1000 chars; utf8mb4 makes that 4000 bytes, over MySQL's 3072-byte index limit, and a hash column is more ceremony than a duplicate bookmark is worth.
- ⚠️ **`POST .../visited` needs `visited` in `EnsureModulePermission::UPDATE_ENDPOINTS`** — otherwise the middleware derives `create` from POST and a view+update user gets a spurious 403.
`Services\Bookmarks\LinkMetadata` parses `<title>`, Open Graph/Twitter cards and the favicon out of the first 200 kB of the response; **raw HTML is never stored** (the `VideoUrl` rule) and a failure is a **200 with empty fields**, not a 5xx — a page that 403s a bot is ordinary, and it must not block manual entry.

**Songs module + playlists (Tasks §15)** — ⚠️ **the earlier "a song is a `videos` row" decision was reversed by the user on 2026-09-03.** A song now has its own module (`song`), because a sidebar section, an admin toggle and per-module CRUD rights in the role matrix all hang off a `modules` row — none of that is reachable from a sub-section. Schema: `songs` (`BelongsToUser`, `HasGallery`, `visibility`) with the **full music field set** — `title`, `artist`, `album`, `year`, `genre_id`, `duration`, `tags`, `rating` (1–10), `url`/`platform`/`embed_url`, `thumbnail_path`, `is_favorite`, `play_count`/`played_at` — plus `song_genres`, a **per-user dictionary** (`SongGenre`, page `/song-genres`, `ensureDefaults()` seeds 8 genres lazily). ⚠️ The global `genres` table is deliberately *not* reused: it holds TMDB movie genres, and "Rock" would show up in the movie genre picker. A song carries **one** `genre_id` (tags cover the rest) — a pivot can be added later if that proves too thin. ⚠️ **A song is multi-genre since 2026-09-07** (`DECISIONS.md` §5, answered against my recommendation): pivot `song_genre_song`, and `songs.genre_id` is **gone**. The consequences mirror `game`'s — the list filter is `whereHas` (AND across a comma-separated list), deleting a genre with `move_to` **adds** the songs to the target (`syncWithoutDetaching`) so the song's other genres survive, and `PurgeService`'s `type` mode reads the pivot. ⚠️ That special case is now a **map** (`PurgeService::GENRE_PIVOTS`), not an `if`: there are two multi-genre domains, and a third would otherwise add a third branch. ⚠️ The column drop is **MySQL-only** — sqlite cannot `DROP COLUMN` while a foreign key names it, so on the test database the dead column survives and nothing reads it. The URL goes through the same `VideoUrl` allowlist and the same `VideoMetadata` probe as videos, so no HTML is ever stored. **Playlists live in the song module** (`playlists` + `playlist_song`, routes inside `module:song`, pages `/playlists` and `/playlists/:id`). Ordering exists at **two levels**: `playlists.sort_order` (`POST /playlists/reorder`) and `playlist_song.sort_order` — the latter set by `PUT /playlists/{playlist}/songs`, which takes the **whole ordered list**, so add / remove / drag-reorder are one operation and `sort_order` can never desync. The reverse direction (`PUT /songs/{song}/playlists`) backs the playlist multi-select on the song form. ⚠️ **Both pivot endpoints are `PUT`, not `POST`, on purpose** — `EnsureModulePermission` derives `create` from POST, so a user with update-but-not-create rights would get a spurious 403. `songs.visibility`/`playlists.visibility` exist (`private` default, §16) and are switched from **`/profile`'s `VisibilityManager`** like every other domain (§6.1); on the record itself public only shows as a badge, because that toggle deliberately lives in one place. **A song carries its own files and notes since 2026-09-11 (Tasks §7.4)**: `song_files` (`kind` = `image|doc`) + `song_notes`, the 2026-09-03 section-table rule, exactly `video_files`/`video_notes`' shape — rendered by `components/SongDetail.tsx` (photos · notes · documents) behind a paperclip button in the list. ⚠️ **There is no `audio` kind on purpose**: a song's own recording is `songs.url`, and an uploaded track would add tens of megabytes per record to the quota; the task asked for "like `video_files`". ⚠️ `Song::booted()` deletes the files one at a time despite the SQL cascade — a cascade fires no model event, so the file on disk and the quota counter would be left behind (pinned by a test that deletes **through the model**, which is the path `PurgeService` takes). Drag & drop is `lib/dragReorder.ts`: native HTML5 DnD, **no library added** (dnd-kit would cost ~40 kB on an already 1 MB bundle); it also returns `moveBy()` because native DnD is not keyboard-accessible, which is why the up/down buttons stay.

**Playback (Tasks §7.2, built 2026-09-11)** — one player for the whole app: `lib/player.tsx` (`PlayerProvider`/`usePlayer`, the queue), `components/PlayerBar.tsx` (the bottom strip) and `components/PlayerStage.tsx` (the only place media actually plays). **Frontend only — no backend change**: `embed_url` and `POST /songs/{song}/played` / `POST /videos/{video}/watched` already existed. Where it starts: a song's cover on `/songs` (queue = the **filtered list**), `/playlists` (a row's ▶ fetches the playlist first, since the list payload carries only `songs_count`), `/playlists/:id` (header "play all" + each row) and `/videos` (header "play all" + a per-card ▶; **the thumbnail still opens `VideoDetail`** — one video is better watched in the wide modal than in a 256 px strip). Eight things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The provider sits in `App.tsx` above `<AppShell>`, and `<PlayerBar />` outside `<Routes>`.** That is the whole feature: a player that unmounts on navigation cannot finish a playlist. The old `components/SongPlayer.tsx` modal was deleted for exactly this reason — it could not survive a route change, and two embeds sounding at once is worse than one.
- ⚠️ **The allowlist is `lib/embed.ts` now, not `VideoEmbed.tsx`.** `isAllowedEmbed`/`ALLOWED_EMBED_HOSTS` mirror `VideoUrl::EMBED_HOSTS`; `playableEmbedSrc()` only *adds query params* (`autoplay`, and for YouTube `enablejsapi`+`origin`) to the URL the backend built. **Raw HTML is still never stored or rendered** — the project's hard rule.
- ⚠️ **`enablejsapi`+`origin` are load-bearing, not decoration.** Without them a YouTube frame neither sends nor accepts `postMessage`, so "it ended" is unknowable and auto-advance — the point of the task — silently does nothing.
- ⚠️ **Every platform names the same event differently**, so `embedEventFrom()` is one decoder: YouTube's `{event:'infoDelivery', info:{playerState}}` (0/1/2), Vimeo's `{event:'ended'|'play'|'pause'}` and Dailymotion's `event=video_end` query-string form all collapse to `ready|playing|paused|ended`. A second copy of this mapping would drift the day a player changes.
- ⚠️ **The `message` listener checks `e.origin`.** Any frame on the page can post to us, and an unchecked message would skip a track.
- ⚠️ **The frame is recreated per track (`key`), not reused with `loadVideoById`.** Reuse is smoother but racy: a command sent before the player is ready is dropped and the track never starts. A fresh frame with `autoplay=1` is correct by construction — the browser's sticky activation (the user already clicked play) permits it.
- ⚠️ **`ended` is guarded by the activation counter (`seq`)**, because YouTube can send it twice and the queue would jump two tracks.
- ⚠️ **"Played" is counted on activation, not on finish** — the same moment the old button counted it, now including auto-advanced tracks. A failed counter is swallowed: it must not interrupt playback.
- ⚠️ **`--player-h` is the only channel between the bar and the rest of the page** — `<main>` reads it as bottom padding and the queue toast adds it to its own `bottom`. The variable exists only while something is playing. The bar's z-index lives in `lib/layers.ts` (`LAYER_PLAYER`, 50: above the header, below modals).
- ⚠️ **`platform === 'other'` says so out loud** ("this source cannot play in the app — open the original") instead of rendering a black box; auto-advance genuinely cannot work there. The i18n namespace is **`playback.*`, not `player.*`** — `player.vimeo.com` in the allowlist would otherwise trip the i18n audit's "key-like literal outside `t()`" check.

- `Services/Video/VideoSearch` — `search()` covers **everything** (title, description, tags, notes, source/platform, URL) with an mb-aware Levenshtein for near-miss terms and hand-rolled relevance; `similar()` powers `GET /videos/{video}/similar` from the user's own library (shared tags + title similarity + same platform/type). **FULLTEXT is deliberately unused** — sqlite tests have none and it handles Georgian poorly.
- `Services/Video/VideoMetadata` — backs `POST /videos/metadata`; `hasYoutubeKey()` reports whether a YouTube API key is configured so the UI can degrade gracefully.
- `lib/videoDuration.ts` (frontend) — `isDirectMediaUrl()` + `probeMediaDuration()` read `.mp4`/`.webm` length from a hidden `<video>` element in the browser, so the server never downloads the file. `formatDuration()` renders it.

**Tables are named per section (user decision, 2026-09-03)** — there is **no universal `attachments`/`notes` table any more**. Each section owns its own: `gallery_images` (still polymorphic — one photo mechanism for movie / series / cast_member / song), `video_files` (`video_id` FK, `kind` = `image|doc`) and `video_notes` (`video_id` FK). The trade-off was accepted explicitly: a future module that needs files brings its **own** `<module>_files` / `<module>_notes` migration, in exchange for a table name that says what is inside it. Shared behaviour lives in two small pieces, not in a shared table: `Concerns\StoredFile` (delete the file from disk + release the quota on `deleted`) and `Concerns\HasGallery` (the `galleryImages()` morphMany, the `galleryVideos()` morphMany added in §8.1, and **`deleteGalleryMedia()`** — renamed from `deleteGalleryImages()` because after videos the old name lied, and a lying cleanup name is exactly how orphan rows and leaked quota happen).

**Disk folders are per module too (user decision, 2026-09-04)** — the section-per-table rule was half-applied while the folders stayed flat, so `posters/` was shared by two modules, `videos/` held both thumbnails and attached photos, and `documents/` read as if every module's documents belonged there. Now **one module = one root**, and `App\Support\StorageFolder` is the **only** place a folder string is written:

| | |
|---|---|
| `account/avatars` | `users.avatar_path` |
| `movies/posters` · `series/posters` | `poster_path` (TMDB **and** manual uploads) |
| `videos/thumbnails` | `videos.thumbnail_path` |
| `videos/files/images` · `videos/files/docs` | `video_files.path` by `kind` |
| `videos/downloads` | `videos.download_path` (§7.1 — **private disk**, a private sub-folder of a public root) |
| `songs/thumbnails` · `songs/files/{images,docs}` | `songs.thumbnail_path` · `song_files.path` by `kind` |
| `books/covers` · `books/files/{ebooks,images,docs}` | `books.cover_path` · `book_files.path` by `kind` |
| `boardgames/images` · `boardgames/files/{rules,images,docs}` | `board_games.image_path` · `board_game_files.path` by `kind` |
| `games/covers` · `games/files/{images,docs}` | `games.cover_path` · `game_files.path` by `kind` |
| `notes/files/{images,videos,docs}` | `note_entry_files.path` by `kind` |
| `bookmarks/thumbnails` | `bookmarks.thumbnail_path` (og:image stays remote) |
| `{module root}/fields` | `<module>_field_values.value_path` (§6 phase 4b — **no new root**) |
| `gallery/images` | `gallery_images.path` |
| `chat/files/{images,videos,docs}` | `messages.attachment_path` (§16.3 — **private disk**) |
| `cast/photos` | `cast_members.photo_path` (global dictionary, not a module) |
| `backups` | `database_backups.path` (§22 — **private disk**; pseudo-module `backup`, no `modules` row) |

`MediaDownloader::poster()` therefore takes a **morph alias** (`movie`/`series`) — in the old flat `posters/` a movie and a series with the same slug overwrote each other. Migration `2026_09_04_000001_move_uploads_into_module_folders` moves the files and rewrites the columns: it **copies first and updates the column after** (an interrupted run leaves a stray copy, never a broken link) and defers every delete to a second pass (one physical file could be referenced by two rows). `StorageMeter::UPLOAD_FOLDERS` = `StorageFolder::ROOTS` + `LEGACY_ROOTS` and the orphan scan is now **recursive** (`allFiles`), so the old flat folders still get swept and a new sub-folder needs no code change. Adding a module = one constant here + one entry in `ROOTS`.

**Books module (Tasks §12)** — `book`, built 2026-09-04 on the same recipe as `song`. Four tables: `books` (`BelongsToUser`, `HasGallery`, `visibility`), `book_genres` (**per-user dictionary**, `/book-genres`, `ensureDefaults()` seeds 10 genres lazily), `book_files` (`kind` = `book|image|doc` — the ebook itself is `book`, max 50 MB) and `book_notes` (`is_quote` + `page`, so a quote is the same row with two extra fields, not another table). ⚠️ **Bilingual text is flat columns** (`title_ka`/`title_en`, `description_*`) with **no translation table**: those exist for movies because TMDB serves two languages and §7's translator is built on them, whereas Open Library is monolingual — the API shape stays identical to `MovieResource` either way. ⚠️ **Reading progress is stored in two units** (`progress_page` + `progress_percent`) and `Book::syncProgress()` writes both at once — page wins when `pages` is known, percent is the only unit for audiobooks; nothing else may write those columns. `PATCH /books/{book}/status` to `read` also completes the progress, and `PATCH …/progress` promotes `to_read` → `reading` → `read`, so the two never disagree. **Enrichment is Open Library** (`Services/Books/OpenLibraryClient`) — chosen over Google Books because it needs **no API key at all**; the flow mirrors TMDB (`POST /books/lookup/candidates` → `POST /books/lookup`), never auto-matching, and the form merges the search row with the work/edition draft because each carries fields the other lacks (`pages`/`isbn` vs description/publisher). ⚠️ **A source outage is a 503, not a 500** (2026-09-14): `OpenLibraryClient` had no try/catch, so a `cURL error 28` timeout surfaced as a **500 with the exception text in a red toast** while adding a record. Every call goes through one guarded `get()` now and `blocked()` separates „the source is not answering" (**503 `openlibrary_unavailable`**, registered in `lib/errors.ts`) from „I found nothing" (200 + an empty list) — the `BggClient`/`LinkMetadata` rule; manual entry is never blocked. ⚠️ **A Georgian-script query calls nothing at all** (§5.7, 2026-09-11): Open Library only answers Latin, so the response is `notice: 'manual_only'` and the form says „fill it in by hand“ — deliberately not an empty list, which read as „no such book exists“. **SerpApi cannot replace it**: its `google_books` engine and `google&tbm=bks` are both gone (verified live, 400 — an error does not spend the quota), so a Georgian book is entered by hand. ⚠️ A downloaded cover is `cover_source = 'openlibrary'`, is named after the shared `cover_id`, **does not count against the quota** (19.4/B) and is **never deleted with the record** — deleting it would break another account's cover; the same rule TMDB posters follow. Only `cover_source = 'upload'` is metered and deleted. `Book::booted()` deletes `book_files` one by one for the usual reason (a SQL cascade fires no model event, so the disk and the quota counter would drift).

**Board games module (Tasks §14)** — `board_game`, built 2026-09-04 on the books recipe. Four tables: `board_games` (`BelongsToUser`, `visibility`), `board_game_genres` (**per-user dictionary**, `/board-game-genres`), `board_game_files` (`kind` = `rules|image|doc`) and `board_game_notes`. ⚠️ **The gallery lives in `board_game_files` (`kind = 'image'`), not `gallery_images`** — that is the existing rule, not an exception: `gallery_images` holds photos *downloaded from TMDB*, while a user's own *uploads* live in the section table (exactly like `video_files.kind = 'image'`). ⚠️ **The title is single-language** (`title`), unlike a book's — §14 doesn't ask for two and BGG is English-only. ⚠️ **Mechanics are gone — removed whole on 2026-09-13** (the user: „take mechanics out of the filter and out of everything"; „ამიღე" means *delete*, so the column went too — MySQL-only drop, `songs.genre_id`'s precedent). The genre (`genre_id`) is the only classification a board game carries. The removal touched eleven places — column, `casts`, `normalizeMechanics()`, the controller's validation/filter/search/save, the resource field, `BggClient`'s `boardgamemechanic` parse, `FieldCatalog`, the form field (and with it the file's `TagSelect` import), the list card's chips, the API types — and **a leftover `module_user.settings.fields.mechanics` is harmless**, because `PUT /modules/{key}/fields` ignores unknown keys by design. ⚠️ **Price hangs off the shop link** (`links[].price`/`currency`), not off the record — one `price` column could not describe two shops. ⚠️ **The players filter is a comma-separated list and it ORs** (`?players=2,4`, 2026-09-13) — unlike every other list filter in the project, which ANDs: „fits 2 **or** 4 players"; AND over a *range* column is almost always empty, so the panel says out loud which one it is. Two behaviours changed with it and both are pinned: a record with **no range at all is now outside the filter** (the old `whereNull` branch matched it for every count, so „unknown" and „fits anything" were the same thing), and a non-numeric or out-of-range value is a **422** rather than an empty list that reads as „the library is empty" (cap `MAX_PLAYERS_FILTER` = 12). Enrichment is `Services/BoardGames/BggClient` (XML API 2 — the only XML source in the project; `SimpleXML`, all parsing inside try/catch). ⚠️ **BGG is unreachable from this machine (verified 2026-09-04)**: `boardgamegeek.com` answers with a Cloudflare bot challenge (403) and the API with 401, so `BggClient::blocked()` separates "source down" from "no results" and the endpoints return **503 `bgg_unavailable`** — otherwise an empty list would read as "this game doesn't exist". A downloaded image is `image_source = 'bgg'`, named after `bgg_id`, doesn't count against the quota and is **never deleted with the record** (the shared-file rule TMDB posters and book covers follow). **Georgian shops are live since 2026-09-11 (Tasks §7.2)**: `Services/BoardGames/GeorgianShops` scrapes **corners.ge** (WooCommerce) and **puzz.ge** (OpenCart) — the two sources the user named, and both *do* answer from this machine, unlike BGG. `GET /api/board-games/shops?query=&shops=` returns `offers[]` (title · url · image · price · old_price · currency · in_stock · shop) + `sources[]`. Six things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **It is a `GET`, not a `POST`.** `EnsureModulePermission` derives `create` from POST, so searching the shops while *editing* an existing game (update rights, no create) would have been a spurious 403 — the same trap `PUT /playlists/{playlist}/songs` avoids.
- ⚠️ **Three different silent failures, and they must stay distinguishable.** Page didn't open → `sources[].ok = false` ("the shop isn't answering"); opened and found nothing → `ok: true, count: 0`; found the item but not the price → `price: null` ("price unknown"). None of them is a 5xx — a shop's broken markup must never block adding a record (the `LinkMetadata` / `bgg_unavailable` rule).
- ⚠️ **The real markup differs from what §7.2 described** (verified 2026-09-11, fixtures committed). On corners.ge the listing uses `span.price`, not `p.price` (that one is the single-product page) — so the selector is on the **class**, not the tag; discounts are `<del>`/`<ins>` as documented. On puzz.ge the listing is `div.price` → `span.price-normal`, discounted `span.price-new` + `span.price-old`; the `.product-price-*` names in the task are the product page's. **Both sets are accepted** so one disappearing doesn't take the other with it.
- ⚠️ **puzz.ge lazy-loads images** — `src` is a base64 placeholder and the real URL is in `data-src`, so reading `src` would give every row the same grey square.
- ⚠️ **The price belongs to the shop link, not to the record** — it goes into the existing `links[].price`/`currency`, and no column was added: two shops can't fit in one column.
- ⚠️ Parsing is `DOMDocument`/`DOMXPath` (**no library added**), with `libxml_use_internal_errors` around it (`BggClient`'s trick), and a successful response is cached 15 minutes — **a failure is never cached**, or one flaky second would read as "unavailable" for a quarter hour. The tests run on the shops' **real markup** (`backend/tests/Fixtures/{corners,puzz}-search.html`), which is what makes them worth having.

**Games module (Tasks §11)** — `game`, built 2026-09-04 after the user approved §11.1's field list as-is (§19.1). Six tables: `games` (`BelongsToUser`, `HasGallery`, `visibility`), `game_genres` (**per-user dictionary**, `/game-genres`, `ensureDefaults()` seeds 13 RAWG-style genres lazily), **`game_genre_game`** (the pivot), `game_videos` (§11.2), `game_files` (`kind` = `image|doc`) and `game_notes`. Four things differ from every other module and are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The genre is a per-user dictionary even though §11.1 says "shared polymorphic `genres`, like movies".** §11.1 was written 2026-09-02, one day *before* the per-user rule was set for songs; RAWG's genres are `Shooter`/`RPG`/`Platformer`/`Massively Multiplayer`, which in the global table would show up in the **movie** genre picker.
- ⚠️ **But it stays multi-genre** (pivot `game_genre_game`), unlike book/board game's single `genre_id` — §11.1 says "genres", plural, and a game really is Action + RPG + Adventure at once. Consequences: the list filter uses `whereHas` (not `whereIn` on a column), `PurgeService`'s `type` mode special-cases `game`, and deleting a genre with `move_to` **adds** the games to the target (`syncWithoutDetaching`) instead of overwriting a column — the game's other genres must survive. There are no tags; genres and platforms cover that axis.
- ⚠️ **There is no `year` column** — `Game::year` is an accessor over `release_date`. Two units for one fact drift apart (the trap `Book::syncProgress()` fights), so sorting by "year" orders by `release_date` in SQL. **`franchise` has no dictionary table either, for the same reason** (Tasks §5.1): it is the game's own text column and RAWG returns it that way, so the picker list is aggregated straight out of it by **`GET /api/games/franchises`** (name + count, `owner`-scoped) and `components/GameFranchiseDialog.tsx` renders name · parts already in the library · **"add a new part"** (a RAWG search that creates the record immediately, independently of the parent form). ⚠️ The list filter `?franchise=` is an **exact** match, not `like` — otherwise "Mass Effect" would swallow "Mass Effect: Andromeda".
- ⚠️ **Videos live in `game_videos`, not in the `videos` table.** That table is the video *module's* library (own type dictionary, search, stats); a game's walkthrough is part of the record and must not appear in the videos list. The URL still goes through the `VideoUrl` allowlist and `GameVideo::applyUrl()` is the single place that (re)derives `platform`/`embed_url` — raw HTML is never stored. `kind` defaults to `walkthrough`, which §11.2 calls the mandatory one.

Screenshots live in **two** places and that is the existing rule, not an exception: RAWG-**downloaded** shots go to `gallery_images` (morph alias `game`, §11.3 → §10's mechanism), the user's **uploads** to `game_files.kind = 'image'` — exactly like video and board game. Enrichment is `Services/Games/RawgClient` (chosen over IGDB in §11.4 because it needs one free key, not a Twitch OAuth loop); `RAWG_API_KEY` is **optional** — without it the endpoints return **503 `rawg_unavailable`** and the form says "source unavailable" rather than "nothing found", the same distinction `BggClient` makes. **`Services/Games/IgdbClient` was added 2026-09-07 as a *fallback*** (`DECISIONS.md` §7, answered against my recommendation): `candidates()` tries RAWG first and only falls through to IGDB when RAWG brings nothing back, and `lookup` accepts `rawg_id` **or** `igdb_id`. Four things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **503 means *both* sources failed** (`GameController::blocked()`). One working source returning nothing means the game really wasn't found, and calling that "source unavailable" would be a lie.
- ⚠️ **A candidate row carries `source`**, and an IGDB row has `rawg_id: null` — sending that to `lookup` would 422, so `fetchRawgDraft()` picks the body from `candidate.source`.
- ⚠️ **`cover_source` stays `'rawg'` even for an IGDB cover.** That column answers "uploaded or fetched", and it is what `Game::deleteCover()` and the quota rule key on; a third value would need a branch in both places, and the first one forgotten would either delete a shared file or leak quota forever.
- ⚠️ **Matching (§16.2) still keys on `rawg_id` only** — `PublicDomain::MATCH` takes one identity per domain, so an IGDB-only game simply doesn't take part, which is the documented "null identity column" rule. `IGDB_CLIENT_ID`/`IGDB_CLIENT_SECRET` are both optional; the Twitch token is cached (`Cache` key `igdb.access_token`) and dropped on a 401 so the next call re-fetches. The query language is **APIcalypse** (a SQL-ish body on a POST), which is why this is its own client and not a flag on `RawgClient`. A downloaded cover is `cover_source = 'rawg'`, named after `rawg_id`, doesn't count against the quota and is **never deleted with the record**. **HowLongToBeat times are entered by hand** — no official API (§11.4). ⚠️ **`hltb_*` are minutes, not hours, since 2026-09-11 (§2.5)**: the columns were `decimal(5,1)` hours, so „2h 20m“ was stored as 2.3 and read back as 2h 18m. They are `unsigned int` minutes now — the same unit `movies.runtime` uses — and the form is the shared `DurationInput`. The migration converts the values **in PHP, not with `UPDATE … * 60`**: the old column capped at 9999.9, so 200 hours in minutes would have been silently truncated by a write into the same column.

**Notes module (Tasks §13)** — `note`, built 2026-09-04. It is the only module whose content is **purely the user's own** — no external source, so no lookup/enrichment at all. Five tables: `note_entries` (`BelongsToUser`, `visibility`), `note_categories` (**per-user dictionary**, `/note-categories`, `ensureDefaults()` seeds 6 lazily), `note_entry_files` (`kind` = `image|video|doc`; video capped at 100 MB), `note_reminders` (§13.2) and `note_notifications` (the delivery queue + log, §13.3). Seven things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The table is `note_entries`, not `notes`.** Under the 2026-09-03 rule the universal `notes` table is gone and the name now means "a note attached to some other record" (`video_notes`, `book_notes`…). A note here is a standalone entity. The module key and the API base are still `note`/`/notes`.
- ⚠️ **§13.1 asked for `HasAttachments`, which no longer exists.** Its replacement is the section's own `note_entry_files` + `Concerns\StoredFile`, so quota accounting and disk deletion come for free — same shape as `video_files`.
- ⚠️ **A reminder carries its own `timezone`.** The app runs on UTC (`config/app.php`), the user does not: "every day at 09:00" without a zone fires at 13:00 in Tbilisi. `mode = once` stores an absolute UTC instant and needs no zone; every recurring mode stores wall-clock `times_of_day` + `timezone` (the browser's `Intl` zone). **`NoteReminder::computeNextAt()` is the only place `next_at` is derived** — a second formula would double-fire. ⚠️ **The window (`starts_at`/`ends_at`) is the opposite — absolute UTC**, like `remind_at`: it is compared straight to `next_at` (also UTC), so the zone maths exists in exactly one place.
- ⚠️ **There are six modes since 2026-09-11 (Tasks §5.5)**: `once` · `interval` · `daily` · `weekly` · **`monthly`** · **`yearly`** (`month` + days), plus **`repeat_count`** (total firings; `null`/`0` = forever). Three traps: **`weekday` was replaced by `weekdays` (a JSON array)** — keeping both would store one fact twice, so the migration backfills the array and drops the column **on MySQL only** (sqlite can't, and nothing reads the dead column — the `songs.genre_id` precedent); **day 31 clamps to the month's length** in `NoteReminder::atDay()`, because Carbon's default overflow turns `Feb 31` into `Mar 3` and would silently pick the wrong month; and **`repeat_count` is decided in exactly one place** (`exhaustedAfter()`), for the same reason `next_at` has one formula. Every periodic mode picks the **nearest** of its candidates.
- ⚠️ **Since 2026-09-13 (stage 7) every slot is a list and every mode has a window.** `time_of_day` → **`times_of_day`** (several times a day) and `day_of_month` → **`days_of_month`** (several dates a month); the singular columns were **dropped, not kept alongside** (`weekday`'s precedent — the form showed two times while the calculator read one), MySQL-only again. `computeNextAt()` therefore builds the *product* of days × clocks and takes the earliest, and `starts_at`/`ends_at` bound **all six modes**, `once` included — a window is a boundary, not a seventh mode. Three things follow: **on an interval reminder the window's opening is itself the first firing** („from Oct 1, every hour" starts at 00:00, not 01:00); **„it will never fire again" and „it is not active" are one fact** (`nextStateAfterSending()` derives `is_active` from the computed `next_at`, so a finished window, an exhausted `repeat_count` and `once` all deactivate through one line); and **`ends_at`'s `after:starts_at` is conditional** (`Rule::when`) — against an absent `starts_at` Laravel reads the rule's argument as a literal date and 422s a perfectly valid „end only" request.
- ⚠️ **The active/inactive switch PATCHes the whole reminder** (there is no partial update), so `NoteDetail`'s `reminderInput()` is the single place that shape is written. A field missing there would be silently erased by one toggle — which is also why the editor's save path passes `is_active` explicitly: the controller defaults it to `true`, so editing a disabled reminder would quietly switch it back on.
- ⚠️ **`GET /note-reminders/due` is a GET that also fires due reminders.** Deliberate: no cron runs on the dev machine and §13.3 promises the browser channel works "with no dependency", so the SPA's poll drives the same `ReminderDispatcher` the cron does. It cannot be a POST — `EnsureModulePermission` derives `create` from POST, so a view-only user would never receive a reminder.
- ⚠️ **A reminder can never fire twice.** The dispatcher claims each row with a conditional UPDATE (`where next_at = <the value we read>`) *before* sending; a second caller (cron vs. an open tab) gets 0 rows and skips. Sending happens after the claim because Telegram/SMTP can take seconds.

Delivery channels (§13.3): `browser` is a queue row the SPA polls (`lib/noteReminders.ts` → `useNoteReminderWatcher`, mounted in `AppShell` so it works on any page) and shows via `public/sw.js`'s `showNotification()`, falling back to `new Notification()`; `telegram` is one HTTPS call (`Services/Notes/TelegramNotifier`, **no key in `.env`** — the user's own bot token lives in `module_user.settings`). ⚠️ **The email channel was removed on 2026-09-11 (§8.2)** — `NoteReminder::CHANNELS` is `['browser', 'telegram']`, `sendEmail()` and the `Mail` import are gone, and asking for `email` is now a **422 rather than a silent skip**: there is no SMTP here, so a silently accepted channel told the user a letter was on its way. Its replacement is a **log**, not a queue: `GET /api/note-notifications` + `components/NoteNotificationsDialog.tsx` (the bell on `/notes`) keeps every fired reminder **including the read ones**, while `GET /note-reminders/due` still returns only what should pop up and clears on read — a log that empties itself is not a log. A failed send shows `failed` + the reason („no Telegram bot configured“ and „the reminder never arrived“ are different facts). The migration drops `email` from existing rows and puts `browser` in its place when it was the **only** channel, or the reminder would stay „active“ and reach nobody; old `note_notifications` rows stay (they are history, labelled „email (no longer used)“). This is also what removed the `schedule:work` dependency: browser polling delivers while the app is open and nothing is lost while it is closed — it is in the log. Per-user channel config is `module_user.settings` via `NoteChannelSettings`, edited from the gear button on `/notes` (`NoteChannelsDialog`) — not `users.settings`, which is account-wide. A failed send is recorded in `note_notifications.error` and never stops the loop.

**Gallery module (Tasks §10 → §8 „gallery 2.0", rebuilt 2026-09-12)** — `gallery` is a module with **six pages of its own**. Content is rows in **`gallery_images`** (`source`, `category` = `backdrop|poster|logo|actor`, `remote_path`, `width`/`height`) and, since §8.1, **`gallery_videos`** (a *link*, not a file — see below). Both hang off the same parents, and the list of parents is one map, **`App\Support\GalleryParent`**: `cast_member` · `movie` · `series` · `anime` · `song` · `book` · `game`. ⚠️ **That map exists because the same list used to be written three times** (`WebSearchController::TARGETS`, the groups loop, `withOwners()`) and two of the copies only knew `MediaDomain::TYPES` — so a photo imported onto a song landed in the database and then **appeared nowhere in the gallery**. Every parent uses `HasGallery` and calls **`deleteGalleryMedia()`** in `booted()` (renamed from `deleteGalleryImages()` — after videos the old name lied, and a lying name is how orphan rows happen). `gallery_images` stays **polymorphic on purpose** — it is the one shared table the section-per-table rule keeps, because quota accounting (§17) and the delete cascade then come for free; no `visibility` column is needed either, since a photo inherits its parent's visibility (§16). ⚠️ In the storage breakdown a gallery photo is attributed to the **`gallery`** module, not to `imageable_type` (which is `cast_member` for actor photos — not a module key).

**Where it shows up:** `/gallery` is a real section with a **sidebar sub-menu**, and the list of cuts is one constant, `GALLERY_CUTS` in **`lib/galleryCuts.ts`** (the sidebar and the page both import it — two copies would drift, and importing it *from the page* is what un-did the route splitting once): **ყველა ფოტო** `/gallery` · **ბიბლიოთეკა** `/gallery/records` · **მსახიობები** `/gallery/actors` · **ალბომები** `/gallery/albums` (§26; renamed from „უკატეგორიო“ on 2026-09-16, the old path redirects) · **ვიდეოები** `/gallery/videos` · **წყაროები** `/gallery/sources` · **მოდულები** `/gallery/modules`. Drilling into a record opens `/gallery/records/:type/:id` (`pages/GalleryRecordPage.tsx`), and `/gallery/actors/:id` **redirects to `/actors/:id`** — an actor has exactly one page. The record page keeps its inline gallery below the trailer (`components/RecordGallery.tsx`), and the same component backs the gallery's record page with `bare` — one body, two mount points.

⚠️ **The line-divided „მასობრივი ჩამოტვირთვა" block is gone** (the user's direct instruction). It sat two different jobs — browsing and downloading — on one scroll and stripped the download of its context, making you re-pick a scope that was already known. Downloading is now either the page-header button (with a scope picker) or a button **on the group card / record / actor**, and it is always the same dialog, `components/GalleryDownloadDialog.tsx`. `GET /api/gallery` is therefore no longer „the list of records to download from" but the **summary** that feeds the sub-menu counters (photos · records · actors · videos · bytes · storage) — three separate `groups` calls would have downloaded three full lists just to draw a menu.

**Downloading** is `Services/Gallery/GalleryFetcher`. ⚠️ **Counts and sizes are per subject now** (§8.2): `subjects` ⊂ `stills|posters|logos` with `limits`/`sizes` maps. The old single `limit` concatenated the candidates (stills first) and sliced afterwards, so on a record with 40 backdrops and `limit = 20` **no poster ever arrived**. ⚠️ **The size list comes from the subject** (`GalleryFetcher::SUBJECT_SIZES`, TMDB `configuration`, verified live 2026-09-12): backdrop `w300·w780·w1280·original`, poster `w185·w342·w500·w780·original`, logo `w154·w300·w500·original`, profile `w45·w185·h632·original`. ⚠️ **`logos` was removed as a downloadable subject on 2026-09-14** (the user: „I don't need this part at all") — `GALLERY_SUBJECTS` is `stills` · `posters` on both sides. `gallery_images.category = 'logo'` **stays**: old rows and an actor's tagged images still carry it, and what was removed is the *choice*, not the library. A saved `subjects: ['stills','logos']` needs no migration — `readGalleryDefaults()` already filters unknown subjects. The CDN does accept a foreign key (a poster at `w1280` returns 200) but a **nonexistent** one is a 400, so the offered list must be the subject's. ⚠️ **The cast source is three cards, not three chips** (2026-09-14): one shared hint line described only the *selected* source, so choosing meant switching first in order to read what you were choosing. Each card now states its own meaning. ⚠️ **An actor has two sources** (`cast_source` = `profiles|tagged|both`): `/person/{id}/images` (portraits — often only 3–5) and **`/person/{id}/tagged_images`** (stills and posters of the films the person is tagged in — tens; Joaquin Phoenix: 17 vs 47, measured). `both` takes portraits first because a portrait is the actor's photo while a still is the film's. Tagged rows keep **TMDB's own `image_type`** as `category` (`still` → `backdrop`) so the actor page can tell a portrait from a film still, and `original_name` carries the film's title so „which film is this from" has an answer. `tagged_images` is paginated (20/page, capped at `MAX_TAGGED_PAGES` = 10 = `MAX_PER_ACTOR`). Cast selection is `all|female|male|selected`; gender comes from TMDB (`cast_members.gender`, 1=female/2=male) and is **backfilled from one `credits` call** when null, only when the scope is at most `GENDER_BACKFILL_RECORDS` (10) records — one request per record would turn a library-wide plan into minutes; otherwise the plan returns `unknown_gender` and the UI names the number.

**Scope** is `Services/Gallery/GalleryScope` — one builder for both targets, because the actor pool *is* the scope's cast. ⚠️ It is **multi-domain** now (`types[]`: „films, series and anime together" is one run, not three), statuses are a **list**, genres carry a **`genre_mode` = `any|all`** (three genres ANDed usually produced an empty scope that read as „I have nothing"), `ids` accepts the per-domain map `/sync` already uses, and **`scope = off`** exists on the actor target only — „turn the scope off and download on actors generally". ⚠️ Under `scope = ids` a domain with no selected ids contributes **nothing**, not everything (`/sync`'s old permissive reading would have downloaded the whole library). ⚠️ **`cast = selected` is read outside the pool cap**: the pool is the first `CAST_POOL_LIMIT` (1000) members by name and is narrowed further by `cast_q`, so in a 3000-actor library a selected actor could fall outside it and the download would **silently do nothing** — pinned by `GalleryTest::test_selected_actors_survive_a_narrowed_pool`.

⚠️ **„skip the ones that already have photos“ must be one expression, not two** (fixed 2026-09-13): the checkbox was hidden for a dialog pinned to a single actor while `skip_with_photos: true` kept travelling, so the one explicitly selected actor was filtered out of their own plan and the dialog said „0 actors × 20 each = up to 0 photos“ with nothing on screen to explain it. `GalleryDownloadDialog`'s `canSkipWithPhotos` now drives **both** the checkbox and the request, and the filter is off whenever the dialog is pinned to a single record or actor — an explicit choice is not a bulk convenience. ⚠️ **Both plans also return `skipped_with_photos`**, so a zero states its reason: „the scope is empty“ and „they all already have photos“ are different facts and used to render identically. ⚠️ **`POST /gallery/plan` still has two targets** and they are the dialog's two tabs; `record` force-sets `cast = none` even if a client sends one (a still cannot tell you who is in it, so the tab's total and its download must stand on one number). Queue items carry `type: 'actor'` → `POST /gallery/cast/{id}`, so one actor's photos are never duplicated per film. ⚠️ The portrait size (`cast_size`) **and the source (`cast_source`)** must travel through the queue — `ui/queue.tsx` sends both on the actor branch; forgetting one silently downloads the default. ⚠️ `/images` needs `include_image_language=en,null` — the same trap as trailers (§9). Downloads go through `StorageMeter::storeContents()` and the quota is re-checked **per photo**: on exhaustion the fetch stops, keeps what it saved and returns **413**. ⚠️ `limit`/`per_actor` accept `0` = „none" (§3.6). ⚠️ „Make main" writes `poster_source = 'tmdb'`, never `'upload'` (the file is already counted once), and is **rejected for cast photos** (422) because `cast_members.photo_path` is a shared global column. Per-user options live in `module_user.settings` and `readGalleryDefaults()` still understands the **old** flat `limit`/`size` keys, so nobody's saved settings break.

**Routes:** `GET /gallery` (summary) · `GET /gallery/groups?by=record|actor|source|provider|module` · `GET /gallery/photos` · **`GET /gallery/module-photos`** · `POST /gallery/plan` · `GET|POST /gallery/videos`, `DELETE /gallery/videos/{galleryVideo}` · `DELETE /gallery/images/{galleryImage}` · `POST /gallery/images/{galleryImage}/primary` · `GET|POST /gallery/cast/{castMember}` · `GET|POST /gallery/{type}/{id}`. ⚠️ **Everything must stay above `/gallery/{type}/{id}`** in `routes/api.php`, or „groups"/„photos"/„videos" is read as a type. ⚠️ **Thematic categories were removed on 2026-09-11 (§4.5)** — `gallery_themes`, `gallery_images.theme_id` and the assignment routes are gone (the migration is MySQL-only; on sqlite the column and the table stay, because dropping a table whose FK still names the column makes every `insert` fail). `gallery_images.category` stays — that is TMDB's *technical* type and comes from the source.

**The gallery page's cuts (Tasks §4.1 → §8.3, seven since §26)** — they are `GALLERY_CUTS`. `by=record` (**every** parent, not just TMDB domains) · `by=actor` (with a `gender=female|male` chip row) · **`by=source`** (which *domain* it came from) · **`by=provider`** (which *source* brought it — TMDB · Wikimedia · a SerpApi engine) · **`by=module`** (every other module's own images) · **`by=album`** (§26 — the user's own albums, plus the „no album“ card) · all photos. ⚠️ **`source` and `provider` are two different questions** and after web search both need an answer; they are deliberately not merged. ⚠️ **The source cut groups by *domain*, not by record**: a cast photo's parent is the actor (one photo, not one per film), so "did this come from a film or a series" can only be derived from **which domain that actor plays in**; an actor in both shows up in both groups and the UI says so. Its group carries `from: <domain>` with `id: 0`, and drilling in uses `?from=` instead of `owner=` — `GalleryController::sourceQuery()` is the single source for both the count and the contents, so „the group says 40, inside are 37" cannot happen. ⚠️ **The module cut is read-only** (`Services/Gallery/ModuleImages`): those are covers, thumbnails and `<module>_files` images, i.e. **part of the record** — deleting one is editing the record, not a gallery action. Its map (`RECORD_SOURCES` + `FILE_SOURCES`) is checked by `RegistryConsistencyTest::test_every_module_with_images_is_in_the_gallery_module_cut`, because a forgotten module would just vanish from the cut. ⚠️ The `note` module lives on the **private disk**, so its rows carry `private: true` and a `/note-files/{id}` URL — the flag is the backend's to state; a copy of `PRIVATE_ROOTS` in the SPA would drift and expose a private file under a public URL. ⚠️ **A record's gallery actions are one bordered bar** (2026-09-14) — they used to sit directly in a `justify-between` row, so the browser spread the three buttons across the full width and they read as three unrelated controls; `GalleryPanel` now wraps `actions` in a divided group and the buttons are `ghost`. The download button is labelled **„gallery download"** (`gallery.fetch`), not just „download". ⚠️ **A group renders as a stack** through the shared **`ui/photo-stack.tsx`** (five cards since §8.6, so `groups` returns five `previews`), the very component the record page's `CastPhotoStacks` uses. ⚠️ **Per-photo info** is one function, `lib/galleryPhoto.ts::galleryPhotoInfo()`. ⚠️ **Selecting from the right-click menu marks the photo too** (2026-09-14): the tick was drawn only under `picking`, which just the toolbar button set — so a menu selection changed the count above and nothing on the cell. The mark now renders on `picking || checked`, **and** `toggle()` turns picking on, or the toolbar's „delete/download selected" would never appear and the selection would be useless. ⚠️ **„Grouped / mixed" is a toggle, not a cut** (`gallery.layout.*`): on a record's cast sub-section it switches one-card-per-actor against one-card-per-photo, and on the flat lists `sort=new|old|random` does the same job. ⚠️ **`random` is seeded** (`?seed=`) — without a seed page 2 would repeat page 1; sqlite has no `RAND(seed)`, so there the same stable shuffle is done arithmetically.

**The records cut — domain tabs, actors inside, filtering (2026-09-12, the user's nine-stage batch, stage 2)** — „ჩანაწერები“ was the wrong name on a library that holds only films, and a film's *actors* lived in a separate cut, so getting to them meant leaving the film. Three pieces: **`lib/galleryCuts.ts`** (⚠️ its `galleryCutLabel()` was **removed** in §27 — the composed name was the next complaint; see the §24/§26/§27/§28 block below), **`components/gallery/RecordsCut.tsx`** (the domain tabs + the „ფილმები / მსახიობები“ switch inside a media tab) and **`components/gallery/GroupFilters.tsx`** + **`lib/galleryGroups.ts`** (filter · sort · sections). Seven things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The tab name comes from `modules.name_ka/name_en`, not from a new i18n key.** The sidebar already draws module names from there; a second source would show a renamed module under its old name in exactly one of the two places.
- ⚠️ **`have=without` is a `doesntHave`, not a filter on the count.** `photos` is a `withCount` alias, so it cannot appear in a `where`, and `HAVING` over six domains unioned in PHP means nothing. This cut is also the only way the gallery can answer „which record has no photos yet“ — the very question the download button exists for; until now the list started with `has('galleryImages')`.
- ⚠️ **Genre/status/year apply to media domains only**, on both sides (`GalleryController::applyRecordFilters()` and the UI). Song/game genres are per-user dictionaries, a book has one `genre_id`, and status is a dictionary on media but an enum on book/game and absent on song — one shared `where` would be a SQL error, and a shared *control* would silently do nothing. Same rule `applyTitleSearch()` follows.
- ⚠️ **Sorting and sectioning happen on the client** (`lib/galleryGroups.ts`, unit-tested), because both read what is *on screen*: the title in the chosen `contentLang` and the status name from the owner's dictionary. The server keeps its `photos desc` order — that is what picks which 120 groups get previews.
- ⚠️ **Genre sections bucket a record under its first genre only** — `MovieGrid`'s rule: a three-genre film in three sections makes the section totals stop matching the list. A record with no genre/year/status goes to an explicit „უცნობი“ section instead of vanishing.
- ⚠️ **`by=actor&from=<domain>` is one `whereHas` on the domain relation**, which inherits `BelongsToUser`'s `owner` scope — so it is this account's library by construction (the rule `sourceQuery()` already relied on). The standalone actors cut stays: it is every actor, with no domain.
- ⚠️ **`type` on `/gallery/groups` validates against `GalleryParent::recordRule()`, not `MediaDomain::rule()`** — the tabs include song/book/game, and those were a 422 while the very same list already drew them.

**Web search — images & videos (Tasks §7.5/§7.6, built 2026-09-11; distribution and video saving added in §8.4; Serper added 2026-09-14)** — `Services\Serp\SerpApiClient` + `Services\Web\WikimediaImages` + **`Services\Web\SerperImages`** behind `GET /api/web/{status,images,videos,video}` and `POST /api/web/import`. ⚠️ **There are three source categories, not two**: free (Wikimedia) · **own key and own billing (Serper)** · SerpApi's 250-a-month. Only the last moves the counter the UI reads as „N searches left", which is why `usesSerpQuota()` exists and why a source's `credits` are reported separately — folding Serper into either existing list would have made the counter lie or spent somebody else's quota. ⚠️ **Serper is the only paged source** (`paged` in the status payload): the dialog's hand-entered **photo count** (cap 1000) and **pages** (cap 100) come from the user, and the pages field is disabled for every other source rather than silently ignored. ⚠️ **Each requested page is one credit**, so the default stays small (5 pages) and `pages` is lowered to `ceil(limit / 100)` — asking for pages you cannot fill is pure spend. ⚠️ **Duplicates are cut across pages** (`original`), because Google repeats the same image on neighbouring pages. ⚠️ It is also the only source that answers a **Georgian** query well (verified live: „ნინო ქასრაძე" → 113 unique photos over 3 pages), which is exactly the gap Wikimedia's weak search and TMDB's Latin-only index left open. ⚠️ **The route group is `/web/*`, not `/serp/*`, and the controller is `WebSearchController`** — SerpApi is only *one* source in a list. Where it is used: the actor page, a record's gallery block, a gallery group and the video form. ⚠️ **`POST /web/import` distributes across actors since §8.4**: it takes `distribute[]` (cast member ids) and assigns each photo to the first person whose name — in either language — appears in the result's title or link; everything unmatched falls back to **the record**.
