The rule lives on the server **once** (so it is tested and understands the Georgian name), while the client may override a single photo with `images[].target_id`; the response carries `assigned` so the toast can say where things went. ⚠️ **Matching is on names, not faces** — there is no face recognition and none will be added, which is exactly why the default is always the record: a doubtful photo lands on the film, never on a random actor. ⚠️ **One photo goes to one parent**: dedupe is per parent, so attaching the same URL twice would store two files and bill the quota twice. ⚠️ **Video search now persists** (`gallery_videos`): `GET /api/web/videos` existed but had **no caller at all**. `POST /api/gallery/videos` saves the link, the remote thumbnail, the channel and the duration — **nothing is downloaded**, so the quota is untouched (the bookmarks `og:image` rule), and `platform`/`embed_url` are derived by `VideoUrl`'s allowlist (raw HTML is never stored). ⚠️ **Safe search stays off and thumbnails are never blurred** (the user's explicit condition); there is no content-classification logic anywhere in the code and none is to be added. ⚠️ **Two things had to be fixed for that to be true of Serper (2026-09-14)**: the `safe` parameter must be sent as an explicit **`off`**, never omitted — Google applies its own default when it is missing, and since 2023 that default *blurs* flagged images — and the result grid must render **`original`, falling back to `thumbnail` only `onError`**, because Google's own thumbnails (`encrypted-tbn0.gstatic.com`) arrive blurred. Neither blur ever came from our CSS: the `blur` class exists in exactly one place in the project (a movie page's backdrop). Nine things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **One client, one counter.** `SerpApiClient` is the only place a SerpApi request leaves from (the `StorageMeter` rule); a second path would silently desync the count. There are **two** counters and both are needed: ours (`serp_searches` rows — who, which engine, when) and the real one (`GET /account`, which **does not spend quota**, verified). The limit is enforced by both, because ours still works when `/account` is down.
- ⚠️ **`serp_searches` has no `month` column on purpose** — the plan renews on the **7th**, not the 1st, so the window moves; freezing it into a column would drift from the real number. `SERPAPI_MONTHLY_LIMIT=250` is only *our* accounting ceiling.
- ⚠️ **The cache (24 h) is load-bearing, not an optimisation** — re-opening a form or pressing back would eat the 250 in days. A cache hit **writes no row and spends nothing**, and the response says so (`spent`). **A failure is never cached**, or one flaky second reads as "unavailable" all day.
- ⚠️ **Three different empty states, and they must stay distinguishable**: quota gone → **429 `serpapi_quota_exceeded`** (registered in `lib/errors.ts`'s `CODES`), source not answering → 503, found nothing → 200 with an empty list.
- ⚠️ **The query starts from the context, not from an empty field** (§5.2): on a record it opens with the film's title and offers **that film's cast** as chips that *append* to it (a chip fills the field, it never searches — that would spend budget). The dialog also states out loud **where the downloaded photo will attach** (the record you opened it from, even if you typed an actor's name), because that is what decides which §4 group it lands in. The same dialog is now reachable from a gallery group too (§5.1).
- ⚠️ **Searching happens only on an explicit click** — never on save, sync or page load. Same reason `serpDelayMs` was *not* added: every search is its own button, so the queue's pacing setting would change nothing (a setting that does nothing is worse than none).
- ⚠️ **Wikimedia Commons is the default source** — free, **no key**, no limit, license visible in the result — so an ordinary search costs nothing. It **requires a `User-Agent`** (403 without one, verified) and its tag search is weak (it matches file name and description), which is exactly why SerpApi stays in the list. SerpApi's four image engines are `google_images_light` (the default paid one) · `google_images` · `yandex_images` (**not** `yandex`) · `yahoo_images`; `safe=off` exists only on the Google ones and the UI says so instead of quietly "protecting" the user.
- ⚠️ **Each engine names the same fact differently** and that is why the catalogue has a `query`/`results` column per engine: the query parameter is `q`/`text`/`p`/`search_query`, sizes come as `original_width` (Google) · `size.{w,h}` (Yandex) · `dimensions: "1000x1600"` (Yahoo), and on Yahoo `link` is the redirect while `source` is the page — the opposite of Google. `yandex_videos` returns rows with **no title or link**, so they are counted into `dropped` and the UI says "found, but without links" rather than "found nothing" — otherwise the search would be spent and repeated.
- ⚠️ **A video's `published` is a date or nothing — never „9 months ago“** (fixed 2026-09-13). YouTube's `published_date` is a *relative* string and it travelled straight into `POST /api/gallery/videos`, whose `published_at` is validated as a `date` — so **saving a found video was always a 422** and the feature read as „video search doesn't work“. `SerpApiClient::publishedDate()` normalises it to `Y-m-d` (stripping YouTube's `Streamed`/`Premiered` prefixes) or `null`; a bare number is rejected, because `Carbon::parse('2')` is the 2nd of this month. ⚠️ It lives in the **client**, next to the per-engine `query`/`results` columns — that is where an engine's quirks are absorbed — and the SPA formats the value through `useDateFormat()` like every other date.
- ⚠️ **A dead `original` is normal** (hotlink protection), so the thumbnail is saved as a fallback and `gallery_images.is_thumbnail` says out loud that it is one. The row also carries `source_url` (the page) and `remote_path` grew 255 → **1000** chars (MySQL only). **No unique index on `remote_path`** — 1000 utf8mb4 chars is 4000 bytes, past MySQL's 3072 limit (`bookmarks.url`'s precedent), so duplicates are cut in PHP. **HTML is never stored**: the `content-type` is checked and a non-image is not downloaded (a 403 page would otherwise be saved "as a photo"). A downloaded photo **does** count against the quota (unlike TMDB's shared files) — `StorageMeter::storeContents()`, **413** on exhaustion, and what was already fetched stays.
- ⚠️ **A cast member's search tags live in their own table, `cast_member_tags`** (`unique(user_id, cast_member_id)`), never on `cast_members`: that dictionary is **global** (one actor is one row for every account), so a column there would show my tags on someone else's page and two accounts would overwrite each other. The source label is the **backend's** to write (an unknown engine becomes `web`), or the "where did this come from" filter would mean nothing.

**Gallery videos and actor details (Tasks §8.1)** — two additions that look small and are not. **`gallery_videos`** is `gallery_images`' twin: the same polymorphic parents (`videoable`), `BelongsToUser`, and rows written only by `Api\GalleryVideoController`. ⚠️ **It is a separate table on purpose.** A photo is a file (`path`, `size`, quota, disk); a video is a **link** — nothing is downloaded and the thumbnail stays a remote URL (`thumbnail_url`, the bookmarks `og:image` rule). Folding it into `gallery_images` would have made `path` nullable and forced `StoredFile` to ask „is there a file here" on every row, i.e. the quota accounting would branch on a type. ⚠️ **No `path` column** → `StorageMeter::referencedPaths()`, `UPLOAD_FOLDERS` and the orphan scan are untouched. ⚠️ **No `visibility` column** → it inherits the parent's, exactly like `gallery_images`, so `PublicDomain::DOMAINS` needs no row (and `RegistryConsistencyTest` stays happy). ⚠️ `platform`/`external_id`/`embed_url` are written in **one** place, `GalleryVideo::applyUrl()`, through `VideoUrl`'s allowlist — raw HTML/embed markup is never stored (`GameVideo`'s precedent). ⚠️ `url` has **no** unique index (1000 utf8mb4 chars is 4000 bytes, past MySQL's 3072 limit — `bookmarks.url`'s precedent); duplicates are cut in PHP. ⚠️ The model is in `AuditRegistry::MODELS` → `gallery`, and the player got a third `kind`, **`link`**: a gallery video has no row in `videos`, so counting a „watch" on it would write to somebody else's record or 404.

**`cast_members` gained the person's own facts** (`imdb_id`, `birthday`, `deathday`, `place_of_birth`, `biography`, `known_for`, `popularity`, `homepage`, `profile_links` JSON, `details_synced_at`), filled by `Services\Cast\CastEnricher` from one TMDB call (`/person/{id}?append_to_response=external_ids`) behind **`POST /api/cast/{castMember}/resync`**, and rendered by `components/ActorHero.tsx`. ⚠️ **These belong in the global dictionary** — a birthday and an IMDb id are the same for every account, exactly like `name` and `gender`; what is *mine* stays in `cast_member_tags` and in `gallery_images.user_id`. ⚠️ **`profile_links` is JSON, not five columns**: TMDB keeps adding networks (most recently `tiktok_id`) and a column per network means the next one is silently lost; the UI draws only the ones it knows and keeps the rest. ⚠️ **The biography is English-only and gets no translation table** — TMDB practically never returns a Georgian one, so the table would stay empty (the books precedent). ⚠️ **It is an explicit button, not an automatic fill on open**: the TMDB budget is shared and an actor page is opened often; `details_synced_at` records that we tried, so „we asked and there was nothing" and „we never asked" stay different facts. ⚠️ **A source failure is a 200 with `updated: false`**, not a 5xx — the page must still open (`bgg_unavailable`'s rule). ⚠️ The endpoint is called **`resync`** and not `sync` because `EnsureModulePermission::UPDATE_ENDPOINTS` knows that word — if these routes ever move into a module group, a POST would otherwise be read as `create` and an update-only user would get a spurious 403. ⚠️ **`CastMember::booted()` now deletes its gallery media** — an actor is never deleted today, but `morphs()` creates no cascade, so the day it happens every account's photos of that person would be orphaned and their quota held forever.

**Deleting a photo (§1.2, 2026-09-11)** — two different rules, and confusing them is what made „I removed one photo and the film lost its main image“ possible. **In a form** (movie/series/anime/book/game/board game/bookmark) clearing the cover is only a *mark*: the page keeps a `removePoster`-style flag and the deletion happens inside the `PUT` (`remove_poster`/`remove_cover`/`remove_image`/`remove_thumbnail`), so leaving without saving changes nothing — pinned by `GalleryTest::test_updating_a_record_without_the_remove_flag_keeps_the_poster`. **In the gallery** the delete is immediate by design (it is a library, not a form), and ⚠️ **the record's main photo is a gallery file**: „make main“ points `poster_path` at `gallery_images.path`, so deleting that photo takes the record's poster with it. `GalleryController::destroyImage()` clears `poster_path`/`poster_source` first (a dangling path would render as a broken image), and since §1.2 the confirm dialog **says so explicitly** when the selection contains the primary photo — the deletion is allowed, it just can no longer happen unnoticed.

**Gallery scope cards, the uncategorized section, photo moving and the view control (Tasks §24/§26/§27/§28, 2026-09-15)** — the user's words were literal: the gallery's cuts should look like the audit log's cards („ყველა კადრი, პოსტერები — ისევე სტილის, როგორც ლოგებში"), there should be an uncategorized section you can sort and group photos into, a photo should be movable onto an actor / a series / uncategorized, the records tab needed a better general name, and every cut should offer every view variant. Sixteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The card is `ui/scope-card.tsx` and was not re-created for the gallery.** Six places now ask „which cut am I in" (audit · requests · bulk status · purge · gallery categories · gallery domains), so the icon size, how the colour arrives, the active mark and the zero behaviour live in **one** file. `components/gallery/GalleryScope.tsx` adds only the gallery's own map — which cut gets which icon and tone.
- ⚠️ **A card without a count is the chip it replaced.** So `GET /gallery/groups` returns **`facets`**, and the rule is the audit's: **a facet never counts its own cut**. `actorGroups()` builds the cast list *before* the gender filter and `recordGroups()` loops **every** domain before applying `type` — the price is that one domain tab still queries all six, and it is paid deliberately: without it, choosing „actresses" would drop „actors" to zero and the card would stop answering the question it exists for.
- ⚠️ **`galleryCutLabel()` is gone rather than kept as a pass-through** (§27). It composed the records cut's name out of the enabled media modules („ფილმები · სერიალები · ანიმეები"), which is too long for a tab, grows with every module, and is **still wrong** — songs, books and games sit in that cut too. The cut has one general name now (`gallery.cut.records` = „ბიბლიოთეკა") and *which* domains are inside is what the cards say. A helper left behind as a `t()` wrapper would have left both callers wondering whether it still did something.
- ⚠️ **„Uncategorized" is a state, not a second store**: `gallery_images.imageable_type`/`_id` became **nullable**. A separate `is_uncategorized` flag would state the same fact twice and drift from the parent. That nullability is also what makes §25.5 („delete the record, keep its photos") possible at all — `Movie::booted()`'s `deleteGalleryMedia()` finds photos *through* the parent, so detaching is the only way to spare them.
- ⚠️ **An album is not a parent.** `gallery_albums` is the user's own sorting, so a photo can have a parent and an album, one, or neither. Making it a parent would have invented a pseudo-domain inside `GalleryParent`, `withOwners()`, the source cut and `PurgeService` — one with no record and no module. ⚠️ Its name is **single-language** on purpose: the dictionaries are bilingual because their defaults come from code, while here the user types the name and `name_ka`/`name_en` would ask for the same text twice. That is also why it is not a row in `lib/dictionaries.tsx`.
- ⚠️ **Deleting an album never deletes photos** (`album_id` is `nullOnDelete`, with an optional `move_to`) — removing a folder cannot be a way to lose a library. ⚠️ **Its three steps are now two, in the other order (Tasks BUG-04): the rows in one transaction, the files after the commit.** `AlbumVault::reveal()` used to run first, so a failing `update()` or `delete()` left a locked album's photos sitting in `gallery/images` — on the public disk, at a `/storage/...` URL — with the album's `password_hash` still in place, i.e. a failed delete walked straight through §7.9's lock. ⚠️ **The reverse failure is harmless, and that is what makes this order the right one**: the file stays in `gallery/locked` while `path` points at it, and `GalleryImage::servedUrl()` serves the private disk through the API route — the photo still renders, just outside `/storage/*`. ⚠️ `seal`/`reveal` are unusable here (they find photos by `where('album_id', …)`, which finds nothing once the rows have moved), so the delete path reads its photos **before** the change and hands them to **`AlbumVault::placeMany()`**. ⚠️ No compensation is needed any more: after BUG-03 each photo is either fully moved or untouched.
- ⚠️ **`POST /gallery/images/move` moves along two independent axes and a missing key means „don't touch".** `target` changes the parent, `album_id` the sorting; an explicit `null` clears. Merged into one value, putting a photo into an album would have quietly detached it from its film. The backend reads `$request->exists('album_id')`, not `filled()`, precisely so `null` can mean „clear".
- ⚠️ **Nothing moves on disk.** A move is two columns: `path`, the quota and the file are untouched. And the scope is `owner`, so another account's id is a silent `moved: 0` rather than a 403.
- ⚠️ **The move dialog is rendered by `GalleryPhotoGrid`, not by each cut** — one place, every cut, and the state and the portal's JSX sit in the **same** component (the rule `GroupsCut`'s live bug produced: the button was in one return branch, the dialog in the other, and pressing it did nothing).
- ⚠️ **„Move" is in `photoActions()`**, so the toolbar button, the `⋮` menu and the right-click menu cannot disagree. It sits **above** delete and is not `danger`: nothing is destroyed.
- ⚠️ **„Mixed" means the same scope flattened, never „every photo in the library".** That is why `/gallery/photos` gained **`parent`** (`record|actor|none`) and **`type`**: `from=movie&parent=actor` is exactly „photos of the actors in my films", because `from` first unions the record with its cast and `parent` then keeps the cast. ⚠️ Without those two the toggle would silently answer a different question in the actor cut.
- ⚠️ **The toggle is therefore not offered in the source/provider cuts** — there a group *is* a whole domain, so the flat equivalent is literally the „all photos" cut. The project's existing rule: a control that lies is not drawn.
- ⚠️ **`LayoutToggle` is one component for all three places** (the cast sub-section, the groups cut, uncategorized). It had been a segmented control in one place and a `Select` in another, i.e. the same choice with two faces.
- ⚠️ **Collapsed sections are stored, not expanded ones.** A new section (a new genre, a new year) must be born open; storing the open set would make a filter change look like „everything disappeared". The section header is the button — a separate small chevron is effectively unreachable on a touch screen.
- ⚠️ **The album cut draws its zero groups**, which is the opposite of every other cut: an empty folder is exactly where you drag the first photo, so hiding it until it has one would make moving impossible.
- ⚠️ **In the record's gallery, „delete this actor's photos" does not touch the record** and vice versa: a cast photo hangs on the **actor** and shows up in their other films, which is the whole reason it is not duplicated per film.

**The public gallery, and the lock that had to grow up for it (Tasks §7, 2026-09-16)** — the user's words: „სხვა იუზერმა ნახოს ჩემს გალერეაში გასაჯაროებული ფოტოები, ხოლო პაროლიანი ალბომები ვერ ნახოს … გალერეის გასაჯაროება შეგიძლია გააკეთო ფილმების/მსახიობების გასაჯაროებით, როგორც ლოგიკურია". Sixteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The public gallery is computed, never stored.** `Services\Profile\PublicGallery` reuses `PublicProfileService::query()`'s three layers: a photo is public because **its record** is (movie · series · anime · song · book · game), or because it hangs on an **actor who plays in one of my public media records** (the same `whereHas` `sourceQuery()` already uses), or because it sits in a **public album**. `gallery_images` gets no `visibility` column — that would be two sources for one fact.
- ⚠️ **The one exception is a photo with no parent**, which inherits nothing — so the only thing that needed a switch is the **album**: `gallery_albums.visibility` + a `gallery_album` entry in `PublicDomain::DOMAINS`. ⚠️ It is deliberately **not** in `MATCH` (an album has no global identity — `playlist`'s exact precedent) and the module `gallery` becomes `shareable` automatically, because `ModuleController` derives that from `PublicDomain::forModule()`.
- ⚠️ **The public profile's gallery tab renders photos, not albums**, even though its *domain* is `gallery_album`: the records there are folders. Cards would say „3" over two hundred photos, so `counts['gallery_album']` is overridden with the photo count and `GET /public/profiles/{u}/gallery-photos` is its own endpoint with its own shape. ⚠️ Both new public routes must stay **above** `/{domain}` or „gallery-photos" is read as a domain.
- ⚠️ **`AlbumLock::hiddenIds()` reads `Auth::id()`, which is empty for a stranger — i.e. the lock did not work at all on the public path.** It was harmless only because nothing public read `gallery_images`; §7.4 opened exactly that door. `hiddenIdsFor($ownerId)` is the fix and the public query passes the **profile owner's** id explicitly (a logged-in visitor would otherwise have their *own* locked albums computed — a different person's list). Pinned by `PublicGalleryTest::test_a_locked_album_is_stripped_even_for_a_stranger`.
- ⚠️ **A locked photo is now *stripped*, not hidden** (`GalleryController::lockedPhotos()` and `PublicGallery::row()`): the row exists — so the grid can draw a blurred tile and ask for the password — but `path`, `remote_path` and `source_url` never leave. The 423 it replaced was correct but unusable: the user asked for blurred tiles, and a grid cannot draw what it never receives. ⚠️ **The global scope still hides rows** and every count/delete/quota path still opts out explicitly; the placeholder list is built with one deliberate `withoutGlobalScope('album_lock')`, so nothing about the security model got looser.
- ⚠️ **Width and height are sent on purpose**: they are the only two facts besides the id, and without them the grid would jump the moment the real photos arrive.
- ⚠️ **The blur is one static asset** (`frontend/public/locked-photo.svg`), not a generated thumbnail — the user named both options. A generated blur is **derived from the real photo** (colour and composition still show through), a file per photo costs disk and quota, and there is no image library in this project at all.
- ⚠️ **`AlbumVault` physically moves the files** (`gallery/images` ↔ `gallery/locked`, a private sub-folder of a public root — `videos/downloads`' precedent). Removing a path from the response was never enough: `CLAUDE.md` itself recorded that **a remembered `/storage/...` URL still opened**. ⚠️ Moving rewrites `path`, so it runs in a transaction and streams rather than `get()`-ing megabytes into a string; the quota is untouched (same file, different folder). ⚠️ **But a file does not live in a transaction, and that was the bug (Tasks BUG-03).** The old order — write the copy, delete the original, then save `path`, all inside `DB::transaction()` — meant a DB failure rolled the column back to a file that was no longer there: **every photo in the album 404s**. The order is now **copy → commit → delete the old one**, so the worst a failure can leave behind is a spare copy (and the `catch` removes that too), while the real file is still where the row points. ⚠️ The copy is made **before** the transaction opens — copying ten megabytes inside one holds the rows locked for that whole time — and the cleanup skips a target file that **already existed** (`overwrote`): we did not create it, so deleting it would be deleting someone else's photo. ⚠️ **`DB::afterCommit()` is deliberately not used** even though it is exactly for this: `RefreshDatabase` runs each test inside one transaction that never commits, so the callback would **never fire in a test** and the code would do something different in production. The price is that this service must not be called from inside an outer transaction — a nested `DB::transaction()` is a savepoint, not a commit. ⚠️ **A file missing from disk is `null`, not "moved"**: `path` stays put (it used to be rewritten to a second, equally empty address) and `moved` counts only real moves — but an **I/O error is an exception, not a skip**, because a quietly skipped photo on `seal()` means „the album is locked and one file is still on the public disk". ⚠️ `writeStream`'s result went unchecked, so a failed write still deleted the original — the file was simply lost. ⚠️ `POST /gallery/images/move` calls it too — `seal()` only covers the moment of locking, and a photo dragged into an already-locked album would have re-opened the hole.
- ⚠️ **„Unlock" for the vault means the password was removed, not that the session opened it.** A session unlock is temporary; publishing the file again is permanent, and conflating them would make one typed password expose the file forever.
- ⚠️ **`poster_path` was the lock's one remaining bypass** (§7.14): „make main" points a record's `poster_path` at a `gallery_images.path`, and the scope is on the **model**, not on that column — so a locked photo that was a record's poster still rendered, including on the public card. Sealing clears it (`saveQuietly()`, because this is a technical consequence of hiding, not a user edit).
- ⚠️ **The album password is changed with the *account* password now** (§7.8, the user's decision), not with the album's current one. That deliberately **reverses** the older „there is no recovery and that is the point" rule: forgetting an album password is no longer a dead end, while ownership is still proved — by a stronger secret. The error code is `account_password_wrong` (registered in `lib/errors.ts`).
- ⚠️ **The public unlock route does not bind the model.** `EnsureRecordOwnership` checks every route-bound model and an anonymous visitor can never own one — the endpoint would have been a permanent 404. Ownership is checked explicitly instead: the album must belong to *this* profile **and** be public, or a stranger could brute-force somebody's private album through the public door.
- ⚠️ **Its throttle keys on IP + album for anonymous callers** (`album-unlock` already fell back to `$r->ip()`); the limiter also reads `route('album')` now, because the public route deliberately uses a plain integer parameter.
- ⚠️ **The owner sees the same blurred tiles** (§7.15) rather than an empty list, and `GroupPhotos` narrows on `'locked' in page` — `GalleryLockedPage` is its own type, because making `url` optional would have let `undefined` reach an `<img src>`.
- ⚠️ **`/profile` is three summary rows now**: the password form moved **above** the public-profile card (and gained `mb-6`, since it was last), and layers 2 and 3 became a count + a „manage" button opening `PublicModulesDialog` / `VisibilityManager bare`. The rule that survived the move: **while the profile itself is private the module switches are dead** — the dialog says so out loud instead of just disabling them.
- ⚠️ **What this still does not do**: a file that was public *before* the album was locked may already be cached by a browser or a CDN. The move closes the server-side path, not somebody else's cache.

**Albums and the password lock (2026-09-16)** — the user's words: „instead of *uncategorized* introduce the name *album*; editing should open a modal; and let me lock an album with a password — if it is locked and has a password it should be blurred, not one photo visible, and you must not be able to see it from inspect either“. Sixteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The rename is not cosmetic.** The cut was named after its *leftovers* („photos with no parent“) while its content was always albums — that is where you create a folder and sort into it. „Uncategorized“ is now one card **inside** it (`album:0`, „ალბომის გარეშე“), i.e. a state rather than a section name. `/gallery/uncategorized` redirects to `/gallery/albums`, so saved links survive.
- ⚠️ **CSS blur is not a lock and that is the whole design.** A blurred `<img>` has already downloaded the file: removing the class in devtools, or opening `src`, is one click. So the lock lives on the server — **`App\Support\AlbumLock`** plus a **global scope on `GalleryImage`** — and a locked album's photos are simply never in any response. What the inspector cannot find is what was never sent.
- ⚠️ **A global scope, not a `where` in `GalleryController`.** Photos are read by more than ten places (list, groups, previews, summary, the dashboard counter, download plans); one forgotten query would have disabled the lock **silently** — the same reasoning that makes ownership a scope.
- ⚠️ **`whereNotIn` alone is wrong**: in SQL `NULL NOT IN (…)` is `NULL`, so **every album-less photo would vanish** — one locked album would have emptied the whole „no album“ card. The scope is `whereNull(album_id) OR whereNotIn(...)`.
- ⚠️ **Deletion and quota explicitly opt out** (`HasGallery::deleteGalleryMedia()`, `PurgeService`, `GalleryFetcher`/`WebImageImporter` dedupe). A hidden photo that survived its record would sit on disk as an orphan and hold the quota for ever: **the lock hides, it does not make untouchable.** Pinned by `test_deleting_the_record_still_deletes_a_locked_photo`.
- ⚠️ **The counts stay honest.** `GalleryAlbumController::row()` and `albumGroups()` count with `withoutGlobalScope('album_lock')`, so a locked album still says „12 photos“ — a card reading „0“ would be a lie. A number reveals nothing; a *path* does, and that is what never leaves.
- ⚠️ **Opening a locked album is 423 `album_locked`, never an empty list** — the scope would have answered „nothing here“, which the UI reads as „no photos“ and never asks for the password. Both routes into an album (`owner=album:N` and `album_id=N`) go through one `assertAlbumOpen()`; leaving one out would have made the lock a query parameter away.
- ⚠️ **Deleting a locked album requires unlocking first.** Deletion returns its photos to `album_id = null`, i.e. an unguarded delete would have stripped in one click exactly what you just hid — a complete bypass. Same for changing or removing the password: **the current password is required**, unless the album is already open in this session (you typed it once already).
- ⚠️ **There is no recovery and that is the point.** A „forgot it? let's remove it“ button is precisely the door the column exists to close.
- ⚠️ **Unlocking lives in the server session, not in the browser.** A token handed to the client would be readable in devtools and would survive a reload — the very thing being prevented. ⚠️ `/api` only *has* a session on a **stateful** request (Sanctum's cookie mode), so every access goes through `hasSession()` (`AuthController`'s lesson: an unguarded `session()` is a 500 for every other client) and „no session“ always means „locked“. ⚠️ **Both `unlock` endpoints therefore refuse outright when there is no session — 409 `session_required`, checked before validation and before `Hash::check` (Tasks BUG-02).** Reading „no session" as „locked" is right everywhere the lock is *read*, but on the one endpoint that *writes* it that reading silently threw the result away: the correct password answered 200 (`unlocked: true` on the public door) and the album stayed shut. And because there was nothing to store, the public endpoint was a **stateless password oracle** — no cookie needed, guarded only by an IP+album throttle that rotating IPs walks past. ⚠️ A 409 returned *after* the password check would leave that oracle in place under a different code, which is why the test asserts `Hash::shouldNotHaveReceived('check')` and not just the status. ⚠️ The question is asked through `AlbumLock::hasSession()`, never a second `$request->hasSession()` in a controller.
- ⚠️ **`AlbumLock`'s memo is keyed on the user *and* the currently unlocked ids.** Keyed on the user alone it survived into the next request in the same process — in tests (and under Octane) a password set in one request did nothing in the next. The key invalidates itself: a different unlocked set is a different key.
- ⚠️ **`throttle:album-unlock` (10/min per user *and* album)** — this is the only password check outside login, so without a ceiling a four-character password falls in minutes. Per album, because one forgotten password must not freeze the rest of the gallery.
- ⚠️ **The stack renders a locked group as a blurred surface** (`PhotoStack`'s `locked`) — a CSS gradient, no file at all. The blur is honest: there is nothing underneath it to un-blur.
- ⚠️ **The „no album“ card is gone** (2026-09-16). It went in two steps and the second one is the rule: first it moved to the end and was dimmed, then the user removed it outright — „don't have the *without album* folder in albums". It never was a folder; it was the section's *leftovers*, and a section called „Albums" has no business with them. ⚠️ **The state still exists** — a photo with no parent is §26's whole point — it is simply not drawn as a card: it stays in „all photos", in `owner=none`, and „take it out of its album" still works in the move dialog.
- ⚠️ **Removing it forced the flat view onto a new filter.** „Mixed" used to send `owner=none`, i.e. exactly the folder that was just deleted, under another name. `GET /gallery/photos` therefore gained **`album=any|none`** (in an album / in none — `album_id` cannot say either), and the albums cut's flat view is `album=any`: the same scope as the cards, flattened. A control that answers a different question than the cut it sits in is the thing this project refuses to draw.
- ⚠️ **Every card can delete all its photos** — including „no album“ — and that is deliberately **not** the same button as „delete the album“. Removing a folder must never be a way to lose a library (`album_id` is `nullOnDelete` for that reason), so the two actions are separate, each with its own confirmation and its own counted number. ⚠️ A **locked** album does not offer it: you unlock first, like every other write behind the lock. ⚠️ The loop moved out of `GroupsCut` into **`lib/galleryDelete.ts`** (`useDeleteGroupPhotos`), because there is no bulk endpoint — `DELETE /gallery/images/{id}` is per photo, so it re-reads page 1 in chunks with a round cap — and a second copy would have drifted the day one of them learned to invalidate the quota caches.
- ⚠️ **The move dialog picks an album from a list and can create one on the spot** (`components/gallery/AlbumPicker.tsx`, 2026-09-16 — the user: „album creation at the moment of moving, the albums should show up while moving, roughly like a file manager"). A `Select` could not do this: it only offers what **already exists**, so until you had made your first album the whole axis was an empty dropdown — i.e. the most common case („I want a folder for exactly these photos") sent you to another screen and broke the move in half. A newly created album is **selected immediately**, which is the only reason you created it. ⚠️ It is a list rather than a dropdown because a folder is known by its contents: each row carries the photo count and a lock icon, none of which fits a one-line `SelectItem`. ⚠️ The two pseudo-rows („don't change the album" `''`, „take it out of its album" `none`) stay — three distinct answers, and dropping either would make one of them unsayable. ⚠️ Moving **into** a locked album is allowed and the picker says so out loud: the photo disappears from view at once (which may well be the point), and without the warning that reads as „I lost a photo".
- ⚠️ **Editing moved into a modal** (`components/gallery/AlbumDialog.tsx`). It used to be one input wedged into the toolbar, so the focus jumped from the card to the far corner of the screen and an album could hold nothing but a name; the name, the description and the lock are one question and now one window. The dialogs sit in a `const dialogs` rendered by **both** return branches — `GroupsCut`'s live bug (state in the component, JSX in one branch, button does nothing).
- ⚠️ **A locked album's photos also disappear from `/profile`'s upload library**, because `StorageMeter::files()` deliberately does *not* opt out. The quota total still counts them (it is read from `storage_used_bytes`), so the list and the total can differ — that is the intended trade: a thumbnail there would defeat the lock.
- ⚠️ **What this does not do**: the file itself stays on the public disk, so anyone who already knows a photo's exact `/storage/...` URL can still open it. Nothing in the app hands that URL out any more. Making even a remembered URL fail means moving locked albums onto the **private** disk (`StorageFolder::PRIVATE_FOLDERS`, the `videos/downloads` precedent) — a separate, bigger change.
- ⚠️ **Feature tests: `withHeaders()` persists for the rest of the test.** One stateful `Referer` makes every later request in that test stateful too, and the `array` session keeps its data — so an album locked *through the API* stays open in the same session (deliberately: you just set the password) and the test would never see „locked“. `GalleryAlbumTest::lockedAlbum()` therefore creates it through the model.

**Bulk delete — the per-record scope, drill-down and „keep the gallery" (Tasks §25, 2026-09-15)** — three separate holes, all from one request („why can't bulk delete remove one specific bookmark; it should be possible to expand, look and delete a single item; and when you delete a film, ask whether its gallery goes with it"). Eight things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`ids` now exists on all eleven targets.** It had been written only for the media domains and gallery, yet the mode is **domain-independent** (`recordIds()` is a plain `whereIn`, `modelQuery()` knows every domain) — so what was missing was the permission, not the logic, and the consequence was that a single bookmark, note, game, board game, book, song or video could not be deleted from `/purge` at all: you deleted a whole category or nothing.
- ⚠️ **The picker had to become its own endpoint, `GET /admin/purge/records`**, and that fixed a live bug rather than adding convenience: the old pool read the module's own `index()`, which `BelongsToUser`'s `owner` scope cuts to **my** rows — so an admin clearing *someone else's* library was shown their own films and sent those ids to the target account. Pinned by `PurgeTest::test_the_record_pool_belongs_to_the_target_account`.
- ⚠️ **The title is decided on the server.** Eleven domains: some carry `title`, some `title_ka`/`title_en` — one map written on both sides would drift.
- ⚠️ **`media_type` was validated as `in:movie,series`**, i.e. the gallery target refused **anime**. It is `MediaDomain::rule()` now, in all three places it appears.
- ⚠️ **The drill-down narrows the *queue*, never the scope** (`PurgeItemList`): `mode`/`ids` stay exactly as chosen and only which items get enqueued changes, so „counted" and „deleted" still come from one query — the rule the whole of `PurgeService` stands on. Exclusions are stored **by id**, because the plan is re-fetched and an index would point at a different record.
- ⚠️ **A single record's delete needs no typed word.** `DELETE` is typed so a library cannot vanish on one click; deleting one record is the same action as in the section's own grid, i.e. an ordinary confirm. The typed word stays on the bulk run.
- ⚠️ **`keep_gallery` travels with every queue step, not with the dialog** — on the item endpoint too. Sent only once, the first record's photos would survive and every other record's would not.
- ⚠️ **„Keep the photos" and „free up space" are mutually exclusive, and the plan says so**: the files stay on disk and in the quota, so `plan.bytes` excludes them and `kept_photos` is reported as its own number. Promising space that never appears is the failure mode this avoids.

**Translations (Tasks 7)** — `/translations` (page) + a header badge (`GET /translations/summary`). Two services: `Services/Translation/TranslationScanner` decides *what is missing* and `ItemTranslator` fills it. ⚠️ **A translation needs a source**: a record counts as translatable only when a text exists in one language and not the other — text missing in *both* is a `/sync` problem, not a translation one, and treating it otherwise would leave the header badge permanently lit. Order of sources is deliberate: **TMDB first** (free and authoritative — `details($id,'ka')`, and `TmdbClient::genreList('ka')` returns real Georgian genre names, so genres never reach the translator), then `Translator` for the remainder. `Translator` is **bidirectional** (`translate($text,$to,$context)`, `toGeorgian`/`toEnglish`) and exposes `configured()`. ⚠️ **Its one source is Google Gemini since 2026-09-14** (`GEMINI_API_KEY`, free tier, no credit card) — **Claude was removed at the user's instruction**: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` only works on Console credits (a Claude Code subscription is *not* an API key), so there was no free option there. ⚠️ **The free Google Translate fallback went with it**: it returned 429, i.e. it was a *silently failing* source — the UI said "translating" and no text arrived, the same trap that removed the reminders' email channel. Without a key `configured()` is false and the page says only TMDB's text will come. **The key went in on 2026-09-14 and the whole path was verified live** — three real descriptions, correct Georgian, 1.9 / 3.0 / 5.7 s. Six things came out of that live run and every one of them is load-bearing:
- ⚠️ **Thinking must be turned off** (`generationConfig.thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget = 0`). It is not an optimisation: with thinking on, a **236-character** description burned **1183 thinking tokens**, blew past the 45 s timeout and came back as a `ConnectionException`, i.e. the record was silently left untranslated. Off, the same text takes **1.3 s**. ⚠️ The field name is exactly that — `thinkingLevel` is a **400** on `generateContent` (both checked live).
- ⚠️ **But `thinkingBudget` is not universal: every `*-flash-lite` model rejects it with a 400.** So `Translator` sends it, and **on a 400 retries once without it** — a *model list* would have gone stale, and without the fallback a lite model in `GEMINI_MODEL` would have killed translation outright and silently (400 is not transient, so `translate()` just returns `null`).
- ⚠️ **The model is pinned (`gemini-3.5-flash`), deliberately not the `gemini-flash-latest` alias.** On the same day the alias returned a 90 s timeout and a 503 while the pinned model answered in 1.3 s. An alias follows whatever Google points it at today; translation wants stability. Swapping it is one `.env` line (`GEMINI_MODEL`), and the lite fallback above makes any choice safe.
- ⚠️ **Transient failures are retried** (429/500/503 and connection errors, 3 attempts, 2 s apart). Gemini really does answer "this model is currently experiencing high demand"; with a single attempt the field stays empty while the queue reports "done" — the silent skip this project treats as the worst kind of bug. A **400/403 is not retried**: a bad model or key does not improve on the third try.
- ⚠️ **The key travels in the `X-goog-api-key` header, not `?key=`** — a query key becomes part of the URL and lands in logs, proxy records and exception messages.
- ⚠️ **The response's `parts[0]` is not the answer**: 3.x models can return thinking parts (`thought: true`), so `Translator::textOf()` concatenates only the non-thought parts. ⚠️ **`thoughtSignature` is a different thing and filtering on it would delete the text** — it sits on the *same* part as the translation (`{"text": "მატრიცა", "thoughtSignature": "…"}`, seen live). Only `thought` is checked.
⚠️ **`translateDelayMs` defaults to 4000 ms** because the free tier allows ~15 requests a minute; the old 1000 ms meant 60 and would have walked the queue into 429s. ⚠️ **`phpunit.xml` pins `GEMINI_API_KEY` empty** — otherwise the developer's real key would spend live quota from the test suite, and the "is the translator configured" tests would pass or fail depending on whose machine ran them. Existing text is **never overwritten** (the one exception is the explicit `review` mode below); a ka result that contains no Mkhedruli is rejected (`Lang::georgian()`), which is how "the translator handed back the original" is caught. Records go through the queue one at a time (`kind: 'translate'`), genres are one queue unit for the whole dictionary. **Videos are out of scope** — `videos.title`/`description` are plain columns with no bilingual schema. **Where a description came from is visible on the record page** (`<movie>_translations.source`: `tmdb` · `translation` · `manual`). ⚠️ **Editing a description by hand sets `source = 'manual'`** — otherwise a record kept claiming it was machine-translated after the user had rewritten it, i.e. the badge lied. ⚠️ It is only re-stamped **when the text actually changed**: merely opening and saving the form must not relabel TMDB's text as hand-written. ⚠️ The badge reports the source of **the language actually rendered** — with `ka` empty the `en` text is shown, so the `en` source is the honest one to name.

**Translation sources, log and quota (2026-09-14)** — the user's instruction was literal: *you* pick whether it translates with Google or from TMDB, „what was translated by what" must be visible on the page and in the log, and Gemini's limit must show used/remaining. Nine things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`sources[]` travels with every queue item, not with the dialog.** `ui/queue.tsx` sends one request per record, so the choice lives on `QItem.sources` — without it the *second* record would silently fall back to the server default and „TMDB only" would quietly become „both".
- ⚠️ **An empty `sources` array is a 422 (`no_translation_source`), an absent key means both.** The first is the whole point („it must not happen by itself"); the second keeps an older SPA and the CLI working.
- ⚠️ **`persist()` used to label TMDB's own text as a machine translation.** `<domain>_translations.source` was hard-coded to `translation`, so the record page's badge lied whenever TMDB supplied the Georgian. It is now written from `providers[field]` — the same map the API returns and the audit log stores. Pinned by `TranslationTest::test_tmdb_text_is_not_labelled_as_a_machine_translation`.
- ⚠️ **`AuditLog::ACTION_TRANSLATE` is its own action and could not be `update`.** The text lands in `<domain>_translations`, which is **not** in `AuditRegistry::MODELS` — so `AuditObserver` never saw a translation at all and the log had no record of it. `new_values` is the field → provider map (that *is* „what by what"), `old_values` keeps the requested source list. It is filed **on the record**, and skipped records write nothing — a 300-record run must not bury the log under 300 „skipped" rows.
- ⚠️ **The `/translations` log is read from `audit_logs`, not from a second table.** One fact, one place; the same rows `/audit` renders. `translation_usages` deliberately does **not** back it — that table counts *calls* (one record can be two), so mixing the two would make both numbers wrong.
- ⚠️ **The quota window is a Pacific day, not a UTC one** (`TranslationUsage::QUOTA_TIMEZONE`). Google's free-tier daily limit resets at midnight Pacific; counting by UTC day would be wrong for 7–8 hours out of every 24.
- ⚠️ **The counter is *ours* and the UI says so.** Gemini exposes no usage API (unlike SerpApi's `GET /account`, which returns the real number), so `translation_usages` is the only source and a call made by another program is invisible to it. `GEMINI_DAILY_LIMIT` (1500) / `GEMINI_RPM_LIMIT` (15) are our accounting ceilings; `0` turns the check off.
- ⚠️ **A blocked call writes no row, a failed one does.** The first never reached Google (recording it would inflate our own counter); the second did — Gemini's quota counts rejected requests, which is exactly why a 429 arrives. Pinned by `test_an_exhausted_quota_never_reaches_gemini`.
- ⚠️ **Exhaustion is a named failure, never a silent skip.** `Translator::lastError()` separates „the source fell over" from „the limit is gone" (the `bgg_unavailable` rule), and `ItemTranslator` turns a nothing-filled run with `gemini_quota_exceeded` into `ok: false` — otherwise the queue would report „done" over an untranslated library.

**Translation `review` mode (2026-09-14)** — the one place in the project where **existing text is overwritten**, which is exactly why it is a named, default-off flag and not a quiet behaviour: `POST /translations/{type}/{id}` and `/translations/plan` take `review: true`, and Gemini compares the record's **Georgian synopsis** with the English original and corrects it. Verified live the day it was built (1.6 s; a synopsis with wrong case endings came back correct). Nine things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **It is a separate flag, not a third `source`.** The two axes are different: a *source* fills an **empty** field, review rewrites an **existing** one. Folded into the list it would have made „TMDB only + review" impossible — which is the most useful combination there is, since TMDB is what produced the text being checked.
- ⚠️ **Description only, never the title.** `<domain>_translations.source` is a **row**-level column and in practice describes the description; rewriting the title would leave that row still claiming „it's TMDB's" over machine-edited text, i.e. the record page's badge would lie. A title is also one or two words — „correcting" TMDB's official Georgian name is more likely to do harm.
- ⚠️ **`ka` only.** TMDB's English text is the original, not a translation, so there is nothing to check it against.
- ⚠️ **Only `source = 'tmdb'` is eligible.** `manual` is the user's own writing (overwriting it is the worst damage this code could do) and `translation` is Gemini's own output — re-checking it with the same model is spend, not verification. Pinned by `test_review_never_touches_manual_or_machine_text`.
- ⚠️ **The English original must be TMDB's too.** If the `en` text was written by Gemini from the `ka` in the same pass, comparing against it is a circle — we would be checking a translation against the translation of itself.
- ⚠️ **The reviewed text becomes `source = 'translation'`** (the user's answer, 2026-09-14): the card has two things to say — „it's TMDB's" or „a machine translated it" — and reviewed text *is* the machine's now. `providers` keeps the distinct value **`review`**, so the audit log and `/translations`' list can still say exactly *what did what*; the audit row's `old_values` carries `review: 1`.
- ⚠️ **„No correction needed" and „the call failed" are one answer (`null`)** on purpose — in both cases the text must not change, so the caller's behaviour is identical; the reason stays in `lastError()`. The model is asked to *return the text unchanged*, never to emit a sentinel: a marker like `NO_CHANGE` would eventually land in somebody's synopsis.
- ⚠️ **`TranslationScanner::reviewable()` mirrors those limits exactly.** The plan must not queue a record the translator will refuse — a 300-item run that is 95% „skipped" is indistinguishable from a broken feature. `summary()` counts it in the **same pass** and returns it as `reviewable`, deliberately **outside `total`**: `total` is the header badge („you have N to translate"), and review is a choice, not a gap — inside the sum it would light the badge forever. The page therefore names the number separately and opens the dialog on it, or a fully translated library could never reach the mode at all.
- ⚠️ **`Translator::ask()` is the single call site** behind both `translate()` and `review()` — the quota check and the `translation_usages` row exist once. A second copy is precisely how „our own counter" would drift from what was actually spent. Review's usage row is labelled `review <context>`, and it costs **one** call (the original travels in the same prompt). The genre dictionary deliberately gets no review: a genre name is one or two words from TMDB's official list.

**Approvals (`approval_requests`)** — registration is open but a new account starts empty: the user asks for modules on `/modules` and a super-admin approves on `/admin`. Genres are global, so a non-admin's `DELETE /genres/{id}` returns **202 + a pending request** instead of deleting (super-admin deletes directly). **Quota-increase requests (Tasks 17.4) are the third type** (`storage_increase`, `POST /api/requests/storage`). All three share one table and `AdminRequestController::approve()`; the genre side runs through `Services/Genres/GenreRemover`, which counts/reassigns across **all** users. ⚠️ `approval_requests.type` is a **plain string, not an enum** — an enum would need a migration per type and sqlite (tests) renders it as a `check` constraint that rejects new values; the set of types lives in `ApprovalRequest::TYPE_*`. Adding a type = one constant + one handler in `approve()`. A pending request can be approved from `/requests` **or** from `/users/{id}` (Tasks 1.3) — same endpoints, and `lib/display.ts::requestLabel()` is the single label renderer for all three views.

**Storage quotas (Tasks 17.1/17.3)** — `users.storage_quota_bytes` (default 1 GB, admin-editable on `/users/{id}`) + `users.storage_used_bytes`, a **cached counter** maintained by deltas. `Services/Storage/StorageMeter` is the **single definition of what counts** (decided in Tasks §19.4, option B): **only the user's own uploads** — avatar, a *manually uploaded* poster (`poster_source = 'upload'`), video/song thumbnails, `video_files` and `gallery_images`. **TMDB posters and actor photos do not count** (shared files the user never chose). Every upload must go through `StorageMeter::storeUpload()` (guards the quota, then stores, then adds) and every delete through `deleteUpload()` / the `StoredFile` trait's `deleted` hook (`GalleryImage`, `VideoFile`) — any other path silently desyncs the counter. ⚠️ **Releasing a record's own upload belongs in the model's `deleting` hook, never in the controller** (`Movie::deletePoster()`, `Series::deletePoster()`, `Video`/`Song::deleteThumbnail()`, `Book`/`Game::deleteCover()`, `BoardGame::deleteImage()`). `PurgeService` deletes with a bare `$record->delete()` *precisely so the events fire*, so cleanup written in `destroy()` is skipped by bulk delete: until 2026-09-06 movie/series/video/song did it in the controller, which left the file on disk, kept the bytes counted forever, and made `plan()`'s "bytes freed" a lie (book/game/board_game had always done it in the model — that asymmetry is what exposed it). Pinned by `StorageManagementTest::test_deleting_a_record_releases_its_upload_at_model_level`, which deletes **through the model**, not the endpoint. ⚠️ Only `*_source === 'upload'` is released; a TMDB/RAWG/BGG/Open Library file is shared and must survive the record (a second test pins that too). Over quota returns **413 `storage_quota_exceeded`** (deliberately not 422, so the frontend can tell it from validation); `lib/errors.ts` translates that code via `errors.*` — **a new machine code must be added to its `CODES` list**, otherwise the toast shows the raw string, and `isApiCode(e, code)` is there for when the UI needs a distinct state rather than a toast (e.g. `bgg_unavailable`). `StorageMeter::files()` also backs both file lists — the admin one on `/users/{id}` and the user's own upload library — through one shared `components/StorageLibrary.tsx`, so the two views can never disagree. ⚠️ **Since 2026-09-13 the user-facing storage is one block on `/profile`** (`components/StorageCard.tsx`): total + bar + per-module breakdown + recalculate → **the upload library expanded** → the increase request → orphan cleanup. The 2026-09-11 split (library on `/profile`, everything else on `/settings`) put **one fact on two pages** and each page carried a link to the other — those links were the admission that the split was wrong. ⚠️ **`/settings` keeps exactly one storage thing: `StorageAllocations`** — „how much is allowed" is genuinely a setting, „how much I have and what I uploaded" is an account fact. `components/StorageFilesCard.tsx` is gone (folded in), and `StorageAllocations` gained a `bare` prop because it now has its own section heading instead of sitting under one. ⚠️ **`WebQuotaCard` moved to `/profile` too but stays its own card** — SerpApi's monthly *search* budget is not disk, and one block showing both would read as a single number. ⚠️ **Orphan cleanup is still `super_admin`-only** — moving the block to a user page does not change who may run a global cleanup (the backend gate is unchanged). ⚠️ **In that library one click selects, it does not open** (§2.9's rule); opening and downloading are separate buttons. Bulk delete rides on the *same* `DELETE /api/storage/files` with `paths[]`/`all` (one rule, one place) and goes through `StorageMeter::deleteOwnFiles()`, which snapshots `files()` **once** — a per-path loop meant one full DB+disk scan per file. `POST /api/storage/files/download` zips a selection via `StorageMeter::archiveOwnFiles()`. ⚠️ **The zip does include private-disk files and that is deliberate**: §17.5's rule is that a private file must not leave the server *without an ownership check*, and this route is behind `auth:sanctum` and resolves every path inside that user's own `files()`. The per-file download button still stays hidden for private files — it points at `/storage/*`, which has no auth. ⚠️ **Each row carries `private`** (`StorageFolder::isPrivate($path)`), because `/storage/*` cannot reach the private disk: without it the page drew a broken `<img>` and a 404 download link for every note attachment, chat media and `<module>/fields` upload (fixed 2026-09-06). The flag is the **backend's** to state — a copy of `PRIVATE_ROOTS` in the SPA would eventually drift and expose a private file under a public URL. Private rows render a lock icon and get no download button; downloading them stays in each section's own ownership-checked route. Each row carries `owner_type`/`owner_id`, which is how **`DELETE /api/storage/files`** routes a single delete (`StorageMeter::deleteOwnFile()`: clear the column or drop the `video_files`/`gallery_images` row); it looks `path` up **inside that user's own `files()`**, so another account's file — and any shared TMDB poster — is a 404 by construction. **Orphan cleanup (`GET|POST /api/storage/orphans[/clean]`) is global, not per-user** and therefore `super_admin`-only: uploads share folders, so a file nothing references has no owner left. It scans only `StorageMeter::UPLOAD_FOLDERS` (recursively — see the folder table above) and skips anything touched in the last hour (an upload may still be in flight). ⚠️ **Every new upload column must also be listed in `StorageMeter::referencedPaths()`** — anything missing there is classified as an orphan and *deleted* by the admin's cleanup. `games.cover_path` and `game_files.path` were missing from 2026-09-04 until it was caught the same day; `note_entry_files.path` was added with the module and `messages.attachment_path` with chat media (§16.3). **Increase requests (17.4)** ride on `approval_requests` (see *Approvals* above): the user asks on **`/profile`** (inside the storage block since 2026-09-13), an admin applies it from `/requests` or `/users/{id}`. ⚠️ `payload.requested_bytes` is the **desired total quota, not a delta** — a delta would compound if two requests were approved; `granted_bytes` lets the admin hand out less than asked without it counting as a rejection. **Per-module allocation (17.2) is live**: `module_user.storage_limit_bytes` (nullable) + `PUT /api/storage/allocations`, edited on `/settings` (`components/StorageAllocations.tsx`) — **the only storage control left there**. ⚠️ `null` means *no dedicated limit* (the module draws from the shared pool) while `0` means *uploads blocked* — that is why the column is nullable and why the API takes `null` to clear. The sum of all limits may not exceed the account quota (checked **before** writing, 422 `allocation_exceeds_quota`). ⚠️ **No upload site needed changing**: the module is derived from the folder (`StorageFolder::moduleFor()`), which `storeUpload()`/`storeContents()` already receive — but that map **must agree with the `module` field in `files()`**, or an upload would be counted against one module and checked against another's limit. Over a module's limit returns **413 `module_quota_exceeded`** (a separate code from `storage_quota_exceeded`: the user still has room overall, they capped this module themselves). ⚠️ Shrinking a limit below what is already used **never deletes files** — it only refuses new uploads. Pre-flight estimates (17.3) are **not needed for `/sync`** — it only fetches TMDB media, which 19.4/B excludes from the quota — they matter only for the gallery (§10).

**Data export — „ჩემი მონაცემები" (FEAT-06, 2026-09-19)** — a multi-user app had no way for an ordinary user to take their own library with them: the files zip (`POST /storage/files/download`) packs *files*, and `/admin/backups` is `super_admin` and every account at once. `GET /api/export` (what I have) + `GET /api/export/{module}?format=json|csv` fix that, backed by **`App\Support\ExportDomain`** (the registry) and **`Services\Export\RecordExporter`** (the rows), with the block on `/profile` (`components/DataExportCard.tsx`) sitting **below** the storage card — files above, records below, two halves of one question. Nine things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`PublicDomain::card()` could not be reused, and the reason runs both ways.** That shape is deliberately *narrower* than the record (it is for a stranger's eyes), while an export wants completeness; and conversely **`note` is absent from `PublicDomain` by design** (§16.5 — private documents never surface publicly) yet is one of the most important things to be able to export. "Who may see it" and "may I take my own text" are different questions.
- ⚠️ **`$record->toArray()` is never used.** It carries `user_id`, `status_id`, `sync_status`, `sort_order` — internal numbers that mean nothing outside this database — and it would silently change shape on the day a migration adds a column. A file format that changes by itself is not a format, and FEAT-07's import will stand on exactly this one.
- ⚠️ **`status` *and* `status_name` both go out.** The key never changes (it is what an import matches on); the name is what a human reads in Excel and can be renamed in the dictionary any day. Dropping either loses one of the two.
- ⚠️ **Explicit `user_id`, not the `owner` scope.** The scope reads `Auth::id()`, so run from a worker or the console it is empty and **every account's records would land in one file** — the lesson `RunBatchItem`'s `Auth::setUser()` already taught. `PurgeService::modelQuery()` writes the owner explicitly for the same reason.
- ⚠️ **`lazyById`, not `chunk`** — `chunk()` walks by offset, so a record deleted mid-export makes every later page skip one row silently (BUG-23's migration hit the same trap), and the response streams rather than being assembled in memory (`artisan serve` is single-threaded).
- ⚠️ **CSV needs a BOM and a formula guard.** Without the BOM Excel opens Mkhedruli as mojibake. A cell starting `=`/`+`/`@` is executed as a formula by Excel and Sheets, and the text here comes from **outside**: a bookmark's title is read off a remote page by `LinkMetadata`, a synopsis comes from TMDB. ⚠️ A minus is escaped **only on a non-numeric value** — otherwise `-5` would become text and the guard would corrupt the data it protects. `fputcsv`'s `escape` is explicitly `''` (RFC 4180; the default `\` is non-standard and PHP 8.4 deprecates omitting it).
- ⚠️ **Dates are wall-clock in the app timezone, not ISO-UTC**, with the zone stated once in the JSON envelope — the same reason `lib/dates.ts` uses `sv-SE` instead of `toISOString()` (BUG-15). Excel neither parses nor converts a `Z` suffix, so a Tbilisi evening would read as the previous day.
- ⚠️ **`AuditLog::ACTION_EXPORT` is its own action and the row is written *before* the stream starts.** `view`/`visit` describe opening one record, not carrying a whole module off the server. Once headers are sent a failed write could not surface, and a broken download would still read as "done" — "who, when, which module, how many records" is a fact about the *request*, not about the bytes.
- ⚠️ **Two new `RegistryConsistencyTest` checks, both against silent omission**: every record-bearing module is either in `MODULES` or in `NOT_EXPORTED` **with a reason** (only `gallery` is there — its content is *files*, which the zip already serves), and every field name is really a column, an accessor or a declared virtual. The second is not redundant: a row's key *is* the field name, so a typo (`titel_ka`) would pass a shape check and leave a silently empty column — verified by introducing one and watching the test go red.
- ⚠️ **No `module:`/`permission:` middleware on the routes.** `EnsureModulePermission` reads the module from a route parameter named `type` (`@type`); here it is `module`, so the middleware would have checked `movie`'s permission for every module. Both checks are explicit in the controller, exactly as `VisibilityController::guard()` does. Both routes are **`GET`** — an export is a read, and a POST would be derived as `create`, so a view-only role could not take its own data.

**CSV import from another service (FEAT-07, 2026-09-19)** — bringing a library over from Letterboxd/IMDb (films), Goodreads (books) or Steam (games) was a one-record-at-a-time job. `GET /api/import/sources` · `POST /api/import/plan` (multipart) · `POST /api/import/item`, backed by **`App\Support\ImportSource`** (which format, which column means what), **`Services\Import\CsvReader`**, **`ImportPlanner`** and **`RowImporter`**, with the three-step page at **`/import`** and a new `import` kind in the shared queue. ⚠️ **This is not the closed "link bot"** (deleted 2026-09-05): that one went looking for watch links on other people's sites; here the user uploads their own file and nothing external is consulted except the sources the app already uses. Twelve things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The plan asks the external source nothing, and that is the central decision.** An 800-row Letterboxd file would be 800 TMDB calls *just to show a number*, and then 800 more to import. The plan does only what is free: read, map the columns, and look for a **local** duplicate. The external search lives on the per-item step, where `syncDelayMs` pacing applies — the same shape as `/sync` and `/purge`.
- ⚠️ **The file is never stored.** The plan returns the full, already-normalised list in its response, so the item step does not need the file again: no temp folder, no quota, no expiry to manage. Changing the mapping means re-posting the same file, which the browser already holds.
- ⚠️ **The BOM is stripped by hand.** A CSV saved by Excel starts with three bytes, so its first column is named `\u{FEFF}Date` — which meant the signature detection and the column mapping **failed silently on exactly the files people upload most**. The fixture (`tests/Fixtures/letterboxd-watched.csv`) deliberately starts with one, and a test asserts that it does, or the first test would be checking nothing.
- ⚠️ **The delimiter is sniffed** (`,` `;` tab `|`): a European Excel writes `;`, and the wrong delimiter yields a one-column table — i.e. a meaningless "the file is empty" error rather than a wrong answer you can see.
- ⚠️ **Goodreads writes ISBNs as `="9780…"`** (an Excel trick so the number keeps its leading zeros). Read literally, the ISBN **never matched**, so every book looked new and the exact lookup silently degraded to a title search.
- ⚠️ **A rating of `0` means "unrated", not "worst".** Goodreads writes `0` for an unrated book, so copying it across would have marked a whole library one star. Letterboxd's 0.5–5 is scaled ×2 onto the app's 1–10; `ImportSource::rating()` is the one place that conversion lives.
- ⚠️ **Status is matched by `role`, never by name** (§6.4). My „ნანახი" and your „ვნახე" are the same thing with different keys, so a role is the only thing an outside file can be answered with — pinned by a test that renames the dictionary row first. It is set through `applyStatus()`, because that is the only writer of `watched_at` (BUG-05's lesson), and the file's own date replaces "now" when it parses.
- ⚠️ **Duplicates are checked twice and both are needed.** The plan checks locally (`imdb_id`/`isbn` first, then title+year); the item step checks again, because `tmdb_id` is only known *after* the search. "Already have it" is the server's word, exactly as `/purge`'s `plan()` and `run()` share one query.
- ⚠️ **Identity quality differs per source and the UI says so.** IMDb's `Const` is exact; Letterboxd has only title+year, i.e. a search that can be wrong; Goodreads has an ISBN; Steam's `appid` **cannot be queried on RAWG's free API at all**, so a game is found by name — the appid still travels, because it is the only thing that separates two rows with the same name.
- ⚠️ **Genre/category are left empty, deliberately.** "Status and type are mandatory" is a **form** rule (`validated()`'s `$must`), not a schema constraint, and `POST /{domain}/from-tmdb` — one-click add from discover — bypasses it in exactly the same way. Mapping a Goodreads shelf onto a per-user genre dictionary would be guesswork; the half that *is* in the file (status) is filled.
- ⚠️ **An unrecognised format is a 422 (`import_source_unknown`), not an empty plan** — an empty list reads as "the file is empty" and sends the user looking for another file instead of picking the source by hand. The response carries the headers so they can.
- ⚠️ **No `module:`/`permission:` middleware on the routes**: the module is decided by the file's *contents*, not by the URL, so `permission:@type` would have nothing to read. Both checks are explicit in the controller (`VisibilityController::guard()`'s shape), and `AuditLog::ACTION_IMPORT` records only what the model event cannot know — **which source** a record came from.

**Statistics — `/stats` (FEAT-08, 2026-09-19)** — 19.10's "counts only, status breakdown later" had stayed "later": the dashboard is numbers, and "how much did I watch this year", the genre and score distributions and the month-by-month picture existed nowhere, although every input was already stored (`watched_at`, `rating`, `status.role`, `genreables`). `GET /api/stats?year=` answers it in one request for every enabled module, built by **`Services\Stats\LibraryStats`** with the driver split isolated in **`App\Support\SqlDate`**. Nine things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **This does not replace the dashboard and does not reverse 19.10.** A dashboard card answers "what do I have" (one number and a link); this page answers "what did I do". Six numbers on a card would have wrecked the navigation, which is exactly what that decision protected.
- ⚠️ **No chart library was added.** recharts is ≈100 kB gzip — twice what this project refused dnd-kit over — and every cut here is a horizontal bar, i.e. one `div` and a `width: %`. The value is in the numbers, not in axes.
- ⚠️ **`year()` and `strftime('%Y', …)` are not interchangeable, and `whereYear()` does not save you.** Laravel abstracts the *filter*; `group by` takes an expression and has no abstraction at all. Written one way it works on MySQL and silently returns an **empty group** on sqlite — the chart draws zeroes and nothing errors. `SqlDate` returns a **string**, not an `Expression`, because the caller needs to append `as bucket`, and because `pluck(Expression)` is read by Laravel as a *column name* (it failed with `Undefined property: stdClass::$integer)`).
- ⚠️ **Each cut gets a fresh query from a `$base` factory, never a shared builder.** `select('id')` and `groupBy` mutate the builder **in place**, so one shared object silently corrupts the next cut — that is precisely why the year list came back empty on the first run.
- ⚠️ **Status runs on two mechanisms at once** (§6.4): six domains resolve the name out of the **owner's** dictionary here (returning an `id` would tell the page nothing), while book/game/board game hand back the enum key and the name lives in `ka.json`.
- ⚠️ **A polymorphic genre must be cut by `genreable_type`.** `genreables` is one table for all three media domains, so without the filter a series' genres appear in the film cut — pinned by its own test.
- ⚠️ **The "other" row preserves the sum.** Genres past the top 12 collapse into one row, or the bars would stop adding up to the total and the chart would quietly lie.
- ⚠️ **`done_at` does not exist everywhere and that is not an omission** — a book has no "when did I read it" column. Substituting `updated_at` would put two different facts in one column, the trap that keeps a video's `watched_at` separate from its status. Those modules simply get no month cut.
- ⚠️ **The year picker lists *activity* years, not release years** ("a 2011 film" and "watched in 2011" are different facts), and the month cut always returns **all twelve months, zeroes included** — otherwise the year stretches unevenly and "nothing in July" is indistinguishable from a July that was never in the list.
- ⚠️ **Ratings are bucketed in PHP, deliberately.** `rating` is `decimal(3,1)` and SQL's `round()` returns a different type per driver, while the number of distinct values is under ten — so grouping is cheap and the bucketing stays verifiable.

**Seasons and episodes (FEAT-09, 2026-09-19)** — `series.seasons`/`episodes` were only *counts*, so the "watching" status could never say where you stopped. Two tables now answer it: **`tv_episodes`** (TMDB's fact) and **`episode_watches`** (mine), with `TmdbClient::tvSeason()`, `Services\Episodes\EpisodeSync` (fetch) and `EpisodeProgress` (progress, next, status), behind `GET|POST|PATCH /api/media/episodes/{type}/{id}` and rendered by `components/EpisodeTracker.tsx`. Eleven things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The table is `tv_episodes`, not `series_episodes`, because it serves anime too.** `animes.tmdb_id` is also a TMDB *tv* id — `TvEnricher` fills both from `/tv/*` — so keying on a `series` row would have produced two nearly identical tables. One endpoint covers both domains, the same shape as `/media/sync/{type}/{id}`.
- ⚠️ **The split is about meaning, not normalisation.** An episode's name and air date are the same for every account, so `tv_episodes` has **no `user_id`** and sits with `genres`/`cast_members` as a global dictionary; what is mine is the watch row. Storing a 500-episode show once per account would multiply it by the number of accounts.
- ⚠️ **"Watched" is the existence of the row, not an `is_watched` column** — two sources for one fact is the trap `Book::syncProgress()` fights. `watched_at` only answers *when*.
- ⚠️ **Fetching is an explicit button, never part of sync.** The episode list is **one TMDB call per season** (`append_to_response` cannot bring it), so wiring it into the ordinary sync would mean hundreds of requests across a library for data most records never need.
- ⚠️ **Season 0 is skipped.** On TMDB that is "specials" (recaps, behind the scenes) and it is not counted in the season total — including it would leave a fully watched show permanently under 100%.
- ⚠️ **The status only moves forward** (`todo` → `doing` → `done`), and by **role**, never by name (§6.4). "I finished it and I'm rewatching" is a legitimate state that an automatic downgrade would silently erase.
- ⚠️ **"Next" is the first *unwatched* episode, not the one after the last watched.** A show can have a gap in the middle (or gain an episode later), and "the next one" would bury it forever.
- ⚠️ **Marking a whole season builds its id list on the server.** The browser only has the page it loaded; a season has to be complete.
- ⚠️ **Writing is one `upsert()` per season**, not `updateOrInsert()` in a loop: one query instead of twenty, and the update column list is explicit, so `created_at` is not rewritten on every sync.
- ⚠️ **`still_path` deliberately does not exist.** `RegistryConsistencyTest` looks for every `*path*` column in `StorageMeter`'s map, and the accordion does not need thumbnails — one fewer column is one fewer exception.
- ⚠️ **`RegistryConsistencyTest` caught both new models**, which is the guard working: every model is either in `AuditRegistry::MODELS` or in `NOT_LOGGED` **with a reason**. Both are in the second — an episode tick is one click (a season is twenty at once, the same reasoning that keeps chat reactions out), and `TvEpisode` is TMDB's fact rather than a person's action.

**"Coming soon" — `GET /api/upcoming` (FEAT-10, 2026-09-19)** — an upcoming event (the next episode airing, a game release, a note falling due) appeared nowhere: `status = todo/doing` plus a date is already information, and it was quietly losing its value. `series.next_air_at`/`next_season`/`next_episode` (and the same on `animes`) are filled during the ordinary sync, `Services\Calendar\Upcoming` collects the window, and `components/UpcomingCard.tsx` draws it on the dashboard **above** the cards — dated information goes stale, so it cannot sit at the bottom. Eight things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **It costs no extra TMDB call.** `next_episode_to_air` already arrives inside `tvDetails()`, so the calendar is a by-product of the sync that was happening anyway.
- ⚠️ **The field is always overwritten, never filled with `?:`.** Every other field is written "only when empty" because a user's text must not be clobbered — this one is a **moving fact**: a preserved old date would stand in the past forever. Clearing it on `null` matters just as much, because a finished show has no next episode.
- ⚠️ **`tv_episodes.air_date` (FEAT-09) is deliberately not a second source.** It only exists once the user has explicitly fetched episodes, so leaning on it would make the calendar appear and disappear according to an unrelated action — the `Book::syncProgress()` trap.
- ⚠️ **Books, board games and films cannot appear, and that is not an omission** — they carry only a `year`, not a date, and placing them on 1 January would be an invented fact.
- ⚠️ **Notes are in** (`due_at`): the date is the user's own, but today it is visible **only inside the notes section**, i.e. invisible while working anywhere else. §16.5 forbids notes on a **public profile**, not on the owner's own dashboard.
- ⚠️ **The window starts at the beginning of today, not at "now"** — an episode that aired this evening still belongs to today.
- ⚠️ **"S05E03" is built in the UI**, from two numbers the server returns: a composed label is language-dependent and would then exist in two places.
- ⚠️ **`Http::fake()` *appends* stubs, it does not replace them.** `Factory::fake()` pushes each URL through `stubUrl()` and never clears the list, so calling `Http::fake()` a second time **silently changes nothing** — the first matching stub still wins. A test that needs two different responses must keep one stub and vary the data behind it (a closure over a property), which is exactly how this feature's clearing case is pinned.

**Bulk delete (Tasks 20)** — `/purge` + `Services/Purge/PurgeService`, `super_admin` only. ⚠️ **The most destructive code in the project.** Three rules hold it together: the scope is always **explicit** (`mode` + params; `all` is its own mode, so an empty filter can never mean "everything"), `plan()` and `run()`/`runOne()` share one query so *counted* and *deleted* cannot drift, and deletion always goes **through the model** (never `delete()` on a query) so the `deleting` events fire — files, quota counter and polymorphic pivots. `target = 'gallery'` deletes only the photos and keeps the records. **Which scope fits which target is one table, `PurgeService::TARGET_MODES`** (mirrored on the frontend as `PURGE_TARGET_MODES` in `api/account.ts`), and statuses are per-domain too, so `status=read` on a movie is a **422 `invalid_status`** instead of a silently empty scope. ⚠️ **Since §6.4 the status list is read through `PurgeService::statusesFor($target, $userId)`, not straight off `TARGET_STATUSES`**: six domains have a per-user dictionary, so the valid keys are **the target account's** — `/purge` deletes somebody else's library, and checking their renamed „watched“ against *my* defaults would 422 a perfectly good request. `TARGET_STATUSES` survives as the built-in fallback (a film is `watched`, a book `read`, a board game `owned`, a note `done`) and `RegistryConsistencyTest` pins it against `StatusDomain::defaultKeys()`.
