Adding a module = one row in each of the four maps (`TARGET_MODES`, `TARGET_STATUSES`, `SECTION_TABLES` for its `<module>_files`/`<module>_notes` — the notes model may be `null`, as `note` has files but no attached-notes table, `UPLOAD_COLUMNS` for the metered upload column — `note` has none) + the model in `modelQuery()`/`morphAlias()`. ⚠️ The `type` mode reads a **different column per domain** (`type_id` on video, `category_id` on note, `genre_id` elsewhere, a pivot on game) — it is one `match`, not an `if`. ⚠️ **The `tag` mode is the dangerous one, because its `match` arm is `'tag' => null`** (Tasks BUG-16): the SQL scope is the *whole library* and the cut happens afterwards, in PHP — so a target the filter does not recognise is not "nothing", it is **everything**. That list used to be hand-written (`['video', 'song', 'book', 'note']`) beside a `TARGET_MODES` that allowed five, so `/purge` on a bookmark with `mode=tag` planned and **deleted the account's every bookmark** — and because `plan()` and `run()` share one query, the plan showed that same total and read as the tag's count. It is derived now (`PurgeService::supportsTag()`, one source), an unsupported target is a **422 `mode_not_supported_for_target`** rather than a silent "all", and `RegistryConsistencyTest` pins the one assumption left: a `tag` target must really have a `tags` column. On the frontend the same two facts live in `PurgePage`'s `DICTIONARIES` and `STATUS_NAMESPACE` maps; a module missing from them silently renders an **empty** type list or a raw i18n key (that is what happened to `game` until 2026-09-04). ⚠️ `keep_favorites` is applied to **every** target, so the checkbox is shown for every target — it used to be hidden on video/song/gallery while still being sent. The UI is a two-step: plan → typed `DELETE` confirmation → **per-item queue** (`enqueuePurge`, queue kind `purge`), one short `POST /admin/purge/item` per record, so progress shows and `artisan serve` never blocks. `plan.items` is the queue; on the gallery target it lists **only records that actually have photos** (`galleryOwnerIds()`) — a cast photo counts for every record that actor appears in, so the second record legitimately reports "skipped". An id outside the target account is a **200 + `skipped`**, not an error: one bad id must not break the loop.

**Audit log (Tasks §4)** — `/audit` + `audit_logs`, built 2026-09-10. The requirement was literal: "everywhere, absolutely every module, full logging". ⚠️ **`Services/Audit/AuditLogger` is the single write point** and `Observers/AuditObserver` feeds it — the observer is attached in **one loop** in `AppServiceProvider::boot()` over **`App\Support\AuditRegistry::MODELS`**, so no controller contains logging code and no module can be forgotten. Adding a model to the log is **one row in that map**; `RegistryConsistencyTest` now fails if a `modules` row has no model there. Eight things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`moduleFor()` asks the model first (`auditModule()`), then the map** — `Status` is one table serving six modules (`statuses.module`), so a static class→module row would file every status change under one section and the log's module filter would never find it.
- ⚠️ **`AuditRegistry::typeFor()` must not call `getMorphClass()`** — `enforceMorphMap()` *throws* for a model outside the map, and most logged models (`Module`, `Role`, every `<module>_files`) are outside it. The map is read directly (`Relation::morphMap()`), falling back to snake_case.
- ⚠️ **`labelFor()` reads raw attributes only**, and consults accessors solely when their relation is *already loaded*. `Movie::$title_ka` sits on the `translations` relation: reading it inside the `created` event **cached an empty collection** on the model, and the real title became unreadable afterwards. Logging must never mutate what it logs.
- ⚠️ **`AuditLogger::$tableExists` memoises only `true`.** A `false` memo silently disabled logging for the whole process whenever something asked before the migration ran (`migrate:fresh`, the test bootstrap, the first request of a deploy) — which is exactly how the first version shipped broken.
- ⚠️ **`update` logs only the keys that actually changed** (`getChanges()`, `updated_at` stripped), with both sides under the same keys — that *is* §4.1's "old and new side by side". A `touch()`-only change writes nothing.
- ⚠️ **"Which section did they enter" comes from the SPA** (`lib/audit.ts` → `POST /api/audit/visit`), not from middleware: in an SPA a section change is a route change, not an HTTP request — one page fires five GETs and the browser's Back button fires none. Repeats inside a one-minute window are dropped server-side (`AuditLogger::visit()`), and the module is derived from `modules.route_base`, never a second hand-written list.
- ⚠️ **`admin:audit` is the fourth `Role::ADMIN_RESOURCES` entry** (mirrored in `RolePage`'s `ADMIN_RESOURCES`), so viewing and cleaning are separate rights — `EnsureAdminAccess` derives `view` from GET and `delete` from DELETE. That is also why **`GET /admin/audit/plan` is a GET**: as a POST the middleware would read it as `create` and a view-only role would get a spurious 403.
- ⚠️ **`AuditLog::PROTECTED_ACTIONS` (`chat_delete`) survives the cleanup** (§4.7's rule). The deleted message itself lives on in `messages`, but *who deleted it, when and how* exists only in the log, so wiping it would leave the recovery possible and the accounting gone. `plan()` reports the protected count separately so "I selected 100, 97 were deleted" is never a mystery.
- ⚠️ **The module and action cuts are two rows of coloured cards, not a chip wall** (2026-09-14, stage 10 — `components/audit/AuditScope.tsx`; the action row moved from tabs to cards on 2026-09-15 at the user's request, because eleven identically-styled tab labels made „delete" and „create" look the same and only reading told them apart). ⚠️ **The action's colour and icon are a map, `ACTION_STYLE`** — delete red, create green, edit blue, translate orange — and its values are **theme tokens, not hex** (`var(--destructive)`…), which works because `modAccent()` only ever puts them inside `color-mix()`; that also means dark mode needs no second list. ⚠️ **Both rows are the same `ScopeCard`**, so icon, colour, count and behaviour exist once. ⚠️ **No animation is written here** — the cards are `<button>`s, so the global icon rules in `index.css` already shake the bin and make the pen write. Five things about it: **„all" is its own element**, not „nothing selected", and carries the total; **the choice is single** (tab semantics — „all *or* a specific one"; clicking the active one returns to „all"), while `AuditFilters.modules`/`actions` stay lists so the API and a multi-key filter are unchanged; every tab/card **states its own count** through **`GET /admin/audit/summary`**, where **each facet excludes its own cut** (`filtered($request, ['module'])`) — otherwise choosing one module would drop every other card to zero and „what is stored elsewhere" would stop having an answer — and the counts come from the **same `filtered()`** as the list, so „the card says 40, inside are 37" cannot happen; it is a **`GET`** for `plan`'s reason; and **`module=none` is a real cut** (`whereNull`) — login/logout/register carry no module, so `whereIn` could never reach them and the cards could never sum to the total (on the live database that is 268 of 2280 rows). ⚠️ A zero card stays visible and clickable, only dimmed — „nothing happened here" is an answer. ⚠️ **`modAccent()` and the gold fallback live in `lib/modules.tsx`**, shared with `Sidebar`; the colour travels as inline `--mod`/`--mod-soft`, never as a class (Tailwind cannot generate one from a hex).
- ⚠️ Cleanup (§4.7) shares `filtered()` with the list, and `ids` **narrows** the filter rather than replacing it — a selection can never reach outside what was shown. `confirm: "DELETE"` is required, `purge`-style.

**Chat brought up to Messenger level (Tasks §10, 2026-09-16)** — the user's words: „ჩატის ფუნქციონალი მინდა სრულყოფილი, ანალოგი მეტა ინსტაგრამი და ფეისბუქი". Of the twelve things asked for, three already existed (media, uploads, opening the profile), two were partial and seven did not exist. Fifteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The auto-scroll bug was in what it watched, not in how it scrolled.** The effect hung on `thread.data.length`, so (a) switching to another conversation whose page happened to be the same length **did not fire at all** and you landed mid-thread, and (b) the 5-second poll grows the length, so reading history threw you to the bottom. It now stands on an **„am I at the bottom?" ref** plus an instant scroll on mount/conversation change, and a „new messages ↓" pill when you are not.
- ⚠️ **History is a cursor, not a page number** (§10.8). `fetchThread()` always fetched page 1, `meta.last_page` was never read and there was no „load older" button at all — **the SPA could not read past the last 50 messages**. A page number is also the wrong instrument here: a new message pushes the list, so „page 2" means different rows every time and produced duplicates. `before_id` cannot suffer that.
- ⚠️ **`around_id` must not mark the thread read.** Jumping into history from a search hit or a pin is not „I have read everything" — pinned by `ChatParityTest::test_a_jump_leaves_the_unread_count_alone`.
- ⚠️ **A jump is its own view, not a merge.** Between the `around_id` window and the newest page there can be a hole, so merging would silently draw a conversation with a gap in it. The thread stays in that window (with the message highlighted) until „back to the latest" is pressed.
- ⚠️ **Muting silences the badge and leaves the row honest** (§10.5): `unreadCounts()` is untouched and only `unreadTotal()` drops muted conversations. Mute means „don't disturb me", not „hide it". ⚠️ Two columns (`muted_at` + `muted_until`), **not JSON**, because `unreadCounts()` is a raw `DB::table()` query — a JSON value could not be used there without driver-dependent SQL (the `jsonLike()` trap). ⚠️ An **expired** mute reads as unmuted from one `where`, so no cron is needed to clean it up.
- ⚠️ **The theme belongs to the conversation, not to a participant** (Messenger's semantics) and its value is a **key, not a hex**: the palette lives in the SPA, so changing it needs no migration. That is the *opposite* of `modules.color`, where the colour really is data. ⚠️ **The dark variant is mandatory** — one light palette is an ink blot on the dark theme.
- ⚠️ **Light/dark is chosen by CSS, not by JS.** `chatThemeStyle()` emits *both* sets as variables and three lines in `index.css` (`.fb-chat` / `.dark .fb-chat`) pick. `useTheme()` is unusable here: it hangs its own `useState`, so a second call is a second state that never updates on toggle. And Tailwind cannot build a class from a variable, so this is hand-written CSS by necessity — the same reason the `.lucide-*` rules exist.
- ⚠️ **Pinning is allowed to both participants.** Unlike „delete for everyone" it is not destructive and it sits at the conversation level; `pinned_at`/`pinned_by` copy `removed_at`/`removed_by`'s exact shape. The list goes through `scopeVisibleTo()`, so a deleted or self-hidden message **drops out of the pins by itself**, with no second write.
- ⚠️ **Reactions are one per person** (`unique(message_id, user_id)`) — Messenger/Instagram semantics, i.e. exactly what was asked for. Widening later is safe, narrowing is not. Re-tapping the same emoji removes it; a different one replaces it. ⚠️ `emoji` is `varchar(32)`, because one ZWJ sequence runs to 25 bytes. ⚠️ **Not in `AuditRegistry`** — a reaction is one click and would bury the log.
- ⚠️ **`with('reactions')` in the thread query is not an optimisation**: without it a 50-message page is 50 extra queries. `payload()` therefore checks `relationLoaded()`, since the same shape is reused in the send response where the relation is not read.
- ⚠️ **Nicknames are their own table, not a pivot column** (§10.11). A pivot column works only because there are exactly two participants — and the schema deliberately avoids that assumption (it is written out in `message_hides`' migration). Three parties are involved: **whose view** it is, **whom** it names, and where. ⚠️ **The real name still travels** in the response, so the profile link and identity are never lost.
- ⚠️ **No emoji library was added.** All three candidates are expensive or broken: `emoji-picker-react` is ≈100–110 kB gzip (two `dnd-kit`s, which this project refused over 40 kB), `emoji-mart` loads a large async payload, and `frimousse` fetches the emoji **from a CDN** — i.e. offline the picker is empty. `lib/emoji.ts` is ~180 curated emoji on the `@radix-ui/react-popover` the project already ships. The honest price: no skin tones and not the full ~3700 set.
- ⚠️ **„Seen" needed one field, not a schema change** (§10.3): `conversation_user.last_read_at` was already stored for **both** sides and simply never left the server. The mark is drawn **only on the last of my own bubbles** — one per message would turn the thread into a wall of ticks.
- ⚠️ **`GlobalSearch::escape()`/`like()` moved into `App\Support\Like`.** The chat search needs the same two rules, and a second copy is exactly the duplication `DictionaryKey` was created to end. Chat search also matches `attachment_name`: a media message often has no `body`, so text-only search could never find a file.
- ⚠️ **`markThreadRead()` was dead and is gone** (§10.4) — it had no caller, and the `GET` already marks the thread read.

**Chat message deletion (Tasks §4.5/§4.6)** — `DELETE /api/chat/messages/{message}` with `scope=self|both`. ⚠️ **The row stays in the database** (recovery), so this is hiding, not deleting: `messages.removed_at`/`removed_by` + **`message_hides`** + `Message::scopeVisibleTo($userId)`. ⚠️ **The two scopes are two different facts and since 2026-09-14 they live in two different places** (audit §B2): `both` belongs to the *message* (the author removed it for everyone) and stays in `removed_at`/`removed_by`; `self` belongs to the **viewer** and is a row in `message_hides (message_id, user_id, hidden_at)`. Keeping both in one shared triple was a live bug: B hid a message for themselves, then A hid it for themselves, `removed_by` was **overwritten**, and the message **reappeared for B** — one participant's deletion silently undid the other's. Four things follow: **it is a table, not two columns** (`hidden_by_a`/`hidden_by_b` would bake "there are exactly two participants" into the schema — the very assumption that made participants a pivot instead of `user_one_id`/`user_two_id`); **`removed_scope` was dropped** (after the backfill it could only hold `both`, so a later `where('removed_scope','self')` would silently return nothing — `weekday`'s rule; MySQL-only drop, sqlite keeps the dead column); **`unreadCounts()` uses `NOT EXISTS`, not a join** (a join would double-count the day a message has two hides); and **`Message::REMOVAL_SCOPES` is now the API's vocabulary, not a column's values**. ⚠️ **The column is deliberately not called `deleted_at`** — that name is `SoftDeletes`' contract, and if anyone added the trait later every query would silently filter, including "deleted for me only", which the *other* side must still see. ⚠️ **`scope` has no default**: asking the question is the feature (the old behaviour always deleted for both). ⚠️ **`both` requires authorship**; hiding for yourself is anyone's right since it only touches your own view. ⚠️ **The file stays on disk and on the sender's quota** — it is part of the recovery; freeing space is the separate, explicit `DELETE /api/chat/files/{message}`. Removed messages also stop counting as unread (`ChatService::unreadCounts()`) and drop out of the conversation list's "last message", which is per-viewer for the same reason.

**Public profiles (Tasks §16.1)** — `/u/{username}`, backed by `GET /api/public/profiles/{username}` and `GET /api/public/profiles/{username}/{domain}`. ⚠️ **Five routes make up the entire surface outside `auth:sanctum`** (besides `/health` and `register`/`login`), and the inventory is spelled out at `routes/api.php`'s public block: the profile head, the gallery-photos page, a single gallery file, `POST .../albums/{album}/unlock` and `/{domain}`. **Four are read-only and the fifth is not** (Tasks GAP-06 — the comment used to say "both are read-only", wrong on both counts): `unlockAlbum` checks a password and changes the **server session**, guarded by `throttle:album-unlock` (keyed on IP + album for an anonymous caller) and an explicit check that the album belongs to *this* profile and is public. "Public profile" means a shareable link, so requiring a login would make it something else. The whole mechanism switches off with one env var: `PUBLIC_PROFILES=false` (`config/mediary.php`) makes `PublicProfileService::resolve()` return `null`, so every public endpoint 404s without touching anyone's settings. **Three layers must all be open** for a record to appear, each `private` by default: `users.profile_visibility` → `module_user.is_public` → `<record>.visibility`. **All three layers are edited on `/profile` since 2026-09-11 (§6.1)**: `components/PublicProfileCard.tsx` holds layers 1+2 (both toggles **save immediately** — an unsaved visibility switch is a dangerous misunderstanding) and now embeds `components/VisibilityManager.tsx` for layer 3 — domain chips, search, an all/public/private cut, pagination and bulk switching, backed by `GET /api/visibility/{domain}` and `PATCH /api/visibility/{domain}`. ⚠️ **The per-record toggle is gone**: `VisibilityToggle.tsx` now exports only `VisibilityBadge` (the record still *shows* its state, it just isn't changed there) — one fact managed in two places is what made "where is this setting" a guessing game. ⚠️ The list reuses `PublicDomain::card()` rather than each module's `index`: nine domains need one narrow shape, and it is the same one the public profile draws. ⚠️ **`all: true` is an explicit scope** and an empty selection is a **422**, never "everything" (`PurgeService`'s rule); another user's id in `ids[]` is silently skipped, not an error. Five things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`withoutGlobalScope('owner')` + an explicit `user_id` is mandatory** in `PublicProfileService::query()`, and the reason is two-sided: unauthenticated, `BelongsToUser`'s `owner` scope does **nothing** (every user's rows would come back); for a logged-in visitor it would cut to **their own** library (someone else's profile would look empty). Both directions are pinned by `PublicProfileTest`.
- ⚠️ **A bookmark's identity is its own `url`** (`PublicDomain::MATCH`) — there is no external dictionary for links, but a URL is already a global key, so two people who saved the same page do match.
- ⚠️ **`note` can never be public** (§16.5's strict rule — that module holds private documents). It is absent from `PublicDomain::DOMAINS`, so `module_user.is_public = true` has no effect and `PUT /modules/note/public` returns **422 `module_not_shareable`**. The `visibility` column on `note_entries` still exists; it just can't surface anything.
- ⚠️ **The public card is a narrow hand-rolled shape** (`PublicDomain::card()`), *not* `MovieResource`/`BookResource`/… . Those return the full record (`sync_status`, `ge_url`, my progress, file counts) and one new field added later would silently leak to strangers. Here a field appears only when it is written explicitly.
- ⚠️ **`playlist` is a domain but not a module** — it lives inside `song` (§15). That is why `App\Support\PublicDomain` exists as its own map instead of leaning on `modules.key`, and why `VisibilityController` does its own `hasModule()`/`hasPermission()` checks rather than using `module:@type`/`permission:@type` middleware.
- ⚠️ **One endpoint covers all nine domains**: `PATCH /api/visibility/{domain}/{id}` (the `/media/sync/{type}/{id}` pattern), not eight per-module routes. `PATCH` deliberately — `EnsureModulePermission` derives `create` from POST. A non-public profile is **404, not 403**: "this user exists but is hidden" is itself information. Admin force-private (Tasks 1.3) rides on `PATCH /admin/users/{user}` → `profile_visibility` and is deliberately two-way.

**Matching (Tasks §16.2)** — `GET /api/matches/{username}` (per-domain counts + similarity) and `GET /api/matches/{username}/{domain}` (the shared records, with both sides' status/rating), rendered as the "matches" tab on `/u/{username}` (`components/MatchPanel.tsx`). **`GET /api/matches` (no username) is the "similar taste" catalogue** — every public profile ranked by similarity, with `?q=` search over username/name — rendered on **`/people`** (`pages/PeoplePage.tsx`, sidebar link). ⚠️ It is a **catalogue as well as a ranking**: profiles with *zero* shared records stay in the list (§16.2 asked for "search/catalogue of public profiles", which §16.1 never had) — they just sort last. ⚠️ `MatchService::MAX_PROFILES` (50) caps how many profiles one request compares: the comparison is **exact, not approximate**, so it grows linearly; past the cap the response carries `truncated: true` and the page says so rather than pretending the list is complete. **When 50 stops being enough, the fix is a cache keyed on a content stamp** (row count + `max(updated_at)` per user/domain), *not* a TTL — a TTL would keep counting a record for minutes after it was made private. ⚠️ Unlike the public profile these are **inside `auth:sanctum`** — a comparison needs a second side and that is the current user. **Both profiles must be public**; if the caller's is not, the answer is **409 `profile_not_public`** (a distinct state, not an empty result), because the comparison is symmetric — computing it from the caller's private rows would hand the same number back to the other side. Four things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **"the same record" is one map, `PublicDomain::MATCH`**: `tmdb_id` (movie/series), `rawg_id` (game), `openlibrary_id` (book), `bgg_id` (board game), and the **pair** `platform`+`external_id` (video/song — the same id on a different host is a different thing). A null identity column means the record **does not take part** in matching at all.
- ⚠️ **„Done“ is asked in exactly one place, `PublicDomain::isDone()`** — because two mechanisms now run side by side (§6.4): the six dictionary domains answer with `role === 'done'`, while game/book/board_game still compare an enum key (`finished` / `read` / `owned`). Writing that `if` twice is precisely what would drift. `PublicDomain::countsDone()` answers the other half: **song has no status at all**, so its `both_done` is `null` ("not counted"), never `0` ("zero matched") — video used to be in that group and no longer is.
- ⚠️ **`playlist` is deliberately not matchable** even though §16.2 listed it: two users' identically named playlists are not the same object and there is no external dictionary for a song list. Its contents already match through the `song` domain.
- ⚠️ **Both sides go through `PublicProfileService::query()`** — the same three-layer query that feeds the public profile. A separate "matching query" is deliberately not written: two sources would drift and one of them would eventually let a private row through. **There is no cache** (§16.2 asked for one): this is not a cross-user join over full tables but two narrow reads of each side's *public* rows via the new `(user_id, visibility)` index, and a TTL cache would keep counting a record for minutes after it was made private. Revisit when one account has thousands of public records — and then key the cache on a content stamp, not on time alone.

**Field builder** — which fields a module's form shows. **Since 2026-09-11 (§6.5) the catalogue holds *every* field of every form, not just the optional ones.** (Its design doc was deleted 2026-09-07; everything load-bearing is in this paragraph.) Storage is `module_user.settings['fields']` (no migration); API `GET|PUT /api/modules/{key}/fields`; UI is a "Fields" section on `/modules/{key}`. ⚠️ **Only overrides are stored** — the built-in catalogue lives in code (`App\Support\FieldCatalog`), so a field added there appears for every account automatically and a removed one disappears without leaving junk. ⚠️ **Structurally mandatory fields carry a `locked` flag** (a video's/song's/bookmark's `url`, every module's title): they are listed — so the editor no longer lies about being "the module's fields" — but their `enabled`/`required`/`public` switches are disabled, and that is enforced in **`FieldCatalog::for()` itself**, not just in the UI, so a hand-written `PUT` cannot turn off a title either. (Until §6.5 such fields were simply absent from the catalogue; that was stricter but made the editor show a fraction of the form.) ⚠️ **A newly added catalogue entry defaults to `enabled = true`** (`FieldCatalog::defaults()`), or adding a field in code would change nothing until every user found the toggle. ⚠️ **There is no ordering UI any more** and `PUT /modules/{key}/fields` no longer accepts `sort_order` — the order is the catalogue's, i.e. the code's. Custom fields keep their own ordering, because nothing else defines it for them. ⚠️ An unknown key in `PUT` is **ignored, not 422** (the catalogue changes; an older SPA must not break), and writing fields **must not clobber the rest of `settings`** — gallery options and note channels live in the same JSON blob. **Phase 2 (2026-09-06) adds labels/placeholders (ka+en), `required` and ordering.** The form side is `lib/fields.ts` → **`useModuleFields(moduleKey)`** (`shows` / `required` / `label` / `placeholder` / `isMissing`) — the single place a label is resolved. ⚠️ **The default label lives in i18n, not in the backend** (`fields.name.<module>.<key>`): an un-overridden label comes back as `null` so the SPA falls back to the translated string, and **an empty input clears the override** rather than writing an empty label. ⚠️ **`required` is form discipline, not a schema constraint** — the field is in the catalogue precisely because the column is nullable, so the backend does *not* enforce it. Its label was renamed in §6.5 ("don't save it empty") because "required field" read as a database rule. ⚠️ **An unknown field key is treated as visible**, so a form that hasn't been wired up yet never silently loses inputs. ⚠️ **Only add a catalogue entry once the form actually honours it** — a toggle that changes nothing is worse than no toggle. **Every form is wired, and since §6.5 for every one of its fields** — roughly 100 catalogue entries across the nine modules (title/url/status/genres/cover/description/… , not just the handful of optional extras it started with). Each one honours `fields.shows()` / `fields.required()` / `fields.label()` / `fields.placeholder()`, so the label override typed in the editor actually reaches the form. ⚠️ `movie` and `series` share one form but get **separate** catalogue entries — the config is per-module, so hiding a field on series must not hide it on movies. Widening the list is cheap: one row in `FieldCatalog` + `shows`/`label`/`required` in the form + two i18n labels. **Phase 4a (per-field public flag) is live too**: a field with `public: false` is dropped from `PublicDomain::card()`. ⚠️ The **record owner** decides, never the viewer — `PublicProfileController` reads the owner's config and `MatchService` reads *the other side's*, because the card is theirs. ⚠️ `id`/`domain` are never hidden (the card can't render or link without them). ⚠️ **Every new field attribute must also be added to `ModuleController`'s validation rules** — `validate()` returns only validated keys, so an unlisted attribute drops the whole `fields` array from the payload and the `PUT` 500s (this actually happened with `public`). **Phase 3 (custom fields) is live since 2026-09-06** (`docs/DECISIONS.md` §2, answer "a separate table"): a definition lives in `module_user.settings['custom_fields']` (it belongs to the module) and its **value** in `<module>_field_values` (it belongs to the record) — one table per module, `CustomFields::TABLES`. ⚠️ Adding a module means adding its `<module>_field_values` table **in that module's own migration, behind a `Schema::hasTable()` guard** (`bookmark` does this): the two 2026-09-06 migrations iterate `CustomFields::TABLES`, so on a fresh database they already create it, while an existing database needs the new migration to. ⚠️ **Nine tables, one controller** (`CustomFieldController`, `{module}` param — the `/visibility/{domain}/{id}` pattern): `GET|PUT /api/modules/{key}/custom-fields` (whole list; removing an entry deletes it) and `GET|PUT /api/custom-fields/{module}/{id}` (values). ⚠️ **The value is split into typed columns** (`value_text`/`value_number`/`value_date`/`value_bool`) — in one text column a number would sort alphabetically (`"10" < "9"`), losing exactly what the table was chosen for. ⚠️ **A key never changes**: renaming edits the label, since the key is what ties the stored values to the field. ⚠️ **Deleting a definition deletes its values**, or the same name later would resurrect old answers. ⚠️ **An empty value deletes the row** — "not filled in" and "empty text" are the same thing. ⚠️ `components/CustomFieldsCard.tsx` **saves itself** (values never travel in the record's `PUT`, so none of the seven forms changed their save path) and its button is `type="button"`, or it would submit the parent form; on an unsaved record it shows a hint instead of inputs, since a value needs a `record_id`. ⚠️ **A custom field never reaches the public card** and gets no `public` toggle — `PublicDomain::card()` is a narrow hand-built shape. ⚠️ The built-in field list on `/modules/{key}` **filters customs out** (`PUT /modules/{key}/fields` ignores unknown keys, so a switch there would do nothing); they have their own editor, `CustomFieldsEditor`. **Phase 4b (the `file` type) is live since 2026-09-06, so §6 is complete.** The value stays in the same row — four new columns (`value_path`/`value_name`/`value_mime`/**`value_size`**) — and the endpoints are `POST /api/custom-fields/{module}/{id}/file` + `GET|DELETE .../file/{key}[/{file}]`. **A `file` field takes up to `CustomFields::FILE_MAX_COUNT` (10) files since 2026-09-11 (Tasks §7.3)**, and five things about that are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The row is still one file; multiplicity is a schema change**, not a form one. `<module>_field_values` gained `sort_order` and the unique index went `(record_id, field_key)` → `(record_id, field_key, sort_order)`. **Dropping the index instead would have let every other type hold two answers too** — a text field with two rows, silently picked up by `values()`. A non-`file` row always sits at `sort_order = 0`, so for it the index is exactly as strict as before (and `setValues()` therefore pins `sort_order` in its `updateOrInsert` match).
- ⚠️ **On MySQL the new index must be created before the old one is dropped.** `record_id` has a foreign key and MySQL was using the old unique's leftmost prefix for it, so the drop fails with *"Cannot drop index … needed in a foreign key constraint"* — this actually happened on the first run. The migration also checks `hasColumn`/`hasIndex` **per step**, because that failed run left the column added and the index unchanged, and a single combined guard would have skipped the table on retry.
- ⚠️ **A file field's value is always a list, even with one file** — a shape that changes with the count would force an `Array.isArray()` check at every use, and one of them would eventually be forgotten. Each entry carries an **`id`**: names are not unique, so "delete this one" cannot be said otherwise.
- ⚠️ **`{file}` is optional on both the GET and the DELETE**, so the old single-file URLs still behave as they did (serve the first / clear the whole field).
- ⚠️ **Retyping away from `file` now deletes every row of that field**, not just the first. With one file that was a single empty row; with several it would leave `sort_order > 0` junk that nothing reads and that a later write would have to step around. ⚠️ **The upload is a separate endpoint on purpose**: the card sends the whole draft, so a `file` key travelling in the values `PUT` would be read as an empty value and an ordinary "save" would silently delete the file — `setValues()` therefore skips type `file` outright. ⚠️ **No new disk root**: the file lands in the module's own root (`movies/fields`, `videos/fields` — `StorageFolder::customFields()`), which is what keeps §17.2's per-module limit, the recursive orphan scan and `notes/fields` landing on the **private** disk all working unchanged. ⚠️ It is served **only** through the API route on **both** disks (the frontend reuses `PrivateImage`/`PrivateFileLink`) — two different paths would eventually put a private file behind a public URL. ⚠️ **`HasCustomFields` (on all nine models) is mandatory**: the SQL cascade that was enough in phase 3 fires no model event, so the file on disk and the quota counter would leak on record deletion. ⚠️ **Changing a field's type away from `file` deletes the file too** — for other types a stale value is merely unread, but a file would sit on disk, inside the quota and unreachable. ⚠️ **`value_size` is what gets released**, never the size read off disk (the `StoredFile` rule). ⚠️ The path must be in **`StorageMeter::referencedPaths()`** (it is, sourced from `CustomFields::TABLES`) or the admin's orphan cleanup deletes live files. Caps live in `CustomFields::FILE_MAX_KB` (25 MB) / `FILE_MIMES`. ⚠️ **`FILE_MIMES` must never accept active content — `svg` was removed on 2026-09-17 (Tasks SEC-05)**: outside `notes/` the file sits on the **public** disk, so `/storage/<module>/fields/<hash>.svg` opened without auth, as `image/svg+xml`, on the app's origin — a script in it ran with any viewer's session. `mimes:` decides by **content** (`guessExtension()` → `finfo`), so an SVG renamed to `.png` is refused too. `CustomFieldTest::test_no_upload_rule_accepts_active_content` checks this list **and every `UploadLimits::KINDS` list** against svg/html/xml/js/php-style formats — adding one to any upload rule turns it red.

**Chat (Tasks §16.3/§16.4)** — `/chat` (+ `/chat/:id`), `GET|POST /api/chat/{conversation}` and friends; rules live in `Services/Chat/ChatService`. Three tables: `conversations`, **`conversation_user`** (participants **and each side's `last_read_at`**) and `messages`, plus `user_blocks`. ⚠️ **Participants are a pivot, not `user_one_id`/`user_two_id` columns** — two columns turn every query into `where(a) orWhere(b)` and leave nowhere to put "read", which is per side. ⚠️ **But the pivot's unique `(conversation_id, user_id)` cannot say „these two meet once", and that was Tasks BUG-07**: `between()` looked for an existing conversation with a plain `SELECT` and created one when it found nothing, so two tabs (or polling plus a manual open) both got „no" in the same instant and **two** conversations appeared — the thread split for good, each client's messages landing in whichever row it had cached. A pair constraint cannot live on the pivot, so the key lives on the conversation: **`conversations.pair_key` = `min:max`, unique**, with `between()` reading it first and catching `UniqueConstraintViolationException` to re-read. ⚠️ **`min:max`, not „who started it"** — otherwise A→B and B→A are two rows and the index guards nothing. ⚠️ **Nullable on purpose**: `NULL` never collides with a unique index on either engine, so a conversation with more than two participants stays possible, and the migration can leave an already-duplicated pair's second row without a key instead of refusing to build the index — that row is **not deleted**, because the list is built from participation and its messages would otherwise vanish. ⚠️ A `Cache::lock` was the other option and was refused: a lock is an agreement between processes, the index is the fact itself. ⚠️ **`BelongsToUser` is deliberately not used**: its `owner` scope assumes one owner, while a conversation has two — access is decided by **participation**, checked explicitly, and someone else's conversation is a **404** (that it exists is itself information). ⚠️ **Writing requires both profiles to be public** (409 `profile_not_public`) — the same gate as matching. ⚠️ **A block stops messages in *both* directions** (403 `chat_blocked`): blocking is one-sided, but restricting only the blocker would let the blocked person keep writing to the very person who blocked them. ⚠️ **Your own message never counts as unread**, or the badge would light up the moment you sent it. Delivery is **polling** (thread 5s, list 15s, badge 30s) — no worker, as §16.3 specified. **Media is live since 2026-09-06** (`docs/DECISIONS.md` §1 answered "disappears for both"): `POST /api/chat/{conversation}` takes a `file` on the **same** endpoint (its presence picks text vs. upload, so the block/public gate is written once), `GET /api/chat/files/{message}` serves it and `DELETE /api/chat/files/{message}` removes it. Five things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`chat/` is a private root** (`StorageFolder::PRIVATE_ROOTS`) — a private conversation's image must not open through `/storage/*`, so the file leaves the server only through the participation-checked `file()` route (404, not 403, for a stranger).
- ⚠️ **The sender's quota pays** (§16.4) — the upload goes through `StorageMeter::storeUpload()`, so exhaustion returns **413** while text and emoji still send (they never touch the disk).
- ⚠️ **"file deleted" is not a column.** A media message can never be created without a file, so `type ∈ MEDIA_TYPES` + empty `attachment_path` *is* the deleted state (`Message::attachmentDeleted()`). A flag would say the same thing with a migration and two sources that could disagree.
- ⚠️ **Only the author may delete** — the file is on their quota, so a recipient's button would silently free someone else's space. `StorageMeter::deleteOwnFile()`'s `message` branch does the *same* thing (clear the columns, keep the row) so the storage page and the chat can't disagree.
- ⚠️ **Deletion releases the recorded `attachment_size`, not the size read off disk** — the same rule `StoredFile` follows. In tests `UploadedFile::fake()` reports a different size than it writes, and subtracting the disk value silently desynced the counter.
- ⚠️ In the storage breakdown chat is the pseudo-module **`chat`**, alongside `account`: it has no `modules` row, so it can't take a per-module allocation and draws from the shared pool. Both `SettingsPage` and `StorageLibrary` special-case the two keys.
- ⚠️ **An uploaded file leaves the server only through `App\Support\SafeMime::response()`** (Tasks SEC-04, 2026-09-17). `file()` used to answer with the **client's** multipart `Content-Type`, `inline`: Alice sent an `.html` as `text/html`, Bob clicked the message — a plain same-origin link, `/api/chat/files/{id}` — and Alice's script ran with **Bob's** session. Participation checks cannot help: Bob is a legitimate participant. Five things hold it together: the MIME is read **from the file's contents** (`finfo`, the local adapter's `mimeType()`), never from the upload header and never from the stored `attachment_mime` column (old rows still hold the client's claim); `inline` is an **allow-list** of types that render without running script — raster images, video, audio, PDF, `text/plain`/`text/csv`/`application/json` — and ⚠️ **SVG, HTML and XML must never join it**; everything else is `application/octet-stream` + `attachment`; ⚠️ the list must cover every media format the app *accepts* (`UploadLimits`' video list includes `ogg`/`mov`/`m4v`), or the fix quietly turns ordinary videos into downloads; and **`X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`** is set by `SafeMime` *and* globally by `SetSecurityHeaders` (`$middleware->append`, so throttle/auth/404 answers carry it too — `/storage/*` statics are served outside Laravel and do not). New uploads store `SafeMime::ofUpload()` (server-side `getMimeType()`), never `getClientMimeType()`.

**Per-user credentials — „მონაცემები" / `/credentials` (Tasks §21, built 2026-09-15)** — the seven external keys (TMDB · Gemini · RAWG · IGDB · SerpApi · Serper · YouTube) lived only in `backend/.env`, i.e. **one key for the whole installation**. In a multi-user app that meant nobody could bring their own key, and — worse — the two *metered* sources counted their spend across everybody: `TranslationUsage`/`SerpSearch` both said in their own docblocks „the limit belongs to the account, not to the user", so one person could burn another's Gemini day or the month's 250 searches. Now `user_credentials` (`user_id`, `provider`, **encrypted** `credentials` JSON, `limits` JSON, `is_active`, `verified_at`, `last_error`) holds each account's own keys, and **`Services\Credentials\CredentialStore` is the single read point** — all seven clients stopped calling `config('services.*')` (`grep -rn "config('services\." app/` now returns nothing but comments). Fifteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **This is deliberately not a `modules` row.** In this project „module" means a content library: `RegistryConsistencyTest` demands a purge target, a `DashboardController::COUNTERS` entry and an `AuditRegistry` model for every `modules` row, and a key has no „records". More importantly a module can be **disabled for everyone** by an admin — one switch would have turned off every account's translation. It is a plain section, `/dictionaries`' shape: own table, own page, no registry entanglement.
- ⚠️ **`.env` stays as the installation default and is not deleted.** Three states exist, not two — `user` (mine) · `shared` (`.env`) · `none` — and the UI names the one in force. Removing the shared layer would have switched off every source the moment the feature shipped, on an installation that is single-user today.
- ⚠️ **„My key" is decided by the `required` fields, never by „any field is filled".** Gemini has `key` (required) and `model` (not); writing only the model must **not** flip the account to „own key", because the call would still go out on the shared key while the quota was counted against *me* — both counters would then be wrong. IGDB needs **both** `client_id` and `client_secret` for the same reason (its Twitch OAuth does not work with one).
- ⚠️ **An optional field still falls back individually**: my key + empty model = my key and the installation's default model. Otherwise saving a key would silently leave the model blank and translation would break.
- ⚠️ **`CredentialStore::quotaOwner()` is the whole point of the task.** It returns my id when I use my own key and `null` when the key is shared; `TranslationUsage::usedToday($provider, $userId)` and `SerpApiClient::usage()` both take it. Without that one predicate „your own limits" is a lie — the key would be mine and the counter everybody's. Pinned by `CredentialTest::test_own_key_means_own_quota` and its shared-key twin.
- ⚠️ **The per-request memo is not an optimisation.** `TmdbClient` is built from the container on every call and `Translator` checks the quota on every translation — without `CredentialStore::$memo` a 300-record sync would fire hundreds of identical `select`s. ⚠️ Because it is **static**, `Tests\TestCase::setUp()` clears it: with `RefreshDatabase` the next test's user id 1 would otherwise inherit the previous test's (deleted) row.
- ⚠️ **`TmdbClient` reads its key lazily now, not in the constructor.** The client can be resolved before the request's user is known, so a key frozen at construction time could belong to someone else. The constructor argument survives only as an explicit override (tests, CLI).
- ⚠️ **Caches keyed on the key, not on the user** (`CredentialStore::fingerprint()`): IGDB's Twitch token (`igdb.access_token.<hash>`) and SerpApi's `GET /account` (`serpapi:account:<hash>`) describe one specific key, so a shared cache key handed my token to somebody else and produced a 401 nobody could explain. ⚠️ The **search-result** cache stays shared on purpose — it does not depend on the key and sharing it saves quota.
- ⚠️ **The secret never travels in the *list*.** `GET /api/credentials` returns `masked` (`••••a1b2`) and, for non-secret fields, the value; `CredentialProviders::isSecret()` is the single place that decides which is which, so a new provider cannot leak by omission. `CredentialTest::test_the_api_never_returns_a_secret` greps the raw response body for both the own and the shared value. ⚠️ **Since §21.8 the full value *can* be read — but only through its own endpoint** (see below), never as a field of a payload the page fetches on every open.
- ⚠️ **A missing field is „unchanged", an empty one is „delete".** The form always starts with an empty secret input (the server never sent it), so if empty meant „unchanged" a plain Save would be a no-op — and if it meant nothing at all, clearing a key would be impossible. The SPA therefore sends only fields that actually changed.
- ⚠️ **Saving a key clears `verified_at`.** A stamp earned by the previous key is a lie about the new one.
- ⚠️ **The audit log records the fact, never the value** — `subject_type = 'credential'`, `new_values.fields` is the list of field *names*. `/audit` renders `new_values` on screen, so a key written there would be permanently visible to every admin. No new `AuditLog::ACTION_*` constant was added (the action is `update`/`delete`), which keeps `ACTIONS`, the i18n namespace and `AuditPage`'s `ACTION_STYLE` untouched.
- ⚠️ **„I saved it" and „it works" are different facts**, so `POST /credentials/{provider}/test` exists and each provider gets its **cheapest** call: Gemini's `GET /v1beta/models` (not a translation — that would spend the daily quota to prove the quota exists), SerpApi's `GET /account` (documented as free), TMDB's `/configuration`, IGDB's Twitch token. ⚠️ **Serper has no free endpoint**, so its test is flagged `costs_credit`, requires an explicit `confirm` (422 without it) and the button says „1 credit". ⚠️ `accepted` rejects an *absent* field, so the rule is applied only when it costs — combining it with `requiredIf` in one rule blocked the six free providers too.
- ⚠️ **`limits` is a separate, unencrypted column.** A limit is shown as a number in the UI; putting it inside the encrypted blob would mean decrypting a secret to answer „what is my limit". ⚠️ `null` = the installation default, `0` = no limit — two different facts that `Translator` and `SerpApiClient` already read that way.
- ⚠️ **Telegram's bot token was left in `module_user.settings` on the first pass and moved here on 2026-09-15 (§21.9)** — see below. The first reasoning (“it is already per-user”) missed the point that matters: that blob is **plaintext and is returned with every module list**, so a token granting full control of a bot was travelling to the browser in the clear.

**Showing and copying a secret, and „where do I get this key" (Tasks §21.8, 2026-09-15)** — the user's instruction was literal: wherever a key or a token is entered it must look like a password **and** be viewable and copyable, the bot's token included, and every one of them needs a modal explaining how to obtain it. Ten things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`GET /api/credentials/{provider}/reveal` is a separate endpoint, not a field on the list.** The list is fetched every time the page opens, so a full key living there would sit in the browser cache, the network tab and every screenshot. Here it crosses the wire only when the eye is pressed — „view" is an *action*, not page data.
- ⚠️ **Only my **own** value is revealed; the shared `.env` key never is.** That key belongs to the installation, so handing it to any logged-in account would be a way to walk off with somebody else's (paid) key. Whoever needs it can read `backend/.env`. Pinned by `test_reveal_never_returns_the_shared_key` and its „another user" twin.
- ⚠️ **Revealing is written to the audit log** (the field *names* only, never the value) — extracting a key is exactly the kind of action that should leave a trace.
- ⚠️ **Non-secret fields are skipped by `reveal`** — the model name already comes in the list, and returning it twice would make two places responsible for one fact.
- ⚠️ **`type="password"` was removed from the key fields on 2026-09-15, at the user's instruction, and the reason is that it never solved the problem.** The server does not return a stored key, so the input was **empty** and the mask lived in the `placeholder` — grey and faint, i.e. **a saved key looked exactly like an empty field**. `components/ui/secret-input.tsx` now renders a stored secret as a *filled* row instead of an input: the dots are ordinary foreground text (`••••••••••••a1b2`) with eye · copy · pencil beside them, and typing is impossible until you press the pencil — which is what says „something is already here". ⚠️ **The mask length is fixed (`MASK_DOTS = 12`), not the real one**: it has to draw a filled field, four dots were too short for that, and repeating the true length would itself be a hint about the key.
- ⚠️ **While editing, the text is shown in the clear — deliberately.** A key is pasted, not typed, and hiding it exactly when you need to check what you pasted is backwards. Hand-drawing dots over an *editable* input is also what breaks the caret and selection, which is why the „filled row" is a separate state rather than a masked input.
- ⚠️ **Account passwords use a different component (`PasswordInput`) and keep `type="password"`.** That is a different problem: there is no stored value to display, the hiding is needed *while typing*, and the browser's password manager, autofill and native reveal all hang off that input type. Copy is absent there on purpose.
- ⚠️ **„Stored“ is `masked && value === '' && !editing`** — one typed character means you are writing a *new* value, and still showing the old mask at that moment would be a lie. The cancel (×) button only appears when an old value exists, or it could not answer „back to what".
- ⚠️ **A super-admin sees the shared `.env` key as a filled row too** (`shared_hint` is passed as the mask), which is what makes „the key is in `.env`" visible on the page at all; for everyone else the field stays empty with a „using the shared key" placeholder.
- ⚠️ **A revealed value is never written into the input.** It would then count as „changed", so an ordinary Save would rewrite the same key and clear `verified_at`. It is display state, held separately.
- ⚠️ **`navigator.clipboard` does not exist outside a secure context.** localhost is fine, but the same dev server opened over the LAN IP has no such object — calling it there produced a silent „nothing happened". There is a `document.execCommand('copy')` fallback, and the button confirms with a tick, because writing to the clipboard is otherwise invisible.
- ⚠️ **Copying is opt-out (`copyable`).** On an account password it is pointless (you typed it) and on a login form leaving a password in the clipboard is actively harmful — so login, registration and the profile's three password fields get the eye and no copy button. Every other token gets both.
- ⚠️ **The Telegram bot token became a secret field too** (it was plain text), and it needs **no** `onReveal`: that value already arrives with `module_user.settings`, so the eye works locally. ⚠️ Worth knowing: unlike `user_credentials`, it is stored and returned in plaintext — consciously left as it is, since this task was about showing *more*, not less.
- ⚠️ **`components/CredentialHelpDialog.tsx` serves all eight sources including Telegram** (`provider="telegram"`), even though the bot token does not live on `/credentials` — a second, near-identical modal would have drifted within a week. ⚠️ **The steps are one newline-separated string, not `s1`…`s5`**: Serper needs two steps and IGDB four, so a fixed set of keys would either print blanks or cut a step — and the locale files contain no arrays anywhere, so introducing one would also touch the i18n audit script. ⚠️ **The docs link moved to the bottom of the steps**: „open the documentation and figure it out" is precisely where people stop, and the Google Cloud key is spread over four screens.

**Everything that is in `.env` is visible in „მონაცემები" (Tasks §21.9, 2026-09-15)** — the user's question was direct: why do the keys written in `.env` not show up on the page, and the Telegram bot's too. Three separate answers, and each one changed something:
- ⚠️ **The shared `.env` value can now be revealed — but only by a `super_admin`.** The first pass refused it for everyone, reasoning that an installation key belongs to the account rather than to a person. That is still true for an ordinary user (revealing it would be a way to walk off with somebody else's paid key), but a super-admin **can already read `backend/.env`**, so hiding it from them was pure inconvenience. `reveal` therefore falls back to the shared value for super-admins and marks each field with `owner: user|shared`, because „my key" and „the installation's key" look identical once revealed and you would not know which one you had copied. ⚠️ The **list** still never carries a full value — reveal stays its own endpoint.
- ⚠️ **The Telegram bot token is a provider now** (`CredentialProviders::TELEGRAM`, fields `bot_token` + `chat_id`), moved out of `module_user.settings` by `2026_09_15_000003_move_telegram_into_credentials`. ⚠️ **The reason is not tidiness**: that pivot column is plaintext JSON *and is returned with every module list*, so a token that grants full control of a bot was being handed to the browser in the clear and stored in the clear. Here it is encrypted, masked and revealed only on demand. ⚠️ It has **no `config` fallback** — a shared bot token is meaningless (everyone's reminders would come from one bot, and `chat_id` is personal by definition), so `shared()` is `null` and the state is either „mine" or „not set". ⚠️ `chat_id` is deliberately **not** secret: it is a plain number and hiding it would only make debugging harder.
- ⚠️ **`NoteChannelSettings` reads the new home first and the old pivot second.** The migration copies the values, but a restored dump or another machine may still carry only the old ones — and a reminder that goes quiet without saying why is the worst outcome. ⚠️ **The old keys were left in the pivot „on purpose" — and that was the bug (Tasks SEC-12, reversed 2026-09-17).** The leftover was not unread: `ModuleController::index()` returned the whole pivot JSON as `user_settings`, so the plaintext token kept reaching the browser on every module list, every dump and every `/backups` file — exactly what this section set out to stop. `2026_09_17_000001_strip_plaintext_telegram_from_module_settings` now **backfills first, field by field** (a credential holding a token but no `chat_id` used to take `chat_id` from the pivot — deleting that row blindly would have silenced reminders), never overwrites a value already in `user_credentials`, removes only the two keys and leaves gallery/fields/`status_sections` untouched; its `down()` deliberately restores nothing. `ModuleSettings::RETIRED_KEYS` is the single list: `withoutRetired()` strips them from both `GET /modules` and the `PUT /modules/{key}/settings` response, and `merge()` refuses to write them from a request (keys already in the database are left for the migration, which is the only safe place to delete them). ⚠️ **Its first live run failed with `DecryptException`, before writing anything — and that is worth knowing beyond this migration**: the one `user_credentials` row had been encrypted on the original machine with a **different `APP_KEY`**. `UserCredential::fields()` swallows that and reads the row as empty, so `CredentialStore` silently fell back and the reminders had been running on the plaintext pivot copy alone. ⚠️ **That silence is gone since 2026-09-18 (Tasks GAP-11)**: `UserCredential::isReadable()` is the one predicate, `GET /api/credentials` carries **`undecryptable`** per provider (deliberately *beside* `source`, not a fourth value of it — `source` answers „which key is in force" and on such a row the honest answer really is `shared`/`none`), `/credentials` explains the state and what to do about it, and the first unreadable read writes one `Log::warning` per row. ⚠️ **Writing a new key over such a row used to be a 500** — the one recovery a user has — because the dirty check decrypts the original; `forgetUnreadable()` nulls it in memory first. The migration therefore re-encrypts such a row with the pivot's values, and ⚠️ **it must null the row's original `credentials` first**: Eloquent's dirty check (`originalIsEquivalent()`) decrypts the *original* value of an encrypted cast on `save()` and dies on the same MAC error. ⚠️ Moving the database between machines means moving `APP_KEY` with it.
- ⚠️ **Two editors, one storage.** `NoteChannelsDialog` still edits the token where you configure reminders, but it now calls `PUT /api/credentials/telegram` instead of writing the pivot. Two *editors* are fine; two *storages* are what drift. ⚠️ It also had to learn the same „empty means unchanged" rule, or saving just the chat id would have silently wiped the token.
- ⚠️ **The rest of `.env` is shown read-only** (`meta.installation`, `super_admin` only): `YTDLP_BINARY`, `FFMPEG_BINARY`, `MYSQLDUMP_BINARY`, `MYSQL_BINARY`, `ALLOW_REGISTRATION`, `PUBLIC_PROFILES`, `SAFE_HTTP_ALLOW_PRIVATE`. These are **not** credentials and cannot be per-user — a binary path is a fact about *this machine* — but „why is it not shown" was a fair question, so they are listed with an explicit note that they are changed in `backend/.env`. ⚠️ The list is spelled out by hand and deliberately excludes `database`/`mail`/`aws`: a page that printed the DB password would be a much worse answer than the one it replaced.
- ⚠️ **`GOOGLE_CSE_API_KEY`/`GOOGLE_CSE_ENGINE_ID` were deleted from `.env` rather than given a card** — nothing in `app/` or `config/` reads them (verified by grep). They are the leftover of Tasks §7.5's „source a", replaced by Wikimedia/SerpApi/Serper. A card for them would have asked for a key the app never uses.
- ⚠️ **`$user->is_super_admin` does not exist on the model** — it is a `UserResource` field, while the model has `isSuperAdmin()`. Written as an attribute it evaluates to `null`, i.e. the check silently fails closed and the feature just does nothing. Caught only because the test asserted the admin *could* see the value; a test that only checked the refusal would have passed against a permanently broken branch.

**Database backup — `/backups` (Tasks §22, built 2026-09-15)** — taking the library to another computer used to mean a hand-run `mysqldump`. Now `database_backups` (`user_id`, `path`, `name`, `size`, `status`, `error`, `driver`, `tables`, `note`, `source`, `started_at`, `finished_at`) + `Services\Backup\DatabaseDumper` (the binaries) + `BackupRunner` (quota, status, safety copy) + `POST /api/admin/backups`, `GET .../{id}/download`, `POST .../import`, `POST .../{id}/restore`, `DELETE .../{id}`, all `super_admin`. **Verified live 2026-09-15**: a real dump is 82 tables / 810 KB gzipped / 0.5 s, and a restore of that file into a scratch database brought back all 82 tables and 360 movies in 1.5 s. Thirteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`super_admin`, not `admin_access:`.** A dump is every account's every record plus hashed passwords and private chats — that cannot hang off one section's permission. Same reasoning as `admin/purge` and `admin/modules`.
- ⚠️ **`backups/` is a private root** (`StorageFolder::PRIVATE_ROOTS`). Under `/storage/*` the entire database would be one guessable URL away. The only way out is the ownership-checked `GET /admin/backups/{id}/download`, and the SPA fetches it as a **blob** — an `<a href>` cannot reach the private disk.
- ⚠️ **It counts against the creator's quota** (the user's own condition — „it will affect storage"): `StorageMeter::storeLocalFile()` claims the bytes, the `StoredFile` trait releases them on delete, and the row shows up in the storage library under the pseudo-module **`backup`** (`chat`/`account`'s precedent — no `modules` row, so no per-module allocation, it draws from the shared pool).
- ⚠️ **`database_backups.path` must be in `StorageMeter::referencedPaths()`** — without it the admin's orphan cleanup classifies every dump as an orphan and **deletes** it. `RegistryConsistencyTest` enforces this for any column whose name contains `path`.
- ⚠️ **`deleteResolved()` needs a `database_backup` branch too**, or the storage library would list the file and its delete button would silently return `false`.
- ⚠️ **The dump runs detached** (`BackgroundProcess` → `php artisan backups:run {id}`), because `artisan serve` is single-threaded and a restore can take minutes. ⚠️ **A dispatch that fails deletes the row** — a written-but-never-started row would show „running" at 0% forever (§B1's lesson), so the answer is 503 `background_unavailable` and no row.
- ⚠️ **`status` distinguishes four facts, and `started_at` is what makes the fourth possible**: `running` · `running` + stale · `ready` · `failed` + `error`. `updated_at` could not answer „when did *this run* begin" (it moves on any edit — the `watched_at`/`hltb_*` trap), so a dead background process would have read as „still running" forever. A **null** start time counts as stale.
- ⚠️ **The SPA polls only while something is genuinely running** (`status === 'running' && !stale`): a stale run can never change by itself, so polling it would never stop.
- ⚠️ **Restore replaces the database — including `database_backups` itself.** That is not a detail: the row that says „restoring" is inside the data being overwritten, so „did it succeed" would vanish at the moment it mattered. `BackupRunner::restore()` therefore snapshots both rows and **re-inserts them after** the restore, with `DB::table()` rather than Eloquent (so `AuditObserver` does not invent two „created" entries, and the `id` — which the file on disk is tied to — is preserved).
- ⚠️ **A safety dump is taken first, and a failed safety dump aborts the restore.** Running an irreversible operation without the copy that makes it reversible is the one thing this feature must not do.
- ⚠️ **The audit entry is written *before* the restore starts**, since the restore overwrites `audit_logs` too — that way „who started it" survives inside the restored dump as well.
- ⚠️ **Files on disk do not follow the database.** A restored `users.storage_used_bytes` can disagree with what is actually on this machine, so the response carries `recalculate_storage` and the UI says to run `mediary:storage-recalc`.
- ⚠️ **`pint`'s `single_quote` fixer silently broke gzip detection**, and this is worth remembering beyond this feature: `"\x1f\x8b"` was rewritten to a **single-quoted** string, where the escape stops being an escape — the comparison then ran against four literal characters (and `\x8b` became two UTF-8 bytes), so `isGzip()` always returned `false`. Caught only by a live run. Binary literals are now compared through **`bin2hex()`**, which no quoting style can change.
- ⚠️ **Compression is a stream filter, not an external `gzip`** (`zlib.deflate` with `window => 31` for the gzip header): one required binary is enough, a second would break the feature on Windows. Whether a dump is compressed is read from the **file name** (`.gz`), so there is no second source of that fact. ⚠️ `isGzip()` checks the **content**, because an imported file comes from another machine and may be named anything.
- ⚠️ **The password travels in `MYSQL_PWD`, never as `--password=`** — an argument is visible in the process list.
- ⚠️ **Missing binaries are a state, not a crash**: `available()` → **503 `mysqldump_unavailable`** (`ytdlp_unavailable`'s rule), and the page says so up front instead of letting a button fail. On this machine both are found automatically at `C:/xampp/mysql/bin` via `DatabaseDumper::COMMON_PATHS`; `MYSQLDUMP_BINARY`/`MYSQL_BINARY` in `.env` override it.
- ⚠️ **Import and restore are two steps.** Uploading a file is not a decision; replacing the database is, and it stands on a typed `RESTORE` (the `/purge` word). The uploaded row is marked `source = 'upload'`, because nothing has validated its contents yet.
- ⚠️ **`message` *is* the machine code** (`['message' => 'mysqldump_unavailable']`) — `lib/errors.ts` compares `CODES` against `message`, so a separate `code` field would never be translated. All three new codes (`mysqldump_unavailable`, `background_unavailable`, `invalid_backup_file`) are in `CODES` and in both locales.

**⚠️ A child process launched from a web request on Windows loses its environment — `App\Support\ProcessEnv` (2026-09-17)** — opening the backup viewer failed with **`ERROR 2004 (HY000): Can't create TCP/IP socket (10106)`** while *the same code worked from the CLI*. Five things are worth knowing, because this affects **every** `Symfony\Process` in the app, not just backups:
- ⚠️ **The cause is two lines in `Symfony\Process::getDefaultEnv()`**: on Windows it takes `getenv()` and then `array_intersect_ukey($env, $_SERVER, 'strcasecmp') ?: $env` — i.e. the parent environment is **cut down to its intersection with `$_SERVER`**, and the full list only survives when that intersection is **empty**. Before Laravel boots it *is* empty (measured: 0 keys), so the child gets everything; after boot Dotenv writes the 62 `.env` keys into `$_SERVER` too, the intersection jumps to 62, the `?:` fallback stops firing and the child receives **only the `.env` variables**.
- ⚠️ **`SystemRoot` is the one that matters.** Winsock loads its provider catalogue through that path, so without it `WSAStartup` returns `WSAEPROVIDERFAILEDINIT` (**10106**) and `mysql.exe` cannot open a socket at all. The error mentions TCP/IP, which sends you hunting the database, the port and the password — none of which are involved.
- ⚠️ **CLI hides it**: there `$_SERVER` already contains the whole environment, so the intersection is complete. That is exactly why a live check from `tinker` passed and the same call from the browser failed — one code path, two SAPIs. **Verify process-spawning features through `artisan serve`, not only from the CLI.**
- ⚠️ **The fix is explicit hand-off, not patching Symfony**: `Process::start()` merges our `$env` **first** (`$env += array_diff_ukey($this->env, $env, 'strcasecmp')`), so whatever `ProcessEnv::for()` returns always reaches the child. It forwards `SystemRoot`, `PATH`, `TEMP` and the rest of the Windows basics, and passes `$extra` (e.g. `MYSQL_PWD`) through untouched.
- ⚠️ **`DatabaseDumper` *and* `YtDlp` both go through it** — yt-dlp's `available()`/`probe()` run inside a web request too, so they were silently exposed to the same breakage. ⚠️ **`BackgroundProcess` is not affected**: it shells out through `popen()` + `cmd /c start /B`, so the environment is inherited by the shell.

**The backup viewer and partial restore (Tasks §11, 2026-09-17)** — the user's words: „ბაზის ასლში … იყოს ასევე ნახო რა თეიბლებს შეიცავს და კონკრეტული თეიბლის ჩანაწერების ნახვა — რამე ვიუვერი რომ იყოს; ასევე შეგეძლოს კონკრეტული თეიბლის აღდგენა, ან კონკრეტული ჩანაწერის, ან სრული". Verified live the day it was built: **82 tables, a 1.79 s import, 360 movies over 23 columns**, and the scratch database dropped on close. Fourteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **„What is inside this backup" needs no restore at all** (§11.1). `database_backups.tables` could not answer it — it is a **number, not a list**, and `null` on an imported file. `table_map` (JSON) is filled from **one pass** of the dump: `countTables()` already read the whole file, so the name, the byte size and the `INSERT` count now come from that same pass. A second pass on a 400 MB dump would be double the price.
- ⚠️ **`inserts` counts *statements*, not rows** — one `INSERT` holds hundreds of tuples. The list says so out loud, because the number otherwise reads as „three records".
- ⚠️ **No SQL parser was written, and that is the central decision.** `mysqldump` does not use `--complete-insert`, so the column names are **not in the `INSERT`** and a `CREATE TABLE` parser would be needed too; splitting tuples is then a real state machine (`\0 \n \r \\ \' \" \Z`, plus commas and parentheses inside text) — i.e. code that fails **silently**. On top of that every page would re-parse the whole file (`gzseek` is not random access). The viewer imports the dump into a **temporary database** instead, and from then on every question is real SQL: paging, sorting, filtering, `COUNT(*)`.
- ⚠️ **The honest price is stated in the UI, not only in a comment**: the scratch database is a second copy of the data on disk and **the quota cannot see it** (it is not a file). Closing the viewer drops it.
- ⚠️ **The scratch database is named from the live one** (`<db>_inspect_<id>`), so two installations on one server cannot overwrite each other and two backups can be open at once.
- ⚠️ **Table and column names are never interpolated as they arrive.** They are checked against `information_schema` and only a known name reaches the query — an identifier is the one thing bindings cannot protect, and `order by` takes no bindings at all.
- ⚠️ **Row counts come from `COUNT(*)`, not `information_schema.table_rows`** — on InnoDB the latter is an estimate and is often out by a factor of two.
- ⚠️ **`users` and the infrastructure tables can never be restored on their own** (the user's answer). `RestoreScope::children('users')` reports **65 incoming foreign keys** here, so „restore only users" (delete + write) would **wipe every account's entire library** and bring back nothing but the users. `statuses` has six `SET NULL` children, i.e. restoring it would silently strip the status off every record. The blocked list is **written by hand**, not computed: a threshold would answer differently one day, a list is visible in review.
- ⚠️ **`warns` is the opposite — it is computed** from `information_schema`, because the schema changes and a hand-written list would go stale on the first migration. The UI names which tables will be hit.
- ⚠️ **`TRUNCATE` is unusable** — InnoDB refuses it on a table a foreign key points at. So a table restore is a `DELETE`, and that is exactly what fires the cascades the warning is about.
- ⚠️ **Polymorphic columns have no FK at all** (`gallery_images.imageable_*`, `castables`, `genreables`, `audit_logs.subject_*`), so a broken relation makes **no noise** and cannot show up in `warns`.
- ⚠️ **`REPLACE INTO` is never used** for a single row: it is `DELETE`+`INSERT` and **fires cascades** — `REPLACE INTO users` would delete that account's 65 child tables. It is `INSERT … ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE` (Laravel's `upsert()`). ⚠️ That still fires on **any** unique index, so a row with a different `id` could quietly overwrite **another** row; the clash is checked and named beforehand. ⚠️ A missing parent is a refusal that **names the parent**, and `SET FOREIGN_KEY_CHECKS = 0` is never used. ⚠️ The identifier is read from `information_schema`, because it is not always `id` (`castables`, `genreables`, `module_user` are composite; `cache`/`sessions` are strings).
- ⚠️ **The typed word is the table's own name, not `RESTORE`.** A word copied off the button is always the same and trains the fingers; the table name forces you to look at exactly what you are emptying. ⚠️ **The SPA must not fill it in** — `restoreBackupTable()` takes `confirm` as an argument, because passing the table name automatically would turn the guarantee into decoration.
- ⚠️ **A safety dump runs first and a failed one aborts the restore** — `BackupRunner::safetyDump()` became public for exactly this, rather than being written a second time. A single row needs no typed word: it is reversible and no worse than deleting one record in its own section.

**Non-module section colours and animations (Tasks §23, 2026-09-15)** — the twelve sections that are not modules (dictionaries · genres · bulk status · sync · translations · people · chat · modules · requests · users · roles · audit, plus the new credentials and backups) had a colour only in the sidebar, and it was assigned **by family**, so the five admin rows were indistinguishable from each other. Page headers had no colour at all: `PageHeader` reads `modules.color`, and these sections have no `modules` row. Six things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **One registry, `lib/toolSections.ts`** (colour + icon per section), read by `Sidebar` *and* `PageHeader`. The old `TOOL_ACCENT` was private to `Sidebar.tsx`, which is exactly why the header could not use it — and two copies would have drifted into „blue in the menu, green on the page".
- ⚠️ **Each section has its own tone now, not a family tone.** The admin five sit in adjacent hues (indigo → violet) so the group still reads as a group, and the social pair stays neighbouring — but colour can once again tell „users" from „roles", which is the job it exists for.
- ⚠️ **The values are `var(--tool-*)` in `index.css`, with a separate dark-theme set.** These are raw colours, not aliases, so `:root` alone would leave a dark page with a light-theme ink blot. Tailwind cannot build a class from a variable, so the colour travels as inline `style` — the same mechanism modules already use.
- ⚠️ **`module` and `tool` are never passed together.** One is a library section, the other a tool; two accents on one header would answer „where am I" twice.
- ⚠️ **Two entrance animations, not one**: the bar drops in (`fb-header`) and the icon tile pops slightly later (`fb-header-icon`), so the eye reads the section before it notices the colour. ⚠️ The tile's `animation-fill-mode: backwards` is mandatory — it has a delay, so without it the icon would render full-size and then jump back to start growing. Both sit under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`; colour does not, following the existing icon rule.
- ⚠️ **`ChatPage` was hand-rolling its `<h1>`** and therefore could never take a section colour; it uses `PageHeader` now. `ModulePage`/`UserPage` deliberately keep their custom headers — those show a *record's* identity (a module's own icon and name), not the section's. `RolePage` does the same, and since 2026-09-15 its tile is coloured by the **role's** scope rather than the section's (see below).

**The field builder's lock can be opened — and two adjacent bugs (2026-09-16)** — the user's words: „the editor says *name\* is locked, enabled/required are not managed, only the label* — as a super-admin you should not restrict me; just **warn** me what may happen if I turn a required field off, and that it is not advisable". Thirteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The decisive finding came first: today's lock was already a no-op in the form.** **No form called `fields.shows()` on a locked key** — all nine drew it unconditionally, and two files even said in a comment that checking „has no point". So relaxing only the backend would have produced a switch that stores a value, shows it off in the editor and **changes nothing on the form** — the project's own „a toggle that does nothing is worse than no toggle". The nine render sites in eight files were therefore the bulk of the work, and the part most easily skipped.
- ⚠️ **`locked` stays a catalogue fact; the new state is `unlocked`** (`module_user.settings.fields.<key>.unlocked`). Both travel in the response, so the UI can say „locked, **but you opened it**" — returning `locked: false` would have deleted the warning the moment it was needed most.
- ⚠️ **`fields.*.unlocked` had to be added to `ModuleController`'s validation.** `validate()` returns only validated keys, so an unlisted attribute drops the whole `fields` array and the `PUT` 500s — this already happened once, with `public`.
- ⚠️ **Re-locking resets the flags to `true`.** Otherwise you could leave a field hidden *and* „locked", i.e. a broken form with a page that claims everything is fine. Pinned end to end: locked → unlocked → disabled → re-locked.
- ⚠️ **Only a `super_admin` may unlock** (the user's answer). Technically the config is the caller's own (`/modules/{key}/fields` never edits anyone else's), so the blast radius is one person's forms — but the *value* of a lock is stopping an accidental click, and a super-admin is the one user who has said out loud they accept the consequence. ⚠️ On the model it is **`isSuperAdmin()`**, never `$user->is_super_admin` — that attribute lives on `UserResource` and evaluates to `null`, i.e. the check fails closed and silently. ⚠️ The refusal is **silent, not a 422** — the file's existing rule for unknown keys.
- ⚠️ **The warning is a `useConfirm` with `variant: 'destructive'` and no typed word.** The typed `DELETE`/`RESTORE` is reserved for irreversible data loss; this is reversible config, and over-using the word devalues it.
- ⚠️ **The text is different for `movie`/`series`/`anime`** (`fields.unlockWarningTmdb`). Discover → `POST /{domain}/from-tmdb` takes a bare `Request` and `HasStatus`'s hook fills the status, so adding still works there. One text that is wrong for three of eleven modules would cost the warning its credibility.
- ⚠️ **„Reset this module's fields to defaults" is the guarantee** (`DELETE /api/modules/{key}/fields`, the user's answer). ⚠️ It drops **only** `settings['fields']` — that JSON blob also holds gallery defaults, note channels and the status sidebar layout, so dropping the whole thing would delete four other features. Custom fields are untouched: those are data, not config.
- ⚠️ **⚡ A silent dead submit existed *without* any unlock, and it is the reason §4 led with a bug fix.** `status` · `type_id` · `genres` · `category_id` · `genre_id` · `genre_ids` are all switchable today; hide one and the form draws its block with `hidden`, `submit()` still runs `pickErrors` unconditionally and returns — **and the error text is painted on the hidden element**. No toast, no request, no visible error: Save simply did nothing, in eight forms. The fix is `hiddenPicks()` in `lib/requiredPicks.ts` plus a toast that **names the field**; turning the local check off would not do, because the backend would 422 on the same invisible field.
- ⚠️ **Column name ≠ catalogue key**, and that map is one global constant: the server answers with `genre_id`/`category_id`/`genre_ids`, the catalogue calls them `genre`/`category`/`genres`. Eight little per-form maps would have drifted on the first rename.
- ⚠️ **⚡ `AdminUserController::syncModules()` rewrote `enabled_at` on every save** — `sync($ids)` calls `updateExistingPivot` on already-attached rows, so granting **one** module stamped „enabled just now" on all the others, and that column answers exactly one question. It now computes the real difference: detach only what was removed, attach only what is new. ⚠️ **What it did *not* do is destroy `settings`** — this was believed to be data loss and it is not: `sync()` updates the existing pivot row instead of re-inserting it, and `settings` is not in the update payload (verified in `attachNew()` and by a test that passes against the old code). Worth writing down, or the next pass will „discover" the same non-bug.
- ⚠️ **No field needs to stay unopenable**, because `/modules/{key}` is reachable from the sidebar regardless of the broken form, hiding a field never touches a column, and the editor itself deliberately lists fields from the catalogue regardless of `enabled` — **keep it that way**, or the switch that broke the form would disappear with the form.
- ⚠️ **`id`/`domain` in `PublicDomain::card()` stay unstrippable.** The `public` unlock must never reach them: the card cannot render or link without them.
