**Explanations became icons — `ui/info-hint.tsx` (2026-09-16)** — the user's words: „instead of these information blocks, put an icon and a tooltip on the page; red icon if something is critical, a blue `i` if it is informational — one element may carry both, each showing its own independent text", and, when asked: „**required fields get the red triangle**, everything else the ordinary `i`". Fourteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **It could not be built on `Tooltip`, and that is verified in the installed source, not assumed.** `@radix-ui/react-tooltip` drops touch outright (`pointerType === "touch"` → `return`), closes on `onPointerDown` and again on `onClick` — i.e. **a tap opens nothing**. Moving text into a Radix tooltip would have *deleted* it on a phone. `InfoHint` is a `Popover` (already a direct dependency, already used raw twice) with hover *and* click. Pinned by `ui/info-hint.test.ts`, which would fail the moment anyone moves it back.
- ⚠️ **Two texts means two triggers and two windows**, not one tooltip with two paragraphs — „each showing its own **independent** text". Severity leads: red first, then blue.
- ⚠️ **The glyph differs, not only the colour** (triangle ↔ circle). Colour alone says nothing to a colour-blind reader — the project's own rule from the roles work.
- ⚠️ **Blue is free, red needs a class, and that asymmetry is the mechanism.** `index.css`'s `@layer base` gives `.lucide-info` the blue `--icon-info` and `.lucide-triangle-alert` **amber**; a base-layer colour is a *default* that a component class overrides. ⚠️ And the lucide class is the **icon id**: `AlertTriangle` → `lucide-triangle-alert`.
- ⚠️ **`onOpenAutoFocus` must be prevented.** `GlobalSearch` already records that `Popover` steals focus and interrupts typing — a hover-opened explanation must never take the caret out of the field you are in. `modal={false}` for the same reason `ActionMenu` uses it: no focus trap, so it composes inside `ModalShell`.
- ⚠️ **The pointer must be able to travel into the content** (`onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave` on the content too), or a long explanation closes before you reach it.
- ⚠️ **The prop is already-translated text, never a key.** A `tKey` prop would be a key-like literal outside `t()` — exactly what the i18n audit's check 4 exists to catch.
- ⚠️ **`FieldHint` became a thin wrapper and its 179 call sites were not touched.** They gained touch and keyboard for free; the one visible change is that a required field now shows a red **triangle** instead of a red `i` — the user's own answer, and it makes „critical" one shape everywhere.
- ⚠️ **Nine locale strings already fused an informational sentence with a critical clause** and were split into `X` + `XWarn` (`translate.reviewWarn` had already established the suffix). ⚠️ `web.pagesHint`'s `{{max}}` had to stay on the half that is called with `{ max }`, or a literal „{{max}}" prints on screen. ⚠️ After a split the **`⚠️` character is stripped from the string** — the red triangle *is* the sign, and both would say it twice. Six keys keep theirs because they stay prose.
- ⚠️ **`PageHeader` and `GroupPhotos` gained `hint`, on the *title* row.** The `subtitle` slot is `truncate`d, so a button there would clip — and that same `truncate` is the strongest argument for the whole change: `audit.subtitle` (81 chars), `chat.subtitle` (67) and `purge.subtitle` (64) **were already being cut off with an ellipsis**, so moving them into an icon *recovers* information.
- ⚠️ **`subtitle` is not deprecated — it keeps the live facts.** A count, a found-results number, the database name: those change on every load. 11 of the 28 subtitles stayed for exactly that reason.
- ⚠️ **The classification rule, so the list never has to be rebuilt**: an icon may hide text the reader can act **without**. Text stays prose when it is (1) **conditional** — it appeared because of the current state (`gallery.estimateOver`, `castSearchNarrows`, „source unavailable"); (2) a **step's instruction** — `StepSection.hint` and „how to fill this field in" (the user's explicit answer: the ~30 `*.urlHint`/`*.lookupHint`/`*.tagsDedupeHint` stay); (3) a **live fact**; (4) an **empty state's** explanation. Of what does become an icon: **red** when ignoring it loses data, spends money or quota, exposes something, or cannot be undone — **blue** otherwise.
- ⚠️ **An icon needs something to sit beside.** The conversion anchors it to a heading, a label, a modal title or a toolbar row; where a paragraph is a section's *only* text with no anchor (`gallery.byModuleHint`, the two modal intros), it stays prose — a lone `i` floating above a grid labels nothing.
- ⚠️ **On a checkbox the two halves split across the two mechanisms**: the informational half stays as the label's own sub-line (it explains the control you are about to tick — rule 2) and only the **critical** half becomes a red triangle beside the label (`fields.required`, `fields.public`, `purge.keepGallery`).
- ⚠️ **`TabInfo`'s nine dashed boxes were deliberately left** (the user's answer): they already carry an icon and sit at the top of a tab body as a caption, and inside a modal cards/popovers would push content below the fold.

**Card-style cuts everywhere — `ui/cut-tabs.tsx` + `lib/cutStyle.ts` (2026-09-16)** — the user's words: „on the reminders page turn all / active / paused / finished into card-like tabs with matching colours and icons", and „**everywhere** there is something tab-like or a switcher, make it a card-style tab". The component already existed (`ui/scope-card.tsx`, five consumers), so the work was a registry, two variants and nine conversions — and, just as importantly, **writing down what must not be converted**. Thirteen things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **`GalleryScope` was not copied — it moved and became `ui/cut-tabs.tsx` (`CutTabs`).** It already did exactly this job (options → `ScopeCard`s, icon/colour from a map), it just sat in `components/gallery/` with a gallery-only map. Nine other cuts needed the same thing, so it either came up into `ui/` or a second copy was born. ⚠️ The rename also removed a real collision: **`GalleryScope` is also a *type*** in `api/gallery.ts` (the download scope), so one file could not import both.
- ⚠️ **`lib/cutStyle.ts` is the one registry, in `lib/actionStyle.ts`'s exact shape.** Before it, **three** of the nine cuts had no icon or colour at all (reminder states, visibility, the non-module domains) and two had one in the wrong place — `RequestsPage`'s local `STATUSES` and `GALLERY_SCOPE_STYLE`, which lived in `components/`. ⚠️ Values are **theme tokens (`var(--…)`), never hex**: `modAccent()` puts them in `color-mix()`, so a hex would *work* and silently lose dark mode — pinned by `lib/cutStyle.test.ts`.
- ⚠️ **`ReminderCard` reads the same registry now**, and that is the point of the registry rather than a detail: its local `STATE_TONE` had its own three colours, so the same reminder would have shown one colour on its card and another on its tab. ⚠️ Its axis was **split** on the way: the *frame* still says „running / not" (solid ↔ dashed) and the **colour** now separates all three — previously one class answered both, so „paused" and „finished" were identically grey although one comes back with a click and the other needs editing.
- ⚠️ **`statusTone()` is still unusable here** — `lib/statusStyles.ts` returns Tailwind class strings, not CSS variables. If a status cut ever becomes cards, a tone→token map goes in `cutStyle.ts`.
- ⚠️ **`size="sm"` and `layout="inline"` are not cosmetics.** Four of the nine cuts (`VisibilityManager` ×2, `StorageLibrary` ×2) live **inside a card or a toolbar row**, where a page-level tile reads as a second page header; and the fixed `grid-cols-2 sm:3 lg:4 xl:6` stretches a three-option cut across six columns with dead gaps. `inline` sets `[&>*]:min-w-36` on the container, because a flex card with no floor collapses to its text.
- ⚠️ **A module cut takes the *module's* colour and icon, never the registry's** — `color` + `node` override it. The registry answers only what `modules.color` cannot: `cast` · `playlist` (not modules), and `account` · `chat` · `backup` (pseudo-modules in the storage breakdown).
- ⚠️ **`moduleMeta()` gained `color`** (`PublicProfileService`), or the public profile's domain cards would have been uniformly grey while the owner sees eleven colours. It already exposed `icon`, so this leaks nothing new.
- ⚠️ **`count === undefined` ≠ `count: 0`** — the first means „this cut has no number" (`/purge`'s rule), the second „it is empty". A zero card stays visible and clickable, only dimmed.
- ⚠️ **A facet must not count its own cut** (the audit/gallery rule). The four conversions that carry numbers are safe for different reasons and each was checked: reminders count client-side over the whole list, search keeps its separate *overview* query, storage counts over all files, visibility reads `meta`.
- ⚠️ **The singular keys `record`/`records` are a deliberate alias.** Callers name one concept two ways (the gallery cut says „records", the download dialog's flow says „record"); one extra registry line beats making the caller pass an icon and a colour by hand.
- ⚠️ **`ScopeCard`/`ScopeGroup` stay the primitive and `AuditScope`/`StatusBulkPage`/`PurgePage` keep using them directly** — they already render cards and build their options from sources the registry does not own. `CutTabs` is the convenience *over the registry*, not a mandatory wrapper.
- ⚠️ **`ui/chip.tsx` and `ui/tabs.tsx` must not be deleted.** `Chip` still serves multi-select filters, removal marks (`remove` has no card equivalent) and suggestion chips — 7 files; `Tabs` still serves the three in-modal cases — 3 files.
- ⚠️ **What must NOT become a card, and why** (checked 2026-09-16; re-proposing any of these is the regression this list exists to stop). **`LayoutToggle`** — its own docblock says it is *not* a cut: it renders the **same** scope two ways (§28), so two page-level slabs would contradict a written decision. **`SortPick`** (ordering, not a cut) · **list ↔ grid** in `StorageLibrary` · **the franchise toggle** on `LibraryPage` (a boolean with a two-state tooltip). **Every multi-select**: `FilterPanel`'s selected chips, `GroupFilters`' genre/status/year chips, board games' players (an OR filter) — a card cannot carry `remove`. **Form fields**: the reminder editor's mode/interval/weekday/day-of-month/time rows (31 day cards would be absurd), `StatusDialog`'s role, the icon pickers. **Every `RadioGroup` scope picker** (sync · translate · bulk video · bulk status · purge · dictionaries · genres · gallery move · gallery download) — each option **expands its own sub-form inline** (a record picker, a „move to" select, a typed `DELETE`), which a card row cannot hold, and `RadioGroup` is the correct semantics. **`MatchPanel`** (an accordion — the row expands), the chat conversation list and `WebSourcePicker` (multi-select source cards carrying quota). And the two the user was asked about and chose to leave: **the gallery's „has / has no photo" filter** (it lives in a filter toolbar beside the search box, the layout toggle and the sort pick — three page-level cards there would outweigh the filter itself) and **`SongDetail`/`VideoDetail`'s in-modal tabs** (cards would push the content below the fold).

**Sidebar and status icons — the two-tier `ModuleIcon` (2026-09-16)** — the user's words were „go over films, series and every sidebar sub-menu and fix any icon or colour that doesn't fit; many sub-menus have unsuitable icons". **There was one cause and it was a live bug**: `ModuleIcon`'s `ICONS` map held **21 names** and an unknown one fell back to `LayoutGrid` *silently*, so `note`, the „favourite" and „downloaded" pseudo-sections and **three of the four watch statuses** (`undecided` · `watching` · `watched`, in movie · series · anime · video) all drew the same grey grid — 11 distinct names, 16+ sidebar rows. Neither `tsc` nor lint nor any test could see it: an icon name is just a `string`. Ten things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The map is two tiers now, and both are needed.** ~90 hand-picked icons render **synchronously** (a sidebar row must never wait on a chunk); anything else is resolved lazily through lucide's own `dynamicIconImports` (≈2000 icons, one tiny chunk each — measured 225–559 bytes), with `LayoutGrid` only while loading or when the name is not an icon at all. The full library is deliberately **not** imported statically: that is ~1.5 MB.
- ⚠️ **The static list still has to be complete for everything *code* writes** (`ModulesSeeder`, `StatusDomain::defaults()`, the dictionaries' `DEFAULTS`, `PSEUDO_SECTIONS`) — pinned by **`RegistryConsistencyTest::test_every_default_icon_is_drawn_without_a_lazy_fetch`**, which reads the names **out of `ModuleIcon.tsx`** and asserts anchors first so a broken parse fails loudly instead of passing vacuously. Verified by removing `NotebookPen` and watching it fail.
- ⚠️ **`GROUPS` is the single source** — `ICONS` (lookup) and `ICON_GROUPS` (the picker's layout) are both derived from it. Two hand-written lists would drift on the first addition; `ModuleIcon.test.ts` pins that they still cover each other exactly.
- ⚠️ **Two naming conventions are live at once and that is deliberate.** Old rows and code defaults store the **React export name** (`NotebookPen`); the extended picker stores **lucide's own id** (`notebook-pen`). `iconId()` converts the first to the second, and only in that direction — `CLAUDE.md`'s existing warning that the React name ≠ the icon id (`DownloadCloud` → `cloud-download`) is exactly why the reverse can't be trusted. `LinkIcon` → `link` is our one hand-written alias (lucide's `Link` collides with the router's).
- ⚠️ **The picker is one component, `ui/icon-picker.tsx`.** That grid had been written out **eight** times character-for-character (`StatusDialog`, `VideoTypeDialog` and six genre/category dialogs), so „add groups to the picker" was an eight-file change and one forgotten copy meant one dictionary offering a different list.
- ⚠️ **The ≈2000-name list loads on the first keystroke, not when the dialog opens** — otherwise every edit of any dictionary row would pull a 58 kB chunk to draw a grid nobody searched. Results are capped (`MAX_RESULTS`), because each rendered icon is its own fetch.
- ⚠️ **A search hit that is already in the groups is stored under the *group's* name**, never its kebab twin — two spellings of one icon in the database would light „selected" in two places.
- ⚠️ **Default status icons were repeating other sections' icons**, which is worse than being merely ugly: `Bookmark` (`to_watch`/`to_read`/`open`) is the **bookmark module's** icon and `ListChecks` (`done`/`read`) is the **bulk-status section's** — i.e. the same picture meant two different things in one menu. They are `Clock`/`Circle`, `CheckCheck`/`BookOpenCheck` and `Archive` (was `Wrench`, a spanner on „archived") now. Same for `/people` ↔ `/users`, which were **both** `Users` → `ContactRound` / `UserCog`.
- ⚠️ **The migration only rewrites rows that still hold the old default** (`where icon = <old>`) — `ModulesSeeder`'s rule for `color`, so a hand-picked icon survives. `down()` is symmetric rather than „restore everything".
- ⚠️ **The gallery's records cut is `LibraryBig`, not `Film`** — that cut holds songs, books and games too, so a film reel said something untrue; and albums are `Folders`, not `FolderHeart` (a heart means favourite).
- ⚠️ **Measured, since the project is bundle-sensitive**: the initial chunk went **385 kB → 365 kB** (115 → 107 kB gzip — the eight inlined pickers collapsing into one shared component outweighs +70 static icons), and `dist/assets` went 155 → 1896 files but only **2.6 → 3.0 MB** of real bytes; `du` reports 9.2 MB purely as 4 kB-block overhead on tiny files.

**Scope cards, module colour on `/modules`, the roles rework and the sidebar's active state (2026-09-15)** — one batch, five parts, from the user's own words: the requests filter (queue / approved / rejected / all) should look like the audit log's tab-cards, the same in bulk status change and bulk delete; modules should get their colours and animations; the roles UI/UX should be redone completely; and „in the lower menus, when you have entered, it is not visible which one is active".
- ⚠️ **The card moved out of the audit log rather than being copied** — `components/ui/scope-card.tsx` (`ScopeCard` + `ScopeGroup`), read by `/audit`, `/requests`, `/status` and `/purge`. Four pages ask the same question („which cut am I standing in"), so icon size, how the colour arrives, the active mark and the zero-count behaviour exist **once**; four copies would have drifted into exactly the „it looks like this in the audit and different in requests" the request was fixing. The action icon/colour map went the same way (**`lib/actionStyle.ts`**), because the roles matrix draws the very same four actions — delete red with a shaking bin, create green, edit blue.
- ⚠️ **The second line is a count, a hint, or nothing — `count` is optional, never `0`.** `/purge` deliberately has no number: it deletes from **another account's** library (selectable further down the page), so this account's record count would be a lie there; the real numbers come from the plan. A zero card stays visible and clickable, only dimmed — „nothing happened here" is an answer.
- ⚠️ **Requests' counts ride in the list response, not a second request** (`AdminRequestController::index` → `additional(['counts' => …])`; `fetchAdminRequests()` returns `{items, counts}`). A card without a number is the same colourless pill it replaced, and two sources would produce „the card says 4, inside are 3". ⚠️ Unlike the audit's `summary()` the facets deliberately **do not** exclude their own cut — the four statuses are mutually exclusive, so „exclude yourself" would print the total on every card.
- ⚠️ **`/status` reads its per-domain numbers from `GET /dashboard`.** The record list only loads for the *active* domain, so the other cards have no source of their own; the dashboard endpoint is one short request and is usually already cached.
- ⚠️ **`/modules` was the only place a module stayed grey.** Eleven identical cards, while the sidebar, the page header and the audit cards all give each module its own tone — so the colour now comes from the same `modules.color` (`modAccent()`, inline `style`, since Tailwind cannot build a class from a hex), with `MODULE_ACCENT_FALLBACK` where the sidebar's `<nav>` parent does not exist. `ModulePage`'s header tile took the same colour, or one section would have two faces.
- ⚠️ **`.fb-card` is the entrance animation and the delay is inline, not a class** — Tailwind cannot generate `animation-delay` from an index, i.e. eleven cards would need eleven classes. **`backwards` is mandatory** (the `fb-header-icon` rule): with a delay and without it the card paints fully first and only then starts animating. The stagger is capped (`STAGGER_MAX_MS` = 240ms), or the last card visibly lags behind.
- ⚠️ **Roles: the identity is one registry, `lib/roles.ts`** (`roleScope`/`roleTone`/`roleIcon`, plus the `ANY_MODULE`/`ADMIN_PREFIX`/`ADMIN_RESOURCES` mirrors of `Role::*`). The list and the detail page draw the same role, so „3 modules" cannot say one thing in the grid and another inside. ⚠️ **The colour is the scope of power, not decoration**: gold = unrestricted, violet = reaches the **admin sections** (other people's accounts, the log), blue = ordinary module rights, grey = an empty role — and the icon says the same, because colour alone means nothing to a colour-blind reader.
- ⚠️ **`roleScope()` ignores keys whose action list is empty.** The old summary was `Object.keys(permissions).length`, which counted an entry with no actions left as a module — so the list printed „1 module" over a role that granted nothing. ⚠️ It also counts `admin:*` **separately** and never as a module: a role with the user panel and the audit log open used to look exactly like one with three films.
- ⚠️ **The permission table became cards, and the reason is not taste.** `min-w-[520px]` meant horizontal scrolling on a phone, i.e. while ticking a box you often could not see whether the column was „delete" or „edit". Each permission is now a **named, coloured button** (`PermToggle`, icon and tone from `actionStyle()`) and one module is one `PermCard` — the same component serves modules, `*` and the admin sections, since the only difference is colour and hint.
- ⚠️ **The column toggle was hidden in the table head** — clicking `<th>` switched the whole column and only a `title` said so. It is its own control now, and the page states out loud how it differs from `*`: **`*` is a rule** that also covers a module added tomorrow, the row marks **today's** list one by one. Standing side by side in the old table with no explanation, the two were indistinguishable.
- ⚠️ **The bulk control sits in one collapsed strip above the module list** (the user's follow-up the same day: „these aren't needed, only the last one, but collapsed and expandable"). It was a full-width card at the top, so the list — the everyday work — started a screen down, while a bulk switch is needed rarely.

**The wildcard permission is gone (2026-09-15)** — the user's instruction was literal: „ეს არ მჭირდება, ჩახსნიე ეს ფუნქციონალი" about the „all modules" (`*`) block. It is removed from the UI, from `Role`, from the validator and from the data. Seven things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **It could not simply be deleted.** The system role **`user` stood entirely on `{"*": [view,create,update,delete]}`** (see `create_roles_table`), i.e. dropping the key would have taken every ordinary account's access to every module overnight. `2026_09_15_000004_expand_wildcard_role_permissions` therefore **expands** it into one entry per module first and removes it after — rights stay identical. Verified on the live database: the `user` role now carries all eleven modules explicitly and `hasPermission('movie','delete')` is still true.
- ⚠️ **The expansion is a union, not an overwrite.** A role could hold both `{"*":["view"], "movie":["delete"]}`; the hand-written entry must survive the expansion.
- ⚠️ **An empty `modules` table is the normal case, not a failure.** On a fresh database migrations run **before** seeders, so there is nothing to expand — which is exactly why the default role is filled by **`ModulesSeeder::fillDefaultRole()`** instead: that class owns the canonical module list. ⚠️ It fills **only a completely empty** permission set (a fresh install) and never `null` (which means unrestricted, so filling it would *reduce* rights) — the same „don't overwrite what a human chose" rule `COLORS` follows.
- ⚠️ **The price is real and accepted**: a module added tomorrow is no longer covered automatically and must be ticked on every role. That was the one thing `*` existed for.
- ⚠️ **`down()` deliberately does not restore the wildcard** — after expansion you cannot tell which module came from the mask and which was typed by hand, so „restoring" would silently delete hand-granted rights.
- ⚠️ **Removing it uncovered a live bug in `AdminRoleController::cleanPermissions()`**: the allow-list was *module keys only*, so **every `admin:<resource>` grant was silently dropped on save** — you ticked „Users"/„Audit log" in the matrix, got „saved", and the permission never existed. It survived because every existing test wrote `permissions` **straight onto the model**, never through `PUT /api/admin/roles/{id}`. `RoleApiTest` now covers the API path (and was confirmed to fail against the old code).
- ⚠️ **The tests that used `'*'` were rewritten, not deleted**: `OwnershipTest` asks the same security question with every module listed explicitly, which is what the mask expanded to anyway.
- ⚠️ **Admin sections keep their own colours** from `lib/toolSections.ts` (the same tones the sidebar and page headers use for `/users`, `/roles`, `/requests`, `/audit`), and `*` still deliberately does not reach them.
- ⚠️ **The role page's summary is computed from the working copy**, not the saved one, through the same `roleScope()` — „how much am I granting" is visible before saving and cannot disagree with the list afterwards.
- ⚠️ **The sidebar's tool rows had no active state at all** — fifteen rows (dictionaries … purge) rendered identically and the colour appeared only on hover, i.e. it vanished the moment the mouse left. That is what „it is not visible which one is active" meant. `toolLink(path)` now matches **sub-routes too** (`/users/3`, `/modules/song`, `/chat/12`): exact equality would switch the mark off on a record page, i.e. „I went in and the menu went dark".
- ⚠️ **Sub-items were marked with `--mod-soft` (16%) and no line** — nearly invisible. The active row now speaks the module rows' language: a 2px left line in the module's colour, the same colour behind it and the icon in the accent. The fill is **`--mod-fill` (28%), a new third variable** — `soft` is a *background* (a card's icon tile) and `fill` is a *mark*; collapsing them to one value would either recolour the cards or leave the mark invisible again.
- ⚠️ **`border-l-transparent` lives only in the idle variant** (the module rows' existing trap): both classes write `border-left-color` at equal specificity and Tailwind emits the named colour last, so leaving the track colour in the shared class silently makes the active line transparent.
- ⚠️ **Eighteen hand-written sub-row class strings became `subRow(active)`** and the eight „add" rows became `SUB_ADD` — „add" is an *action*, not a cut, so it has no active state by construction.
- ⚠️ **The section of the current route opens itself.** Marking the active sub-item is pointless inside a collapsed section: `/videos` showed a closed „Videos" row and no cut at all. Media domains already had this; the rest did not. ⚠️ It **only opens, never closes** — a second section opened by hand is the user's intent and a route change does not own it (unlike the media effect, where the domain really does change).
- ⚠️ **`git checkout -- <file>` is not a safe undo in this repo** (learned the hard way here): the working tree carries uncommitted work, so restoring one page silently reverted the §23 header accent and the underline removal on it. Rewrite the file instead.

**The server runs on Georgian time (Tasks §8, 2026-09-16)** — the user's words: „ყველგან ისე გადაიკეტოს, რომ დრო იყოს იმ ქვეყნის მიხედვით, სადაც არის ლოკაცია; ამ ეტაპზე თბილისი". `config/app.php` is `env('APP_TIMEZONE', 'Asia/Tbilisi')` now (env-driven, because "wherever the location is" must be one line, not a code change) and `2026_09_16_000004_shift_timestamps_to_tbilisi` adds **+4 hours to every `timestamp`/`datetime` column**. Nine things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **This is a change of data semantics, not of display.** The SPA already showed Tbilisi time (Laravel serializes ISO-8601 and the browser renders locally). What changed is what the string in the database *means* — so without the migration every historical row would have silently moved **4 hours earlier**.
- ⚠️ **`date` columns are deliberately untouched**: a release date or a birthday is a calendar date, not a moment, and a four-hour shift would move some of them a day.
- ⚠️ **+4 is a constant only because Georgia has no DST** (UTC+4 all year). On a DST zone this migration would be wrong and would need a per-row conversion.
- ⚠️ **It is not idempotent** — running it twice adds 8 hours. The `migrations` table guards that; a hand-run does not. And `migrations` itself is skipped, since it is the log of this very run.
- ⚠️ **The real trap was `->utc()`, not the config.** **Eloquent does not convert on write but does on read**: `fromDateTime()` prints a Carbon in *its own* timezone, while `asDateTime()` parses the stored string in the **application's**. Under `UTC` those coincided, so the six scattered `->utc()` calls were harmless; under `Asia/Tbilisi` each one wrote a moment four hours early. `App\Support\AppTime` (`zone()` · `now()` · `at()`) is the single answer, and `NoteReminder`, `ReminderDispatcher` and `TranslationUsage::windowStart()` all go through it.
- ⚠️ **The three user-supplied moments needed mutators** (`NoteReminder::remind_at`/`starts_at`/`ends_at`): the SPA sends ISO-8601 **with an explicit offset**, so the value arrives in a foreign timezone and the same write/read asymmetry cost four hours on every round trip — measured, not theoretical (a window opening at 00:00 came back as 20:00 the previous day).
- ⚠️ **`times_of_day` is a wall clock and must never be converted** — it has its own `timezone` column. That is why the migration touches *columns*, not JSON, and why the mutators list exactly three fields.
- ⚠️ **`TranslationUsage::QUOTA_TIMEZONE` stays `America/Los_Angeles`** — it is Google's quota window and has nothing to do with our location; only the final normalisation comes from `AppTime`.
- ⚠️ **Tests now pin "now" explicitly** (`Carbon::setTestNow(Carbon::parse('…', 'UTC'))`): a bare string is parsed in the default timezone, so every expectation written against UTC drifted by four hours. The tests were always about UTC moments — now they say so.
- ⚠️ **One live-data caveat**: rows written *between* the config change and the migration are already in local time and get shifted a second time. On this machine that was exactly one `audit_logs` row; the repair (`created_at - INTERVAL 4 HOUR` for log rows newer than UTC-now) could not be run from this session.

**Settings** — `frontend/src/lib/settings.tsx` (`SettingsProvider` + `useSettings()`) persists per-user in `users.settings` (`PUT /api/auth/settings`) with localStorage as a local cache and as the one-time migration source from the old single-user state. **Two levels exist**: account-wide `users.settings`, and per-module `module_user.settings` (`PUT /api/modules/{key}/settings`) — the latter also stamps `enabled_at` on first write, since a super-admin may have no pivot row at all.

- `useContentLang()` — the language of *content* (titles, descriptions, genres), independent of the UI language: `auto` | `ka` | `en`. Used by `MovieCard`, `MoviePage`, `LibraryPage`, `GenreSelect`, `MovieMultiSelect`, `GenreItemsManager`, `DiscoverModal`, `ActorPage`, `VideosPage`, `Sidebar`. ⚠️ `ContentLangToggle.tsx` was **deleted on 2026-09-12** — content language comes from settings, not a per-card toggle, and nothing had imported it since 2026-09-03.
- `components/SettingRow.tsx` (`SettingRow` + `NumberSelect`) is the shared row primitive for both `SettingsPage` and `SyncPage`; its `dirty` prop marks an unsaved row.
- **Saving is explicit (Tasks §3).** `set()` only mutates the in-memory working copy; `save()` writes to the backend *and* to localStorage, `revert()` drops unsaved edits, `dirty`/`isDirty(key)` drive the UI. The shared `components/SettingsSaveBar.tsx` renders the save/discard bar on `/settings` and `/sync`. Don't reintroduce auto-save.
- **Five backlog settings landed 2026-09-06 (§18)**: `defaultGrouping` · `cardSize` · `posterQuality` · `autoResync` · `dateFormat`. ⚠️ `defaultGrouping` splits the **grid into sections** (genre/year/status, in `MovieGrid`) and is **not** the franchise toggle — that one is backend's `group=` (a collection collapsed into one card) and both work at once. ⚠️ Genre grouping buckets a record under its **first** genre only, or a three-genre film would appear three times and the section counts would stop matching the list. ⚠️ `posterQuality` is read **on the backend** (`App\Support\UserSettings::posterQuality()` → `MediaDownloader::poster()`), applies at download time, and does not rewrite posters already on disk — `/sync` does that. CLI has no `Auth::id()`, so it falls back to `w500`. ⚠️ **Relative time is the same file but a different setting**: `formatRelative()` / `useDateFormat().relative` take the **UI language**, because „in 3 hours" is a sentence, not a date — with `dateFormat: 'iso'` a Georgian UI would otherwise read English. ⚠️ **Dates go through `lib/dates.ts` (`useDateFormat()`), never a bare `toLocaleDateString()`** — seven call sites each followed the browser locale, so two formats could appear on one page and the setting changed nothing. `iso` uses `sv-SE`, not `toISOString()`, which would shift to UTC and show yesterday during Tbilisi's night hours.

**Frontend media domains** — `frontend/src/lib/media.ts` defines `MediaType` (`movie|series|anime`) + a descriptor (api/route bases), plus the three helpers that keep a new domain from being forgotten: **`mediaKey(base, type)`** (domain-suffixed i18n — `toast.deleted` / `…Series` / `…Anime`; the old `type === 'series' ? a : b` ternaries silently showed movie wording on a third domain), **`MEDIA_NAV_KEY`** and **`emptyMediaIds()`**; `api/media.ts` exposes `createMediaApi` + `mediaApi(type)` and the shared `type`-parameterized calls. Pages (`LibraryPage`/`MoviePage`/`MovieFormPage`) take a `type` prop; `App.tsx` generates those routes **per enabled module** (`/` = movies, `/series` = series) and wraps everything in `Protected`/`ModulesProvider`; `MovieCard`/`MovieGrid`/`DiscoverModal`/`ui/queue`/`ActorPage` are all media-type-aware, and **`components/MediaRecordPicker.tsx`** renders the "specific records" scope for *every* enabled domain (sync and translation dialogs each hand-wrote the movie and series pickers, so a third domain simply had no list). `api/movies.ts` is a thin backward-compat shim over `media.ts`. **Adding a fourth domain = one entry in `MEDIA`, one in `MediaDomain`, a morph alias and the registries below** — the routes, sidebar and `mediaModules` all derive themselves.

**TMDB enrichment** — the core feature (`app/Services`):
- `Tmdb/TmdbClient` — find-by-imdb, search, details, credits.
- `Media/MediaDownloader` — downloads poster (`w500`) + cast photos (`w185`) to `storage/app/public/{movies,series}/posters` and `cast/photos` (served via `/storage/...`, needs `php artisan storage:link`). `poster()` takes the morph alias so the two domains never share a filename.
- `Enrichment/MovieEnricher` — orchestrates. `candidates()` returns a pick-list (avoids wrong auto-match); `draftFromId()`/`lookupDraft()` build a non-persisted draft for form prefill; `enrichMovie()` downloads media, fills **only empty fields** (never overwrites user data), and syncs genres (matched by **slug**) + cast.
- `Support/Trailer` + `TmdbClient::videos()`/`tvVideos()` — the official trailer (Tasks 9). `Trailer::pick()` scores candidates (Trailer > Teaser > Clip, `official` +25, YouTube only) and takes the first payload that yields one, so callers pass `ka` then the default for a ka→en cascade. ⚠️ **`/videos` must be queried with `include_video_language=en,null`, not `language=en-US`** — the latter returns an empty list for many titles because the video is tagged `en` or has no locale at all. The full YouTube URL is stored in `movies.trailer_url`/`series.trailer_url` (never an embed/HTML); `MovieResource` derives `trailer_embed_url` through `VideoUrl` so the player reuses the video module's allowlist.
- `Sync/ItemSyncer` — **bulk** sync of one existing record: the caller picks which `fields` to refresh and the mode (`overwrite` vs only-empty), plus `media` + `only_missing` (skips items whose poster/photos are all on disk without touching TMDB). Drives `POST /media/sync/{type}/{id}`; the loop lives in the frontend queue so `artisan serve` never blocks. CLI equivalent: `php artisan media:redownload [--type=] [--missing]`.
- Requires `TMDB_API_KEY` in `backend/.env`.
- **KA text comes from TMDB, not from a translator**: `TmdbClient` methods take a `$language` param and `App\Support\Lang::georgian()` keeps the response only if it actually contains Mkhedruli (TMDB silently falls back to the original language). `Services/Translation/Translator` is deliberately **not** called from discover/cast/sync — TMDB's own localized response is free and authoritative, and one Gemini call per row would turn a 20-row discover page into 20 requests against a 15-a-minute budget. Translating is `/translations`' job, on an explicit click. ⚠️ **A genre created during a sync gets no `ka` at all (Tasks BUG-23).** All three sites (`MovieEnricher`, `TvEnricher`, `ItemSyncer`) used to write TMDB's English name into **both** locales, and the damage was that the field ended up *filled*: `TranslationScanner::genreMissing()` saw `name_ka` as present, so `/translations` never translated it and a Georgian interface showed English "as Georgian". An empty `name_ka` already falls back to English in `genreName()`, so nothing on screen got worse — only now the scanner can see it, and `TmdbClient::genreList('ka')` fills it for free. Migration `2026_09_18_000006` takes the rows already written; ⚠️ its Mkhedruli test runs **in PHP** (a Georgian `REGEXP` answers differently on MySQL and sqlite) and it collects ids **before** deleting (deleting inside `chunk()` shifts the offset and silently skips every second page).

**Pages & entry points** (`frontend/src/pages`) — `DashboardPage` (at `/`) · `LibraryPage` · `MoviePage` · `MovieFormPage` (all take a `type` prop) · `VideosPage` · `SongsPage` · `BooksPage` (§12 — the record opens in `components/BookDetail.tsx`, a modal with progress, files and quotes; there is no separate book page) · `BoardGamesPage` (§14 — same shape, `components/BoardGameDetail.tsx` holds the gallery, rules and notes) · `GamesPage` (§11 — same shape; `components/GameDetail.tsx` holds the walkthrough videos, screenshots, docs and notes, `components/GameForm.tsx` the RAWG quick-fill) · `NotesPage` + **`NoteRemindersPage`** (`/notes/reminders` — every note's reminders in one list, §13 → 2026-09-14; `components/NoteDetail.tsx` holds the uploads, **`components/NoteReminders.tsx` + `ReminderCard.tsx` the reminders** — one block, opened from all three places through `components/NoteRemindersDialog.tsx`, see the reminders bullet below — and `components/NoteChannelsDialog.tsx` the delivery settings) · `BookmarksPage` (§18 — a flat list with a modal form; sidebar sections are the **statuses**, categories/tags live in the filter panel) · `PlaylistsPage` / `PlaylistPage` (Tasks §15 — playlists live in the song module) · `GalleryPage` (**§8**: six cuts — all photos / records / actors / videos / sources / modules — with a sidebar sub-menu; the bulk-download block is gone and downloading is contextual) · `GalleryRecordPage` (`/gallery/records/:type/:id` — a record's own stills/posters plus the **actors sub-section** with a grouped/mixed toggle) · **`DictionariesPage`** (`/dictionaries/:key` — every module's dictionary on one page, §6.3) · `ActorPage` (**§8.1**: a real inner page — `components/ActorHero.tsx` carries the biography, birthday, place of birth and the official links (IMDb / Instagram / X / Wikidata), then tags + web search, the photo gallery, the saved video links and the filmography) · `GenresPage` · `StatusBulkPage` (at `/status`; domain picked on the page — movies, series *and* videos. Media domains get `MediaBulkPanel` = status change; videos get `components/VideoBulkPanel.tsx` = type + tag add/remove — ⚠️ video **does** have a status since §6.4 and it changes through `VideoStatusController` like the media domains, so the panel covers only what is video-specific) · **`SyncPage`** · `TranslationsPage` · `PurgePage` (at `/purge`, `super_admin` only — Tasks 20) · **`AuditPage`** (at `/audit`, `admin:audit` — Tasks §4: the module/action cuts as cards and tabs with per-cut counts, the remaining filters below them, paginated list, old/new diff modal, manual cleanup) · `SettingsPage` · **`CredentialsPage`** (at `/credentials` — §21: one card per external source, its state (mine / shared / none), limits and a live test) · **`BackupsPage`** (at `/backups`, `super_admin` — §22: dump, download, upload, restore) · `ModulesPage` / `ModulePage` · `ProfilePage` · `ChatPage` (at `/chat` and `/chat/:id` — §16.3) · **`SearchPage`** (at `/search?q=` — the cross-module results page: domain chips with counts, a group per domain and a snippet per match; **not** module-gated, since the search itself spans every enabled module) · `PeoplePage` (at `/people` — §16.2's "similar taste" catalogue; **not** module-gated, since the social layer doesn't care what's in your library) · `UsersPage` / `UserPage` · `RequestsPage` · `RolesPage` / `RolePage` · `LoginPage` / `RegisterPage`. Navigation notes that are easy to get wrong:
- **`/` is the dashboard**, not the movie library (Tasks 2) — movies live at `/movies` and `modules.route_base` was migrated accordingly. `mediaFromPath()` returns `null` for `/`. ⚠️ **The cards are coloured from `modules.color` read through `useModules()`, not from the `/dashboard` payload** (§2.1, 2026-09-11): the same source `PageHeader` uses, so a module is one colour everywhere — a second list of „dashboard colours“ would drift. The „what you have and where you stopped“ subtitle was removed with its i18n key.
- **The admin panel is gone as a single page** (Tasks 1): it is four sidebar sections — `/users` (+ `/users/:id`), `/modules` (+ `/modules/:key`), `/requests`, `/roles` (+ `/roles/:id`). `/admin*` redirects to `/users`. `/modules` is **one** page for everyone: users see their own access, admins additionally get the global toggles and the holder list.
- **`/settings` is reachable only from the profile dropdown** in `Header.tsx` — deliberately not in the sidebar. ⚠️ **Of the whole storage area it keeps only the per-module split** (2026-09-13): the quota, the upload library, the increase request and orphan cleanup are one block on `/profile` (`StorageCard`), because they answer „what do I have", not „what is allowed".
- **`components/ui/action-menu.tsx` sizes itself to its content** (2026-09-14) — it was `w-44` (176 px) with no `whitespace-nowrap`, so a Georgian item („მსახიობის გვერდი", „მოხსნა ჩანაწერიდან") broke **word by word** and a three-item menu drew as nine lines. It is `w-max min-w-44 max-w-[min(20rem,90vw)]` now and `actionItemClass()` carries `whitespace-nowrap`. ⚠️ The fix belongs **here and not in the cast code** — the same menu serves the admin tables, which had the same problem.

**Notes are one shared component — `components/RecordNotes.tsx` + `RecordNoteDialog.tsx` (Tasks §6, 2026-09-16)** — the user's words: „ციტატა და კომენტარი ტექსტები რამე ღილაკის მსგავსად იყოს და შიგნით მოდალი იხსნებოდეს, სადაც იქნება ვინ დაამატა, რა დაამატა, როდის, წაშლა ჰქონდეს და ძებნა". Five modules kept their own copy of the same list (book · video · song · game · board game) and each had drifted differently. Twelve things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **One component was possible because the five tables are the same shape** — `{id, body, created_at, updated_at}` — and **book adds exactly two optional fields** (`is_quote`, `page`). So the difference is one optional branch (`quotes`), not a `switch` repeated in five files.
- ⚠️ **It is body-only.** `VideoDetail`/`SongDetail` draw notes **inside a tab** (with their own `badge` and `TabInfo`), the other three as a `<section>` — a built-in `<h3>` would be a second heading in both. For the same reason the add-placeholder and the „add" label are **props**, not baked in: they are the module's (`videos.notePlaceholder`, `books.quotePlaceholder`…), exactly like `EmptyState`'s buttons.
- ⚠️ **„Going back to where you opened it from" needed no code at all.** `ModalShell`'s stack already does it: a nested modal gets the back arrow **automatically**, the lower modal is hidden with CSS and **stays mounted** — so a half-filled form is exactly as you left it. Writing navigation for this would have re-created the bug the stack exists to prevent.
- ⚠️ **Deleting a note had no confirmation and deleting a file did** — a real data-loss path, not an inconsistency of taste. Both the list row and the dialog now go through `useConfirm({variant:'destructive'})`.
- ⚠️ **The delete button sits *outside* the note button.** The row itself is a `<button>` that opens the dialog; a nested `<button>` is invalid HTML and one click would fire both.
- ⚠️ **Editing existed on the server and nowhere in the UI**: `PATCH` was routed for all five and `api/` had the helper for three. `updateGameNote`/`updateBoardGameNote` were the two four-line additions that made the shared editor work everywhere.
- ⚠️ **The open note is held by `id`, never as an object.** After an edit the list is re-fetched, so a captured copy would keep showing the old text.
- ⚠️ **Search is client-side and that is correct here** — `GET /<module>/{id}/notes` is not paginated, so the list is already fully loaded. Highlighting is `highlightParts()` (`RegExp`-free, so typing `(` is not read as a pattern, and a match is found **inside** a Georgian word). It only appears past `SEARCH_FROM` notes; below that it is noise.
- ⚠️ **„I have no notes" and „the search hid them" are two different facts** and render as two different `EmptyState`s — the second one carries a „clear the search" button, since that is the action that fixes it.
- ⚠️ **„Who added" comes from `useAuth()`, not from the payload.** The `*_notes` tables do have `user_id`, but `BelongsToUser`'s `owner` scope plus `EnsureRecordOwnership` mean it is **always the record's owner** — adding a field to the resource would state the same fact twice. When collaboration ever exists, that one line in `RecordNoteDialog` is where it changes. „Edited" is shown **only when `updated_at !== created_at`**: both stamps are identical at creation, so an unconditional line would label every note as edited.
- ⚠️ **`GameDetail`/`BoardGameDetail` were borrowing `books.notesEmpty`.** A shared component makes that visible rather than acceptable — the text is the caller's, so each module got its own key.
- ⚠️ **The dialog lives in the component's single `return`.** If an early `return` is ever added here, it must move into a `const dialogs` rendered by **both** branches — the live `GroupsCut` bug.

**A book's language is a select with an escape hatch (Tasks §6.1)** — ⚠️ **a strict `ka|en|ru` list would have destroyed data**, and from our own importer: `OpenLibraryClient::language()` deliberately emits `de` and `fr` and falls through to the **raw MARC code** (`spa`, `ita`, `jpn`…) for everything else. Such a value is not in a strict list, so the trigger renders **empty** — the field looks unfilled — and the first save writes `language: null`. The form therefore appends the record's own value as an extra `SelectItem` when it is outside the presets, and **`Rule::in` was deliberately not added to the backend**: it would 422 exactly the values our own importer wrote. ⚠️ `FieldCatalog`'s entry changed `type` to `select` but its **`key` did not change** — a key change orphans every user's `module_user.settings.fields` override.

**`components/ui/data-table.tsx` is the shared table** (§6.4 — search · sortable columns · pagination). ⚠️ Filtering/sorting/paging are **client-side on purpose**: it is handed an already-loaded list. A thousand-row case needs a *separate* server-side component, not a hidden branch inside this one. **Since 2026-09-16 it serves three places** — the "who has this module" modal on `/modules/{key}`, **`/requests`** and **`/users`** — and it grew two props for them: `empty` became a **`ReactNode`** (so `EmptyState` fits; a `string` could not carry "what is empty · why · what is the next button", which is the whole point of §2.4) and **`toolbar`** takes the page's own narrowing controls *inside the same bar* as search and page size (`/users`' role and status selects) — in a row of their own they stopped reading as belonging to the table. ⚠️ Its `columns` array must be **`useMemo`'d by the caller**: `filtered` depends on `columns`, so a fresh array every  render re-sorts the whole list. Two things are easy to get wrong when a hand-rolled table moves onto it:
- ⚠️ **A filter is not a search.** Role/status on `/users` cut the *rows* (they are applied before `rows` is handed over); the search box only matches text. Folding the first into `searchOf` would have made "disabled" match a user whose name contains that word.
- ⚠️ **Sorting a date column reads the raw ISO string, never the rendered text** (`value: (r) => Date.parse(...)`). A formatted date sorts lexicographically into a different order entirely — and both pages were printing a bare `toLocaleString()` before the move, i.e. the project's own date rule (`lib/dates.ts`) did nothing on exactly those two cells.

**`/requests` is a table, not a card stack (Tasks §5, 2026-09-16)** — the user's words: „მოთხოვნები … მას დეითეიბლის მსგავსი სორტირება ჰქონდეს, ასევე სერჩი და გვერდზე რამდენი გამოჩნდეს". Six things are easy to get wrong:
- ⚠️ **The „pending" cut is in the table too, and that is the decision** (asked and answered). It is the only cut where an action happens — which is exactly why a pending request is a **form, not a row**: a review-note textarea and (on quota) a megabyte field do not fit a cell. Both moved into a „review" `ModalShell`, so all four cuts share one view, one sort and one search.
- ⚠️ **`grantOf()`'s rule had to survive the move: an empty field means „exactly what was asked".** `undefined` makes the backend read `payload.requested_bytes`; sending `0` is a `storage_request_out_of_range` **422**, i.e. a perfectly good approval would fail with an unexplainable error. The note applies to **rejection** as well, so it is one field above both buttons.
- ⚠️ **The genre-delete warning stays in the modal body**, not in a cell that scrolls sideways: it is the only place an admin sees *what* is being deleted from **every** account before approving.
- ⚠️ **The four status cards stay above the table and their facets deliberately do NOT exclude their own cut** (unlike `/admin/audit/summary`) — the four statuses are mutually exclusive, so „exclude yourself" would print the total on every card. Don't "fix" it.
- ⚠️ **Type is a column, not a second row of cards** (asked and answered): a card row is for *cuts*, and `AdminRequestController::index()` returns no per-type counts — a card without a number is the colourless pill it replaced. Its icon map is local to the page and deliberately **not** in `lib/cutStyle.ts`, which is the registry of mutually-exclusive cuts.
- ⚠️ **Search covers the module's and the genre's name in *both* languages** — otherwise the same request would be findable or not depending on the UI language.
- **`/u/:username` (`PublicProfilePage`) sits outside `Protected`** — it is the one route that renders without a session. It has no sidebar/header shell by design: a stranger opening a shared link should see the profile, not the app chrome.
- **Sync lives entirely on `/sync`**, not in settings: the runner *and* the `syncDelayMs` pause. The route and sidebar link appear only when a media module is enabled (TMDB doesn't apply to other domains). `/translations` mirrors this with its **own** pause (`translateDelayMs`) — Gemini's rate limit is not TMDB's, so the two are separate settings and `ui/queue.tsx` picks the delay per job kind (`paceOf`: `add`/`purge` → 0, `translate` → `translateDelayMs`, `sync`/`gallery` → `syncDelayMs`).
- Sidebar links: dashboard (first) · **dictionaries** (the card index — **above `/genres`** since 2026-09-14, a plain one-line row like every other tool. ⚠️ **It briefly carried a sub-line („every module's statuses, genres, types and categories") and the user had it removed on 2026-09-15**: it was the one two-line item in the whole navigation, i.e. it broke the very rule the menu is scanned by — one row, one line. The sentence stays where reading it is free, in the index page's subtitle (`dictionaries.navHint`), and `toolLink()` lost its extra-class argument with it, since nothing else ever needed one) · genres (⚠️ deliberately a **different** thing kept next to it: `/genres` is the **global** TMDB dictionary — one for every account, deletion needs an admin's approval — while `/dictionaries` is **mine**; the icons say so, `Library` vs `Tags`) · statuses · sync · translations · people (§16.2) · chat (§16.3, with an unread badge) · modules · requests · users · roles · audit (the last three admin-only); the song section additionally links to `/playlists`. ⚠️ **No module section carries a dictionary link any more** — statuses went on 2026-09-14 and **the other seven on 2026-09-15** (the user: „why is *manage types* inside Video, that dictionary is in a separate menu" — the same for song/book/board-game/game genres and note/bookmark categories). One rule, no exception: a module's sub-items are **cuts of its own list** (statuses, „all", „favourite") plus „add"; managing the list of statuses, types, genres and categories is `/dictionaries`, which is its own sidebar section and already draws a card per dictionary. A link in both places meant two routes to one page and made the separate section look redundant. ⚠️ **The sections themselves stay and that is not an inconsistency**: „watched"/„favourite" filter the records, they do not edit the dictionary. ⚠️ **The record pages keep their „manage genres/types" button** (`SongsPage`, `VideosPage`, …): that is a contextual shortcut from a list to its own dictionary, not a second menu entry. ⚠️ Removing the links also removed the `onX || location.pathname === '/dictionaries/…'` clauses that kept a module row lit on the dictionary page — with no link there, a module row highlighting on another section's page would be a lie. **Book, board game and game show only „all · favourite · add"** (§6.1) — a deliberate deviation from the shared rule, on the user's instruction; status filtering stays in the filter panel. ⚠️ **The gallery section has sub-items again since §8.5** — ყველა ფოტო · ბიბლიოთეკა · მსახიობები · უკატეგორიო · ვიდეოები · წყაროები · მოდულები — and the list is `GALLERY_CUTS` in `lib/galleryCuts.ts`, imported by both the page and the sidebar so they cannot drift. ⚠️ **The records item no longer renames itself** (§27): `galleryCutLabel()` is gone and both sides read `gallery.cut.records`, because the composed name was too long for a tab and still incomplete. (Between §4.5 and §8 it had none: its only item had been „manage themes", and themes were removed.)
- **One shared width container** — `components/ui/page.tsx` (`PageContainer`, or `pageContainer()` for pages that build their own `<main>`, e.g. `MoviePage`'s hero). `wide` = `max-w-7xl 2xl:max-w-[1600px]`, `narrow` = `max-w-4xl` (forms). Don't hand-roll `mx-auto max-w-*` on a new page.
- **Filtering is a right-hand sticky panel** — `components/FilterPanel.tsx` (`FilterPanel` + `FilterGroup`/`FilterOption`/`FilterOptionList`/**`FilterRange`**/`FilterTrigger`), used by `LibraryPage`, `VideosPage`, `SongsPage`, `NotesPage`, `BooksPage`, `GamesPage`, `BoardGamesPage` and `BookmarksPage`. The **applied** filter lives in the URL (`?view=&genre=&year_min=…` / `?view=&type=&tag=` / `?view=&genre=&tag=`) so the sidebar, browser back and shared links agree; the panel only edits a local draft until „გაფილტვრა". ⚠️ **The draft mechanics are `lib/filters.ts` (`useFilterDraft`), not the pages** (rewritten 2026-09-13, stage 6). Three facts used to be hand-written on all eight: the draft↔URL sync effect, `same()` (character-for-character identical on seven pages) and „how many filters are on". That duplication is exactly why **„clear" did not work**: `onClear` only navigated to a filter-less URL, so if the URL was *already* clean (tick three genres, then press clear without applying) nothing changed, the sync effect never ran and the checkboxes stayed ticked. `apply()` therefore writes the draft itself and `clear()` is `apply(empty)` — one definition, both sides. `filterKey()` (order-insensitive, so `['a','b']` and `['b','a']` are one filter) and `filterCount()` are pure and unit-tested (`lib/filters.test.ts`); each page keeps only its own module-level `EMPTY_FILTERS` (which is also where `PanelFilters`' type comes from) and its `writeFilters` URL writer. ⚠️ **Selected values show as chips only while the group is collapsed** (§2.3 added them, stage 6 narrowed it): in an *open* group the checked rows are already at the top, so the chips repeated it and pushed the list two rows down. `FilterGroup` finds them by **walking its own children** for `FilterOption` elements, deliberately: the alternative was threading a `selected` prop through eight pages, i.e. the same fact written twice, and one copy would drift. A chip's X — and the group header's new „clear this group" X — call that row's own `onChange(false)`, so there is still one removal path. ⚠️ **„Checked first" is frozen, not live** — re-sorting on every render made the row you just clicked jump to the top, putting a *different* genre under the cursor; the order is now cached in a ref keyed on the option list + search term (a `useMemo` over `options` would recompute on every click and bring the jump back). ⚠️ `FilterOptionList` grows a search box past eight options — but only when *every* child is a `FilterOption`, or a group that draws its own controls would have them silently filtered away. ⚠️ **Status is deliberately not in the panel** (Tasks 3) — it is the sidebar section (`?view=`); the panel preserves it but never sets it. Long option lists scroll (`fb-scroll`) instead of a show-more button. ⚠️ **No `uppercase` on Georgian group titles** — CSS `text-transform` turns Mkhedruli into Mtavruli, so the class was dropped here and in `GroupFilters`. ⚠️ **A collapsed group's chips carry the X on the right and it reddens on hover** (2026-09-14): on the left it read as a type icon rather than an action. It is a `<span>` inside the chip and not a nested `<button>` (invalid HTML) — the chip itself already *is* the remove button, so `Chip`'s `remove` prop only draws the mark. ⚠️ **„Clear the filter" is `outline`, not `ghost`** — a borderless button read as a different (smaller) size next to „Apply" above it, though both are the same height; the border pairs them and the red still says which one destroys a selection.

**Frontend flow** (`frontend/src`): `api/movies.ts` (axios) → TanStack Query in `pages/` (`LibraryPage`, `MoviePage`, `MovieFormPage`). Add/edit form has a "quick fill" lookup: type a ge.movie/IMDb link or title → `/lookup/candidates` → user picks → `/lookup` (by `tmdb_id`) prefills → on save the movie is created then auto-`resync`ed to download media. Translatable content (title/description/genres) lives in its own card; non-translatable data (poster, year, rating, IMDb, status, favorite) sits in a static box. The KA/EN choice is **no longer a per-card toggle** — it comes from the `contentLang` setting via `useContentLang()` (see Settings above).

## Gotchas

- ⚠️ **A database dump is never committed** (Tasks SEC-01, 2026-09-17). `backend/mediary_backup.sql` had been tracked since the first commit and carried **bcrypt hashes, `remember_token`s, live session ids, private chat** and — in `module_user.settings`, the pre-§21.9 home of the bot token — **a plaintext Telegram bot token** (present in four committed versions; `user_credentials` holds an encrypted copy that needs `APP_KEY`, the pivot copy needs nothing). Four things hold the rule together: `.gitignore` blocks **every** `*.sql`/`*.sql.gz` (a dump anywhere in the tree is the risk, not just this name — a real SQL fixture would need a `!` negation), `setup.sh`/`setup.ps1` run `php artisan migrate --seed` (which offers to create a missing `mediary`, default yes — ⚠️ **never add `--no-interaction` there**: without `--force` it makes `MigrateCommand::createMissingMySqlOrPgsqlDatabase()` return `false`, i.e. the database is silently not created and the migration fails), the first account comes from `mediary:bootstrap-admin`, and a library moves between machines through **`/backups`** (download → upload → restore). ⚠️ **History was rewritten on 2026-09-17** (with the user's explicit yes): `git filter-branch` dropped the dump from all 46 commits and blanked `.env.example`'s TMDB key in the same pass (SEC-06), the two dump-only commits vanished (46 → 44) and `main` was force-pushed `f730e0d` → `42350d0`. ⚠️ **Any clone made before that — the original machine's included — must never push**: its old history would bring the dump and the key straight back. Reset it (`git fetch && git reset --hard origin/main`, after saving local work) or re-clone. ⚠️ Every commit SHA before 2026-09-17 changed, so a SHA quoted in older notes no longer resolves. ⚠️ **`setup.ps1` must stay ASCII-only**: Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads a BOM-less script as Windows-1252, where an em dash's UTF-8 bytes decode to `”` and end the string they sit in — the script had failed to parse for exactly this reason.
- **PHP cURL has no CA bundle on Windows** → HTTP calls use `->withOptions(['verify' => storage_path('cacert.pem')])` (bundle committed at `backend/storage/cacert.pem`). Keep this on any new outbound `Http` call (TMDB, images, Gemini).
- **Frontend updates use `POST` + `_method=PUT`** (multipart-safe method spoofing) — see `updateMovie` in `api/movies.ts`.
- ⚠️ **A session that ran out is answered with a machine code, and the SPA retries the CSRF case once** (Tasks GAP-02, 2026-09-17). `SESSION_LIFETIME` is 120 minutes and the `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie carries the same lifetime, so an open tab used to hit two different English sentences that went straight into the toast — Laravel writes `message` as prose in both cases, which is exactly the rule GAP-01 spent a task enforcing. Seven things are easy to get wrong:
  - ⚠️ **419 is retried, and that is safe for a POST *only because of where the check runs*.** CSRF is validated inside `EnsureFrontendRequestsAreStateful`'s pipeline, **before `$next($request)`** — a 419 means the request never reached the controller, so a repeat cannot create a second record. No other status may be retried on this reasoning.
  - ⚠️ **The new token is never written by hand.** axios reads `X-XSRF-TOKEN` **from the cookie on every dispatch** (`helpers/resolveConfig.js`) and `headers.set()` overwrites, so re-sending the *same* `config` is enough; the adapter works on a `mergeConfig` copy, so the original config is never polluted (which is also why a FormData boundary cannot go stale).
  - ⚠️ **The "already retried" marker lives on the `config`, not in a module-level flag** — several requests fail at once and a global flag would deny the second one its attempt. A second 419 (or a failed cookie refresh) falls through to `UNAUTHENTICATED_EVENT`.
  - ⚠️ **`map()` for 419, `render()` for 401 — and the asymmetry is not a style choice.** `Handler::render()` calls `prepareException()` *first*, which turns `TokenMismatchException` into `HttpException(419)`, and only then looks at the `render()` callbacks — so `render(function (TokenMismatchException …))` would **never fire**. `AuthenticationException` falls to `default => $e` and survives, so there `render()` is the working hook.
  - ⚠️ **401 had to be fixed together with 419, or the headline symptom would have stayed.** After the lifetime runs out the *first* request is usually a background poll (chat 30 s, reminders) — i.e. a **GET**, which CSRF never inspects — so `unauthenticated` reaches the user before `csrf_token_mismatch` does. The 401 callback must return `null` for a non-JSON request or the browser redirect is lost.
  - ⚠️ **Both codes live in `bootstrap/app.php`, not in `backend/app`** — a "every backend code ⊆ `CODES`" scanner that greps only `app/` will miss them (FEAT-01).
  - ⚠️ **`ERR_CANCELED` is not a network failure.** It also has an empty `response`, but it is the user's own doing: every keystroke in the global search aborts the previous request (`api/search.ts`'s `signal`) and the queue has a stop button — "check your connection" on each letter would be a lie. `errors.network` is the SPA's own text, and the one in this list the backend never sends.
- ⚠️ **`User::hasModule()` answers from memory, not from the database** (Tasks PERF-01, 2026-09-17). It used to be `$this->modules()->where(...)->first()` — a **query builder**, so it ignored an eager-loaded `modules` relation and hit the database on every call. That matters because the shape it appears in is `foreach ($modules as $m) { if (! $user->hasModule($m->key)) … }`, in `DashboardController`, `GalleryController`, `ModuleImages`, `GlobalSearch`, `MediaDomain` and `AdminModuleController`. Measured with a fresh `User` instance per request (reusing one instance flatters the result, because the relation cache survives): `/api/dashboard` 25 → 15 queries (`module_user` 11 → 1), `/api/gallery` 43 → 30 (14 → 1), `/api/modules` 28 → 8 (13 → 3), `/api/admin/modules` **164 → 11** (133 → 1). Four things are easy to get wrong:
  - ⚠️ **`loadMissing('modules')`, not a private memo.** It is Eloquent's own relation cache, so a list already fetched with `with('modules')` is reused and invalidation stays the ordinary `refresh()` / `unsetRelation()`. A third mechanism for one fact is what drifts.
  - ⚠️ **Change membership or `is_hidden` on an instance you still hold, and you must `unsetRelation('modules')`.** No live path does today, and that was checked rather than assumed: `setEnabled()`, `syncModules()` and `approveModule()` all check first and write after, `register()` ends with `load('modules')` (which forces a reload, unlike `loadMissing`), and `enabledModules()`/`moduleKeys()` still query every time.
  - ⚠️ **The super-admin branch needs its own memo**, because it asks the one question the pivot cannot answer: a super admin has a module even with no pivot row. It is a per-instance list of active keys, so it never runs for an ordinary account.
  - ⚠️ **The test pins the `module_user` count, never the total.** A total would move with every new counter and the test would become a tripwire for unrelated work; `module_user` is the fact the task is about. It fails on the old model with "11 is identical to 1".
- ⚠️ **`AdminModuleController::index()` was linear in the number of users for two separate reasons, and PERF-01 only removed one** (Tasks PERF-04, 2026-09-17). `with('modules')` was there from the start — the comment even said so — but `hasModule()`/`isGrantedModule()` threw it away, which is the half PERF-01 fixed (164 queries → 11). The remaining half was **`role`, not the pivot**: `users_list` asks `roleKey()`/`isSuperAdmin()` per user, so without the eager load it stayed linear (3 users → 9 queries, 12 → 18, 30 → 36, and 14 of those 18 were on `roles`). `User::with('modules', 'role')` makes it a flat **6 queries at 3, 12 and 30 users alike**. Two things are worth keeping:
  - ⚠️ **The task's own claim that it "resolves automatically after PERF-01" was wrong**, and only measuring showed it. A registry-style assumption about *why* something is slow survives until someone counts.
  - ⚠️ **The test compares two different user counts instead of asserting a constant.** Today's number is 6 and it will move with the next field added to the payload; "does not depend on N" is the fact the task is actually about. It fails on the old controller with "31 is identical to 11".
- ⚠️ **The public gallery reads the cast pivot once per domain, not once per record** (Tasks PERF-02, 2026-09-17). `PublicGallery::publicCastIds()` looped the public records and ran `$record->cast()->pluck(…)` on each — measured at 5 films: 16 queries, at 25: 36, at 60: 71, i.e. ~500 queries on a 500-film profile. ⚠️ **And this is the one domain endpoint outside `auth:sanctum`**, so linear growth there is not only a slow page but a cheap DoS vector. It is one `castables` query per domain now, and the same three endpoints answer in 9/7 queries at 25 and at 60 films alike. Three things are easy to get wrong:
  - ⚠️ **`castable_type` takes the domain key, not `getMorphClass()`** — `query()` already compares `imageable_type` to exactly that (`movie`/`series`/`anime` *are* the morph-map names). Two conventions on one table is what drifts apart later.
  - ⚠️ **Dropping the join to `cast_members` is safe, and that was checked rather than assumed**: `castables.cast_member_id` is `cascadeOnDelete`, so an orphan pivot row cannot exist, and `cast()`'s `orderByPivot` adds nothing to a set of ids.
  - ⚠️ **`publicIds()` is memoised per request, which was a second bug in the same method**: the three-layer public query ran **twice** for a media domain — once for the records' own photos and again from `publicCastIds()` for their actors. The service is injected per request, so the memo lives no longer than that.
- ⚠️ **`StorageMeter::files()` takes an optional module hint, and it is a hint — never the truth** (Tasks PERF-03, 2026-09-17). `usedByModule()` ran the whole ~20-table inventory and filtered it in PHP, and `guardModule()` calls it **per uploaded file** — uploads are batched (`files[]`, `UploadLimits::MAX_FILES`), so a 10-file upload built the inventory ten times: measured 346 queries → **76** (1 file 42 → 15, 5 files 176 → 41). Five things are easy to get wrong:
  - ⚠️ **A second set of `SUM(size)` queries was the obvious fix and it was refused.** `usedByModule()`'s own docblock says why: "what counts" must have exactly one definition, or the limit and the total drift apart. The hint keeps that — it is the *same* code, it just stops asking tables that cannot contribute.
  - ⚠️ **`where('module', …)` stays even with the hint**, so a block that is wrongly skipped is only slower, never wrong. Correctness never depends on the hint being right.
  - ⚠️ **A request-scoped memo of `files()` — the other option the task listed — would have been wrong, not merely redundant.** The rows appear *inside* the upload loop (`storeUpload()` is evaluated as an argument to `create()`), so file #2 would be checked against the snapshot taken before file #1 existed, and the module limit would be overshot silently by up to N−1 files.
  - ⚠️ **The price of the hint is one test**, `test_the_module_hint_never_changes_the_answer`: over 14 modules, `files($user, $m)` filtered by module must equal `files($user)` filtered by module. Without it a mis-labelled block would fail *silently* — the limit would simply engage a little later than it should.
  - ⚠️ **Each block's module is static**, which is what makes the hint possible at all: `movies/posters` is always `movie`, `gallery_images` always `gallery`, `messages` always `chat`, `<module>_field_values` its own module. A block whose module were computed per row could not be skipped this way.
  - ℹ️ `guardModule()` returns early when the module has no allocation, so only accounts that set a per-module limit were ever paying this.
- ⚠️ **TypeScript `strict` is on, and turning it on changed nothing — which is the point** (Tasks DEBT-01, 2026-09-17). `grep strict frontend/tsconfig*.json` used to return nothing, so `tsc -b` silently accepted an implicit `any` and a `null` in a non-nullable position across ~60k lines: every `?? null` / `?.` was a convention, not a check. It compiled clean on the first try (`any` and `@ts-ignore` are both zero in this project), so this is a **ratchet**, not a repair — tomorrow's `function f(x)` no longer passes. Four things are worth keeping:
  - ⚠️ **A flag that reports zero errors has to be proved non-vacuous.** A temporary `function probe(x)` plus `const s: string = null` returns TS7006 and TS2322, i.e. `noImplicitAny` and `strictNullChecks` really do fire. Without that check "0 errors" and "the config is not being read" look identical.
  - ⚠️ **`noUncheckedIndexedAccess` was measured and deliberately left off**: 37 errors in 19 files, and most are the `if (xs.length) xs[0]` shape — safe, merely invisible to the compiler. Its usual answer is `!`, which cancels the check it just made, so it would have bought noise instead of safety. It did surface one real hole, `lib/errors.ts::fieldErrors()` declaring `v[0]` as `Record<string, string>`, which Laravel cannot actually produce; that stays open rather than being changed without a test on the form-error path.
  - ⚠️ **`tsconfig.node.json` gets `strict` too** — a config file is code, and `vite.config.ts` decides the dev proxy and the build.
  - ⚠️ **`vitest.config.ts` was type-checked by nothing at all** and now is: `tsconfig.app.json` only takes `src`, and the node config only listed `vite.config.ts`, so a type error in the test config would have shown up only when the tests ran.
- ⚠️ **Every uploaded file now leaves the server through `SafeMime::response()`, not only chat's** (Tasks SEC-08, 2026-09-18). Note files and custom-field files stored `getClientMimeType()` and handed it back as `Content-Type` with `inline` — SEC-04's shape exactly. Five things are worth keeping:
  - ⚠️ **Ownership does not close this**, which is the whole point: the same rows appear in the admin's `/users/{id}` file library through `StorageMeter::files()`, so a low-privilege account plants the payload and an admin opens it. Self-XSS was the floor, not the ceiling.
  - ⚠️ **SEC-05 already blocks the *upload*, so the live hole is the rows that predate it** — `CustomFields::FILE_MIMES` accepted `svg` until 2026-09-17 and those files are still on disk with the client's claimed type in the column. A test therefore has to write that state **directly**; going through the endpoint can no longer reproduce it. (Checked in the framework: Laravel's `image` rule only accepts SVG with `allow_svg`, so the `image` kind was never a way in.)
  - ⚠️ **This is defence in depth, not duplication.** The `mimes:` list grows over time, and the serving side must not be the only thing that depends on that list being right.
  - ⚠️ **`UploadedFile::fake()` cannot prove the mime fix.** `Illuminate\Http\Testing\File::getMimeType()` derives the type from the **file name**, so the client's claim and the server's answer always agree and the test passes vacuously. A real `UploadedFile` with a deliberately false client type is needed.
  - ⚠️ **Three other `disk->response()` sites were checked and deliberately left alone**: `GalleryController::imageFile()` and `PublicProfileController::photoFile()` serve content the user cannot supply (`GalleryFetcher` only talks to TMDB, `WebImageImporter::extension()` refuses SVG) and the same file is reachable through `/storage/*` anyway, so the change would close nothing and would turn TMDB's `.svg` logos into downloads; `VideoDownloadController::show()` serves yt-dlp output back to its own owner only, and the private disk keeps it out of the admin library.
- ⚠️ **A queue batch carries its owner in its own options** (Tasks SEC-09, 2026-09-18). `GET`/`DELETE /api/batches/{batch}` only did `Bus::findBatch()`, and `Illuminate\Bus\Batch` is **not an Eloquent model** — so `EnsureRecordOwnership` never saw it and `BelongsToUser` never applied. Knowing someone's batch UUID was enough to read their progress and cancel a running sync. `->withOption('user_id', …)` round-trips through `job_batches.options` (verified in `DatabaseBatchRepository`), so no second table states the same fact. The answer is **404, not 403** — that the batch exists is itself information — and a batch with no owner recorded is a 404 too: only pre-fix batches can be in that state, a security check should refuse the unknown, and a batch finishes in minutes anyway.
- **Genres are keyed by `slug`** across manual entry and TMDB sync, so `firstOrCreate(['slug' => …])` (not by `tmdb_id`) avoids duplicate-slug violations.
- **`imdb_id` is unique per user**, not globally (`unique(user_id, imdb_id)`) — two accounts must be able to add the same film. ⚠️ **The *validation* has to say so too, and on `movie`/`series` it did not until 2026-09-14**: `StoreMovieRequest`/`StoreSeriesRequest` (and both update twins) carried a plain `unique:movies,imdb_id`, so the second account got a 422 „this film is already added“ — which also leaked that *somebody* had it. `anime` (built later) had it right and explained why; the two oldest domains were simply missed. The rule is `Rule::unique(...)->where('user_id', $this->user()?->id)`, plus `->ignore(...)` on update, and it is pinned by `MovieModuleTest`.
- **Never name a column `hidden`** (or `visible`, `casts`, `attributes`, `table`, `connection`…): those are protected properties on `Model`, so `$model->pivot->hidden` read from *inside another Model subclass* returns the protected array, not the column — silently, no error. `module_user.is_hidden` and `module_user.is_public` are named that way for this reason.
- **`/storage/*` is public, so a public profile's images are already reachable by URL** — that is consistent (a photo inherits its parent's visibility, §10) but it means making a record private does **not** un-publish an already-shared image URL. ⚠️ This still holds for posters and gallery photos **on purpose**: they are TMDB's public images and the public profile renders them. Only the `note` module moved to the private disk (§17.5) — see the disk rule below.
- ⚠️ **The suite runs in one PHP process, so `phpunit.xml` pins `memory_limit = 512M`** (2026-09-19). With 900+ tests each rebuilding the application, memory only grows, and the suite reached PHP’s **128 MB CLI default** while FEAT-07 was being added. ⚠️ **The failure mode is what makes this worth writing down**: it is not a failing test but `Fatal error: Premature end of PHP process`, on a **different test every run** — so the name in the output is the test that happened to be next, not the cause. Read the line above it (`Allowed memory size … exhausted`) before suspecting whatever was named. ⚠️ It lives in `phpunit.xml` and not on the command line, or local and CI would have different ceilings and only one of them would go red.
- **Tests run on sqlite `:memory:`** (`phpunit.xml`), which has no FULLTEXT and no `ALTER … MODIFY`: those migration statements are wrapped in a `getDriverName() === 'mysql'` check. Keep new MySQL-only DDL guarded the same way.
- ⚠️ **A dead `self::CONST` is a runtime fatal, not a compile error — so an untested branch hides a live 500** (Tasks DEBT-14, 2026-09-19). `GenreItemController::update()` still read `self::RELATIONS` after BUG-19 had moved that list into `MediaDomain`, so `attach`/`detach`/`move`/`replace` **always** died with „Undefined constant" — and died **after** the change was committed to the database, i.e. the user saw an error over an edit that had in fact happened. PHP resolves a class constant only when the line executes, so neither `tsc`'s equivalent nor `pint` nor any other test could see it; only a request through that branch can. ⚠️ **The generalisation is about deletions, not about constants**: removing a shared list, trait or helper is the moment to `grep` its old name repo-wide, because every surviving reference is a fatal on a line nobody runs. ⚠️ **The throw sat after the write, which is why the symptom misleads**: „the request failed" and „nothing changed" are different facts, and a 500 raised while building the *response* says only the first. A bug report of the shape „it errors but the change is there" points at the response, not at the write.
- ⚠️ **The cross-cutting maps are pinned by `RegistryConsistencyTest`** (added 2026-09-06; it also checks `AuditRegistry::MODELS` since 2026-09-10). Adding a module means writing it into several registries, and forgetting one is *silent* — it has bitten twice (`games.cover_path` missing from `referencedPaths()` meant the admin's orphan cleanup **deleted live files**; `game` missing from the frontend `DICTIONARIES` made `/purge` render an empty type list).
