The test derives its expectations from **the schema**, not from a hand-written list: every column whose name contains `path` must appear in `StorageMeter::referencedPaths()` (or in the test's `NOT_DISK_PATHS`, where only `gallery_images.remote_path` sits — it is TMDB's path, not ours), every `modules` row must be a purge target, every target with a `status` mode must have a status list, every table with a `visibility` column must be a `PublicDomain` domain (only `note_entries` is excluded, per §16.5), every `StatusDomain` entry must actually be wired (the `HasStatus` trait, a `status_id` column and a `status` purge scope — forgetting one leaves records with no status or a filter that silently returns nothing) and `PurgeService::TARGET_STATUSES` must match `StatusDomain::defaultKeys()`, and **every purge target must have a `DashboardController::COUNTERS` entry** (added 2026-09-07 — `note` had been missing since §13, so its dashboard card silently rendered `count: null`, and `bookmark` was heading the same way). ⚠️ **That rule carried an exception for `gallery` until 2026-09-14, and the exception *was* the bug**: the dashboard card showed „—" over a gallery full of photos, because the list had been written on the assumption that the module has no records of its own. It does — `gallery_images` is its table, merely polymorphic — so the exception is gone and `COUNTERS['gallery']` is the **pair** `[GalleryImage, GalleryVideo]` (§8.1 put the module's content in two tables; counting only photos would report `0` for a gallery holding nothing but video links, i.e. the same silent bug from the other side). ⚠️ A constant-key test could never have caught this — it answers „is the module listed", not „is the number right" — so `DashboardTest` now hits `/api/dashboard` itself; that endpoint had **no test at all** before. ⚠️ It reads the `referencedPaths()` map **out of the source**, so it asserts three anchor entries first — a parse broken by a future refactor must fail loudly instead of passing vacuously. ⚠️ It earned its keep again with `anime` (§7.1, 2026-09-11), catching `animes.poster_path` and `CustomFields::model('anime')`. ⚠️ **The two 2026-09-06 custom-field migrations now skip a table whose parent does not exist yet**: `CustomFields::TABLES` keeps growing and a module added later (`bookmark` 09-07, `anime` 09-11) sits *after* them, so on a fresh **MySQL** database they were pointing a foreign key at a missing table — sqlite swallowed it, MySQL would not. That table is created by the module's own migration, behind `Schema::hasTable()`. **On the frontend the same job is done by types, not tests**: `PURGE_TARGET_MODES` is `as const satisfies …`, and `PurgeTargetWithType`/`PurgeTargetWithStatus` compute which domains *must* appear in `PurgePage`'s `DICTIONARIES`/`STATUS_NAMESPACE` — those maps are no longer `Partial`, so a new module without a dictionary fails `tsc`.
- **`/storage/*` is served without auth, so there are now two disks** (§17.5, 2026-09-05). `App\Support\StorageFolder::PRIVATE_ROOTS` is the **only** place that decides which: `notes/` and `chat/` (§16.3) → the `private` disk (`storage/app/private-uploads`, no `url()`, not reachable via `/storage/*`), everything else → `public`. Nothing else may name a disk — `StorageMeter::disk()`, `StoredFile::deleteFile()` and the orphan scan all ask `StorageFolder::diskFor($path)`, because one forgotten hardcoded `'public'` writes a private document to the world-readable disk. **A private file leaves the server through exactly one route** (`GET /api/note-files/{id}` → `NoteEntryFileController::show()`), which checks ownership explicitly and answers **404, not 403**, for someone else's file. ⚠️ Adding a private section = one entry in `PRIVATE_ROOTS` + its own policy-checked serving route (chat did exactly this in §16.3).
- **`module_user.settings` is a JSON string column**, written via `syncWithoutDetaching` with `json_encode`. Don't assume Eloquent casts it for you on the pivot.
- **Tags on videos, songs, books, notes and bookmarks are a plain `tags` JSON array**, not a table. Duplicates are stripped case/whitespace-insensitively on **both** sides — `dedupeTags()` in the form (which also toasts) and **`Video::normalizeTags()`** on save. Keep both: the form message is the UX, the model is the guarantee. `Video::normalizeTags()`/`tagKey()` are the one source used by the form save path, `POST /videos/bulk` **and `Song`/`Book`/`NoteEntry`** (which delegate to them), so tag comparison stays identical everywhere. ⚠️ Normalization lives in the **controller**, not in a model mutator — creating a record straight through `Model::create()` (a script, a seeder) will *not* dedupe.
- **`permission:@type` resolves the module from the *route* parameter first, then input** — same order as `EnsureModuleEnabled`. It used to read input only, so `/media/sync/series/5` silently checked the **`movie`** permission (fixed 2026-09-03, covered by `TranslationTest`). A shared `{type}` endpoint that carries the domain in the URL relies on this.
- **A POST that edits existing records needs its last path segment in `EnsureModulePermission::UPDATE_ENDPOINTS`** (`resync`, `watched`, `played`, `visited`, `bulk-status`, `bulk`, `reorder`, `primary`, `download`, `unlock`, `lock`, `move`) — otherwise the permission middleware derives `create` from the HTTP method and a user with update-but-not-create rights gets a spurious 403 **while a create-only role is let through** (Tasks SEC-07: `POST /gallery/images/move` hid and revealed photos on `create`). ⚠️ **Inside a group that already carries a bare `permission:<module>`, a route-level `->middleware('permission:<module>,update')` does not replace it — both run**, so the update-only role still fails the group's `create` check. Either the last segment goes into `UPDATE_ENDPOINTS`, or the route leaves the group (`withoutMiddleware('permission:<module>')`).
- **`react-select` is used for multi-selects** (`TagSelect.tsx`, `GenreSelect.tsx`) with shared styles in `lib/selectStyles.ts`. ⚠️ The Radix `ui/multi-select.tsx` was **deleted on 2026-09-12** — nothing imported it, and a second multi-select sitting in `ui/` is exactly what a new page picks up by mistake. `MovieMultiSelect`/`IdMultiSelect` are a different file and very much alive.
- ⚠️ **`localStorage` is never touched directly — `lib/storage.ts` (`safeGet`/`safeSet`) is the only door (Tasks BUG-24).** `i18n/index.ts` read `localStorage.getItem('lang')` at **module level**, and with "block all cookies and site data" the browser throws a `SecurityError` on *touching `window.localStorage` at all* — so the app died during import, long before `ErrorBoundary` exists, and the page stayed white. ⚠️ The `try` must wrap the **property access**, not just the call: guarding only `getItem()` leaves the real failure open. ⚠️ Failing silently is deliberate — storage here is convenience (language, theme, a settings cache the server also holds), so a warning would offer the user something they cannot act on. ⚠️ A test blocks it by replacing `window.localStorage` with a throwing getter and restores it **by descriptor**; `delete` takes jsdom's storage away for good.
- **Path alias** `@/` → `frontend/src` (no `baseUrl`; `paths` only, per the TS version). Radix component wrappers live in `src/components/ui`.
- ⚠️ **A missing translation key fails silently** — i18next just prints the key, so neither `tsc` nor ESLint notices. That is how 562 of 980 keys ended up missing and pages showed raw `roles.title` / `purge.title` (fixed 2026-09-06; both locales now hold 1487 keys and are in sync). **Run `python frontend/src/i18n/audit.py` after adding UI strings** — it reports missing keys, empty dynamic prefixes (`` t(`a.b.${x}`) ``) and any ka/en drift, and exits non-zero when something is off. ⚠️ Add every key to **both** `ka.json` and `en.json`; the audit treats one-sided keys as a problem. Locale files are plain 2-space JSON **with CRLF endings**, so a small Python script that loads → merges → `json.dumps(..., ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)` round-trips them byte-identically — but it must write with `newline='\r\n'`, or the whole file shows up as changed.
- ⚠️ **The audit has two more checks since 2026-09-07 (Tasks §1.4), and both found real bugs.** (1) **A key-like string literal outside `t()`** — keys are often stored in a map (`HEADLINES = { gallery: 'queue.galleryRunning' }`) and passed to `t()` as a variable, which the `t('…')` regex cannot see; that is how `queue.galleryRunning`/`purging`/`translating` and 24 keys behind `t(cond ? 'a.edit' : 'a.add')` ternaries stayed missing while the audit reported "0 missing". A literal counts as a suspect only when its first segment is a real namespace and it is **not** a prefix of existing keys (`boardGames.statuses` is a `` t(`${ns}.${x}`) `` prefix, not a key). (2) **Interpolation mismatch** — `t('k', { size })` against a text that says `{{bytes}}`; i18next prints the unknown placeholder **as text**, so nothing else catches it. It found 8, including „14 ობოლი ფაილი · {{bytes}}" and five `deleteHint`s that passed `title:` to a `{{name}}` text. ⚠️ **A third check was added on 2026-09-11 (§1.1) and it found six missing texts the audit had been reporting as fine.** A dynamic key's prefix does **not** always end at a dot: `t(`bulkVideo.action_${a}`)` breaks mid-segment, and the old pattern (`.` immediately before `${`) matched nothing at all — so `action_type`, `action_tags_add`, `action_tags_remove` and their three `confirm_*` twins printed raw on `/status` while the audit said „0 missing“. The regex now captures everything up to `${` and compares with a plain `startswith`, which is correct for both shapes. ⚠️ **The convention is now `{{name}}` for every record-name placeholder** (`videos.deleteHint` was the one holdout and was normalised). ⚠️ **A fourth check landed 2026-09-12 and it caught `sync.field.trailer`**, missing since the field was added to `SYNC_FIELDS`: the dynamic-prefix check only asked whether the prefix has *any* children, so seven keys under `sync.field.` made an eighth-member list look complete. The new check pairs a **string-array literal in the code** (`['title', …]`) with a prefix and reports members that have no key. ⚠️ Its precision is the whole design — the first version fired **45 false positives** because every page's `sort` array shares `title`/`year`/`rating`. Four filters bring it to zero: the array must be **reachable** from the file that calls `t()` (same file or one it imports), **≥3 and ≥75%** of members must already have keys, a member is **excused when the same file reads it under another key** (the `v === 'all' ? t('filter.all') : t(`ns.${v}`)` idiom), and **one array belongs to one prefix** — the one it matches best (`GALLERY_CAST_SIZES` scores 3/3 on `gallery.castSizeOption.` and 2/3 on `gallery.sizeOption.`, and only the first is real). `KNOWN_GAPS` holds the one legitimate exception and every entry needs its reason. ⚠️ **A fifth check landed 2026-09-18 (GAP-15) and it enforces a dictionary rather than hunting a bug**: `frontend/src/i18n/GLOSSARY.md` gives each concept exactly one Georgian word and `audit.py`'s `BANNED` list fails the audit when a retired one comes back (`ლინკ` → ბმული · `თამბნეილ` → ესკიზი · `კლავიშ` → გასაღები · `ნიკნეიმ` → მეტსახელი · `ფრენჩაიზ` → ფრანჩაიზი · `ჩამოწერ`/`ჩამოიწერ` → ჩამოტვირთვა · and „სინქრონი" is always „სინქრონიზაცია"). ⚠️ **It reads `ka.json` only, deliberately**: the same words also sit in the repo's Georgian *code comments*, which talk to a developer and are not product vocabulary — checking them would make the rule fire on things no user ever reads. ⚠️ **„note" is three different objects here and each keeps its own word** — the `note` **module's** record is „ჩანაწერი" (that name lives in `modules.name_ka`, so i18n cannot rename it), a note attached to a record (`*_notes`) is „ჩანიშვნა", and an administrative comment (`requests.noteLabel`, `backups.note`) is „შენიშვნა"; collapsing any two is the mistake the glossary exists to name.
- ⚠️ **`components/FileViewer.tsx` is the one online viewer for every uploaded file** (2026-09-14) — image · video · audio · PDF · text open in place, with **download and delete in the same window**; the file name in a list is a button. Wired into all six file lists (note, book, board game, game, song, video). ⚠️ **The kind is decided by `mime`, not by the extension** (`kindOf`, unit-tested): the stored name is a server-generated one and may have no usable extension, while `mime` was recorded at upload. ⚠️ **One component serves both disks, but the disk is the backend's to state**: `note` is private and read as a blob (§17.5), the rest are public via `storageUrl` — `private`/`resolve` are the caller's props, never guessed from the path. ⚠️ **No PDF library was added** (pdf.js is ~300 kB): PDF and text go into an `<iframe>` and the browser's own viewer does the work. ⚠️ **What the browser cannot show (zip, docx…) says so out loud** instead of rendering a black box — the `platform === 'other'` rule from the player. ⚠️ **Download reuses the same blob**: a second request is waste, and on the private disk a `/storage/*` link would not work at all.
- ⚠️ **`App\Support\UploadLimits` is the single map of upload sizes and formats** (2026-09-14) — `image` · `doc` · `video` · `book` · `rules`, read by all seven upload controllers (note/book/board-game/game/song/video files and chat). ⚠️ **The effective ceiling is `min(app rule, php.ini)`** (`effectiveKb()`), which is the whole reason it exists: the dev machine shipped `upload_max_filesize = 2M` / `post_max_size = 8M` while the app promised 100 MB videos, and **PHP discards an oversized body entirely** — `$_POST` and `$_FILES` arrive empty, so validation answered „the file is required" for a file that was plainly selected. That is what „uploads don't work" was. ⚠️ **A `PostTooLargeException` is now a 413 `upload_too_large`**, never the quota's `storage_quota_exceeded`: two different ceilings, and merging them would tell someone with plenty of space that they are out of it. ⚠️ **The numbers are never copied into the frontend** — `GET /api/uploads/limits` feeds both the per-section hints and `components/UploadLimitsCard.tsx` on `/settings`, because a constant in TS would start lying the moment `php.ini` changes. ⚠️ That card is **read-only on purpose**: the ceiling is server configuration, so a „raise it" button would either lie or hand the app write access to `php.ini`.
- ⚠️ **`components/ui/modal-shell.tsx` owns the modal frame, and since 2026-09-14 it has a header row: optional back arrow · title · close X.** ⚠️ **There was no close button at all before** — you left with `Esc` or a click on the backdrop, both invisible and effectively absent on a touch screen. ⚠️ **`onBack` is a separate action, not a synonym for close**: it is passed only when the modal was opened *from another modal* (reminders from the note form/detail), where the parent hides itself and the arrow says out loud that nothing was lost. ⚠️ **Widths grew the same day**: `wide` 3xl → **5xl**, plain lg → **xl**, and `ui/dialog.tsx` 4xl → 5xl — two-column forms and photo grids were squeezed into a pipe. `useConfirm` stays narrow on purpose: it is one question, not a form. ⚠️ **A form's action row belongs *after* everything on the page, outside `</form>`**, wired back with HTML5 `form="<id>"` — `NoteForm` had Save in the middle of the modal with uploads, reminders and custom fields below it, which read as „things that happen after saving".
- ⚠️ **`ModalShell` keeps a modal *stack*, and only the top one is visible (2026-09-14).** The rule is the user's: a new modal opens → the previous one disappears; close it or go back → the previous one returns. It lives **in the component**, not in the callers — the reminders dialog had implemented it by hand, and every future nested modal would have re-written the same code, with one forgotten spot putting „a small window on top of a big one" back. The stack is a module-level array read through `useSyncExternalStore`, registered in a **`useLayoutEffect`** (a plain effect runs after paint, so both modals flashed together for one frame); Context was rejected because a modal is a portal, so the tree shape says nothing about which one is on top.
- ⚠️ **The lower modal is hidden with CSS, not unmounted or closed.** Both alternatives were tried and both threw `Maximum update depth exceeded` inside `@radix-ui/react-presence` (`return null` and `open={top}` alike — the portal was torn down in the same commit that opened the next one). Hiding also buys two things: Radix already understands nested dialogs (focus goes to the top one), and the children stay **fully mounted**, so a half-filled form is exactly as you left it. Pinned by `components/ui/modal-shell.test.ts`, which fails without it.
- ⚠️ **The back arrow appears by itself when a modal is nested** — `onBack` is still an explicit prop for when „back" means something other than „close", but a nested modal without one gets `onClose` behind an arrow, because that *is* what closing it does there.
- ⚠️ **A dialog whose content arrives late needs a *fixed* height, not a `max-h`** (2026-09-14). `DiscoverModal` was one line tall while loading and jumped to full size when the grid landed; it is `h-[88vh]` now. ⚠️ Guessing „the final size" would not have worked — the number of rows follows `per_page`, which is a setting, so the jump would have come back.
- ⚠️ **There are three sizes now — `default` (xl) · `wide` (5xl) · `full` (7xl)** — and `size="full"` is `FileViewer`'s. A document viewer opened from a detail modal used to be *narrower* than the modal behind it, so a PDF or a video was squeezed into a small square. `wide` stays as the shorthand ~20 callers already use.
- ⚠️ **„Clear the filter" is red** (`FilterPanel`, all three controls: clear-all, the group X and the range X) — it is the one action in the panel that **destroys a selection**, while „Apply" only changes the view. The modal's close X speaks the same language (`hover:bg-destructive/10`).
- ⚠️ **`frontend/src/lib/layers.ts` is the only place a z-index is decided** (added 2026-09-07 for Tasks §1.1/§1.2). Popups (Radix `Select`, `Popover`, context/action menus, the header dropdown) portal to the end of `<body>`, so they share a stacking context with every modal and only z-index decides who is visible. They all sat at `z-50` while modals moved to `z-60/61`, drawers/lightbox to `z-80/81` and `useConfirm` to `z-90/91` — so **every dropdown opened inside a modal rendered behind it** and read as "the list doesn't open" (notes/bookmarks category+status, game status, video type, board-game genre delete, gallery "no theme"). Popups are therefore `z-[100]` and tooltips `z-[110]`: **above every layer**, because they are opened *from* the topmost one. ⚠️ react-select needs more than a z-index — its menu was also **clipped** by `ModalShell`'s `overflow-y-auto`, so `reactSelectPortal` (`menuPortalTarget` + `menuPosition: 'fixed'` + a `menuPortal` z-index) must be spread into every react-select usage (`GenreSelect`, `TagSelect`, `MovieMultiSelect`). ⚠️ **And that portal needs `pointerEvents: 'auto'` on `menuPortal`** (fixed 2026-09-09, in `lib/selectStyles.ts`): a Radix modal sets `pointer-events: none` on `<body>` while open and only `DialogContent` is clickable, so a menu portaled into `<body>` **inherits** it — the list opens, the keyboard works, and the mouse selects nothing. It read as "you expand the record picker and can't choose anything" and it hit *every* modal (sync, translations, gallery, tag inputs), which is why the fix belongs in the one style object and not in a caller. ⚠️ Tailwind reads class names out of the source, so these strings must stay literal — `` `z-[${n}]` `` generates nothing.
- ⚠️ **Reminders have their own section, `/notes/reminders`, and the note *form* has none at all** (2026-09-14, after three rejected attempts). The history is the lesson: the block lived only in `NoteDetail` (so the form had no reminders and the list's count badge was a dead label), then it was inlined into the form, then it became a card-button in the form. The user's answer all three times was the same — „it's still inside, in editing". **The entry point was the problem, not the window and not the visuals.** Nine things are easy to get wrong:
  - ⚠️ **A reminder is not a field of the note, so it does not belong in the note's form.** `pages/NoteRemindersPage.tsx` lists **every** note's reminders with state cuts (all · active · paused · finished), inline toggle and delete; the sidebar entry sits under the note module the way playlists sit under song. `NoteForm` is back to being only the note's fields.
  - ⚠️ **Moving it out did not cut the link — it made the link visible from the other side.** Each card names the note it belongs to, and editing opens *that note's* dialog, so there is still exactly one editor. Backend: **`GET /api/note-reminders`** (`NoteReminderController::all()`) returns every reminder of the caller with a narrow `note` (id + title). It must stay **below** `/note-reminders/due` in `routes/api.php` or „due" is read as an id, `note` is attached only `whenLoaded` (inside one note's list it would be noise and an N+1), and the `owner` global scope — not a hand-written `where` — is what keeps another account's rows out.
  - ⚠️ **Opening the reminders dialog *replaces* the modal it was opened from — it does not stack on top** (2026-09-14, the user's own choice: „let that one disappear and this appear; close it and the reverse"). Both modals are `wide`, so the upper one never covered the lower one's edges and read as „small". `NoteDetail`/`NoteForm` therefore own the open state and return the dialog **instead of** their own `ModalShell`; the component itself stays mounted, so a half-filled form is still there when you come back. That is also why `NoteRemindersLink`/`NoteRemindersButton` are plain buttons with an `onOpen` — ⚠️ not a breach of the `GroupsCut` rule, which is that state and the portal's JSX must live in the **same** component: here both live in the parent.
  - ⚠️ **The three upload sections are one component too, `components/NoteUploads.tsx`** (images · documents · videos), mounted in the detail modal **and** the add/edit form — before 2026-09-14 the form had no uploads at all, so you created a note and then had to reopen it in another modal to attach anything. On an unsaved note the sections are drawn but the controls are replaced by a „save first" line (`note_entry_files` needs a `note_entry_id`), and the block sits **outside `</form>`**: a file has its own endpoint and never travels in the note's `PUT`.
  - ⚠️ **The card is one component, `components/ReminderCard.tsx`**, drawn by both the note dialog and the page; two copies would drift within a week. `reminderInput()` moved to `lib/reminders.ts` and is still the single place a reminder's body is written.
  - ⚠️ **Where the entry points are now**: the list's bell badge (opens the dialog directly), the note's **detail** modal (a card), the section — and, since 2026-09-14, a **compact named+iconed button on the edit page** (`NoteRemindersLink`). ⚠️ That button is *not* the card that was removed, and the distinction is the whole point: a **module** must not sit inside a form, while a labelled icon button is a **way out** of it. ⚠️ It sits **outside `</form>`**, on its own row above `CustomFieldsCard` — putting it in the Cancel/Save row was the first attempt and the user corrected it („not *inside* the editing, outside it, on the edit page"): the form's action row is part of the form, so a link there still reads as a form control.
  - ⚠️ **„Separate" must not mean „detached": the dialog states which note it belongs to** (a „Note: <title>" strip at the top). That binding *is* the request — a reminder is a note's fact (`note_reminders.note_entry_id`) and the screen has to say so.
  - ⚠️ **One entry point from all three places** — the form, `NoteDetail` and the list badge open the *same* window; the badge goes straight to it rather than opening the note first. A second route is exactly what produced „I added one and could never find it".
  - ⚠️ **The button's count listens to the cache without fetching** (`useQuery` on `['note-reminders', id]` with `enabled: false`, falling back to the list's `reminders_count`). Live once the dialog has been opened, and no extra request on every form open just to draw a number.
  - ⚠️ **An unsaved note gets a disabled button and a reason.** `note_reminders` needs a `note_entry_id`, so `noteId === null` renders one line and `enabled: noteId != null` keeps the query from firing — showing the inputs would be a promise the form cannot keep.
  - ⚠️ **The whole reminder card opens the editor, but the switch and the delete button stay outside that button** — nesting them would make one activation toggle also open the editor. (`NoteDetail`'s short-lived `focus` prop and its `requestAnimationFrame` scroll are **gone**: they existed only because the block was buried at the bottom of a long modal.)
  - ⚠️ **The dialog is two screens, not one long page** (2026-09-14, the second rework — the user rejected the first *contents* too). The default screen is the **list**; the three-step editor appears only when adding or editing, behind its own „← back" header. Before that the editor was permanently expanded above the list, so „what reminders do I have" hid under a form and the block read as a form rather than a list. Four things carry the visual: a card per reminder (state stripe · mode icon · the schedule in bold · **the one fact that matters on its own line** — „in 3 hours · 14 Sep 09:00" — then small meta chips for channel/repeat/window); **three states, three looks** (`lib/reminders.ts::reminderState()` — `active` · `paused` · `done`, because „I switched it off" and „it will never fire again" need different actions); sorting by state then `next_at` (the server orders by `id`, which buries „what's next" mid-list); and `EmptyState` instead of one grey sentence.
  - ⚠️ **„Preview the next few firings" was deliberately not built.** It would be a **second** `next_at` formula on the client — zones, window, `repeat_count`, month-end clamping — and would drift from `NoteReminder::computeNextAt()` within a day. The card reads the server's `next_at` and nothing else; `lib/dates.ts::formatRelative()` only *formats* it („tomorrow", „in 3 hours"). ⚠️ That formatter takes the **UI language, not the date format**: „in 3 hours" is a sentence, so it must stay Georgian even when `dateFormat: 'iso'`.
  - ⚠️ **The regression is pinned on both sides and neither test is enough alone**: `NoteModuleTest::test_editing_a_reminder_updates_it_instead_of_creating_a_second` pins the API, and `components/NoteReminders.test.ts` pins that the UI actually calls `updateNoteReminder` with the existing id and never `createNoteReminder`.
  - ⚠️ **It was not generalised to other modules** — a reminder is a `note` fact (`note_reminders.note_entry_id`); anywhere else the table would have to become polymorphic, which is a schema decision, not a UI one.

- ⚠️ **The sidebar is coloured from `modules.color`, and the colour travels as two CSS variables — never as a class** (2026-09-13). Until then every module row was the same grey and only the active one had a gold icon, so eleven modules looked like one list. Now the row's icon is in the module's colour, hover **and** active draw a 2px left line in it (`border-l`), and an active sub-section takes a weak tint of the same colour instead of `bg-secondary`. Six things are easy to get wrong:
  - ⚠️ **Tailwind cannot generate a class from a hex** — a templated `` `border-l-[${color}]` `` produces **nothing**, because classes are read out of the source at build time. The colour therefore arrives as inline `style` (`PageHeader`'s rule) in the shape of two custom properties, `--mod` and `--mod-soft` (`color-mix(in oklab, <color> 16%, transparent)`), while the classes stay literal: `hover:border-l-[var(--mod)]`, `bg-[var(--mod-soft)]`, `[&>svg]:text-[var(--mod)]`. **A hover state can only be said in CSS** — JS never sees it — which is why a var and not a computed style.
  - ⚠️ **The variable is inherited, and that is the fallback mechanism.** `<nav>` sets a gold default (`NAV_DEFAULT_ACCENT`), so `modAccent()` writes nothing at all for `color === null` and such a row keeps exactly today's look; the dashboard link and every non-module item (genres, sync, admin…) get the same gold hover line from the same declaration. A second "if there is no colour" branch per row would have been eleven copies.
  - ⚠️ **The colour is an accent, not text.** Only the line, the icon and the weak background use it — a yellow or light-green module name would be unreadable on the light theme, so the name stays `text-foreground`/`text-muted-foreground`.
  - ⚠️ **The eleven identical rows are four shared constants now** (`MODULE_ROW`, `MODULE_ROW_ACTIVE`, `MODULE_ROW_IDLE`, `SUB_ACTIVE`). The class string was written out nine times for the collapsible modules plus the dashboard and the one plain module link; the old active variant carried `[&>svg]:text-gold`, which as a **descendant** rule (`.x > svg`) beats a `text-[var(--mod)]` on the icon itself — leaving one copy behind would have silently kept that module's icon gold.
  - ⚠️ **Every module must actually have a colour, and no two may share one** — pinned by `RegistryConsistencyTest::test_every_module_has_a_colour`. Both halves matter: a colourless module falls back to gold and stops being distinguishable, and a duplicated hex is invisible in code review. On the live database `anime` really was `null` (the module was added after the `color` migration and never reseeded); `db:seed --class=ModulesSeeder` filled it and left `movie`'s hand-picked `#7073ff` alone.
  - ⚠️ **`border-l-transparent` lives in the *idle* variant, never in the shared row class.** Both it and `border-l-[var(--mod)]` write `border-left-color` at the same specificity, and Tailwind emits the named colour **after** the arbitrary one — so keeping the track colour in the shared class left the active row's line silently transparent (hover worked, active did not). Only the 2px width is shared, so rows don't shift.
  - ⚠️ **The tool rows below the modules are coloured too since 2026-09-15** (the user's request). ⚠️ **They started out coloured *by family* and that was corrected the same day (§23): each section has its own tone now**, because the family scheme left the five admin rows identical — i.e. colour stopped answering the one question it is there for. The map moved out of `Sidebar.tsx` into **`lib/toolSections.ts`**, since the page header needs the very same colour and icon. The tones are chosen so neighbours stay legible: my dictionaries gold, the *global* genre dictionary amber (the two sit side by side and are different things — the colour says so), bulk status green, sync blue, translations orange, people violet and chat a neighbouring blue, the admin five in adjacent indigo→violet hues, credentials bronze, backups teal, purge red. The rows also had to gain `[&>svg]:text-[var(--mod)]` — the module rows had it, these did not, so their icons stayed grey no matter what the row's accent was.
  - ⚠️ **Verify in `dist/assets/*.css`**, not in the source — the same rule the radius work established. `npm run build` then grep for `--mod`: all five utilities (`text-`, `bg-`, `border-l-`, its `hover:` variant and the `[&>svg]:` one) must appear, or the colour silently does nothing.

- ⚠️ **There are no underlines in the app, anywhere** (2026-09-15, the user's instruction: „and generally, wherever there are underlines, remove them"). All **38** of them across 30 files were `hover:underline` (plus two permanent ones), and they were removed by *replacing the affordance, not deleting it*: a link that was already `text-primary` now lightens to `hover:text-primary/70`, an uncoloured title or button now turns `hover:text-primary`. Dropping the class alone would have left a dozen clickable titles with **no hover feedback at all** — which is worse than an underline. ⚠️ **The check is `grep -rn "underline" src`**: a new `hover:underline` is a regression, and Tailwind's preflight already removes the browser's own link underline, so nothing needs to be re-disabled globally.
- ⚠️ **Small pills grew their padding twice on request** — first the status badges (`ui/badge.tsx`, `px-2.5 py-1`), then the reminder card's meta chips (`ReminderCard`'s `Meta`, the note chip on `/notes/reminders`) and the card itself (`p-4 pl-5`, state stripe `w-1.5`). ⚠️ `Meta` is deliberately **not** `Badge`: it is the smaller 11px muted chip, so folding the two would have to reintroduce a size prop — the thing `Badge` exists to avoid.
- ⚠️ **Icon hover motion and semantic colour are decided in exactly one place — `index.css`** (2026-09-14, the user's instruction: „wherever there is an icon, add a matching hover animation, and give the icons matching colours"). The app uses **135 distinct lucide icons in hundreds of places**, so a hand-written `hover:` class per icon would have drifted within a week (one place `scale-110`, the next nothing) — the rule is written once and covers every existing *and* future icon. Twelve things are easy to get wrong:
  - ⚠️ **It works because `lucide-react` labels its own output**: every icon renders as `class="lucide lucide-trash-2"`, so CSS can tell *which* icon it is and give each one its own motion (the trash shakes, the X rotates, download moves down, upload up, arrows move along their own axis, the gear turns, the bell swings). Nothing else on the page carries `.lucide`, so the lightbox's and Radix's own SVGs are untouched.
  - ⚠️ **Static colours live in `@layer base`, hover colours outside every layer — and that asymmetry is the whole design.** Tailwind's utilities sit in `@layer utilities`, so a base-layer colour is a *default* that any component class overrides (the header's storage warning stays red at critical instead of turning amber), while an unlayered hover colour still beats `text-muted-foreground` — otherwise the hover colour simply would not show.
  - ⚠️ **`:not(.transition-transform)` is load-bearing.** Where the component manages the transform itself — the sidebar's and the filter panel's collapse chevrons (`-rotate-90` when closed, 8 places) — a global hover `transform` would take that rotation away, i.e. a closed section's arrow would point *down* while hovered. That class is already on every such icon, so it is the natural opt-out.
  - ⚠️ **A specific icon's rule must come after the generic one**: both are unlayered and both weigh `:hover` + one class, so only source order decides.
  - ⚠️ **Star and heart deliberately have no static colour.** Their colour *is* state („is it a favourite"), written by the component — a global gold would have made both states look identical and the button would stop answering the question it exists for. On hover they take `var(--favorite)`, the app's own token, not gold.
  - ⚠️ **The colour list is short on purpose** — only where colour carries meaning (delete · confirm · favourite · link · warning). Colouring every icon would bury the sidebar's per-module colour (`--mod`) and the status tones.
  - ⚠️ **Motion obeys `prefers-reduced-motion`, colour does not**: turning animation off is the user's choice, losing the meaning is not. And the spinner is excluded (`animate-spin` already owns `transform`).
  - ⚠️ **The React name is not the class name, and three rules were silently dead because of it** (caught 2026-09-15): lucide's class comes from its *icon id*, not from the exported alias — `DownloadCloud` renders `lucide-cloud-download`, `UploadCloud` renders `lucide-cloud-upload` and `Wand2` renders `lucide-wand-sparkles`. A wrong name is not an error anywhere; the rule simply never matches. Check a name against `grep -oE 'createLucideIcon\("[a-z0-9-]+"' node_modules/lucide-react/dist/cjs/lucide-react.js` before writing it.
  - ⚠️ **An animation's last keyframe must equal the declared `transform`.** When an animation ends the element snaps back to the declared transform, so a star that pops to `scale(1.38)` and ends at `scale(1)` while the hover rule says `scale(1.24)` visibly jumps at the end. The star, the bell and the pen all end where the hover transform leaves them.
  - ⚠️ **The bell needs `transform-origin: top center`** — without it a swing rotates around the icon's middle and reads as a wobble, not a ring. Its keyframes also *decay* (17° → 14° → 10° → 7° → 4° → 0), because a constant-amplitude swing looks mechanical.
  - ⚠️ **`Pencil` was replaced by `SquarePen` everywhere** (31 call sites, 2026-09-15, the user's instruction): the bare pencil had no shape to animate, while the boxed pen writes — `fb-icon-write` moves it diagonally and tilts it. Edit also carries **its own tone**: a `color-mix` of the info blue with the muted foreground as the resting colour (pure blue on every row would be noise, no colour at all says nothing) and the full blue on hover.
  - ⚠️ **The ancestor list includes `[role='menuitem']` and `[role='option']`**, not just `button`/`a` — a large share of the icons live in context and `ActionMenu` items. The escape hatch is `fb-icon-plain`, which works on a block *or* on a single icon. ⚠️ Verify in `dist/assets/*.css` (the `.lucide-*` classes are runtime, not Tailwind-scanned, so these rules are hand-written CSS by necessity).
- ⚠️ **Rounding is decided in exactly one place — `index.css`'s `@theme inline` block** (2026-09-12). `--radius` alone did almost nothing: only `rounded-sm/md/lg` were wired to it, while **120 call sites use `rounded-xl`/`rounded-2xl`**, which read Tailwind's own scale (0.75rem / 1rem) — so changing the token was *silently* a no-op on nearly every card, modal and panel. The block now overrides the **whole** `--radius-xs` … `--radius-4xl` namespace, and the user's standing instruction („smaller radius, more padding“) is one edit there. ⚠️ **The ceiling is 5px** (the user's literal instruction, 2026-09-12, after the first attempt's 8/10/12px still read as „too round“): `md`/`lg`/`xl`/`2xl`/`3xl`/`4xl` all resolve to `var(--radius)` = 5px, so at class level a „rounder“ option no longer exists and a new page cannot bring the big radius back by reaching for `rounded-2xl`. ⚠️ **Tailwind is not the only source** — the other three are `lib/selectStyles.ts` (react-select is inline styles, not classes; control and menu were 8/10px), `--rdp-day_button-border-radius` and the lightbox's `--yarl__thumbnails_thumbnail_border_radius` (4px, already fine). ⚠️ **The day-picker override is `.rdp-root.fb-rdp`, not `.fb-rdp`**: react-day-picker declares those variables on the *same element* under `.rdp-root` (`border-radius: 100%`), so with equal specificity source order decides — and its stylesheet is a lazy chunk that lands **after** ours. ⚠️ **Verify in `dist/assets/*.css`, not in the source**: `npm run build` then `grep -o "rounded[^{]*{[^}]*" dist/assets/index-*.css` shows what each utility actually compiles to; that is what proved the first attempt had in fact taken effect and was simply still too round. ⚠️ **`rounded-full` was the hole in all of this and was closed on 2026-09-13** (the user: „the border-radius was never fixed, it is still the same“ — and all five elements they pointed at were **chips**). The class survived in **51 places across 34 files**, i.e. exactly the pills: chips and their counters, badges, tags, status marks, progress bars, the quota bar, the `i`/required marker, the step number, the empty-state icon circle and the photo badges. All of those are `rounded-md` (= `var(--radius)` = 5px) now. ⚠️ **Thirteen usages stay round and they are a shape, not a radius**: every **avatar** (`UserAvatar` plus the hand-rolled containers on `Header`, `ProfilePage`, `PublicProfilePage`, `UserPage`, `ChatPage`, `PeoplePage` and the cast photo on `MoviePage`), `ui/switch.tsx` (**both** parts — a 5px track under a round knob is a broken control, so the two move together) and `ui/radio-group.tsx`. ⚠️ **The check that stops this coming back is `grep -rn "rounded-full" src`**: anything beyond those thirteen (plus the one explaining comment in `ui/chip.tsx`) is a regression. ⚠️ Two more non-Tailwind sources were found the same day and are now on the token: the `.fb-scroll` scrollbar (`border-radius: 9999px` → `var(--radius)`) and the lightbox's `--yarl__thumbnails_thumbnail_border_radius` (was the library's own 4px — close, but a *second* source). ⚠️ `src/App.css` still holds two hand-written radii and is **imported by nothing** — dead Vite-template leftovers, deliberately left alone.
- ⚠️ **Tasks §2 left fourteen shared pieces; a new page/form must use them, never re-roll one.** **`ui/badge.tsx`** (**`Badge`** — the small pill for a status, a rating or a score, added 2026-09-15 because the very same string `rounded-[5px] px-1.5 py-0.5 text-xs` was hand-written on the note, book, game, board-game and video lists; the user asked for more padding on „open"/„archived" and five hand edits is exactly what drifts. ⚠️ The **background colour stays the caller's** (`STATUS_BADGE` / `STATUS_TONE` / `bg-secondary`) — those maps already exist and a third copy would be born here. ⚠️ `rounded-md`, never the literal `rounded-[5px]`) · `ui/page-header.tsx` (**`PageHeader`** — a **compact, left-aligned** bar since 2026-09-11: module icon, the module name as a small eyebrow line, the section title, then the actions on the right. ⚠️ **The centred banner it replaced was built on a misreading** — the request had been about the right-hand *filter panel*, and the banner ate a third of the screen on every section. ⚠️ **The eyebrow hides itself when the title already is the module name**, or „Videos / Videos“ would show; it exists because three media domains each have a „Favourites“ section and the section name alone never said *whose*. The background still comes from **`modules.color`**, edited on `/modules/{key}`, so a page passes `module="video"` and nothing else) · `ui/empty-state.tsx` (**`EmptyState`** — the one empty block for every module: *what* is empty, *why* (filter/search vs. nothing added yet) and **the next button**. ⚠️ The buttons are the caller's: „add“, „clear the filter“ or both, so nine modules' specifics don't leak into the component) · `ui/field-label.tsx` (**`FieldLabel`**/`FieldHint` — the red required marker comes from `useModuleFields().required`, the `i` explanation from `fields.hint()` → `fields.desc.<module>.<key>`; the old hand-written `<span className="text-destructive"> *</span>` is gone) · `ui/drag-handle.tsx` + `dragRowClass()` in `lib/dragReorder.ts` (every dictionary page: video types, all five genre lists, note/bookmark categories, the six status lists, playlists) — ⚠️ **the grab cursor lives in `dragRowClass()`, not on the handle** (§1.3): `draggable` sits on the whole `<li>`, so a cursor only on the grip told the user „you cannot pick it up here“ on nine tenths of the row · `ui/duration-input.tsx` (**`DurationInput`** — minutes+seconds with an hours toggle; `unit="seconds"` for video/song, `unit="minutes"` for `movies.runtime` and board-game playtime, and the column keeps its own unit) · `lib/tags.ts` (**`dedupeTags()`** — the twin of `Video::normalizeTags()`; it runs inside `TagSelect`, so every module and board-game "mechanics" dedupe and toast without touching the forms) · `ui/date-picker.tsx` (`DatePicker`/`DateMultiPicker`/`DateRangePicker` on `react-day-picker`; the value is a **local** `YYYY-MM-DD[THH:mm]` string, drop-in for the old `<input type="date">`) · **`ui/time-picker.tsx`** (**`TimePicker`** — added 2026-09-15 on the user's request for „some library, so it's relatively modern". It replaces `<input type="time">`, which was the one field in the app that did not look like the app: the browser draws it, its clock glyph often vanishes on the dark theme and CSS cannot reach inside it. ⚠️ **The library is `react-aria-components`' `TimeField` + `@internationalized/date`, chosen because it is headless** — the markup and classes stay ours (same tokens, same radius) while the hard part (segment arrow-keys, typing, `aria`, locale) is theirs; MUI/Mantine/antd would have dragged a whole design system in. ⚠️ **It is not free: the lazy `date-picker` chunk went 22.9 → 71.7 kB gzip (+49 kB)** — that is the price of `@internationalized/date`'s calendar systems, and it is worth stating out loud next to a project that refused dnd-kit over 40 kB. It only loads on pages that show a date or time picker. ⚠️ **The value stays an `HH:mm` string** — exactly what the backend takes (`times_of_day`, `remind_at`); the `Time` object lives inside the component only, or two formats would start living in two places. ⚠️ **The quick hour/minute columns are an *expanding block*, not a popover**: this field also stands inside the calendar's popover and inside modals, and a popover-in-popover (or a layer clipped by `overflow-y-auto`) is exactly the trap `lib/layers.ts` documents) · `ui/photo-stack.tsx` (**`PhotoStack`** — one group as a fanned card; the record page's cast stacks and the gallery page's groups are the same component, §4.2) · `ui/photo-grid.tsx` (**`PhotoGrid`** — `yet-another-react-lightbox` + selection mode + fixed-aspect thumbnails; `privateDisk` reads blobs through `usePrivateFileUrl`, which is why each cell is its own component. ⚠️ **It also owns "how many to show" (10 · 20 · 30 · 40 · 50 · all, `PHOTO_PAGE_SIZES`, `0` = all)** — added 2026-09-09 because the user asked for it *everywhere a photo appears*, and one component is the only way four call sites can't disagree. ⚠️ **Since 2026-09-13 that control is `NumberPick`, not a row of six pills** (the user: „a select is better — 10 20 30 40 50 all *or other*, and you type it"), so a size outside the presets is now possible at all; `0` is labelled „all" here and „none" in the download dialog (`noneLabel`) and sits **last** in the list (`noneLast`), and a typed size is capped at **1000** — in controlled mode it travels as `per_page`, which `GalleryController::MAX_PER_PAGE` rejects above that. Two modes: pass `pageSize` **+ `onPageSizeChange` + `total`** and the caller's server does the paging (`GalleryBrowser`); pass nothing and the grid slices the list itself and renders its own pager, with the lightbox still seeing the **whole** list so arrows cross pages. ⚠️ The board-game / game / video image grids were hand-rolled (no lightbox, no selection, one opened photos in a new tab) and moved onto it the same day — a new photo grid must not be hand-rolled again. ⚠️ **An empty grid renders `EmptyState`, not a bare `<p>`** (2026-09-12): it drew one grey sentence in the four places a photo can be missing, which is exactly the case `EmptyState` was written for — `emptyIcon`/`emptyHint`/`emptyActions` stay the caller's, since „add“ and „clear the filter“ differ per page) · **`ui/number-pick.tsx`** (**`NumberPick`** — a number chosen from presets with „other" at the end of the same select, so only one control is on screen until a custom value is asked for; the gallery download dialog's per-subject limits and the photo grid's page size are the same component. ⚠️ **The meaning of `0` is the caller's**, never baked in: „none" when downloading, „all" when displaying) · **`ui/chip.tsx`** (**`Chip`**/`ChipRow` — the pill switcher, added 2026-09-12; it replaced **twelve** hand-written copies of `rounded-full border px-2.5 py-1 text-xs` that had **five different paddings** between them. ⚠️ It used to carry `rounded-full` „because a pill must stay a pill" — **that was the bug**: the chips were precisely what still looked round to the user, so since 2026-09-13 the chip and its counter are `rounded-md` like everything else) · **`ui/step-section.tsx`** (**`StepSection`** — a numbered step inside a dialog: number, title, one explanatory line and the section's action on the right; added 2026-09-13 for the two web-search dialogs, which had put context chips, the distribution block, the query, the source picker, three warnings and the grid on one scroll at equal weight, so the order of work (search → pick → download) was invisible. ⚠️ **The number is the caller's**, not the component's: the distribution step only exists when the record has cast, so „results“ is sometimes third and sometimes fourth — a number written in two places would drift) · `lib/layers.ts`. ⚠️ **`modules.color` is global, not per-user** — "the module's colour" must be the same on every account, so it is a column and not `module_user.settings`; `ModulesSeeder` only fills it **on create** (`color === null`), or a reseed would wipe the admin's choice — and **since 2026-09-13 it is read by the sidebar too**, see the next bullet. ⚠️ **The lightbox needs `--yarl__portal_zindex: 80`** (set in `index.css`): its default is 9999, which would put every dropdown *behind* it and bring §1.2's bug back inside the lightbox.
- ⚠️ **A modal must be rendered by every branch that can open it** (2026-09-13, a live bug). `GroupsCut` returns early when a group is open (`if (open) return <GroupPhotos …>`), and the „search the web for photos/videos" buttons live in **that** branch — while the two dialogs were written at the bottom of the *list* branch. So entering an actor's (or a record's) gallery group and pressing either button set the state and **nothing happened at all**; that is what „on an actor nothing opens, nothing" meant. The fix is one `const dialogs = (…)` rendered by both branches, and the rule generalises: **state that opens a portal belongs to the component, the JSX that renders it must not belong to one branch**. Pinned by `GroupsCut.test.ts`, which fails without the fix. ⚠️ Two things this was *not*, both checked live first: `GET /api/web/status` answers fine (five image sources, two video ones) and the `gallery` module is enabled — and a free Wikimedia query (`engines[]=wikimedia`, **spends nothing**: `serp_searches` stayed at 75 rows) proved the actor path works end to end on the server.

- ⚠️ **Photo actions are one list, `lib/photoActions.ts::photoActions()`** (2026-09-13). Until then a photo's actions existed only as hover icons on the cell (info · primary · delete), so on a touch screen and by keyboard they were practically unreachable and right-click opened the browser's own menu. Now the same array feeds **three** renderings of every cell: the footer icon row (`quick` items only), the `⋮` `ActionMenu` (the keyboard/touch path) and the **right-click `ContextMenu`** — `MovieCard`'s component, so no new library and no second visual language. Seven things are easy to get wrong:
  - ⚠️ **A second copy of the list drifts by construction** — one would grow „open the original" and the other wouldn't, and the user hunts for the single place that has the item. That is why the function is pure and lives in `lib/`, with `lib/photoActions.test.ts` pinning it.
  - ⚠️ **Acting on a selected photo means acting on the whole selection.** Right-clicking a checked photo says „delete **5 selected**", an unchecked one says „delete". The grid computes `targets` (`checked ? selected : [item.id]`) and the label follows `count` — an irreversible action that reads two ways is not acceptable.
  - ⚠️ **An action that cannot work is not drawn at all** (never disabled). „Make main" on a cast photo is a backend **422** (`cast_members.photo_path` is a global column) — `PhotoItem.canPrimary` is the flag, and `GalleryPanel` sets it from `category !== 'actor'`; before this the button was drawn and the click was silently swallowed by the caller. The already-primary photo loses the item too and shows a filled star **as a mark**, not as a dead button.
  - ⚠️ **On the private disk „open the original in a new tab" is dropped, but download stays.** The grid reads private files as a blob (`usePrivateFileUrl`), so a download genuinely works (the toolbar has always done it); what cannot work is a *link* — the blob dies with the page. `StorageLibrary` hides its download button for a different reason: it points straight at `/storage/*`, which cannot reach that disk.
  - ⚠️ **„Open" always means the lightbox.** The picking-mode branch lives on the image button, not in the action — while selecting, a menu item labelled „open" used to toggle the checkbox.
  - ⚠️ **The z-index comes from `ContextMenuContent`** (`LAYER_POPUP`, `z-[100]`), which is what lets the grid keep working inside a modal or the lightbox — §1.2's bug was exactly a menu drawn behind its own background.
  - ⚠️ **The group card has its own menu** (`ui/photo-stack.tsx`'s `menu` prop — the content is the caller's, since the stack cannot know what „download" means for an actor, a record or a module). In `GroupsCut` it is again **one list**, `groupActions()`: open · fetch · the actor/record page · the two web searches · **delete every photo in the group**. That last one has no single endpoint — `DELETE /gallery/images/{id}` is per photo, so it re-reads page 1 in chunks until the group is empty (with a round cap, so a silently failing delete cannot spin), and the confirmation **states the count**: in the `source`/`provider` cuts a „group" is an entire domain.

- ⚠️ **Every per-user dictionary lives on one page** (`/dictionaries/:key`, Tasks §6.3) and its entries are one registry, **`lib/dictionaries.tsx`** — video types, the five genre lists, the two category lists and (since §6.4) the **six status lists**, which are generated in a loop because they differ only by domain. ⚠️ `queryKey` there is an **array**, so a dictionary shares the exact cache key the record side reads (`useStatuses` → `['statuses', domain]`); a single string would make two caches and a rename would not show up on the list. The originals were seven near-identical pages (drag & drop, arrows, edit, delete-with-reassign) differing only by endpoint. Adding a module's dictionary = **one row there**; the old paths (`/book-genres`, …) still resolve and redirect, so saved links and sidebar entries don't break. ⚠️ The **edit** dialog stays per-module (it writes to its own endpoint and refreshes its own cache) — only the list, the ordering and the delete flow are shared. ⚠️ The delete dialog is the **game** version for all of them, and its texts are the generic `dictionaries.*` namespace — six copies of the same four phrases in six namespaces is exactly what drifts. **Since stage 8 (2026-09-13) it has three explicit choices: reassign · leave empty · delete the records too** — the last one behind a typed `DELETE` (the `/purge` word). ⚠️ **All eight `destroy()`s share `App\Support\DictionaryRecords`** (validation + the delete): records go **through the model** (`lazyById` → `$record->delete()`), so files, quota, gallery and the audit log are released by the same hooks `PurgeService` relies on, and `move_to` + `delete_records` together is a **422**, never a silent pick. ⚠️ **And so does the *move* branch since Tasks BUG-05 — `DictionaryRecords::move()`, the delete branch's exact twin.** It was a query-builder `update()`, i.e. the same dialog behaved two different ways, and two things went missing: **`watched_at`** (only `HasStatus::applyStatus()` writes it, so deleting a `done` status with a move to `todo` left every record carrying „when I watched it" while its status said the opposite) and **the audit log** (`AuditObserver` sits on model events, so „a hundred of my films changed status" appeared nowhere). ⚠️ The task allowed one explicit `AuditLogger` row instead of a loop for the five plain dictionaries; the loop was chosen because the status case needed it anyway and **two mechanisms on one dialog's two branches is what drifts** — `PurgeService` sets the precedent that a hundred records is a hundred log rows. ⚠️ It takes a `callable $apply` rather than a column name, so the call site says *what* changes (`$r->genre_id = …` vs `$r->applyStatus($target)`) and `App\Support` needs no model knowledge. ⚠️ **On a pivot (song/game genres, `multi: true` in the registry) „this genre's records" includes those that also have other genres** — they are deleted, the number shown is the one deleted, and the dialog says so. The frontend body is built once (`api/dictionary.ts` → `removalBody()`), never in the eight API files. ⚠️ **`/dictionaries` is a card index since stage 8** (two groups: statuses · genres/types/categories; module icon + colour, first five entries, count, „N hidden"), reachable from its own sidebar link; the row of ~14 pills is gone, and an unknown/disabled `:key` redirects **to the index**, not to „the first dictionary" (which silently opened a different one). ⚠️ **`recordsQueryKey` is the list page's cache key, not the domain** — it was `domain` for statuses, so a note/bookmark/video status change invalidated `['note']` while the list lives under `['notes']`. ⚠️ `useDragReorder` accepts **string ids** too (the pseudo-sections have no numeric id); `onDrop` looks the id up in the list, because `Number('favorite')` is `NaN`.
- ⚠️ **The sidebar never lists a dictionary's *entries*** (Tasks §2.7): one rule for every module — **all → statuses → favourite → add**. Video types and song genres used to be listed there and were removed 2026-09-07, together with the two queries that fed them; the full lists belong to the filter panel, which is where they are actually used. ⚠️ Since 2026-09-15 it does not link to the dictionary *page* either — see the sidebar bullet above.
- ⚠️ **A dictionary's name must say which module it belongs to, because in `/dictionaries` it stands next to thirteen others** (2026-09-15 — the same reason `statuses.title*` is per-domain and not a shared „Statuses"). Three were renamed with the sidebar links: „მუსიკის ჟანრები" → **„სიმღერის ჟანრები"** (the module is „სიმღერები"; „Music genres" → „Song genres"), and note/bookmark categories moved to the singular possessive the statuses already used („ჩანაწერის", „ბუკმარკის"). ⚠️ **`dictionaries.navHint` was an empty string in `ka`** — i.e. the index page's subtitle was half a sentence (and, while it lasted, the sidebar's sub-line was blank), in the one language the repo is written in. The audit reports missing and one-sided keys, **not empty values**, so it had been silently green; `python -c` over both locale files is what finds these.
- ⚠️ **`TagSelect` swallows Enter** (Tasks §1.3). Enter used to reach the surrounding form and submit it („The url field is required."), because react-select only calls `preventDefault()` when its menu is open with a focused option. The guard is a wrapping `<div onKeyDown>`, **not** react-select's `onKeyDown` prop: the prop runs *before* react-select's own logic and it bails out on `defaultPrevented`, which would stop the tag from being created at all.
- TMDB search is English/Latin only — Georgian titles won't match; rely on the candidates picker, the ge.movie link (Latin slug), or hand entry — machine translation fills the *description*, not the search.

## Tasks

Current backlog lives in `Tasks.md` (repo root) — **read it at the start of every session**. ⚠️ **`docs/DECISIONS.md` was deleted on 2026-09-14 at the user's explicit instruction ("ყველაფერი წაშალე აბსოლიტურად"), and `docs/` is gone with it — the repo now has no design docs at all.** There is no separate place for open questions any more: **a question that needs the user's answer is written inside the `Tasks.md` task it belongs to**, and once answered the decision stays there (or moves into this file, when the work is done immediately) — one source, never the same decision in two files. ⚠️ **The "❌ წაშლილი / აღარ კეთდება" list went with the file**, so the guard against re-proposing a closed decision is now this file plus git history: before suggesting anything as "new work", check here first (the link bot, the reminders' email channel, games' DLC/patches, image scraping and Georgian book search were all closed deliberately).

- ⚠️ **`Tasks.md` was refilled and emptied again on 2026-09-15**: the user's next batch was §21 (per-user API keys and limits), §22 (database dump/restore from the admin side) and §23 (colours and animations for the twelve non-module sections). All three are built and their load-bearing rules live in this file — see *Per-user credentials*, *Database backup* and *Non-module section colours and animations* above. Two things were verified live rather than only in tests: a real 82-table dump (810 kB gzipped, 0.5 s) restored into a scratch database, and the credentials page reading a personal key with its own quota counter.
- ⚠️ **`Tasks.md` was refilled and emptied again on 2026-09-15 (second batch of the day).** The user's ask was five sections — §24 (gallery cuts as scope cards), §25 (bulk delete: the per-record scope in every module, drill-down and „keep the gallery"), §26 (an uncategorized gallery section with albums and photo moving), §27 (a better general name and ordering for the records cut) and §28 (one shared view control across every cut). All five are built; their load-bearing rules are the two blocks above (*Gallery scope cards…* and *Bulk delete — the per-record scope…*). ⚠️ **That batch also opened the repo's branch history**: everything was committed, `main` was fast-forwarded and every other branch deleted at the user's instruction, and the work continued on **`improve/gallery-and-bulk-delete`**.
- ⚠️ **`Tasks.md` is now an empty file** (emptied 2026-09-15 at the user's instruction: „if anything is left in the tasks, do it, then clear the file — keep the file, delete the content completely"). Nothing was lost by that: the 2026-09-13 batch and the 2026-09-14 audit were already finished, and every load-bearing rule from them lives here. ⚠️ **The one item that was still open was *not* code** — registering MariaDB as a Windows service — and it needs an elevated shell, which no session here has had; it is written out in full under *MariaDB crash recovery* above, which is now its only home. ⚠️ **The health snapshot that used to sit at the bottom of `Tasks.md` is gone on purpose**: it was a copy of what `php artisan test` / `npm test` / `pint` / the i18n audit say in a second, and a stale copy of a number is worse than no copy (last run, 2026-09-15: **578** backend tests, **103** frontend tests, pint, build, oxlint and the i18n audit all green). ⚠️ **That batch's text was never committed, so it is not in git history either** — this file is its only record. The next batch comes from the user. ⚠️ **The section numbers this file cites elsewhere (`§7.6`, `§16.3`, `§2.5`, `§5.7`…) are addresses of *finished* work, kept so a rule can be traced back; they are not current tasks and no longer resolve to anything in `Tasks.md`.**
- ⚠️ **Since 2026-09-17 a finished task is *not* deleted from `Tasks.md` — it gets a status** (the user's instruction when the 64-task audit backlog started; it replaces the older „completed items are deleted" rule). The Summary table has a status column (⬜ open · 🟡 partial — something is left for the user or needs a permission · ✅ done) and each task section a `სტატუსი` line saying what was done and what remains. ⚠️ **Ask before starting every next task** — never chain from one ID into the next on your own. What is worth remembering still moves into this file.
- ⚠️ **`AUDIT-2026-09-14.md` (repo root) is the one leftover companion file** — the long prose version of the 2026-09-14 technical audit, written as a companion to a `Tasks.md` that is now empty, and **untracked by git**. Every rule it produced already lives in this file (§A1–§A5, §B1–§B5, §D1/§D6 above), so it is history rather than reference; it was left in place only because deleting an untracked file destroys it outright.
- ⚠️ **"ამიღე" from the user means *remove*, not *take on*.** Asked to take a point "out of the tasks", delete it — an old section was built once by mistake on the opposite reading and had to be reverted whole.
- **The old "link bot" (automatic discovery of watch links on Georgian/foreign sites) was deleted on 2026-09-05 by the user's decision** — the section, its research doc (`docs/G1-georgian-link-sources.md`) and every trace of code are gone. Don't propose it as pending work.

Their load-bearing rules survive in this file (see *Field builder* and *Auth & multi-tenancy* above), but **the step-by-step recipe for adding a module is gone** — the last written trace is the anime module's build in git history. ⚠️ **The `docs/DECISIONS.md §N` citations elsewhere in this file no longer resolve** (chat media §1 · custom fields §2 · multi-genre songs §5 · IGDB §7 · bookmarks §10) — they are kept only as provenance for a rule, exactly like the `Tasks.md §N` addresses.

## graphify

This project has a knowledge graph at graphify-out/ with god nodes, community structure, and cross-file relationships.

Current state (2026-09-19, after the 2026-09-18 audit backlog): **6599 nodes, 14961 edges, 496 communities**. ⚠️ Past 5000 nodes `graph.html` switches to an **aggregated** community view (496 nodes) — node-level detail needs `--obsidian`. Dated backups of previous graphs sit in `graphify-out/<date>/`.

⚠️ **Invoke it as `python -m graphify <cmd>`.** The bare `graphify` name resolves to an extensionless path Bash cannot execute (`Permission denied`), and `graphify.exe` is blocked by this machine's Application Control policy. `python -m graphify.cli` is the wrong module (exit 255).

Rules:
- For codebase questions, first run `python -m graphify query "<question>"` when graphify-out/graph.json exists. Use `python -m graphify path "<A>" "<B>"` for relationships and `python -m graphify explain "<concept>"` for focused concepts. These return a scoped subgraph, usually much smaller than GRAPH_REPORT.md or raw grep output.
- If graphify-out/wiki/index.md exists, use it for broad navigation instead of raw source browsing.
- Read graphify-out/GRAPH_REPORT.md only for broad architecture review or when query/path/explain do not surface enough context.
- After modifying code, run `python -m graphify update .` to keep the graph current (AST-only, no API cost). Add `--force` after refactors that delete code, or the rebuild refuses to shrink the graph.
- Known warnings, both harmless: `.sql` files contribute nothing without `tree_sitter_sql` (`backend/mediary_backup.sql`), and community labels drift after a rebuild — `python -m graphify label` refreshes them but **needs an LLM API key, so ask before running it**.
